# Appendix D — Safety red-team: all 58 findings with verifier verdicts
Six attackers (bypass inventory, tokens, clamps/show-mode, panic, CFS² writes, LLM tool surface), each finding independently re-verified twice (reproduction; source-level refutation attempt). Consensus severity is the median of the verifiers'. Worktree paths refer to the review machine and are kept only as provenance.

## D1. [critical] Main LR/M-C (and any bus) can be raised +24 dB with one unconfirmed Tier-1 call: compressor make-up gain (and +15 dB EQ) are outside the 'main is Tier 2' guard
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* critical · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** device.yaml:190 (dyn/mgain tier 1, scale mgain 0..24 at :83), device.yaml:708-710 (guarded globs cover only /main/st/mix/* and /main/m/mix/*), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1111-1132 (set_comp: no policy clamp on makeup), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1050-1081 (set_eq_band: clamp_eq_gain = ±15 = full hardware range, no ramp/relative limit), src/x32mcp/server.py:1018-1039, 983-1002, src/x32mcp/policy.py:275

**Mechanism.** The Tier-2 protection of the main outputs is implemented as fnmatch globs on /main/*/mix/*. /main/st/dyn/* and /main/st/eq/* are ordinary tier-1 params, Desk._write only refuses tier>=2, and Policy has no clamp for dyn/mgain at all (only the descriptor's 0..24 dB scale) and a ±15 dB 'clamp' for EQ gain that equals the desk's range. set_comp(on=True, threshold_db=0, makeup_db=24) therefore turns the main-bus compressor into a +24 dB gain stage; set_eq_band adds +15 dB in-band on top. Neither is ramped, relative-limited or confirmed; both also apply to wedge buses/matrices/channels (feedback) and both survive show mode.

**Exploit.** set_comp(target='main.st', on=true, threshold_db=0, ratio=1.1, makeup_db=24) → ok:true, /main/st/dyn/on ON, /main/st/dyn/mgain 24.0. Then set_eq_band(target='main.st', band=3, type='PEQ', freq_hz=2500, gain_db=99, on=true) → ok:true, gain clamped to +15. Same for 'main.m' and 'bus.1'. (set_fader('main.st', 0) is correctly refused GUARDED.)

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_bypass.py::test_exploit_main_lr_plus_24db_without_confirmation — output: 'EXPLOIT-1: main.st dyn/mgain 0.0 -> 24.0 eq/3/g -> 15.0 with zero confirmations'; fakedesk /bus/01/dyn/mgain == 24.0 and main.m likewise; the fader call returned error GUARDED.

**Proposed fix.** Treat make-up gain and EQ boost as level moves: add a policy clamp for dyn/mgain (e.g. makeup_max_db 6 on Tier 1) and guard '/main/*/dyn/*' and '/main/*/eq/*' (device.yaml guarded list) so main dynamics/EQ need the token; apply the relative limit (or a ramp) to EQ/makeup boosts on buses; consider lowering eq_gain_abs_max_db for boosts (asymmetric: cuts to -15, boosts ≤ +6 without force).

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-bypass-0-repro (commit c2761e0, tests/integration/test_verify_bypass0.py, 8 passed). Through the real server tools on a FakeDesk: `await srv.set_comp('main.st', on=True, threshold_db=0, ratio=1.1, makeup_db=24)` returns ok:true with no requires_confirmation, and the fake desk shows /main/st/dyn/on False->True, /main/st/dyn/thr 0.0, /main/st/dyn/mgain 0.0->24.0 (single unramped write each). `await srv.set_eq_band('main.st', 3, type='PEQ', freq_hz=2500, gain_db=99, on=True)` returns ok:true, clamped to +15.0, /main/st/eq/3/g 0->15, /main/st/eq/on True. Identical results for main.m, bus.1, mtx.1, ch.1. Control: `srv.set_fader('main.st', 0)` -> error GUARDED (/main/st/mix/fader), proving only the mix/* path is protected. With `srv.show_mode(True)` both calls still succeed (mgain 24, eq g 15). Static check: policy.tier_for('/main/st/dyn/mgain') == 1, tier_for('/main/st/eq/3/g') == 1, tier_for('/main/st/mix/fader') == 2; Policy has no makeup clamp attribute at all. Root cause matches the claim: device.yaml guarded globs (lines ~708-720) cover only /main/st/mix/* and /main/m/mix/*; blk_dyn_core dyn/mgain is tier 1 with scale mgain 0..24 (device.yaml:83, :190); Desk._write (desk.py:871-875) only refuses tier>=2; set_comp (desk.py:1111-1132) -> _write_params has no policy clamp; set_eq_band (desk.py:1050-1081) uses clamp_eq_gain = ±eq_gain_abs_max_db=15 = full hardware range; server.py set_comp/set_eq_band (983-1039) add no token, ramp, or relative check. Semantically on the X32, dyn on + thr 0 dB + ratio 1.1 + mgain 24 is a +24 dB static gain stage on the main output (compression never engages below 0 dBFS), so this is an instant, unconfirmed +24 dB (plus +15 dB in-band EQ, ~+39 dB at 2.5 kHz) on Main LR — squarely 'loud level change / unconfirmed action on main' = critical. Caveat for honesty: BRIEF.md:64/69 scope Tier 2 to 'main LR/mono fader+mute', so this is a gap in the guard's design rather than a deviation from the written spec; bounded (24+15 dB), not unbounded. Severity stands at critical because the audible outcome is the same as an unconfirmed main fader slam.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-0-repro, test tests/integration/test_verify_bypass0.py (commit c2761e0), run via runtests.sh -s: '8 passed in 2.33s'. Output lines: "SET_COMP main.st {'ok': True, 'summary': \"Main LR 'Main' comp: on, threshold 0.0 dB, ratio 1.1, makeup +24.0 dB\" ...}"; "SET_EQ main.st {'ok': True, ... 'gain_db': 15.0}, 'clamped': [{'value': 15.0, 'requested': 99.0, ...}]}"; "REPRO bypass-0 main.st: dyn/on False->True dyn/mgain 0.0->24.0 eq/3/g 0.0->15.0 with zero confirmation tokens" (same line for main.m, bus.1, mtx.1, ch.1); "SET_FADER main.st {'ok': False, 'error': {'code': 'GUARDED', ... 'address': '/main/st/mix/fader'}}"; "SHOWMODE SET_COMP {'ok': True ... makeup +24.0 dB}", "SHOWMODE SET_EQ {'ok': True ... gain +15.0 dB}" with fakedesk /main/st/dyn/mgain==24.0 and /main/st/eq/3/g==15.0 under show mode; "tier /main/st/dyn/mgain = 1", "tier /main/st/eq/3/g = 1", "tier /main/st/mix/fader= 2". Code refs: src/x32mcp/desk.py:871-875 (_write tier gate only), 1050-1081 (set_eq_band, clamp ±15), 1111-1132 (set_comp -> _write_params, no clamp); src/x32mcp/policy.py:238-240 (tier_for = descriptor globs), 275-283 (clamp_eq_gain ±eq_gain_abs_max_db=15); src/x32mcp/server.py:983-1002, 1018-1039 (Tier-1 tools, no token/ramp); device.yaml:83 (mgain 0..24), :190 (dyn/mgain tier 1), guarded list (/main/st/mix/*, /main/m/mix/* only), policy eq_gain_abs_max_db: 15. Result note: $SP/reports/verify-bypass-0-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix; concretely: (1) add '/main/*/dyn/*' and '/main/*/eq/*' to device.yaml `guarded` so main dynamics/EQ go through the token dance (and add confirming tool variants or a confirm_token param on set_comp/set_eq_band when target family is main); (2) add policy.makeup_max_db (e.g. 6) with a clamp in Desk.set_comp for all families, force/confirm to exceed; (3) make eq gain asymmetric (cuts to -15 free, boosts > +6 require force, tighter in show mode) and apply check_relative to the delta of eq gain / makeup vs the cached current value on output strips (bus/mtx/main); (4) optionally treat dyn/on False->True with nonzero mgain as a level move of +mgain dB for the relative check.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap:
(1) device.yaml:182-196 (&blk_dyn_core) declares dyn/on … dyn/mgain tier: 1 with scale mgain lin 0..24 dB (device.yaml:83); &blk_eq (device.yaml:213-218) declares eq/{band}/g tier: 1, clamp ±15 = the full eq_gain scale (device.yaml:71). The `main:` family (device.yaml:259-269) merges *blk_dyn_core, *blk_dyn_filter, *blk_eq unchanged and only overrides mix/* and mix/{send}/* to tier 2; its own comment says "all mix/* Tier 2". The guarded globs (device.yaml:708-710) are "/main/st/mix/*" and "/main/m/mix/*" only. Descriptor.tier_for (descriptor.py:1041-1046) = 2 if guarded glob else the param tier → /main/st/dyn/mgain, /main/st/dyn/on, /main/st/eq/N/g are Tier 1.
(2) server.set_comp (server.py:1018-1039) and server.set_eq_band (server.py:983-1002) take no confirm_token, never call _confirm/policy.guard, never check show mode or family, and call desk.set_comp / desk.set_eq_band directly.
(3) desk.set_comp (desk.py:1111-1132) → _write_params → _write_value → spec.to_raw (Scale.clamp silently clamps to 0..24, scales.py:403-430; no Policy clamp exists for make-up anywhere in policy.py) → Desk._write (desk.py:871-884), whose only gate is `tier >= GUARDED` → refuse; Tier 1 passes, then ensure_pre_write_snapshot, acquire_write (8 writes ≪ 50/s), conn.set fire-and-forget. No ramp, no check_relative (that lives only in set_level/adjust_level), no family check. desk.set_eq_band (desk.py:1050-1081) applies policy.clamp_eq_gain (policy.py:275-283) whose limit eq_gain_abs_max_db=15 (device.yaml:728) equals the hardware range, i.e. it never bites; again no ramp/relative limit/confirmation.
(4) Show mode: Policy.check_show_mode_allows only blocks scene_recall/scene_save/setup_ringout_eqs/ring_out/ring_out_system and only tightens relative_limit_db, which set_comp/set_eq_band never consult → identical behaviour with show mode on (verified).
(5) Real desk vs FakeDesk: the FakeDesk only stores parameters, but the write is a plain `/main/st/dyn/mgain ,f 1.0` / `/main/st/dyn/on ,i 1` which a real X32 accepts — /main/st/dyn/* is the console's MXDY set and appears in real console scene files (docs/research/scales_params.md:604,614 `/main/st/dyn ON COMP RMS LOG -15.5 … 0.00 …`; §4.5 mgain linf [0,24,0.5], VERIFIED from PDF/X32.c). X32 make-up gain is a static output gain of the dynamics stage whenever dyn is IN, independent of gain reduction; with thr 0 dBFS / ratio 1.1 (or simply on quiet programme at any threshold) the main L/R bus jumps +24 dB pre-fader, instantly (no ramp), typically into bus/DAC clip on a PA. A +15 dB PEQ at 2.5 kHz stacks to ≈+39 dB in band and is a feedback trigger on wedge buses. Nothing in connection.py filters addresses. So a real desk behaves the same; only the audio consequence (which the fake cannot model) is inferred from documented X32 behaviour.
(6) Why not refuted as "works as designed": BRIEF.md §4 does put "EQ/dyn" in Tier 1 and only "main LR/mono fader+mute" in Tier 2, so the code matches the written design — but the review brief §3 asks whether anything dangerous gets past Policy, and the server's own INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:114-118) tell the model that Tier-1 tools including set_comp and set_eq_band are "clamped …, ramped (default 300 ms) and limited to +-6 dB per call (+-3 dB in show mode)", which is false for both. The most-protected outputs (main fader clamped to ≤0 dB even WITH a token) can be driven +24 dB broadband / ≈+39 dB in-band by one or two unconfirmed, unramped Tier-1 calls that survive show mode. That is a loud level change on the PA reachable by a confused/runaway client in a single call → meets the 'critical' bar. Mitigations that exist: panic() still silences it (mix/on path untouched), the auto pre-write snapshot allows restore_snapshot (Tier 2) to undo, and make-up is bounded at +24 dB (not unbounded). Minor corrections to the claim: makeup_db beyond 24 is silently clamped by the Scale (not reported as clamped); 'ratio' is typed float in the tool but 1.1 maps to the '1.1' token fine; everything else in the claim checked out exactly.
>
> *Evidence:* Executed in worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-0-src (branch wt/verify-bypass-0-src, commit a35a8f0), test tests/integration/test_verify_bypass0.py::test_main_makeup_and_eq_boost_unconfirmed (FakeDesk + make_app + `await srv.set_comp(...)`), 1 passed. Output: `set_comp main.st -> True "Main LR 'Main' comp: on, threshold 0.0 dB, ratio 1.1, makeup +24.0 dB"`; `EXPLOIT main.st: dyn/on False->True mgain 0.0->24.0`; `set_eq_band main.st -> True "... EQ band 3: on, type PEQ, freq 2.52 kHz, gain +15.0 dB (gain clamped to ±15 dB)"`; identical ok:true results and fakedesk state (dyn/on ON, dyn/thr 0.0, dyn/mgain 24.0, eq/3/g +15.0) for main.m, bus.1 and mtx.1; control `set_fader main.st 0` -> ok:false GUARDED '/main/st/mix/fader'. With show_mode(true): set_comp(main.st, makeup_db=0) then makeup_db=24 both ok:true (0→+24 dB in one call), set_eq_band band 2 gain -15 then +15 (30 dB swing in one call) ok:true, policy.pending_confirmations == 0 throughout — zero tokens minted. Code refs: device.yaml:83,182-196,213-218,259-269,708-710,728; descriptor.py:1041-1046; desk.py:871-884 (_write gate is tier>=2 only), 1050-1081, 1083-1093, 1111-1132; server.py:983-1002, 1018-1039, 114-118 (INSTRUCTIONS promise ramp/±6 dB for these tools); policy.py:275-283 (clamp_eq_gain = hardware range), 197-207 (show mode list). Real-desk ground truth: docs/research/scales_params.md:407-438 (mgain linf [0,24,0.5] VERIFIED), :604,:614 (/main/st/dyn exists on the console, real scene line with dyn ON).
>
> *Better fix:* Treat gain-producing processor parameters as level moves, not free Tier-1 knobs: (a) add '/main/*/dyn/*' and '/main/*/eq/*' (at least dyn/on, dyn/mgain, dyn/thr, eq/*/g) to device.yaml `guarded:` so main dynamics/EQ go through the token dance like the main fader, and give server.set_comp/set_eq_band a confirm_token path when target.family == 'main' (mirroring set_fader→set_main_fader); (b) add a Policy.clamp_makeup (e.g. policy.makeup_max_db: 6, force/confirm to exceed) applied in Desk.set_comp for every family, and make EQ boosts asymmetric (cuts to −15, boosts ≤ +6 dB unless force) via clamp_eq_gain; (c) apply check_relative to the delta of mgain and of eq gain versus the cached current value so a single call cannot swing 24/30 dB, and have show mode tighten it; (d) fix INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:114-118) so it no longer claims set_comp/set_eq_band are ramped and ±6 dB-limited, or make it true. Also consider the same treatment for preamp trim (+18 dB, Tier 1) which is another unclamped gain stage.

## D2. [critical] panic() is defeated by writers already in flight: a confirmed restore (and ramps, and CFS) keep writing after panic and re-unmute every output
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* critical · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1220 (panic: does not touch self._ramps, no latch), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1312-1341 (restore loop writes '/bus/NN/mix ON …', '/main/st/mix ON …' lines with no re-check), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:1201-1227 (restore_plan renders whole mix sections incl. mix/on), src/x32mcp/server.py:1062-1074 (server.panic does not call cfs.abort), src/x32mcp/desk.py:922-946 (_run_ramp continues after panic)

**Mechanism.** Desk.restore dumps the live desk once, builds all lines, then writes them one limiter slot at a time (a whole-desk restore is seconds to a minute). The MCP SDK serves tool calls concurrently, so panic() can land mid-restore: it mutes the 24 outputs, but the restore task carries on and its later lines for bus/mtx/main mix sections contain 'ON' plus the snapshot fader level, so every output panic muted is switched back on (and main is put back to its snapshot level). panic likewise leaves per-address ramp tasks and a running CFS² ring-out untouched (see the CFS finding).

**Exploit.** snapshot_desk → (desk drifts) → restore_snapshot(id) → restore_snapshot(id, confirm_token) [runs ~2 s+] ; while it runs the operator shouts stop → panic() → 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted' … restore finishes → Main LR, M/C, all 16 buses and 6 matrices are ON again at snapshot levels.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_bypass.py::test_exploit_panic_defeated_by_inflight_restore — panic returned count 24 and /main/st/mix/on was OFF right after it; after the restore completed: 'EXPLOIT-2: after panic + the rest of the restore, outputs back ON: [main/st, main/m, bus/01…bus/16, mtx/01…mtx/06] main fader -0.0098' (main had been at -40 dB before the restore; it is back at 0 dB and unmuted).

**Proposed fix.** Give panic a latch: Desk.panic sets a panic epoch/flag, cancels self._ramps, and Desk._write/restore refuse (until an explicit clear, e.g. the next deliberate unmute/set_main_mute) any write that sets an output mix/on to ON or raises a main/bus/mtx level; restore checks the flag between lines and aborts with 'aborted: panic'; server.panic must also await app.cfs.abort('panic'). Cheapest first step: restore loop `if self._panic_epoch != epoch_at_start: aborted='panic'; break` plus ramp cancellation plus cfs.abort.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-1-repro (branch wt/verify-bypass-1-repro, commit a2e76ae), test file tests/integration/test_verify_bypass1.py, 4/4 pass against the unmodified product code (FakeDesk + real App + server tools).

Mechanism verified by code read and execution:
- Desk.restore (src/x32mcp/desk.py:1312-1341) dumps once, builds every line via nodes.restore_plan (src/x32mcp/nodes.py:1201-1227, one whole-section line per differing section, rendered from the SNAPSHOT values, so a bus/mtx/main 'mix' line carries 'ON <snapshot fader> ...'), then loops `acquire_write(); conn.slash(line)` with no re-check of anything; the only early exit is NotConnected.
- Desk.panic (desk.py:1184-1220) only sends mix/on 0 to the 24 outputs; it sets no flag/epoch, does not touch self._ramps, and server.panic (server.py:1062-1074) only calls desk.panic() (no app.cfs.abort).
- Concurrency is real: the MCP SDK dispatcher spawns one task per request (.venv/.../mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:_spawn -> tg.start_soon at :599-606), there is no lock in _tool (server.py:312-328) or Desk, and the dashboard is a second caller anyway.

What actually happened when executed:
1) test_panic_mid_restore_outputs_come_back_on: snapshot (all outputs ON, main 0 dB) -> drift 32 channels (fader/eq/pan/8 sends) + all 16 bus faders, 6 mtx faders, Main LR and M/C fader to -40 -> confirmed whole-desk restore started as a task -> once ch.01 was restored (main still at -40, restore not done) srv.panic() returned ok count=24 in 0.2 ms at t=0.28 s and the FakeDesk showed all 24 mix/on = 0 -> the restore kept running and finished at t=6.16 s: ok, 344/344 lines, no abort -> FakeDesk: all 24 outputs mix/on = 1 again and /main/st/mix/fader = -0.01 dB (it was -40 dB before the restore and had just been panic-muted). Exactly the claimant's result.
2) test_panic_mid_restore_only_main_drifted (narrower, realistic drift: channels + Main LR fader only): after panic mid-restore and completion, only /main/st/mix/on came back ON, at 0 dB; the 22 buses/mtx and Main M/C whose mix sections had not drifted stayed muted. So 'every output' is re-unmuted only when every output's mix section is in the plan; but Main LR unmuted and raised to its snapshot level seconds after a panic is by itself the critical outcome.
3) test_panic_before_confirm_then_restore_unmutes_everything (broader trigger, no output drift needed): drift only ch.01, mint token (preview says 1 change), panic (24 muted), then call restore_snapshot with the token: accepted (token binds only {id, scope}, not the previewed diff), restore now sees the 24 muted outputs as drift and writes 25 lines in 85 ms -> all 24 outputs ON. A panic that lands anywhere between preview and the restore's internal dump is fully undone.
4) test_panic_does_not_cancel_inflight_ramp: a 1.5 s bus.1 fader ramp -30 -> -5 dB; panic mid-ramp; desk.stats['ramps']==1 after panic, ramp completed to -5.0 dB, bus stayed mix/on=0. Confirms ramps are untouched; not audible by itself (output stays muted) but the level lands high for the later unmute.
CFS part (server.panic not calling cfs.abort) confirmed by code read only (server.py:1064-1074); I did not run a ring-out-in-flight test for it, and CFS writes bus fader/GEQ, not mix/on, so it does not itself re-unmute.

Severity: critical on the given scale — panic is defeated: a confirmed restore (a routine 'undo', allowed even in show mode) that is in flight, or confirmed just after the panic, switches Main LR (and any other output in the plan) back ON and can simultaneously raise its fader to the snapshot level (-40 -> 0 dB observed), seconds after 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted' was reported. The same restore line would equally override a physical/X32-Edit mute of those outputs made mid-restore, since the line rewrites the whole mix section from the snapshot. Conditions to state honestly: (a) the output's mix section must be in the restore plan — it differed from the snapshot at the restore's dump (fader/on/pan/st drift), or the panic landed before that dump (then all 24 are in the plan); (b) whole-desk or output-covering scope. Both are ordinary in the documented snapshot->soundcheck->restore workflow.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-1-repro, commit a2e76ae, file tests/integration/test_verify_bypass1.py (run: $SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_verify_bypass1.py -s -p no:randomly -> 4 passed in 15.82s). Printed results:
(1) VERIFY-BYPASS-1 REPORT: panic {count:24, delivered:'sent', elapsed_ms:0.2} at 0.28 s; outputs_on_right_after_panic: [] ; main_st_on_after_panic: 0; restore_result {ok:true, lines:344, written:344, failed:[], aborted:null, duration_ms:5966.2} done at 6.16 s; outputs_on_after_restore: all 24 (main/st, main/m, bus/01..16, mtx/01..06); main_st_fader_after_restore: -0.0098 (was -40 before restore); main_st_on_after_restore: 1.
(2) main-only drift: on_after_restore ['/main/st/mix/on'], main_fader -0.0098, restore ok 321/321.
(3) panic between preview and confirm: token accepted, 25/25 lines in 85 ms, 24 outputs ON after restore.
(4) ramp: ramps_after_panic 1, fader_at_panic -28.96, fader_final -5.01, bus1_on 0, set_fader ok 'Bus 1 fader -30.0 dB -> -5.0 dB (ramped 1500 ms)'.
Code: desk.py:1312-1341 restore loop (no panic check; only NotConnected breaks), desk.py:1184-1220 panic (no latch, self._ramps untouched), nodes.py:1201-1227 restore_plan renders whole mix sections from snapshot values (ON + fader), server.py:1064-1074 panic tool (desk.panic only, no cfs.abort), server.py:1240-1266 restore_snapshot token payload = {id, scope} only, mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:599-606,_spawn -> tg.start_soon (concurrent tool calls). Notes JSON: $SP/reports/verify-bypass-1-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Claimant's fix is right; concretely: (1) Desk.panic sets self._panic_epoch += 1 (and a sticky self._panicked flag), cancels and awaits all self._ramps, and server.panic also awaits app.cfs.abort('panic'). (2) Desk.restore captures the epoch before its dump and checks `if self._panic_epoch != epoch0: aborted = 'panic'; break` before every conn.slash (cheap, closes the 6 s window to one line). (3) Because a single already-dequeued line can still carry 'ON', additionally have restore (or render_node_line for bus/mtx/main mix sections while panicked) never write mix/on=ON for _PANIC_FAMILIES while the panic flag is set — restore those sections with on=OFF and report 'outputs left muted (panic)'; clear the flag only via an explicit unmute/set_main_mute. (4) Bind the restore token to the previewed change set (e.g. include a hash of the diff or at least the count/sections in the guard payload) or re-diff after confirm and refuse if output mix/on states changed since preview, so a panic between preview and confirm is not silently undone (test 3). (5) _write/_move_level should likewise refuse output mix/on->ON and main/bus/mtx level raises while panicked, so ramps/CFS restarts cannot undo it either.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Core causal chain (restore in flight undoes panic) holds end to end in source and I reproduced it; two sub-claims are overstated (ramps and CFS keep writing but never touch mix/on, so they do not re-unmute).

Chain, verified in code:
1. server.restore_snapshot (src/x32mcp/server.py:1240-1268) after the token: asyncio.wait_for(desk.restore(...), 30 s + sections/40) under @_tool(timeout=None). Desk.restore (desk.py:1312-1341) does ONE live dump (desk.py:648-666 always sweeps the desk, never the cache), restore_plan (nodes.py:1201-1227) emits one whole-section node line per section that differs from that dump, then loops `acquire_write(); conn.slash(line)` with no re-check of anything; only RequestTimeout/NotConnected are handled. Output mix sections render as e.g. `/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0` / `/bus/01/mix ON -10.0 ...` (mix/on + absolute fader in one un-ramped line) and _restore_sort_key orders families ch, auxin, fxrtn, bus, mtx, main -> the output mix lines are the LAST written, maximising the window.
2. Desk.panic (desk.py:1184-1220) only fires 24 `mix/on 0` sets + cache invalidation: no flag/epoch, does not touch self._ramps, and server.panic (server.py:1062-1074) does not call app.cfs.abort. Nothing in Desk._write/restore consults any panic state.
3. Concurrency is real at the server: mcp 2.2 JSONRPCDispatcher._dispatch_request spawns every tools/call into a task group (site-packages/mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:592-606; only 'initialize' is inline, runner.py:496/733); server.py has no lock in _tool (server.py:312-327). So a panic request dispatched while a restore request is pending interleaves with the restore loop.
4. Which outputs come back ON = exactly those whose mix section line is in the plan and not yet written when panic lands: (a) sections that differed from the snapshot at dump time in ANY field (fader/pan/st/mono/mlevel drift is enough — the whole line incl. ON is rewritten), or (b) sections dumped AFTER panic muted them (then all 24 differ on mix/on and all 24 are rewritten ON). The claimant got 'every output' only because their test drifted all 24 faders; with only Main LR drifted, only Main LR is re-unmuted (my probe 1) — but that is the worst one: un-ramped -40 -> 0 dB AND unmuted, after panic returned ok/24 muted.
5. Stronger, sequential variant (no concurrency, any client): preview -> token (binds only {id, scope}, TTL 300 s, device.yaml:738) -> panic() -> the already-agreed confirm call: restore re-plans against the now-muted desk and writes ON to all 24 outputs plus the snapshot main level, although the preview the user agreed to listed 1 change (probe 4: previewed 1 change, wrote 24 lines, 24/24 ON, main 0 dB). This is squarely the 'confused/runaway LLM holding a valid token' threat model and needs no client concurrency.
Mitigations/limits found: (i) stdio transport, single client (server.py:1949); the concurrent path needs a client that issues panic while keeping the restore call pending. A client that sends notifications/cancelled on interrupt/timeout (TS SDK does on abort/timeout) makes the dispatcher (peer_cancel_mode='interrupt', jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:623-628) cancel the handler; CancelledError propagates out of restore's loop (not caught by _tool's `except Exception`) and the restore stops — probe 2 confirms panic then holds. (ii) The token bucket's 50-write burst makes a small restore finish in <100 ms (probe 1: 33 lines in 84 ms), so the window is only wide for large diffs (post-scene-change/whole-desk: hundreds of sections at 50 lines/s = seconds to ~40 s; docstring itself says 'can take a minute'). (iii) Ramps (desk.py:922-946) and CFS ring-out continue after panic but write only faders/GEQ pars, never mix/on: probe 3 shows a 3 s bus ramp completing after panic with the bus still muted; CFS (cfs.py, no mix/on writes) keeps raising a muted bus master toward target — a latent-level hazard on later unmute, not a panic defeat. server.panic not calling cfs.abort is true but belongs to the CFS finding.
Real desk vs FakeDesk: identical. `/`-form node writes carrying ON + fader are the documented transport (transport.md §6.6) and the snapshot/restore round trip was verified live on an X32 Rack FW 4.13 (HANDOVER.md M5: 'restore wrote 3 sections in 9 ms, diff afterwards clean'); dump is 0.28 s live, restore paced by the same 50/s limiter. The FakeDesk applies node lines the same way; no research doc marks this UNCONFIRMED. The physical-mute reflex the GIG_CHECKLIST relies on is defeated the same way by a pending `/main/st/mix ON …` line.
Severity: kept at critical per the rubric ('defeat panic' + loud level change): after panic reports 24 muted, Main LR comes back unmuted with an un-ramped jump to the snapshot level (+40 dB in repro), reachable sequentially (probe 4) with any client and concurrently (probe 1) with a non-cancelling client. If one discounts probe 4 as 'user-confirmed restore', the concurrent-only form is high (narrow window + client-dependent).
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-bypass-1-src (branch wt/verify-bypass-1-src, commit 85cb5c7), tests/integration/test_verify_bypass1.py, 4 passed:
- probe1 (concurrent, minimal drift: 32 ch names + Main LR fader -40): restore task started, panic() mid-flight -> ok count 24, fakedesk ON outputs [] and main fader -39.97; restore then finished '33/33 section(s) written in 84 ms' -> ON outputs ['main/st'], main fader -0.0098 (0 dB, unmuted); bus/01 stayed muted (its mix section was not in the plan).
- probe2 (client cancel): restore.cancel() mid-flight raises CancelledError out of the tool; panic(); 1 s later ON outputs [], main fader still -39.97, ch32 name still 'x32' (loop stopped) -> a client that sends notifications/cancelled defeats the exploit.
- probe3 (ramp): set_fader('bus.1', -15, ramp_ms=3000) in flight, panic(); ramp result ok '−20.0 dB → −15.0 dB (ramped 3000 ms)', bus1 on=False fader=-14.97 -> ramp continues but does NOT unmute.
- probe4 (sequential): preview '1 setting(s) in 1 section(s) would change' (Main LR fader) -> token; panic() ok 24, ON outputs []; restore_snapshot(id, confirm_token) -> ok, 'restore wrote 24 line(s)'; ON outputs now 24 (main/st, main/m, bus/01..16, mtx/01..06), main fader -0.0098.
Source refs: desk.py:1184-1220 (panic, no latch/ramps/cfs), desk.py:1312-1341 (restore loop, no re-check, only RequestTimeout/NotConnected handled), desk.py:648-666 (dump always live), nodes.py:1201-1227 + _restore_sort_key (whole-section lines, outputs last), server.py:312-327 (_tool no lock), server.py:1240-1268 (restore_snapshot timeout=None, payload {id,scope}), server.py:1062-1074 (panic tool, no cfs.abort), mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:592-606 & 623-628 (concurrent spawn; cancel interrupts), server.py:1949 (stdio only), device.yaml:733 writes_per_second 50, :738 confirm_token_ttl_s 300; HANDOVER.md M5 lines 109-117 (restore + panic verified on real X32).
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's fix is right in shape; make it cover the sequential case too. (1) Desk.panic: set self._panic_epoch += 1 (monotonic), cancel and clear self._ramps, and have server.panic await app.cfs.abort('panic') (CFS abort path only lowers). (2) Desk.restore: capture epoch before the dump; in the loop `if self._panic_epoch != epoch0: aborted='panic'; break` BEFORE each slash; additionally never emit ON for main/bus/mtx mix/on while a panic latch is set — simplest: after a panic, restore_plan lines for /main|/bus|/mtx .../mix are rendered with the live OFF kept (or split so mix/on is skipped) until an explicit set_main_mute/unmute clears the latch. (3) Bind the restore token to the change set, not just {id, scope}: include a digest of restore_plan(lines) (or of the diff) in the guard payload and recompute at confirm time; if it changed (e.g. panic muted outputs in between), refuse with a fresh preview instead of writing 24 unmutes the user never saw (probe 4). (4) Optionally ramp or cap upward fader jumps inside restore lines for main/bus/mtx (write mix/on last and fader via _move_level) so an undo can never be an un-ramped +40 dB on Main LR. (5) Cheap defence in depth: a per-Desk asyncio.Lock (or 'busy' refusal) around long writers (restore, apply_patch_plan, ring-out) that panic bypasses but that panic also flips to 'abort requested'.

## D3. [critical] Main LR / Main M-C compressor make-up gain and 6-band EQ are Tier 1: +24 dB (makeup) and 3x +15 dB (EQ boosts) on the PA with unconfirmed, un-ramped calls, allowed in show mode; same calls void the fader ceilings on ch/bus/mtx
*Angle:* clamps · *attacker severity:* critical · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** device.yaml:259-269 (main inherits blk_dyn_core 182-196 and blk_eq 207-212 at tier 1; only mix/* is tier 2), device.yaml:708-721 (guarded globs cover /main/*/mix/* only), device.yaml:83 (mgain scale 0..24, no policy clamp), device.yaml:71+728 (eq_gain scale ±15 == eq_gain_abs_max_db 15, so clamp_eq_gain never bites); src/x32mcp/desk.py:1111-1132 set_comp (makeup_db -> _num -> _write_value, no clamp/ramp/relative check, no Clamped report), desk.py:1050-1081 set_eq_band, policy.py:275-283 clamp_eq_gain; src/x32mcp/server.py:1016-1039 set_comp tool, 981-1002 set_eq_band tool (target resolved via desk.resolve -> 'main','lr','master','mono' all reach main.*)

**Mechanism.** policy.tier_for(address) -> descriptor.tier_for: /main/st/dyn/mgain, /main/st/dyn/on, /main/st/dyn/thr, /main/st/eq/N/g|type|q and /main/st/eq/on are declared tier 1 and match no guarded glob, so Desk._write() (desk.py:871-884) takes the Tier-1 path: no confirmation token, no show-mode check (SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED has no EQ/dyn action), no relative limit (check_relative is only in adjust_level), no ramp (only _move_level ramps). makeup_db is only bounded by the mgain scale, which silently clamps +inf/1e9 to 24.0 (ParamSpec.to_raw) and returns applied=24.0 with no 'clamped' entry. With threshold_db=0 (0 dBFS, never reached) and on=True the compressor is a pure +24 dB gain stage on the main bus. clamp_eq_gain's ±15 limit equals the hardware range, so three wide boosts (LShv/PEQ q0.3/HShv at +15) add ~+15 dB broadband on top; set_eq_band(on=False) bypasses a system/feedback EQ outright. Net: ~+39 dB on Main LR from Tier-1 tools while set_main_fader (+0 dB ceiling, token) is the only thing the design guards. On ch/bus/mtx the same two calls give +24/+15 dB un-ramped, which makes ch_fader_max_db 5 / bus_fader_max_db 0 meaningless as loudness bounds (a wedge bus at 0 dB + 24 dB makeup).

**Exploit.** show_mode(true) (optional — it does not matter); set_comp(target='main.st', on=true, threshold_db=0, ratio=1.1, makeup_db=1e9) -> ok, applied.makeup_db 24.0; set_comp('mono', on=true, makeup_db=24) -> main.m; set_eq_band('main', 1, gain_db=1e9, type='LShv', q=0.3); set_eq_band('main', 3, gain_db=15, type='PEQ', q=0.3); set_eq_band('main', 6, gain_db=15, type='HShv'); or set_eq_band('main.st', 1, on=false). No confirm_token is ever asked for.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_clamps.py::test_exploit_main_bus_compressor_makeup_is_tier1_even_in_show_mode and ::test_exploit_main_bus_eq_boost_is_tier1_even_in_show_mode PASS against FakeDesk with show mode ON: result ok:true, no requires_confirmation, applied.makeup_db==24.0 for makeup_db=inf and 1e9; fakedesk /main/st/dyn/mgain==24.0, dyn/on True, dyn/thr 0.0; /main/m/dyn/mgain==24.0 via 'mono'; /mtx/01, /bus/01, /ch/01 dyn/mgain==24.0; /main/st/eq/1/g==/eq/3/g==/eq/6/g==15.0 with types LShv/HShv written, res.clamped reported only for the EQ gain; set_eq_band('lr',2,on='false') wrote eq/on ON; set_eq_band('main.st',1,on=False) wrote eq/on OFF.

**Proposed fix.** Smallest: add '/main/*/dyn/*' and '/main/*/eq/*' (and '/main/*/preamp/*') to device.yaml guarded so Desk._write refuses them from Tier-1 tools (set_comp/set_eq_band then return GUARDED for mains; add a confirming set_main_eq/set_main_comp later if wanted). Independently, treat gain-adding parameters as level moves in policy: add policy.makeup_max_db (e.g. 6) applied in Desk.set_comp with a Clamped report, make eq_gain_abs_max_db asymmetric (boost ceiling ~+6, cuts to -15) so clamp_eq_gain actually limits something, and run check_relative on increases of mgain/eq gain (current->new) so show mode's ±3 dB applies to them too.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-clamps-0-repro (commit c060719, tests/integration/test_verify_clamps0.py, 3 passed). With show_mode(True) on and set_main_fader('main.st',-3) correctly returning requires_confirmation, the Tier-1 tools still write gain-adding params on the mains with no token, no ramp, no relative check: policy.tier_for gives 1 for /main/st/dyn/mgain, dyn/on, dyn/thr, eq/1/g, eq/on and /main/m/dyn/mgain (2 only for /main/*/mix/* and insert), because device.yaml main: inherits blk_dyn_core/blk_eq at tier 1 and the guarded globs (device.yaml guarded:) list only /main/st/mix/*, /main/m/mix/*. Desk.set_comp (desk.py ~1111-1132) -> _write_params -> _write_value -> _write takes the Tier-1 path; makeup_db passes _num (rejects only NaN) and is silently clamped by the mgain lin scale (0..24) with no Clamped report; set_eq_band's clamp_eq_gain limit (15) equals the hardware range so it never reduces a boost. On an X32 dyn/mgain is a static post-gain whenever dyn/on is ON, so on=True, thr=0 dBFS, mgain=24 is a +24 dB step on Main LR/M-C; three +15 dB wide EQ boosts stack on top. Severity: this is a loud, instantaneous, unconfirmed level change on the PA reachable in show mode, which meets the brief's 'critical' bar; caveats: it is bounded (+24 dB makeup, +15 dB/band EQ = hardware range, not unbounded), writes still pass the rate limiter and snapshot-before-write, and BRIEF.md §4 explicitly places 'EQ/dyn' in Tier 1 and only 'main LR/mono fader+mute' in Tier 2 — so it is a threat-model/design gap the code faithfully implements rather than a code deviation from a documented guarantee. The on='false' string footgun (bool('false') -> True writes eq/on ON) reproduces on direct Python calls only; via MCP pydantic would coerce the string, so it is incidental.
>
> *Evidence:* $SP/runtests.sh <WT> tests/integration/test_verify_clamps0.py -s -> 3 passed. Printed results with show mode ON: set_comp('main.st', on=True, threshold_db=0, ratio=1.1, makeup_db=1e9) -> {'ok': True, 'applied': {'on': True, 'threshold_db': 0.0, 'ratio': '1.1', 'makeup_db': 24.0}} (no requires_confirmation, no 'clamped' key); set_comp('main.st', makeup_db=math.inf) -> ok, makeup 24.0; set_comp('mono', on=True, makeup_db=24) -> target 'main.m' ok; ch.1/bus.1/mtx.1 same -> makeup 24.0. After settle: fakedesk /main/st/dyn/mgain==24.0, dyn/on True, dyn/thr 0.0; /main/m/dyn/mgain 24.0, dyn/on True; /ch/01,/bus/01,/mtx/01 dyn/mgain 24.0; 16 desk writes, zero tokens. set_eq_band('main',1,gain_db=1e9,type='LShv',q=0.3) -> ok, gain 15.0, clamped=[{requested 1e9, limit 15}]; band 3 PEQ +15 and band 6 HShv +15 -> ok, not clamped; fakedesk /main/st/eq/{1,3,6}/g == 15.0, types LShv/HShv written. set_eq_band('main.st',1,on=False) -> fakedesk /main/st/eq/on False; set_eq_band('lr',2,on='false') -> applied on True, eq/on True. Tier probe: {'/main/st/dyn/mgain':1,'/main/st/dyn/on':1,'/main/st/dyn/thr':1,'/main/st/eq/1/g':1,'/main/st/eq/on':1,'/main/m/dyn/mgain':1,'/main/st/mix/fader':2,'/main/st/mix/on':2,'/main/st/insert/on':2}. Control: set_main_fader('main.st',-3.0) in the same session returned requires_confirmation=True.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix; ordering by value: (1) add '/main/*/dyn/*' and '/main/*/eq/*' to device.yaml guarded so Desk._write refuses them from set_comp/set_eq_band (they already raise GUARDED cleanly for tier>=2), and add confirming set_main_eq/set_main_comp if the workflow needs them; (2) add a policy.makeup_max_db (e.g. 6) enforced in Desk.set_comp with a Clamped entry in the result (today the mgain scale clamps silently, so the caller is told applied=24 with no warning), and make the EQ boost ceiling asymmetric (e.g. +6/-15) so clamp_eq_gain actually constrains boosts; (3) treat increases of mgain/eq gain as level moves: read current, apply check_relative (so show mode's ±3 dB bites) and optionally ramp. Also reject non-finite makeup_db/gain_db in _num (math.isinf) rather than letting the scale silently pin them to the top of range.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Causal chain read end to end, no gap found:
1. Tier source of truth: device.yaml:259-269 `main:` merges *blk_dyn_core (182-196, every dyn/* tier 1 incl. dyn/mgain scale mgain 0..24, dyn/on, dyn/thr) and *blk_eq (207-212, eq/on + eq/{band}/type|f|g|q tier 1, g clamp ±15); only mix/* keys are overridden to tier 2. Guarded globs device.yaml:708-721 cover /main/st/mix/*, /main/m/mix/*, /*/insert/*, /*/grp/*, /*/config/source — nothing matches /main/*/dyn/* or /main/*/eq/*. descriptor.tier_for (descriptor.py:1041-1046) = 2 if glob else param tier → 1 for /main/st/dyn/mgain, /main/st/dyn/on, /main/st/dyn/thr, /main/st/eq/N/g, /main/st/eq/on (asserted in my test).
2. Tool path: server.py:1016-1039 set_comp / 981-1002 set_eq_band → desk.resolve (targets.py:37,116-120: 'main','lr','st','master'→main.st; 'mono','m','mc'→main.m) → Desk.set_comp (desk.py:1111-1132: _section_path(t,'dyn') is non-None because device.yaml:638 declares the main dyn node; makeup_db→_num→_write_params→_write_value) / Desk.set_eq_band (desk.py:1050-1081). No show-mode call anywhere on this path (check_show_mode_allows is only invoked from _confirm, scene tools, ringout tools: server.py:335,1130,1157,1588,1746,1792; desk.py:1254,1292; cfs.py:652,727), no check_relative (only adjust_level), no ramp (only _move_level ramps), no token (only _confirm/guard).
3. Desk._write (desk.py:871-884): tier 1 → ensure_pre_write_snapshot → acquire_write (≤4 writes, 50/s bucket never bites) → conn.set fire-and-forget. ParamSpec.to_raw→Scale.clamp (scales.py:426-440) pins any finite/inf number into [0,24] silently (min(max(v,0),24)); _as_float only rejects NaN. set_comp returns applied=24.0 with no Clamped entry (there is no makeup clamp in Policy at all). clamp_eq_gain (policy.py:275-283) limit = eq_gain_abs_max_db 15 == the hardware range, so +15 passes with clamped=None; it only 'reports' for >15 requests and never restricts a boost below full scale.
4. Server INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:114-117) tell the client Tier-1 tools incl. set_comp/set_eq_band are 'clamped (… buses/main/sends 0 dB, EQ ±15), ramped and limited to ±6 dB per call (±3 in show mode)' — makeup +24 dB in one un-ramped write and ±15 dB EQ swings contradict that stated guarantee, and BRIEF §4/DESIGN §13 guard only main fader+mute, so this is a design-level hole in the safety layer, not an implementation slip — but the red-team rubric is about what a confused/jailbroken client can do, and it can do this.
Real desk: addresses and scaling are VERIFIED protocol ground truth (docs/research/scales_params.md §8: /main/st/dyn/* MXDY set, /main/st/eq/[1-6] with Xeqty2; §4.5 mgain linf [0,24,0.5] f=g/24), so ,f 1.0 on /main/st/dyn/mgain is +24 dB make-up on a real X32 exactly as on the FakeDesk; the X32 dynamics 'GAIN' is a static post-compressor gain applied whenever dyn/on is ON regardless of gain reduction. Jim's own scene1.scn (§8) has `/main/st/dyn ON COMP RMS LOG -15.5 2.5 … 0.00` — main comp already ON — so on that desk a single call set_comp('main', makeup_db=24) (one OSC write, no on/thr needed) raises Main LR by +24 dB minus current GR (≈+18 dB on loud program at 2.5:1, full +24 dB on quiet passages/speech); with thr 0/ratio 1.1 it is a flat +24 dB step into the PA. Three +15 dB wide boosts on the 6-band main EQ stack on top (overlapping LShv/PEQ q0.3/HShv sum to ≥+15 dB broadband, more where they overlap), and eq on=False removes any system/feedback cuts. FakeDesk differs only in having no audio; protocol acceptance is identical (real desk accepts any float, no server-side validation, fire-and-forget so no error could even come back).
Minor corrections to the claim: (a) 'inf' cannot arrive over MCP JSON, but 24 or 1e9 can and behave identically; (b) the on='false'→ON sub-item only holds for direct Python callers — through MCPServer.call_tool pydantic coerces "false"→False (observed); (c) main has no preamp/* params, so the suggested '/main/*/preamp/*' glob is moot. None of these touch the core finding. The 'voids ch/bus/mtx fader ceilings' framing is fair: dyn is pre-fader on every strip, so +24 dB makeup (Tier 1, un-ramped, no relative check, allowed in show mode) on a wedge bus clamped at 0 dB or a channel clamped at +5 dB defeats those ceilings as loudness bounds; that part alone would rate high.
Severity stays critical: unconfirmed, un-ramped, show-mode-permitted +24 dB (up to ~+39 dB with EQ) level change on Main LR / Main M-C from Tier-1 tools, while the only main-bus control the design guards (set_main_fader, 0 dB ceiling + token) is bypassed entirely.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-clamps-0-src (commits 6ebda8f tests, d4fadc6 fix experiment). tests/integration/test_verify_clamps0.py — 4 passed against FakeDesk via the real MCP validation path (srv.server.call_tool with JSON args) with show_mode(True): test_tiers_of_main_dyn_and_eq_addresses: policy.tier_for == MIX(1) for /main/st/dyn/mgain|on|thr, /main/st/eq/1/g, /main/st/eq/on, /main/m/dyn/mgain, /main/m/eq/3/g; GUARDED for /main/st/mix/fader|on, /main/m/mix/fader, /main/st/insert/sel. test_main_makeup_24db_via_call_tool_json_args_show_mode_on: with fake main dyn pre-set ON (as in Jim's scene), call_tool('set_comp',{target:'main',makeup_db:24}) → ok:true, no requires_confirmation/confirm_token, applied.makeup_db 24.0, no 'clamped' key; fakedesk /main/st/dyn/mgain == 24.0; {target:'lr',on:true,threshold_db:0,ratio:1.1,makeup_db:1e9} → ok, applied 24.0/0.0/True; {target:'mono',on:true,makeup_db:24} → target main.m, fakedesk /main/m/dyn/mgain 24.0, dyn/on True. Captured desk.write events: [/main/st/dyn/mgain 24.0 tier 1 tool set_comp, /main/st/dyn/on True tier 1, /main/st/dyn/thr 0.0 tier 1, /main/st/dyn/ratio '1.1' tier 1, /main/m/dyn/on True tier 1, /main/m/dyn/mgain 24.0 tier 1] — single writes, no ramp. test_main_eq_plus15_x3_and_eq_bypass_via_call_tool_show_mode_on: set_eq_band main bands 1/3/6 gain_db 15 type LShv/PEQ/HShv q 0.3 on true → ok, applied.gain_db 15.0, clamped None; fakedesk eq/1/g, eq/3/g, eq/6/g == 15.0, eq/on True; then {band:1,on:false} → ok, fakedesk /main/st/eq/on False; on:"false" via call_tool → applied.on False (pydantic coerces; claim's ON result is Python-direct only). test_contrast_main_fader_is_guarded: same session, set_main_fader('st',0.0) → requires_confirmation true; set_fader('main',0.0) → GUARDED. Fix experiment (d4fadc6): adding "/main/*/dyn/*" and "/main/*/eq/*" to device.yaml guarded → test_verify_clamps0_fix.py passes (all four main calls → error.code GUARDED, /main/st/dyn/mgain stays 0.0, ch.1 set_comp still ok); full suite with globs: 911 passed, 2 failed: tests/test_device_yaml.py::test_tier2_params_are_consistent_with_guarded_globs (doc-consistency: main dyn/eq params must also be declared tier:2) and tests/test_targets_events_config.py::test_settings_defaults_and_env (worktree directory-name artifact, unrelated). Protocol ground truth: docs/research/scales_params.md:602-620 (§8 main dyn/eq VERIFIED, real scene `/main/st/dyn ON COMP RMS LOG -15.5 2.5 1 0.00 …`), :417 (mgain linf [0,24,0.5] f=g/24).
>
> *Better fix:* Claimant's smallest fix works but needs one more edit: adding "/main/*/dyn/*" and "/main/*/eq/*" to device.yaml `guarded:` makes Desk._write raise GUARDED for set_comp/set_eq_band on main.st/main.m (verified), but tests/test_device_yaml.py::test_tier2_params_are_consistent_with_guarded_globs then requires the main: family to restate the dyn/*, dyn/filter/* and eq/* keys with tier: 2 (they are inherited tier 1 via YAML merge) — do both, and drop the '/main/*/preamp/*' suggestion (main has no preamp params). Then add confirming Tier-2 tools (set_main_eq / set_main_comp via _confirm + guarded=True) if main EQ/comp control is wanted at all. Independently of mains, treat gain-adding parameters as level moves everywhere: (1) policy.makeup_max_db (e.g. 6, device.yaml policy + clamp on dyn/mgain) applied in Desk.set_comp with a Clamped report instead of the silent Scale.clamp pin to 24; (2) split eq_gain_abs_max_db into eq_boost_max_db (~+6) / eq_cut_max_db (−15) so clamp_eq_gain actually constrains boosts below the hardware range; (3) read-before-write in set_comp/set_eq_band and run policy.check_relative on increases of mgain and EQ gain (current→new) so the ±6/±3 dB-per-call rule the INSTRUCTIONS advertise really covers them, and optionally ramp mgain like a fader; (4) consider making eq on=False on bus/mtx/main (removing cuts = effective boost) at least relative-checked or Tier 2 on outputs.

## D4. [critical] A restore_snapshot in flight re-opens every output panic() just muted (24/24 within 0.3 s, at snapshot levels)
*Angle:* panic · *attacker severity:* critical · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/desk.py:1312-1341 (restore loop: acquire_write → conn.slash(line) per section; no cancellation/epoch check), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:1201-1227 (restore_plan renders whole mix sections '/bus/01/mix ON -20.0 …' incl. the ON token; order ch,auxin,fxrtn,bus,mtx,main so the outputs come last), src/x32mcp/server.py:1240-1268 (restore allowed in show mode; docstring: 'a full restore of a very different desk can take a minute'), desk.py:1184-1220 (panic does not signal restore)

**Mechanism.** restore() dumps the live desk, builds one '/ ,s' line per differing section and writes them one rate-limiter slot at a time awaiting each echo. Any strip whose mix section differs (a fader moved since the snapshot) gets its full mix line rewritten, which includes mix/on=ON from the snapshot. panic() arriving mid-restore mutes the 24 outputs, then the restore loop simply continues and writes the bus/mtx/main mix lines after it: every output is un-muted again and set to the snapshot's fader level, not the level the operator had when they hit panic. Nothing in Desk ties in-flight multi-write operations to the panic.

**Exploit.** restore_snapshot('latest') → confirm → (whole-desk restore running, seconds to a minute on a real desk) → loud problem → panic() → ok 24 muted → restore keeps writing → all buses, matrices, Main LR and M/C are ON again a fraction of a second later, mains at the snapshot level.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_panic.py::test_C1_restore_in_flight_unmutes_outputs_after_panic PASSES on HEAD code (C1_restore_across_panic.json): snapshot taken, all faders drifted to -30 dB, restore of 56 lines started, panic() fired after line 5 (count 24, 0.2 ms) — restore finished 56/56 in 200 ms and the fake desk then showed all 24 of /bus/01-16, /mtx/01-06, /main/st, /main/m mix/on = 1 (un-muted) with /main/st/mix/fader = 0.0 dB (operator had -30). With the fix sketch test_panic_epoch.py::test_restore_stops_at_a_panic passes: written 5/56, aborted 'panic() ran during the restore…', 0 outputs un-muted, main still -30.

**Proposed fix.** Give Desk a panic epoch: panic() increments self._panic_epoch; restore() captures it before the dump and breaks out of the line loop with aborted='panic() ran during the restore' when it changes (implemented in worktree desk.py restore()). The operator can re-issue the restore deliberately afterwards. Same epoch is used by _write/_ramp (next finding).

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Reproduced from scratch in my own worktree ($SP/worktrees/verify-panic-1-repro, branch wt/verify-panic-1-repro, commit 9035b58, file tests/integration/test_verify_panic1.py, 6 tests, all PASS on HEAD a408a2a product code).

Mechanism verified in code: Desk.restore (src/x32mcp/desk.py:1312-1341) dumps live, builds restore_plan lines once, then loops `acquire_write(); conn.slash(line)` with no cancellation/epoch/panic check; only NotConnected aborts. nodes.restore_plan (nodes.py:1201-1227) emits a whole mix section per differing strip — rendered lines are literally `/bus/01/mix ON 0.0 ON +0 OFF -oo`, i.e. they carry mix/on=ON and the snapshot fader — and _restore_sort_key orders ch→…→bus→mtx→main, so the 24 output lines come last. Desk.panic (desk.py:1184-1220) just fire-and-forgets mix/on 0 and invalidates cache; it signals nothing to in-flight multi-write operations. server._tool (server.py:312-328) has no serialization, and the installed MCP SDK (mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:_spawn → tg.start_soon per request) dispatches tools/call concurrently, so a panic() call while restore_snapshot is awaiting is a real path. restore_snapshot is allowed in show mode (server.py:1240 docstring; _confirm only blocks SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED which excludes restore) — confirmed by test_restore_allowed_in_show_mode_too.

Results (through the server tools, FakeDesk): snapshot with outputs ON/main 0 dB; operator drift: 32 ch + 24 output faders to -30 dB; restore plan = 56 lines. (a) panic after line 5 (in-loop hook): panic ok, 24 muted in 0.2 ms; restore continued 56/56 in 199 ms, not aborted; afterwards 24/24 of bus01-16, mtx01-06, main/st, main/m mix/on=1, /main/st/mix/fader=0.0 dB (operator had -30). (b) genuinely concurrent panic from a separate asyncio task once >=5 lines written: panic landed at line 14, 24 muted mid-restore, end state 24/24 un-muted, main 0 dB. (c) sleep-50 ms variant: panic landed during the pre-restore dump (0 lines written) — still 24/24 re-opened (the dump then sees OFF≠snapshot ON and writes ON). (d) show mode on: identical, 24/24 re-opened. (e) Bonus: the same loop also defeats the operator's PHYSICAL reflex — muting Main LR and slamming its fader to -90 on the (fake) front panel after line 5 is overwritten when the /main/st/mix line arrives: main ends ON at 0 dB. (f) Boundary: if only channel sections differ at dump time (no output mix section in the plan) a mid-restore panic sticks (0/24 re-opened) — so the precondition is 'at least one field of an output's mix section (fader/on/st/pan/mono/mlevel) differs from the snapshot when restore dumps, or panic precedes the dump'. After any soundcheck snapshot this is the normal case (bus/main faders move constantly), so I do not downgrade to PARTIAL.

Severity: critical per the given scale — it defeats panic (outputs re-open a fraction of a second later with no further client action) and can produce a large level jump on Main LR (here -30/−90 → 0 dB, +30 dB or from silence to full) at the moment the operator has just declared an emergency. The Tier-2 confirmation covered the restore's diff as previewed before the emergency, not overriding a subsequent panic. On a real desk the window is seconds to a minute (docstring), far wider than the 200 ms fake.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-panic-1-repro (commit 9035b58). Test file: tests/integration/test_verify_panic1.py — `runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_verify_panic1.py` → 6 passed in 5.5 s. JSON results in $SP/reports/verify-panic-1-repro/: C1_after_line_5.json {restore 56/56 written 199 ms, aborted null; panic count 24, 0.2 ms; muted_right_after_panic 24; unmuted_when_restore_finished 24; main_st_after on=true fader≈0.0; operator had -30; sample line '/bus/01/mix ON 0.0 ON +0 OFF -oo'; 24 output mix lines of 56}. C1_concurrent_task.json {lines_written_at_panic 14, muted_mid_restore 24, restore 56/56 208 ms, unmuted_at_end 24, main on/0 dB}. C1_sleep_50ms.json {panic at 0 lines (during dump), unmuted 24}. C1_show_mode.json {show mode on, unmuted 24}. C1_front_panel.json {front-panel Main LR mute + fader -90 after line 5 → main ends on=true fader≈0.0}. C1_boundary.json {only ch drift: 32 lines, unmuted_at_end 0 — outputs untouched when their mix sections match at dump time}. Code refs: desk.py:1312-1341 restore loop (no panic/epoch check, only NotConnected breaks), desk.py:1184-1220 panic (no signal to in-flight ops), nodes.py:1201-1227 + render_node_line (whole mix section incl. ON token, outputs sorted last), server.py:312-328 _tool (no serialization), server.py:1240-1268 restore_snapshot (timeout=None, allowed in show mode), mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py _spawn/tg.start_soon (concurrent tools/call).
>
> *Better fix:* The proposed panic-epoch (panic() bumps Desk._panic_epoch; restore captures it before the dump and breaks with aborted='panic() ran during the restore' when it changes, checked before each acquire_write/slash) is the right minimal fix and should also cover _ramp/_move_level and CFS ring-out loops. Two additions: (1) check the epoch AFTER the dump as well as per line, since my sleep variant shows a panic landing during the multi-second dump is equally undone; (2) the epoch only covers the MCP panic path — the physical-mute variant (test_front_panel_mute_mid_restore_is_also_undone) is not fixed by it. To narrow that, either exclude mix/on (and optionally fader) of main/bus/mtx from whole-section restore lines and write them as separate leaf sets re-diffed just before writing, or re-read each output's mix section immediately before writing it and skip/abort if mix/on went OFF or the fader dropped since the dump. Optionally also have panic() set a short 'outputs latched' window during which any write that would set main/bus/mtx mix/on=ON or raise their fader is refused unless it is an explicit unmute/set_main_* call.

> **Verifier 2: PARTIAL (critical)** — Mechanism holds end to end inside the server; the title's "every output / 24-of-24" and the narrated MCP exploit overstate reach, and the proposed fix misses the worse variant.

Causal chain (all verified in source):
1. server.py:1240-1268 restore_snapshot: after the token is consumed it awaits desk.restore() under wait_for(30 s + sections/40) inside @_tool(timeout=None) (server.py:312-328). _confirm() → check_show_mode_allows('restore_snapshot') passes in show mode (reproduced, V2). No lock/serialisation of tool calls anywhere in server.py.
2. desk.py:1312-1341 restore(): ensure_pre_write_snapshot → dump() → nodes.restore_plan(snap, live) computed ONCE from the pre-panic dump → `for line in lines: await policy.acquire_write(); await conn.slash(line)`. Only RequestTimeout (skip line) and NotConnected (abort) are handled; there is no epoch, latch, re-read or cancellation check between lines. panic() (desk.py:1184-1220) just fires 24 conn.set mix/on 0 and signals nothing.
3. nodes.py:1201-1227 restore_plan is SECTION-granular: any section with any differing field is re-rendered from the snapshot as its full leading run. Output mix sections are [mix/on, mix/fader, …] (device.yaml:621 bus, :634 mtx, :643-644 main), so a bus/mtx/main whose fader (or pan/st/mono/mlevel) differs gets '/bus/01/mix ON -20.0 …' — ON comes from the snapshot. Family order ch, auxin, fxrtn, bus, mtx, main puts the output lines late in the loop.
4. Concurrency is real at the protocol layer: mcp 2.2 jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:606 spawns every tools/call handler with tg.start_soon, so a panic request received while the restore handler is parked in `await conn.slash()` runs immediately; the restore task then resumes and writes the remaining lines after the 24 mutes, on the same UDP socket, in order.
5. Real desk: '/' node-style multi-value write is CONFIRMED (transport.md §6.6:524-540, DOC 4560-4593: listed values applied in node order) and the live M5 restore round-trip was verified on X32RACK FW 4.13 (HANDOVER.md:116). So a real X32 sets mix/on=ON and the fader to the snapshot value from that one datagram, un-ramped. Timing differs only in length: bucket 50 + 50 writes/s (policy.py:375-396, device.yaml:733), echo ≈3 ms on hardware → first 50 lines at echo speed then 20 ms/line: 56 lines ≈0.3 s, 500 ≈9 s, a 2000-section 'very different desk' ≈40 s — a longer exposure window than on the FakeDesk, with the output lines near its end.

Where the claim is narrower than stated:
(a) Only outputs whose mix SECTION already differed from the snapshot at the pre-panic dump are re-opened. The claimant forced all 56 faders to −30 dB; in a realistic session (V1: agent had moved Main LR, Bus 1 and 20 channel faders since the session-start snapshot) exactly 2/24 re-opened — but those are Main LR (−30 → 0.0 dB, un-muted, no ramp) and the touched wedge, i.e. the ones that matter. Outputs untouched since the snapshot stay muted.
(b) The narrated sequence (restore running → operator says panic → panic ok → restore keeps writing) needs an MCP client that sends the panic tools/call while the restore request is still outstanding and NOT cancelled. The stdio server permits it (concurrent dispatch, no tool timeout), and runaway/agent-framework clients that fire calls without awaiting are squarely in the stated threat model. But a sequential chat client (Claude Desktop/Code, the primary deployment per BRIEF §6a) cannot emit panic until the restore returns unless the user interrupts, and an interrupt that sends notifications/cancelled makes the mcp 2.2 dispatcher (jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:623-628, default 'interrupt') cancel the handler → CancelledError propagates out of conn.slash → the restore STOPS (V3: stopped at 3/24 lines, panic held, 0 re-opened). So with the primary clients the likely outcome of 'stop! panic!' is a half-applied restore plus a panic that holds — unless the client abandons the request without cancelling.
(c) The same defect defeats the operator's documented emergency reflex (BRIEF.md:90: mute on X32-Edit/the desk, not via chat) with ANY client and no concurrency at all: a Main LR mute pressed on the desk mid-restore is re-opened at the snapshot level by the '/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0' line (V5, reproduced). The proposed Desk panic-epoch cannot see that; it fixes only the in-process panic() path.

Severity re-assessment: kept at critical. When it fires it is an un-ramped re-opening of Main LR (and any touched bus/matrix) to the snapshot fader level immediately after an emergency mute — 'defeat panic / loud level change' in the rubric — it is deterministic given an in-flight restore, behaves identically on a real X32, is allowed in show mode, and via (c) is reachable in the primary deployment without any exotic client. The '24/24 in 0.3 s' headline should be read as 'every output the session had touched, Main LR first among them, within the remaining restore window (sub-second to tens of seconds on hardware)'.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-panic-1-src (branch wt/verify-panic-1-src, commit d752ca7), tests/integration/test_verify_panic1.py, 5/5 pass against HEAD a408a2a product code, driven through the server TOOLS (srv.snapshot_desk / srv.restore_snapshot confirm dance / srv.panic / srv.show_mode on one App+FakeDesk; restore run as an asyncio task exactly as the MCP dispatcher spawns a tools/call). Observations in $SP/reports/verify-panic-1-src/*.json:
V1_realistic_touched_outputs.json — snapshot 'session start'; then ch1-20 → −25, bus.1 → −30, main.st → −30; whole-desk restore = 22 lines ('/ch/01/mix …' ×20, '/bus/01/mix ON 0.0 ON +0 OFF -oo', '/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0'); panic() fired while line 3's echo was awaited → ok, 24 muted, 0.2 ms; restore finished 22/22 in 89 ms; final desk: unmuted outputs = ['/bus/01/mix/on','/main/st/mix/on'] only, main.st fader 0.0 dB (operator had −30), the other 22 outputs still muted.
V2_all_outputs_show_mode.json — show_mode(True) then confirmed restore (allowed); all 24 output faders drifted; panic between plan and line 1: 24/24 observed muted on the fake desk right after panic, then restore 24/24 lines in 140 ms → all 24 mix/on back to 1, main 0.0 dB. Matches claimant's C1 (their C1_restore_across_panic.json: 56 lines, panic after line 5, 24/24 re-opened, main −30 → 0).
V3_cancel_then_panic.json — same setup, but the restore task is cancelled after line 3 (what notifications/cancelled does in mcp 2.2 'interrupt' mode): srv.restore_snapshot raised CancelledError, restore_lines_attempted=3, then panic ok 24; after 0.3 s: unmuted_outputs_final=[], main.st still −29.98 dB → a cancelling client is NOT exposed.
V4_panic_then_restore.json — panic first, then restore preview lists 'Bus 1 … unmuted → muted' ×24 and the confirmed restore re-opens them: the intended undo, not the race (bounds the claim to panic-after-dump).
V5_physical_mute_mid_restore.json — no panic(): fakedesk.set('/main/st/mix/on',0) and '/bus/01/mix/on',0 (front-panel mutes) after restore line 3 → restore 21/21; final main_on=1, main fader 0.0 dB (re-opened by '/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0'), bus1_on=0 (its section was not in the plan). Same defect, not covered by a panic epoch.
Source refs: desk.py:1316-1331 (loop, exception handling), desk.py:1184-1220 (panic, no signalling), nodes.py:1201-1227 + device.yaml:621/634/643-644 (section lines start with mix/on), server.py:312-328 (no serialisation), server.py:1240-1268 (timeout=None, show-mode allowed), .venv/.../mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:606 (tg.start_soon per request) and :623-628 (cancel → scope.cancel), docs/research/transport.md:524-540 (§6.6 CONFIRMED multi-value '/' write), docs/HANDOVER.md:116 (live restore verified), policy.py:375-396 + device.yaml:733 (50/s, bucket 50 → real-desk window length), docs/BRIEF.md:90 (physical emergency reflex).
>
> *Better fix:* The panic epoch is necessary but not sufficient. (1) Keep it: panic() bumps Desk._panic_epoch (and sets a 'panicked' latch); restore() captures the epoch after dump() and checks it before every acquire_write/slash, aborting with aborted='panic() ran during the restore'; _run_ramp/_write check it too; CFS aborts to its start master. (2) Make restore unable to re-open an output from a stale plan regardless of who muted it: before writing any bus/mtx/main mix-section line (24 sections max; ideally ch/auxin/fxrtn too), GET '<strip>/mix/on' (≈3 ms on hardware, no write token) and, if the desk is now OFF while the dump said ON, render the line with OFF instead of the snapshot's ON (report those strips under kept_muted so the operator can un-mute deliberately) — or write output sections leaf-by-leaf and only touch mix/on when mix/on itself was in the confirmed diff. This covers panic(), X32-Edit/front-panel mutes and mute groups pressed mid-restore, none of which an in-process epoch can see. (3) After panic(), latch the Desk: refuse restore_snapshot, ramps, CFS raise steps and Tier-1 unmute of bus/mtx (main is already Tier 2) until an explicit, confirmed clear (or a short hold-off), so nothing in the process re-opens an output without a fresh human decision. (4) Have restore_snapshot honour ctx cancellation explicitly and document that clients must send notifications/cancelled on interrupt; consider serialising multi-write tools (restore, apply_patch_plan, ring-outs) behind one Desk 'bulk operation' slot that panic() cancels.

## D5. [critical] Ring-out RAISE overrides an operator's emergency pull-down with one un-ramped absolute write of belief+step (+47 dB in one datagram)
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* critical · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1174-1176 (nxt = ses.master_db + step; ses.master_db is belief, never re-read), cfs.py:1115-1124 (_write_master → set_level(..., ramp_ms=0)), cfs.py:1186-1195 ('after' is NOT a read-back: desk.py:970-974 returns the clamped request), src/x32mcp/desk.py:955-967 (relative guard deliberately removed from absolute moves 2026-09-20; `force` unused), desk.py:991 (`before` is read but only used for ramp interpolation, and ramp_ms=0 → single step)

**Mechanism.** _run_ringout keeps the bus master in ses.master_db as its own belief (last value it requested). Each RAISE writes the absolute value belief+step_db through Desk.set_level with ramp_ms=0. set_level reads the live fader (`before`) but _move_level applies only the family ceiling — the ±relative_max_db guard no longer applies to absolute moves — and with ramp_ms=0 policy.ramp_steps returns [to_db], i.e. one write. So if the operator pulls the bus down by hand mid-run (the normal human reaction to a howl), the very next dwell tick slams it back to belief+1 dB in a single un-ramped OSC write, and the run carries on to the target and reports DONE. The cfs module doc (line 33-35) still believes 'a bigger step is refused by the relative clamp on the first raise' — that clamp is gone.

**Exploit.** LLM: ring_out(bus=1, target_gain_db=-6, step_db=1, dwell_ms=1500) → confirm token → run. Operator hears feedback at ≈-13 dB and yanks Bus 1 master to -60 dB (or -oo) on the surface. ≤ dwell_ms later the server writes /bus/01/mix/fader = -12 dB (belief+1) in one datagram: +48 dB instantaneous on a live monitor bus with open mics, straight back into the howl; continues to -6, backs off to -9, reports DONE.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_cfs.py::test_x3_operator_pulldown_is_overridden_by_next_raise — on the RAISE stage at master ≥ -14 dB the fake's /bus/01/mix/fader is set to -60.0 (front-panel move, cache invalidated); observed: the next desk.write to /bus/01/mix/fader is belief+1.0 (-13.0) → jump ≥ 40 dB asserted (47 dB), report final_stage DONE, end_master_db -9.0, fake fader -9.0. PASSED.

**Proposed fix.** Before every RAISE read the live fader (get_strip/leaf, cache already invalidated by the /xremote push) and compare with ses.master_db: if actual < belief − 0.5 dB treat it as operator intervention → set abort_reason='operator moved the master' and finalize WITHOUT any upward write (back-off relative to `actual`, never above it); always compute nxt = min(actual, belief) + step. Additionally restore a relative guard for CFS raises (Desk.set_level(..., max_delta_db=step) or use adjust_level(+step)) and ramp them (ramp_ms ≥ 100) so no single datagram can move a bus master more than step_db.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Reproduced from scratch in my own worktree ($SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-0-repro, commit 2b19a81, tests/integration/test_verify_cfs0_pulldown.py, 5 passed) against the real code at a408a2a with a FakeDesk + Desk + Policy + CfsManager (same fixture shape as tests/integration/test_cfs.py).

Static chain (all verified in source):
- cfs.py:1174 `nxt = min(ses.master_db + ses.step_db, target)` — ses.master_db is only ever assigned from `after` (cfs.py:1191/1194/1284), i.e. the manager's own last request; grep shows no read-back of the live fader anywhere in _run_ringout/_finalize_levels.
- cfs.py:1115-1124 `_write_master` → `Desk.set_level(target, db, ramp_ms=0, force=force)`; its docstring ("force bypasses the relative clamp") is stale.
- desk.py:983-995 `set_level` reads `before = await self._leaf(address)` (so the Desk DOES see the operator's -60 / -oo — my test asserts before_db == -60.0 / '-oo') but desk.py:948-980 `_move_level` never references `force` and applies only `policy.clamp_level` (family ceiling/floor); the comment at desk.py:959-965 documents the deliberate removal of the relative guard from absolute moves (2026-09-20). desk.py module doc lines 20-23 agree: only adjust_level calls check_relative.
- policy.py:438-440 `ramp_steps(..., ms<=0)` returns `[to_db]` → one _write → one OSC datagram (steps==1, ramp_ms==0 in the result dict; also confirmed by hooking FakeDesk._store: the fake's fader sequence is … -14.03, -59.99 (operator), -13.01, -11.99 …).
- cfs.py:1186-1195: `after` is the clamped request echoed by _move_level (desk.py:970-975 `written = after`), not a read-back, so the loop never notices; it raises on to -6, backs off to -9 and reports final_stage DONE, aborted False, abort_reason None; fake fader ends at -9.0.
- cfs.py module doc lines 33-35 ("a bigger step is refused by the relative clamp on the first raise") and cfs.py:660 comment describe a clamp that no longer exists on this path.

Dynamic results (printed by the tests):
- pull-down to -60 with cache invalidated: next set_level {before_db:-60.0, after_db:-13.0, requested_db:-13.0, steps:1, ramp_ms:0, force:False} → +47 dB in one datagram; report DONE, end -9.0.
- pull-down to -60 relying only on the fake's /xremote push (no manual invalidate): identical (+47 dB, DONE).
- pull-down to -oo: next write -13.0 from before '-oo', steps 1 → effectively +77 dB (from the -90 taper floor) in one datagram; DONE, end -9.0.
- Aggravating variant not in the claim but supporting its fix: pull-down to -60 and immediately cfs.stop() → the 'always-safe lowering' forced BACKOFF (cfs.py:1280-1284, final = belief - abort_backoff_db) writes -20.0 over a live -60.0: a +40 dB UPWARD single-datagram 'back-off' (force=True, steps 1). So even the correct human/LLM reaction (stop) slams the bus up.

Conditions: none narrower than claimed — default server args (step 1 dB, dwell 1500 ms) take exactly this path; any operator move below belief during a dwell is overwritten within ≤ dwell_ms by belief+step, and any stop/abort/DONE back-off is computed from belief too. The absolute level never exceeds the confirmed target/ceiling (≤ 0 dB), but the write lands the bus back at (1 dB above) the level the operator just fled because it was howling, with open mics, un-ramped, and defeats the only human override available during an autonomous Tier-2 run. Per the rubric (loud level change on a live bus / re-ignites feedback, which is acoustically unbounded until clip) I keep it critical.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-0-repro (branch wt/verify-cfs-0-repro, commit 2b19a81). Test file: tests/integration/test_verify_cfs0_pulldown.py — 5 passed in 17.65 s via $SP/runtests.sh.
Output:
 test_operator_pulldown_to_minus60_is_overridden_by_next_raise[cache-invalidated]: PULLDOWN at belief -14.0 -> next set_level: {'before_db': -60.0, 'after_db': -13.0, 'requested_db': -13.0, 'steps': 1, 'ramp_ms': 0, '_force': False}; all bus-fader OSC writes: [-19,-18,-17,-16,-15,-14,-13,-12,-11,-10,-9,-8,-7,-6,-9]; report: final_stage DONE, aborted False, abort_reason None, start -20.0, max -6.0, end -9.0; fake fader -9.0.
 [xremote-push-only]: identical.
 test_operator_pulldown_to_minus_inf...: before '-oo' (before_db -inf) -> after_db -13.0, steps 1, ramp_ms 0; DONE, end -9.0.
 test_server_default_dwell_same_behaviour_single_datagram_count: raw values stored by the FakeDesk for /bus/01/mix/fader around the event: [-14.97, -14.03, -59.99, -13.01, -11.99, -10.98] → exactly one datagram takes it from -60 to -13.
 test_pulldown_then_stop_backoff_is_also_an_upward_jump: BACKOFF write after pull-down+stop: {'before_db': -60.0, 'after_db': -20.0, 'requested_db': -20.0, 'steps': 1, 'ramp_ms': 0, '_force': True, '_fake_before': -59.99}; report ABORT 'stopped by operator', max -14.0, end -20.0.
Code refs: src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1174 (nxt from belief), 1115-1124 (_write_master ramp_ms=0), 1186-1195 (after = echoed request), 1280-1284 (back-off from belief), module doc 33-35 + line 660 (stale relative-clamp assumption); src/x32mcp/desk.py:948-980 (_move_level: `force` unused, no check_relative, comment 959-965), 991 (before read but only feeds ramp_steps); src/x32mcp/policy.py:438-440 (ms<=0 → [to_db]). Result JSON: $SP/reports/verify-cfs-0-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix; make it cover the back-off too. Concretely in cfs.py: (1) before every RAISE and before the BACKOFF/DONE write, read the live master (desk.get level / _leaf after invalidate(address)) → `actual`; if actual < ses.master_db − 0.5 dB set abort_reason='operator moved the master', set ses.master_db = actual and finalize with final = min(actual, belief − back) so no finalize write is ever above `actual` (skip the write entirely if actual ≤ final). (2) Compute nxt = min(actual, belief) + step. (3) Route CFS raises through adjust_level(+step, ramp_ms≥100) or give _move_level a max_delta_db that is enforced when called from CFS, so a single datagram can never move a bus master by more than step_db regardless of belief. (4) Fix the stale docs (cfs.py 33-35, 660-662, _write_master docstring) so nobody relies on the removed clamp. Optionally subscribe to desk.update pushes on the master address during a run and treat any foreign write as operator intervention immediately rather than at the next dwell tick.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Causal chain read end to end, no gap found:
(1) Belief, never re-read: cfs.py:892-896 seeds ses.master_db from preflight; the only updates are cfs.py:1191/1194 (from `after`) and 1284 (back-off). `grep master_db` shows no read of the live fader anywhere in the loop, and Desk._on_push (desk.py:282-289) only invalidates the read cache — nothing notifies CfsManager of a front-panel move.
(2) Absolute target from belief: cfs.py:1174 `nxt = min(ses.master_db + ses.step_db, target)`; target ≤ min(bus ceiling 0 dB, ringout.master_ceiling_db 0, target_gain_db) (cfs.py:672-673, device.yaml:725,830).
(3) One un-ramped datagram: cfs.py:1121 `_write_master` → `Desk.set_level(..., ramp_ms=0, force=False)`; desk.py:991 reads `before` (the operator's −60) but _move_level (desk.py:947-977) applies only clamp_level (family ceiling, policy.py:254-272) — the comment at desk.py:955-961 records that the ±relative guard was deliberately removed from absolute moves on 2026-09-20 and `force` is now unused there; policy.ramp_steps(before, after, 0) returns [after] (policy.py:439-440) → a single `_write` (desk.py:871-883: tier check, snapshot, rate limiter, conn.set). Nothing compares `before` with the value written.
(4) `after` is not a read-back: desk.py:968-972 returns after_db = the clamped request (`written = after if not superseded`), so cfs.py:1186-1195 always sees progress, sets belief = nxt and carries on to target → BACKOFF → DONE. The cfs comment at 1186 ('after is the read-back') and the module doc at cfs.py:33-35 plus the step check at cfs.py:660 ('else the first raise is refused by the relative clamp') are stale: that clamp no longer exists for set_level.
(5) The detector path does not rescue it: if the howl was detected first, HOLD→NOTCH→VERIFY passes precisely because the operator's pull-down dropped the band, `tamed` is True and the loop resumes raising from belief (cfs.py:1158-1167 → 1174).
Conditions: any ring_out / ring_out_system run (Tier-2 confirmed once at start) where anyone moves the bus/main master down on the surface, X32-Edit or another OSC client between two RAISE ticks — the ordinary human reflex to the howl that ring-out deliberately provokes. Within ≤ dwell_ms (default 1500 ms) the server writes belief+step absolute; magnitude = (belief+step) − operator's value, i.e. +47 dB from −60, +77 dB-equivalent from −∞; bounded above only by min(0 dB, target) and it lands 1 dB above the level that was already ringing. It repeats every dwell until target, then BACKOFF writes belief−3 absolute too. This contradicts BRIEF.md:43 ('single source of truth is the desk … anyone might be touching the front panel concurrently') and defeats the physical emergency reflex BRIEF.md:90 relies on instead of panic().
Real desk vs FakeDesk: identical or worse. An OSC /bus/NN/mix/fader write sets the DSP level the instant the datagram lands and the motor fader follows; X32 faders are not touch-sensitive, so a released fader stays at −13 and keeps climbing; a held fader is re-asserted by the surface only after the DSP has already jumped (a full-level transient into open mics each tick). The FakeDesk's /xremote push behaves like the real desk's (fakedesk.py:432-441), and my run with NO manual cache invalidation shows set_level did read before_db=−60.0 from the pushed value and still wrote −13.0 in 1 step.
Related, same root cause (also reproduced): stop()/abort after a pull-down takes the 'always safe, forced' BACKOFF (cfs.py:1280-1285) which writes belief−6 absolute = an UPWARD +40 dB move over the operator's −60 (fake ended at −20.0 after operator set −60 and stop() was called).
Severity stays critical: a single un-ramped +40…+77 dB write on a live monitor/main bus straight back into a feedback condition, triggered by the most likely operator action during the procedure, overriding the human's emergency pull-down and reporting DONE.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-0-src (commit f6a2a70), tests/integration/test_verify_pulldown.py, 4 passed. Instrumented desk-side (fake value captured at policy.acquire_write, i.e. just before each datagram) and via a set_level spy:
