- test_next_raise_after_pulldown…[invalidate=True] and [invalidate=False] (fake's own /xremote push only): fader writes (desk value before → written): …(−14.97→−14.0), (−59.99→−13.0), (−13.01→−12.0)… →(−7.0→−6.0),(−5.99→−9.0). set_level result for that call: before_db=−60.0, after_db=−13.0, steps=1, ramp_ms=0. Jump +47 dB in one write; report final_stage DONE, abort_reason None, end_master_db −9.0, fake fader −9.0.
- test_pull_to_minus_inf_is_also_overridden: (−inf→−13.0) single write, run DONE at −9.0.
- test_stop_after_pulldown_backoff_raises…: operator sets −60 then cfs.stop(): stages […RAISE, BACKOFF, ABORT], abort_reason 'stopped by operator', fake fader ends at −19.97 (= belief −14 − 6), i.e. the 'lowering' back-off raised the bus +40 dB.
Code refs: cfs.py:1174 (nxt from belief), cfs.py:1115-1124 (_write_master ramp_ms=0), cfs.py:1186-1195 (after treated as read-back), cfs.py:1280-1285 (backoff from belief, force=True), desk.py:986-996 (set_level reads before, unused for a 0 ms ramp), desk.py:947-977 (_move_level: ceiling clamp only, relative guard removed, returns clamped request as after_db), policy.py:430-447 (ramp_steps → [to_db] for ms<=0), policy.py:254-272 (clamp_level ceiling/floor only), device.yaml:725,729-732,826-830, cfs.py:33-35 & 660 (stale belief that the relative clamp bounds a raise), BRIEF.md:43,90.
>
> *Better fix:* Make the desk the source of truth for every master move in the session, not just RAISE: (a) before each RAISE and before BACKOFF/abort, read the live fader (Desk.get/leaf; the /xremote push has already invalidated the cache, else force a read) and if actual < belief − 0.5 dB set abort_reason='master moved externally (actual X vs expected Y)' and finalize with final = min(actual, belief) − back, additionally guarded so no finalize/back-off write is ever ABOVE the live value (skip the write if final ≥ actual); if actual > belief + 0.5 dB also abort (someone else is pushing it up). (b) Compute nxt = min(actual, belief) + step and write raises with a per-call bound: either Desk.adjust_level(+step) (keeps the relative guard) or give _move_level a max_delta_db check against the `before` it already reads when called from CFS, plus ramp_ms ≥ 100 so no single datagram moves a bus/main master more than step_db. (c) Optionally subscribe to desk pushes on the session's master address while armed and set abort_reason immediately (wake the dwell) instead of waiting for the next tick. (d) Fix the stale comments/docs (cfs.py:33-35, 660, 1186) and add the pull-down, pull-to-−∞ and stop-after-pull-down cases as regression tests (see tests/integration/test_verify_pulldown.py in worktree verify-cfs-0-src).

## D6. [critical] F1: +24 dB (comp make-up) and +15 dB broadband (EQ shelf) on Main LR and any bus through TIER-1 set_comp / set_eq_band — no token, no ramp, no relative limit, allowed in show mode
*Angle:* llm-surface · *attacker severity:* critical · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** device.yaml:190 (dyn/mgain tier 1, scale mgain 0..24 dB), device.yaml:207-212 (blk_eq tier 1, incl. eq/{band}/type), device.yaml:258-268 (main strip inherits blk_dyn_core+blk_eq; only mix/* is tier 2), device.yaml:708-721 (guarded globs cover /main/st/mix/* but not /main/*/dyn/* or /main/*/eq/*), src/x32mcp/server.py:1016-1039 (set_comp docstring 'makeup 0..24 dB … Tier 1'), server.py:981-1002 (set_eq_band), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1111-1132 (set_comp: no clamp on mgain beyond the scale, no ramp), desk.py:1050-1081 (set_eq_band: gain clamped ±15 but type LShv/HShv at 20 kHz/20 Hz makes it broadband), src/x32mcp/policy.py:243-283 (ceilings exist only for faders/sends/EQ gain)

**Mechanism.** The Tier-2 boundary for the PA is drawn around /main/st/mix/* (fader 0 dB ceiling, mute). But the main strip's dynamics and EQ sections are Tier 1 in device.yaml and not matched by any guarded glob, so Desk._write's tier check passes and Policy applies no clamp/ramp/relative rule to them. set_comp(main.st, on=True, threshold_db=0, ratio=1.1, makeup_db=24) yields a compressor that never compresses plus +24 dB of make-up = +24 dB of pure gain after the (guarded) main fader. set_eq_band(main.st, 1, type='LShv', freq_hz=20000, gain_db=15) is a +15 dB lift of the whole spectrum; bands stack (6 bands on main). Same on a mix bus feeding IEMs/wedges (bus fader ceiling 0 dB is irrelevant). Nothing is ramped (single writes), the ±6/±3 dB relative limit never applies to EQ/dyn, and show mode does not block it. INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:114-118) tells the model these are ordinary clamped Tier-1 moves.

**Exploit.** show_mode(true) (optional) → set_comp(target='main.st', on=true, threshold_db=0, ratio=1.1, makeup_db=24) → ok:true, /main/st/dyn/mgain = 24.0, dyn/on = ON. Then set_eq_band('main.st', 1, type='LShv', freq_hz=20000, gain_db=15, on=true) and bands 2,3 as HShv@20 Hz +15 → ok:true each. set_comp('bus.3', on=true, threshold_db=0, ratio=1.1, makeup_db=24) → ok:true. No confirm_token was ever requested (policy.pending_confirmations stayed at the one probe token).

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_llm_surface.py::test_F1_main_makeup_gain_and_shelf_boost_are_tier1 PASSES: after the calls fakedesk.value('/main/st/dyn/mgain')==24.0, '/main/st/dyn/on' True, '/main/st/eq/1/g'==15.0 type LShv, eq on; '/bus/03/dyn/mgain'==24.0; all with show mode ON and set_fader('main.st',…) correctly refused GUARDED in the same test. F11 probe: makeup_db=60 is accepted and lands as 24.0 (scale clamp only); type='BU24' (24 dB/oct crossover) at 20 kHz on the Main LR EQ is also accepted Tier 1 (silences the PA).

**Proposed fix.** Treat gain-bearing processing on outputs as guarded: add '/main/*/dyn/*', '/main/*/eq/*' (and arguably '/mtx/*/dyn/mgain', '/bus/*/dyn/mgain') to device.yaml guarded:, or give Policy a clamp for dyn/mgain (e.g. ≤ +6 dB Tier 1, more needs the dance) and refuse shelf/PEQ boosts > +6 dB on main/mtx/bus without a token; apply check_relative to EQ-gain and make-up deltas; restrict main/mtx eq type changes (crossover types) to Tier 2. Update INSTRUCTIONS accordingly.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-0-repro (branch wt/verify-llm-surface-0-repro, commit 8698fe6), test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_llm_surface_0.py — 4 tests, all PASS against unmodified src (27 passed together with test_server_tools.py).

Mechanism verified in code: device.yaml:182-196 (&blk_dyn_core, dyn/mgain tier 1, scale mgain 0..24 dB at device.yaml:83) and device.yaml:207-212 (&blk_eq, eq/{band}/type|f|g|q tier 1) are merged into bus (251), mtx (254-259) and main (261-270); only main mix/* is tier 2, and the guarded globs (device.yaml:710-722) cover /main/st/mix/*, /main/m/mix/* but nothing under /main/*/dyn or /main/*/eq. Policy.tier_for('/main/st/dyn/mgain')==1, '/main/st/eq/1/g'==1, '/main/st/eq/1/type'==1 vs '/main/st/mix/fader'==2 (printed in test). Desk.set_comp (desk.py:1111-1132) → _write_params → _write_value → _write (desk.py:871-884): the only gate is tier>=GUARDED; no clamp beyond the scale's 0..24 range, no ramp, no check_relative. Desk.set_eq_band (desk.py:1050-1081) clamps gain to ±15 only; type/freq unrestricted, so LShv@20 kHz / HShv@20 Hz +15 dB is a whole-band lift, and main/mtx accept eq_type_mtx crossover tokens (BU24 etc., device.yaml:102,261). Show mode (policy.py:224-236) blocks only scene/ringout actions, so it is irrelevant here. server.py set_comp/set_eq_band (981-1039) never call the confirmation guard.

What actually happened when executed (show mode ON throughout): set_fader('main.st',-1) → GUARDED and set_main_fader('st',-1) → requires_confirmation (control: the fader path is guarded); then set_comp('main.st', on=True, threshold_db=0, ratio=1.1, makeup_db=24) → ok:true, summary "Main LR 'Main' comp: on, threshold 0.0 dB, ratio 1.1, makeup +24.0 dB"; FakeDesk /main/st/dyn/mgain=24.0 (raw 1.0), dyn/on True, thr 0.0, ratio '1.1'. set_eq_band('main.st',1,type='LShv',freq_hz=20000,gain_db=15,on=True) → ok:true, clamped None; bands 2,3 HShv@20 Hz +15 → ok; desk shows eq on, 1:LShv 20 kHz +15, 2/3:HShv +15. set_comp on bus.3, main.m and mtx.1 with makeup 24 → ok:true, mgain 24.0 and dyn on for each. policy.pending_confirmations stayed at 1 (the probe token) throughout — no token minted or consumed. makeup_db=60 → ok, lands as 24.0 with no 'clamped' report, and desk.write_count increments by exactly 1 (single unramped write); EQ gain +15 → −15 (30 dB swing) in one call in show mode with no force. type='BU24' @ 20 kHz on Main LR band 1 with eq on → ok:true (high-passes the PA at 20 kHz = silence). Every desk.write event carries tier:1.

Severity: critical per the given scale — an unconfirmed, unramped, single-call +24 dB (make-up) plus up to +15 dB per stacked shelf band on the Main LR / mono / matrix / bus outputs is a loud level change to the PA/IEMs that the Tier-2 main-fader gate was meant to prevent; it works in show mode. One nuance: on the X32 the main-bus dyn/EQ sit pre-fader in the main strip, not 'after the fader' as the claim words it, but with the main fader at its normal position the output level change is identical, so this does not weaken the finding. Also note BRIEF.md §4 deliberately lists 'EQ/dyn' as Tier 1, so this is a design gap in where the Tier-2 boundary is drawn rather than code deviating from spec — the safety consequence is the same.
>
> *Evidence:* Test run: `$SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_llm_surface_0.py -v` → 4 passed (test_F1_main_lr_makeup_and_shelf_boost_need_no_token_even_in_show_mode, test_F1_no_ramp_single_write_per_param_and_out_of_range_makeup, test_F11_crossover_type_on_main_lr_eq_is_tier1, test_F1_evidence_dump). Evidence dump (-s) output: SET_COMP: Main LR 'Main' comp: on, threshold 0.0 dB, ratio 1.1, makeup +24.0 dB {'ok': True, 'applied': {'on': True, 'threshold_db': 0.0, 'ratio': '1.1', 'makeup_db': 24.0}} | SET_EQ: Main LR 'Main' EQ band 1: on, type LShv, freq 20 kHz, gain +15.0 dB {'ok': True, ..., 'clamped': None} | RAW mgain: 1.0 RAW eq1 g: 1.0 RAW eq1 f: 1.0 | tier_for /main/st/dyn/mgain = 1, /main/st/eq/1/g = 1, /main/st/eq/1/type = 1, /main/st/mix/fader = 2 | desk.write events: /main/st/dyn/on True tier 1, /main/st/dyn/thr 0.0 tier 1, /main/st/dyn/ratio '1.1' tier 1, /main/st/dyn/mgain 24.0 tier 1, /main/st/eq/on True tier 1, /main/st/eq/1/type LShv tier 1, /main/st/eq/1/f 20000.0 tier 1, /main/st/eq/1/g 15.0 tier 1 — no policy.confirm_required event. In the main test: show_mode True, set_fader('main.st') → GUARDED, set_main_fader → pending token; after exploits fakedesk /main/st/dyn/mgain==24.0, /bus/03/dyn/mgain==24.0, /main/m/dyn/mgain==24.0, /mtx/01/dyn/mgain==24.0 (all dyn/on True), /main/st/eq/{1,2,3}/g==15.0, policy.pending_confirmations unchanged (1), /main/st/mix/fader still 0.0. makeup_db=60 → applied 24.0, write_count +1, no clamped key. BU24@20 kHz on main.st band 1 → ok, desk type 'BU24'. Code refs: device.yaml:83,190,207-212,251-270,710-722; src/x32mcp/desk.py:871-884 (_write tier gate only), 1050-1081 (set_eq_band), 1083-1093 (_write_params), 1111-1132 (set_comp); src/x32mcp/policy.py:224-236 (show mode list), 238-283 (clamps only for fader/send/EQ gain); src/x32mcp/server.py:981-1002, 1016-1039 (no _guard call), 114-118 (INSTRUCTIONS advertise these as clamped Tier 1).
>
> *Better fix:* Cheapest effective fix is in device.yaml, since Policy.tier_for already honours guarded globs: add "/main/*/dyn/*", "/main/*/eq/*", "/mtx/*/dyn/mgain", "/mtx/*/eq/*/type" (crossover types) and arguably "/bus/*/dyn/mgain" to guarded:, then route set_comp/set_eq_band on those families through the existing _guard confirmation dance in server.py (same pattern as set_main_fader). Complement with a Policy clamp for dyn/mgain (e.g. Tier-1 ceiling +6 dB on outputs, reported as Clamped) and apply check_relative to EQ-gain and make-up deltas (read current value first, as adjust_fader does) so a 0→+15 shelf or 0→+24 make-up jump needs force or a token; restrict eq type changes to crossover tokens (BU*/BS*/LR*) on main/mtx to Tier 2. Update INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:114-121) and BRIEF §4 to say output-strip EQ/dyn boosts are guarded.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (critical)** — I tried to break the causal chain at every link and could not.

(1) Tiering. Descriptor.tier_for (descriptor.py:1041-1046) = 2 only if a guarded glob matches, else the param's own tier. device.yaml:708-721 guarded globs cover /main/st/mix/*, /main/m/mix/*, /*/insert/*, /*/grp/*, /*/config/source … — none matches /main/*/dyn/* or /main/*/eq/*. The main strip (device.yaml:258-268) merges blk_dyn_core (dyn/mgain tier 1, scale mgain lin 0..24 dB, line 190/83) and blk_eq (eq/{band}/g tier 1 clamp ±15, eq/{band}/type tier 1, lines 207-212) and overrides eq type with the 14-entry eq_type_mtx enum (line 261, includes BU6..LR24 crossovers); only mix/* is re-declared tier 2. Verified in-process: policy.tier_for('/main/st/dyn/mgain'|'/main/st/eq/1/g'|'/main/st/eq/1/type'|'/main/m/dyn/mgain'|'/bus/03/dyn/mgain'|'/mtx/01/dyn/mgain') == MIX while '/main/st/mix/fader'/'mix/on' == GUARDED.

(2) Write path. server.set_comp (server.py:1016-1039) → desk.resolve → Desk.set_comp (desk.py:1111-1132): only precondition is _section_path(t,'dyn') is not None, which holds for main/bus/mtx (nodes list device.yaml:616/629/638); values go through _write_params → _write_value → spec.to_raw (scale clamp only: 60 → 24) → Desk._write (desk.py:871-884): tier check passes (tier 1), snapshot, acquire_write, conn.set — one fire-and-forget OSC message per parameter. No Policy.clamp_*, no check_relative, no ramp_steps anywhere on this path. set_eq_band (desk.py:1050-1081) is the same except gain goes through clamp_eq_gain (±15, soft, ok:true) — type and freq are unconstrained beyond the enum/scale, so LShv@20 kHz or HShv@20 Hz +15 dB is a broadband +15 dB lift and the six main bands stack (+90 dB requested gain, verified on the fake). The _tool decorator (server.py:312-328) adds only timeout/envelope; check_show_mode_allows is called only for scene/ringout/setup actions (server.py:335,1130,1157,1588,1746,1792; cfs.py:652,727), and show mode otherwise only tightens relative_limit_db, which this path never consults. No token: policy.guard/_confirm is never invoked by set_comp/set_eq_band.

(3) Real desk vs FakeDesk. docs/research/scales_params.md §8 (lines 602-615) verifies /main/st/dyn/* (MXDY set) and /main/st/eq/[1-6]/type|f|g|q with the 14-entry Xeqty2 enum from X32CfgMain.h, the PDF and a real console scene ('/main/st/dyn ON COMP RMS LOG -15.5 … 0.00 …', '/main/st/eq/1 LShv 79.6 +0.00 2.0'); §4.5 line 417/434 verifies mgain linf [0,24,0.5] f=g/24. HANDOVER §4a confirmed Tier-1 EQ writes land on the real FW 4.13 desk. Nothing in HANDOVER/research distinguishes the fake from the real desk for these addresses, so a real X32 applies /main/st/dyn/mgain ,f 1.0 = +24 dB make-up instantly. With thr 0 dBFS and ratio 1.1 the compressor applies essentially no gain reduction, so it is +24 dB of static gain on the PA (and identically on any mix bus feeding IEMs/wedges, main.m, or a matrix). Minor correction to the claim's wording: X32 output dynamics sit pre-fader (dyn/pos selects pre/post-EQ), not 'after the main fader' — irrelevant to the outcome: the guarded 0 dB fader ceiling does not bound added processing gain; the only bound is digital full scale, i.e. the main bus driven up to 24 dB hotter / into hard clipping, unramped, in one packet.

(4) 'By design' does not refute it. BRIEF §4 and DESIGN §10 do place EQ/dyn in Tier 1, so this is a boundary drawn in the wrong place rather than a coding slip — but the severity rubric is consequence-based (loud level change on the PA with no token), REVIEW_BRIEF §3 explicitly asks 'can a clamp be widened by argument choice' (shelf type + corner frequency + band stacking turns the ±15 dB per-band clamp into a broadband +15…+90 dB request; make-up has no policy clamp at all), and INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:114-118) tells the model that set_eq_band/set_comp are 'clamped … ramped (default 300 ms) and limited to ±6 dB per call (±3 dB in show mode)', which is false for both tools — so a documented guarantee to the client is also defeated. Recovery exists (panic mutes main mix/on; auto pre-write snapshot + Tier-2 restore), but that is after the room has heard it.

Conditions: any connected session; caller only needs Tier-1 tools set_comp / set_eq_band with target 'main.st' | 'main.m' | 'bus.N' | 'mtx.N'; works with show_mode on; no force flag, no confirm_token; 4 writes, far under the 50/s rate limit. Severity stays critical.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-0-src, commit 252a957, tests/integration/test_verify_f1_src.py — 6 passed (runtests.sh, 2.05 s):
- test_tiers_of_main_processing_addresses: policy.tier_for → MIX for /main/st/dyn/mgain, /main/st/dyn/on, /main/st/eq/1/g, /main/st/eq/1/type, /main/st/eq/on, /main/m/dyn/mgain, /bus/03/dyn/mgain, /mtx/01/dyn/mgain; GUARDED for /main/st/mix/fader and /main/st/mix/on.
- test_main_makeup_24db_tier1_no_token_no_ramp_in_show_mode: show_mode(True); srv.set_comp('main.st', on=True, threshold_db=0, ratio=1.1, makeup_db=24) → ok:true, applied {on:True, threshold_db:0.0, ratio:'1.1', makeup_db:24.0}; fakedesk /main/st/dyn/on True, /main/st/dyn/mgain 24.0 (was 0.0); desk.write events: exactly 4 total, exactly ONE for /main/st/dyn/mgain with tier=1 tool=set_comp (0→+24 dB in a single OSC packet, no ramp); policy.pending_confirmations unchanged; same call on main.m, bus.3, mtx.1 → ok:true and /main/m/dyn/mgain, /bus/03/dyn/mgain, /mtx/01/dyn/mgain all 24.0; in the same session set_fader('main.st') → GUARDED and set_main_fader('st') → requires_confirmation (the fader boundary holds, the processing boundary does not exist).
- test_makeup_over_range_is_scale_clamped_not_refused: set_comp('main.st', makeup_db=60) → ok:true, applied 24.0 (scale clamp only, scales.py:402-414).
- test_main_eq_broadband_shelves_stack_tier1: show mode on; band1 LShv@20000 Hz +15 on=True → ok; band2 HShv@20 Hz gain 25 → ok, soft-clamped to 15 with 'clamped' populated; bands 3-6 LShv@20 kHz +15 → ok; fakedesk eq/on True, sum of /main/st/eq/1..6/g == 90.0 dB; six eq/*/g desk.write events, one each, all tier 1; pending_confirmations == 0.
- test_relative_limit_never_consulted_for_eq_or_makeup: show mode (relative_limit_db == 3.0): 0→+15 dB EQ gain and 0→+24 dB make-up both ok:true without force, while adjust_fader('ch.1', +4) → RELATIVE_TOO_LARGE.
- test_crossover_type_on_main_eq_is_tier1: set_eq_band('main.st', 1, type='BU24', freq_hz=20000) → ok:true, fakedesk type 'BU24'; same on ch.1 → BAD_ARGUMENT (main/mtx-specific enum, device.yaml:261/102).
Code refs: device.yaml:83,190,207-212,258-268,708-721; descriptor.py:1041-1046; desk.py:871-884 (_write), 886-895 (_write_value), 1050-1081 (set_eq_band), 1083-1093 (_write_params), 1111-1132 (set_comp); policy.py:243-283 (only fader/send/EQ-gain clamps; check_relative never called from EQ/dyn); server.py:312-344, 981-1002, 1016-1039, 114-118 (INSTRUCTIONS). Real-desk ground truth: docs/research/scales_params.md:417,434,602-615; docs/HANDOVER.md §4a (EQ writes verified live).
>
> *Better fix:* Draw the Tier-2 boundary around gain on outputs, not just the fader: (a) add '/main/*/dyn/*' and '/main/*/eq/*' to device.yaml guarded: (cheap, matches the existing '/main/*/mix/*' intent; set_comp/set_eq_band on main then return GUARDED and a confirming variant can be added later); (b) give Policy a make-up clamp applied in Desk.set_comp for every family (e.g. mgain ≤ +6 dB Tier 1 on bus/mtx/main, anything higher via the token dance) — the scale's 0..24 is a wire range, not a safety limit; (c) in Desk.set_eq_band apply check_relative to the gain delta (read current g first, as set_level does) so show mode's ±3 dB actually bites, and refuse boosts > +6 dB on bus/mtx/main shelves/PEQ without force; (d) restrict eq type changes on main/mtx (crossover types BU*/BS*/LR*, and arguably LCut/HCut) to Tier 2; (e) fix INSTRUCTIONS and the set_eq_band/set_comp docstrings, which currently promise ramp + ±6 dB limits these tools do not have. (a)+(b)+(e) is the minimal set that closes the +24 dB path on a real desk.

## D7. [critical] F2a: panic() is undone by the server's own in-flight restore_snapshot — every output comes back ON seconds after the panic
*Angle:* llm-surface · *attacker severity:* critical · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1062-1074 (panic tool only calls desk.panic), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1220 (panic: 24 mix/on=0 writes, does not cancel ramps/restores/CFS), desk.py:1312-1341 (restore: plan computed up front, lines written sequentially at 50/s through conn.slash), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:1201-1227 (restore_plan emits one whole-section line per differing section, e.g. '/main/st/mix ON -10.0 +0', so any fader/pan difference rewrites mix/on too; order puts bus/mtx/main mix after all channel sections)

**Mechanism.** A confirmed whole-desk restore takes seconds (≤50 lines/s; 'a full restore of a very different desk can take a minute'). Its line list is fixed at start from the snapshot. If the operator calls panic() while it runs, panic writes mix/on=0 to main/bus/mtx, but the restore task keeps going and later writes '/bus/NN/mix ON …', '/mtx/NN/mix ON …', '/main/st/mix ON …' (the snapshot had them unmuted), re-opening every output with no further confirmation. panic() has no notion of 'latch outputs muted' and does not cancel Desk._ramps, Desk.restore or the CFS session. MCPServer dispatches tool calls concurrently, so this interleaving is reachable from a client.

**Exploit.** snapshot_desk('soundcheck'); desk drifts (levels/names); restore_snapshot(id) → token; restore_snapshot(id, confirm_token) starts; ~0.3 s later operator says STOP → panic() → ok, 24 muted; restore finishes → all 24 outputs are ON again at the snapshot's levels.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_llm_surface.py::test_F2a_restore_in_flight_unmutes_outputs_after_panic PASSES and prints 'F2a: 24/24 outputs came back ON after panic: [/main/st/mix/on, /main/m/mix/on, /bus/01/mix/on, …]'. The test asserts /main/st/mix/on==0 and /bus/01/mix/on==0 right after panic (panic landed mid-restore) and ==1 after the restore task completed.

**Proposed fix.** Make panic a latch: Desk.panic() sets a 'panicked' flag that (a) cancels all running ramps and any Desk.restore loop, (b) calls CfsManager.abort('panic'), and (c) makes Desk._write/restore/conn.slash refuse (or strip) any write that would set an output mix/on to ON until an explicit, confirmed clear (e.g. set_main_mute/unmute after panic requires the Tier-2 dance or a dedicated panic_clear tool). At minimum server.panic must await app.cfs.abort() and cancel restore/ramp tasks before returning.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-llm-surface-1-repro (tests/integration/test_verify_f2a.py, committed aa702b2, 3 passed). Code path confirmed by reading: server.py:1062-1074 panic tool only awaits desk.panic(); desk.py:1184-1220 Desk.panic() fires 24 mix/on=0 sets and sets no latch, cancels no ramps/restores/CFS; desk.py:1312-1341 Desk.restore() computes `lines = restore_plan(snap.state, live, ...)` once up front and then loops `acquire_write(); conn.slash(line)` with no re-check; nodes.py:1201-1227 restore_plan emits one whole-section line per differing section (fields for bus/main 'mix' = on fader st pan mono mlevel), so any fader/pan difference in an output's mix section re-writes mix/on=ON from the snapshot; sort order puts bus/mtx/main after every ch/auxin/fxrtn section, i.e. at the tail of a multi-second restore. Concurrency is reachable: the installed MCP SDK dispatches each JSON-RPC request via task-group start_soon (mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:671-686), restore_snapshot runs with _tool(timeout=None) and panic with _tool(timeout=10) as independent coroutines.

Results:
 A (drift moved bus/mtx/main faders + 32 ch; panic 0.55 s into a 1.65 s, 120-line restore): all 24 outputs read OFF right after panic, then 24/24 read ON after the restore finished, main fader back at the snapshot's 0 dB. Exactly the claim.
 C (no output drift at all; panic 50 ms after the confirmed call, while restore is still in ensure_pre_write_snapshot/dump): the plan is computed AFTER panic, sees every output OFF vs snapshot ON, emits 120 lines (vs 96) and turns 24/24 back ON. This widens the claim: on a real desk the dump phase alone is seconds, so a panic any time in that window is always undone regardless of drift.
 B (drift only on input channels, panic after the plan was computed): 0/24 came back ON — the output mix sections were not in the fixed plan. This is the one narrowing condition: outputs whose whole mix section (on/fader/st/pan/mono/mlevel) equals the snapshot at plan time stay muted. In practice any bus/main fader touched since the snapshot (the normal reason to restore) or a panic during the dump phase defeats panic, so the finding stands.

Severity critical per the scale: panic (the documented never-blocked emergency stop) is silently reversed seconds later by the server's own already-running action, re-opening mains/buses/matrices at snapshot levels with no further confirmation — a loud event right after the operator asked for silence. No mitigation exists in code (no 'panicked' flag anywhere in desk.py/cfs.py/server.py).
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-1-repro, test file tests/integration/test_verify_f2a.py (commit aa702b2), run via $SP/runtests.sh <wt> tests/integration/test_verify_f2a.py -s → "3 passed in 6.35s". Printed results:
F2a-A: {'t_panic_s': 0.554, 't_restore_done_s': 1.651, 'restore_done_at_panic': False, 'restore': {'lines': 120, 'written': 120, 'failed': [], 'duration_ms': 1482.6}, 'on_after_panic': [], 'on_after_restore': [/main/st/mix/on, /main/m/mix/on, /bus/01../16/mix/on, /mtx/01../06/mix/on] (24), 'main_st_fader_after': -0.0098}
F2a-B (channels-only drift, panic after plan): lines 96, on_after_panic [], on_after_restore [] (0/24 — panic holds).
F2a-C (panic during dump phase, no output drift): t_panic 0.05 s, lines 120 (24 more than B = the 24 output mix sections added because the plan saw them OFF), on_after_panic [], on_after_restore 24/24 ON.
Code refs: src/x32mcp/server.py:1064-1074 (panic tool), 1240-1268 (restore_snapshot, timeout=None, awaits desk.restore after token); src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1220 (panic: no latch/cancel), 1316-1330 (restore: plan fixed at line 1319, loop writes every line via conn.slash with only acquire_write between); src/x32mcp/nodes.py:1201-1227 (whole-section lines, output families sorted last); device.yaml:590/621 + main/mtx mix sections include mix/on with fader; mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:606,671-686 (per-request start_soon → concurrent tool calls). Result note: $SP/reports/verify-llm-surface-1-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed latch. Concretely: (1) Desk.panic() sets self._panicked=True (cleared only by an explicit confirmed unmute of an output / a panic_clear Tier-2 tool), cancels all self._ramps, and sets an abort flag that Desk.restore's loop checks before each line (return aborted='panic'); server.panic also awaits app.cfs.abort('panic'). (2) While panicked, Desk.restore/restore_plan must strip or force mix/on=OFF for main/bus/mtx sections (render the section line with 'OFF' regardless of snapshot) and _write/_write_value must refuse mix/on→ON on _PANIC_FAMILIES; this also covers scenario C where the plan is computed after the panic. (3) restore should re-validate against a plan-time generation counter so any panic between confirmation preview and execution invalidates the token/plan rather than silently proceeding.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no server-side gap:
1. server.py:1062-1075 panic tool -> desk.panic() only. desk.py:1184-1220 panic(): acquire_write(panic=True) (policy.py:375-379 returns immediately), then 24 fire-and-forget conn.set('<out>/mix/on', 0); it sets no flag, holds no lock, cancels nothing. The only asyncio.Lock in Desk is _snapshot_lock (desk.py:223/851) used by ensure_pre_write_snapshot — restore's write loop does not hold it.
2. server.py:1239-1260 restore_snapshot: after the token is consumed it runs desk.restore() (bounded only by wait_for 30 s + sections/40). desk.py:1312-1341 restore(): ensure_pre_write_snapshot -> live = await self.dump() (a full /node sweep of 2103 nodes; 'takes a few seconds on a real desk' per snapshot_desk docstring) -> lines = restore_plan(snap.state, live, ...) computed ONCE -> for each line: policy.acquire_write() (50/s bucket, device.yaml:733) + conn.slash(line). Nothing in the loop re-checks desk state or any panic latch.
3. nodes.py:1201-1227 restore_plan emits one whole-section '/'-write per differing section rendered from the SNAPSHOT's values via _leading_run over node.fields; descriptor fields are /main/st/mix=(on,fader,pan), /main/m/mix=(on,fader), /bus/NN/mix=(on,fader,st,pan,mono,mlevel), /mtx/NN/mix=(on,fader). So any output whose fader/pan/st/mono/mlevel differs from the snapshot gets '/main/st/mix ON -10.0 +0'-style lines that also write on=ON. _restore_sort_key orders families ch,auxin,fxrtn before bus,mtx,main, so output mix lines come late in the run — maximising the window in which a panic can land before them.
4. Concurrency is reachable: MCPServer sits on mcp 2.2's jsonrpc_dispatcher, which spawns every non-inline request in the task group (.venv/.../mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:560-606, _spawn at 670-686); tools/call is not inline. Transport is stdio (server.py:1949), single client, so the interleaving needs that client to deliver panic while the restore request is still pending OR abandoned-without-cancel (user hits stop, client drops the request but sends no notifications/cancelled -> server task keeps running -> next-turn panic). If the client DOES send notifications/cancelled, the SDK's default 'interrupt' mode cancels the handler scope and the restore stops (my test D confirms a cancelled restore re-opens nothing) — i.e. the only protection is client behaviour, which the server cannot rely on and which the panic docstring ('as fast as possible… never blocked') implicitly promises against.
5. Real desk vs FakeDesk: '/' node-style write with partial value lists in node order is CONFIRMED on hardware (docs/research/transport.md §6.6, DOC 4560-4593) and restore itself was verified live (HANDOVER.md:116 'restore wrote 3 sections in 9 ms, diff afterwards clean'); panic verified live (HANDOVER.md:114). The desk has no interlock between a mix/on=0 leaf write and a later '/main/st/mix ON …' node write, so a real X32 behaves exactly as the fake. Real-desk window: dump phase = seconds, write phase = N_differing_sections × 20 ms (bucket capacity = writes_per_second, policy.py:206) up to ~40 s for a very different desk (2103 nodes) — the tool docstring itself says 'can take a minute'.
Precision on the claim's headline: 'every output comes back ON' is the claimant's construction (they drifted all 24 output faders). Exact conditions: an output is re-opened iff the snapshot has it ON AND its mix section is in the plan, which happens when (i) any field of that section (fader/pan/st/mono/mlevel/on) differed between snapshot and live at plan time — Main LR fader drift between soundcheck and show is the normal case, so the PA is the most likely single output to come back (my variant B: only /main/st/mix/on re-opened, 23 others stayed muted); or (ii) panic lands during restore's live dump: the sweep then reads the outputs as OFF, every one differs from the snapshot's ON, and all 24 are rewritten ON even with zero output drift (my variant C: 24/24). Case (ii) is a seconds-wide window on a real desk and is arguably worse than the claimant's scenario.
Severity: this defeats the emergency stop — outputs (at minimum Main LR in the common case, all 24 in the dump-phase case) re-open with no further confirmation seconds after panic reported '24 outputs muted', at the snapshot's (show-level) fader, bringing back whatever hazard prompted the panic. Per the rubric 'defeat panic' = critical. Mitigating factors (level bounded by the snapshot, requires a confirmed restore in flight, client-dependent concurrency) narrow the preconditions but do not break the chain; I keep critical.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-1-src, commit a4ea938, tests/integration/test_verify_f2a.py — 4 passed (runtests.sh … -s):
A (claimant's setup: 32 ch names+eq drifted, all 24 output faders drifted): restore 88/88 lines in 1.03 s; panic mid-run -> asserted all 24 mix/on==0; after restore completed 24/24 outputs back ON.
B (realistic: channel drift + only /main/st/mix/fader moved -10->-5): 65/65 lines; after panic all 24 muted; after restore back ON == ['/main/st/mix/on'] exactly; /bus/01/mix/on stayed 0 (its section was not in the plan).
C (NO output drift; panic injected when restore's live dump reads /ch/10/config, via a conn.node wrapper): plan grew to 88 lines (64 ch + 24 output mix sections read as OFF) and 24/24 outputs back ON.
D (control): restore task cancelled before panic -> nothing re-opened after 0.5 s.
Code refs: server.py:1062-1075 (panic tool), desk.py:1184-1220 (panic: no latch/lock/cancel), desk.py:1312-1341 (restore: dump once, plan once, sequential slash writes, no re-check), nodes.py:1201-1227 + descriptor fields /main/st/mix=(on,fader,pan) (whole-section lines rewrite on), policy.py:375-379 (panic bypass), policy.py:206 (bucket capacity = 50), server.py:1949 (stdio), mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:560-606,670-686 (concurrent request spawn), docs/research/transport.md:524-538 (§6.6 '/' write CONFIRMED), docs/HANDOVER.md:114-117 (panic and restore verified on hardware).
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's fix direction is right; concretely and minimally: (1) Desk.panic() sets self._panicked_at and a set of latched output prefixes; (2) Desk.restore() checks the latch before each line and either aborts with DeskError('PANICKED') or strips/forces 'OFF' for the mix/on field of any /main|/bus|/mtx mix section (render the line with on=OFF) while latched; also re-dump or drop output mix sections from a plan computed before the panic; (3) Desk._write/_write_value refuse raw writes that set a latched output's mix/on to ON unless guarded=True via set_main_mute/unmute carrying an explicit post-panic confirmation (make unmute of a panicked bus Tier 2 until cleared); (4) server.panic additionally cancels in-flight restore/ramp tasks (track them in Desk, e.g. self._restore_task) and awaits app.cfs.abort('panic'); (5) add a panic_clear (Tier 2) that lifts the latch. Cheapest stopgap if a latch is too invasive: have restore() take a generation counter snapshot at plan time and abort the loop if Desk.panic() bumped it, returning aborted='panic'.

## D8. [high] CFS² 'cuts only / -3 dB steps' is validated against its arm-time belief, not the desk: a band the engineer cut deeper by hand is written back UP (+9 dB boost at the ringing frequency)
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/cfs.py:886 (existing gains read once from preflight), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:410-416 (_DeskGeqWriter validates against self.gains), src/x32mcp/detector.py:567-571 (NotchController._apply: new = belief + step), src/x32mcp/policy.py:405-425 (validate_notch(current=belief)), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1403-1431 (set_geq_band writes absolute gain, no read-compare)

**Mechanism.** NotchController/_DeskGeqWriter keep a private copy of the GEQ gains taken at preflight; xremote pushes of /fx/N/par/* only invalidate the Desk read cache, they never update that copy. When the engineer (feedback_watch is explicitly the human-in-the-loop mode) pulls a GEQ band down on X32-Edit during the session, the next detection at that band plans belief(0)-3 = -3 dB, validate_notch(0, -3) passes, and set_geq_band writes the absolute -3 dB over the desk's -12 dB: a +9 dB boost exactly where it is ringing. Conversely a band the engineer reset to 0 gets belief-3 = -6 in one 6 dB step. The documented guarantees (cuts only, -3 dB per action) hold only relative to CFS²'s memory.

**Exploit.** setup_ringout_eqs([1,2]) (+token) → feedback_watch(1) → engineer sets FX5 GEQ band 22 (2.5 kHz) to -12 dB on the console → ring appears at 2.4 kHz → CFS² 'cuts' band 22 to -3 dB.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_bypass.py::test_exploit_cfs_boosts_a_band_the_engineer_cut_deeper — 'EXPLOIT-9: engineer had band 22 at -12 dB; CFS² 'cut' wrote it to -3.0 dB (a +9.0 dB boost at the ring)'; fakedesk /fx/5/par/22 went 0.1 (-12 dB) → 0.4 (-3 dB).

**Proposed fix.** Validate against the desk, not memory: before each notch read the live band (get_fx is one cached /node read; the push already invalidated it) and use current = min(live, belief); if live < planned new depth, skip the write (already deeper) and adopt live into gains; also subscribe the writer to /fx/{slot}/par pushes to keep gains current. Keep validate_notch but feed it the live value.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-bypass-2-repro (commit 9fd4b27), file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass2.py, 4 tests, all pass against unmodified HEAD a408a2a code (25 passed together with tests/integration/test_cfs.py).

Mechanism verified in the code: cfs.py:886 builds `existing` once from the preflight GEQ read and hands copies to NotchController (cfs.py:888) and _DeskGeqWriter (cfs.py:889/408); detector.py:567-570 `_apply` computes new = max(-9, belief + step) and validates (belief, new); cfs.py:413 `_DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain` calls policy.validate_notch(self.gains.get(band, 0.0), gain_db) — belief again; desk.py:1403-1431 set_geq_band writes the absolute raw gain with no read/compare. Desk._on_push (desk.py:282-284) only invalidates the read cache on an /xremote push; CfsManager subscribes to nothing but connection.state (cfs.py:522) and frames, so the engineer's front-panel GEQ change never reaches either private copy. In my test the Desk-facing read (desk.get_fx(5) and validate_ringout_eqs) DID return -12 dB for band 22 after the push, and policy.validate_notch(-12, -3) raises BOOST_FORBIDDEN — the truth was one cached /node read away but CFS never consults it.

Results:
(1) test_repro_cfs_writes_band_up_after_engineer_cut — feedback_watch(1) (belief band22=0, existing_cuts=[]), engineer sets /fx/5/par/22 raw 0.1 (-12 dB), ring at 2.4 kHz (open-loop SyntheticRta, as the repo's own untamable-ring test does): CFS² planned -3.0, desk /fx/5/par/22 went 0.1 -> 0.400, i.e. -12 -> -3 dB = +9.0 dB at the ringing band; the desk.write event shows gain_db=-3.0 accepted by policy.
(2) test_repro_converse_engineer_resets_band_then_cfs_jumps_6db — CFS cut band 22 to -3, engineer resets to 0 (raw 0.5), next detection wrote -6.0 absolute: desk 0.5 -> 0.300, a single -6 dB step (documented -3 dB/action).
(3) test_repro_writer_and_controller_validate_against_belief_directly — no detector timing: after the hand cut, ses.nc.plan() returns band 22 depth -3 and ses.writer.set_band_gain accepts it; policy.validate_notch(live=-12, -3) raises BOOST_FORBIDDEN.
(4) test_repro_closed_loop_adjacent_merge_boosts_engineer_cut — CLOSED loop with the fake desk's own /meters/15 analyser (GEQ cuts do attenuate the RTA): CFS cuts band 22 to -3, engineer deepens band 22 to -12, a ring then appears at 2 kHz (band 21). merge_adjacent_bands=1 makes plan() 'deepen' the neighbouring notch 22 from belief -3 to -6: desk band 22 went -12 -> -6 (+6 dB at 2.5 kHz) while the actually-ringing band 21 stayed at 0 dB. GEQ writes: [(22,-3.0),(22,-6.0)]. This shows the condition is reachable without an open-loop analyser and the ring need not even be at the hand-cut band.

Conditions (not narrower than claimed): requires someone other than CFS² to change the ring-out GEQ between preflight and a detection — exactly the feedback_watch human-in-the-loop workflow (engineer on X32-Edit/front panel), or any other OSC client, or the MCP client itself via a scene/snippet recall. The only path that would refresh belief is re-arming (new preflight).

Severity: high per the given scale — defeats the documented guarantees 'cuts only / never boosts' (README:238, BRIEF §4, DESIGN §10 validate_notch: new_db <= current_db) and '-3 dB per action' relative to the desk; the +9 dB (up to +12 from -15) lands precisely at a frequency already identified as ringing, during live gain-raising. Not 'critical' because the write is bounded to the GEQ range (max result -3 dB absolute at that band, never above 0 dB) — it is a bounded undo of the engineer's cut, not an unbounded level change, and panic is unaffected.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-2-repro (branch wt/verify-bypass-2-repro, commit 9fd4b27). Test file: tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass2.py. Run: `$SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass2.py -s -p no:randomly` -> 4 passed. Printed output:
'REPRO: engineer had band 22 at -12.0 dB; CFS² 'cut' planned -3.0 dB; desk /fx/5/par/22 raw 0.1 -> 0.400 (-3.0 dB) => change +9.0 dB at the ring' / 'geq writes: [-3.0]'
'REPRO-converse: engineer reset band 22 to 0 dB; next CFS² write planned -6.0; desk raw 0.5 -> 0.300 (-6.0 dB): a -6.0 dB single step'
'REPRO-merge (closed loop): ring at 2 kHz; CFS² wrote band 22 -> -6.0 dB; desk band 22: -12.0 -> -6.0 dB (+6.0 dB); band 21 (the ring) = +0.0 dB' / 'geq writes so far: [(22, -3.0), (22, -6.0)]'.
Asserted in-test: after the /xremote push desk.get_fx(5)['geq']['a']['bands_db'][21] == -12.0 and validate_ringout_eqs(desk,[1])[1].bands_db[21] == -12.0 (Desk knows), while cfs._ses.writer.gains.get(22,0)==0 and cfs._ses.nc.gains.get(22,0)==0 (CFS belief stale); policy.validate_notch(-12.0, -3.0) raises PolicyError BOOST_FORBIDDEN. Code refs: src/x32mcp/cfs.py:886-889 (existing read once, copied into NotchController and _DeskGeqWriter), cfs.py:410-416 (validate_notch(self.gains.get(band,0.0), gain_db)), src/x32mcp/detector.py:567-571 (_apply: new = max(notch_max, belief + step); validate(belief,new)), detector.py:552-561 (plan: adjacent-merge deepens a believed notch), src/x32mcp/policy.py:405-425 (validate_notch trusts caller's current_db), src/x32mcp/desk.py:282-284 (_on_push only invalidates cache), desk.py:1403-1431 (set_geq_band absolute write, no read-compare), cfs.py:522 (CfsManager subscribes only to connection.state).
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's fix is right in direction; concretely: (1) in cfs._notch (or _DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain) read the live band before writing — `live = (await desk.get_fx(slot))['geq'][side]['bands_db'][band-1]` is one cached /node read that the /xremote push has already invalidated — and feed validate_notch(current=live, new); (2) make NotchController plan from the live value: add `nc.observe(band, live_db)` that updates _gains (and _notches for cuts) before plan(), so new = max(-9, min(live, belief) + step); if live <= planned depth, skip the write, adopt live, and count the band as already cut; (3) subscribe CfsManager to conn.on_update for f'/fx/{slot}/par/' during a session and push decoded gains into both nc and writer so belief tracks the desk between detections (covers scene/snippet recalls too); (4) keep _DeskGeqWriter.validate_notch as the last gate but against max information: validate_notch(min(live, belief), new). Also apply the same live read in the adjacent-merge path so a hand-deepened neighbour is not 'deepened' upward while the ringing band goes uncut.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain read end to end, no gap found:
1. The ONLY read of the ring-out GEQ's band gains is provision.preflight → GeqStatus.bands_db, consumed once in CfsManager._open_session (src/x32mcp/cfs.py:886) to seed both NotchController(existing=…) (cfs.py:888 → detector.py:527-533 _gains) and _DeskGeqWriter.gains (cfs.py:889 → cfs.py:407). grep confirms no other consumer of bands_db/get_fx in cfs.py; nothing in _consume/_notch/_notch_and_verify/_verify_decay (cfs.py:1017-1250) re-reads /fx/N/par.
2. Desk._on_push (desk.py:282-284) and _on_write_event only call _invalidate_address → the 2 s read cache is dropped; no callback reaches CfsManager, NotchController or _DeskGeqWriter. CfsManager subscribes only to 'connection.state' events (cfs.py:522) and RTA frames.
3. On a detection, NotchController.plan/_apply computes new = max(notch_max_db, belief + notch_step_db) from self._gains (detector.py:566-568) and calls policy.validate_notch(belief, new) (policy.py:405-421) — passes for belief 0 → -3. _DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain validates again against self.gains (cfs.py:413), same belief, passes. Desk.set_geq_band (desk.py:1403-1431) converts to raw (dB+15)/30 and _write()s it: tier check (par/* is tier 1, device.yaml:291), snapshot, rate limiter, conn.set — no read-compare, no relative limit (relative limit is adjust_level only, desk.py:20/1005). The X32 GEQ par is an absolute linear float (docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md:197-216, VERIFIED), so the desk band jumps from whatever the engineer set to CFS²'s absolute value.
4. Pre-existing deeper cuts read AT preflight are handled correctly (_can_deepen: -12 > -9 is False → plan returns None) — so the defect is strictly mid-session divergence, which is exactly the documented operating condition: README:137 'The engineer is also touching the console … while the server is running', README:891 'Concurrent operators. X32-Edit and the front panel may be used at the same time', and feedback_watch is the hand-operated mode (README:710 'you bring the wedge up by hand'). The documented hard limit 'cuts only and never boosts' (README:238, BRIEF.md:75-76, DESIGN.md:457) therefore holds only relative to CFS²'s private copy, contradicting design rule 'Desk is the source of truth … no shadow state beyond a 2 s cache' (DESIGN.md:24, BRIEF.md:43).
5. Reach is wider than the claim states: with merge_adjacent_bands=1 (device.yaml:819) a ring ONE GEQ band away from a notch CFS² already owns is merged into that notch, so if the engineer deepened CFS²'s own -3 dB cut to -12 (the natural 'it's still live there' move), a new ring at 2 kHz makes CFS² write band 22 (2.5 kHz) to -6: a +6 dB boost at 2.5 kHz AND the real 2 kHz ring is left un-notched. No physical coincidence (ring inside an already-cut band) is needed for this variant. Same _notch path serves ring_out/ring_out_system (Tier 2); there VERIFY then fails, 'deepens' to -9 (still +3 vs -12), cannot deepen → abort/backoff. An LLM-reachable variant also exists (untested): restore_snapshot/recall_scene are not refused while a watch session is armed (server.py:1240-1270 has no CFS-mode check), so a confirmed restore that puts cuts on the GEQ mid-watch produces the same desk-deeper-than-belief state.
Real desk vs FakeDesk: identical. The fake's set() pushes over /xremote like the surface (fakedesk.py:432-441); the real desk echoes parameter changes to xremote clients (transport.md:240) — irrelevant either way because the push is only used for cache invalidation. The write is absolute on both. Probe A used an open-loop SyntheticRta so a ring could be presented under a -12 dB cut (on a real stage: the operator raising the wedge further, which watch mode asks for); probe B used the fake's own closed-loop RTA with a ring at 2 kHz that is not under the 2.5 kHz cut, so no modelling liberty was taken.
Severity re-assessed: HIGH (not critical). It defeats the documented cuts-only/never-boost and -3 dB-per-action guarantees against the actual console and writes a boost at or next to a ringing frequency on a live wedge, but it is bounded (worst case engineer -15 → CFS -3 = +12 dB in one 1/3-octave band, never above 0 dB gain), needs a concurrent manual (or confirmed-restore) change to the same GEQ during an armed session, and does not defeat panic or produce an unconfirmed Tier-2 action.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-2-src (branch wt/verify-bypass-2-src, commit 2d5ae64), tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass2.py, 3 passed (results in $SP/reports/verify-bypass-2-src/result.txt):
A test_A_engineer_cut_midsession_is_written_back_up — feedback_watch(1) on a flat FX5 GEQ; fakedesk.set('/fx/5/par/22', 0.1) as a front-panel move; waited for the /xremote push to arrive at our X32Connection; Desk.get_fx(5) then reports band 22 = -12.0 (cache honoured the push) while cfs._ses.writer.gains[22] and nc.gains[22] are still 0.0; ring at 2.4 kHz → desk.write event {'gain_db': -3.0, 'band': 22, 'side': 'A'}; fakedesk /fx/5/par/22 0.1 → 0.4: 'A: desk band 22 was -12.0 dB; CFS² wrote … -> desk now -3.0 dB (change at the ringing band: +9.0 dB); policy.validate_notch was given current=0.0 not -12.0'. policy.validate_notch(-12.0, -3.0) called directly raises BOOST_FORBIDDEN — the guard exists but is fed the belief.
B test_B_adjacent_ring_deepens_known_notch_over_engineers_deeper_cut — closed loop with the fake's own RTA: CFS² cuts band 22 to -3 (desk 0.4); engineer deepens to -12 (pushed); new ring at 1950 Hz (GEQ band 21) → 'B: ring at ~2 kHz; CFS² wrote band 22 -> -6.0 dB; desk band 22 -6.0 (was -12.0: +6.0 dB), band 21 +0.0' — boost at 2.5 kHz via merge_adjacent_bands=1, the 2 kHz ring not notched at all.
C control test_C_preexisting_deeper_cut_before_preflight_is_respected — same -12 dB set BEFORE feedback_watch: existing_cuts reports -12.0, 2+ detections at 2.4 kHz, zero GEQ writes, band stays -12.0 — proving the defect is purely the stale mid-session belief.
Code refs: cfs.py:886-889 (single read, two private copies), cfs.py:410-416 (writer validates vs self.gains), detector.py:566-570 (_apply from _gains), policy.py:405-421, desk.py:282-284 (push → invalidate only), desk.py:1403-1431 (absolute raw write, no read-compare), device.yaml:291 (par tier 1), :816-819 (step -3, max -9, merge 1); docs: README.md:137,238,710,891; DESIGN.md:24,457; BRIEF.md:43,75-76; fx_routing_scenes.md:197-216 (absolute (dB+15)/30 on the real desk); transport.md:240 (real desk pushes parameter changes).
>
> *Better fix:* Two layers. (1) Last-line guard in _DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain (cfs.py:410): read the live band before writing — live = (await desk.get_fx(slot))['geq'][side]['bands_db'][band-1] (one /node read of /fx/N/par, served from cache unless a push/TTL invalidated it, so it costs an RTT only when someone actually touched the GEQ; well inside the 100 ms budget) — and call policy.validate_notch(current=live, new); if new >= live - eps, do NOT write (already at/below), adopt live into gains and return a 'skipped, desk deeper' result instead of raising, so _notch can log it and the planner can move on. This restores 'cuts only' against the console regardless of planner state. (2) Make the planner desk-truthful: add NotchController.resync(band -> live_db) and call it in CfsManager._notch before plan() with the live gains for the target band and its ±merge_adjacent_bands neighbours; adopt live outright (desk is source of truth) rather than min(live, belief) — min() turns an engineer's reset-to-0 into a single -6 dB write, violating '-3 dB per action', whereas adopting live re-cuts 0 → -3. A band found at ≤ notch_max_db becomes non-deepenable so plan() opens the correct new band (fixes probe B leaving the 2 kHz ring alone). Optionally also conn.on_update-subscribe CfsManager to f'/fx/{slot}/par/' pushes and decode raw*30-15 into nc/writer gains for zero-latency resync, but keep the pre-write read as the authoritative check (UDP pushes are lossy and absent for writes made through our own connection, e.g. restore_snapshot). Separately, refuse restore_snapshot/recall_scene (or at least scope touching /fx/{slot}) while cfs.mode is not IDLE, or force a GEQ resync after them.

## D9. [high] CFS² master writes are absolute from belief and panic does not abort CFS²: the ring-out keeps raising a panicked (muted) bus to target, and stop()/back-off RAISES a master the operator pulled to -oo
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1115-1124 (_write_master: set_level(db) absolute, force=True on back-off), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1254-1294 (_finalize_levels: final = ses.master_db - back, line 1280), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1147-1198 (_run_ringout raises from ses.master_db), src/x32mcp/server.py:1062-1074 (panic does not call cfs.abort), src/x32mcp/desk.py:982-995 (set_level never compares with the live value)

**Mechanism.** The session tracks master_db from its own writes. Raises write belief+step and the DONE/ABORT back-off writes belief-3/-6 through Desk.set_level, which is an absolute move with no relative guard. If the human lowers the bus on the console (the natural reflex to a howl) or Claude's own panic() mutes it, CFS² neither notices nor stops: it continues stepping the fader up (silently while muted, so no detections → 'target reached, no feedback detected', leaving the wedge at target-3 for whoever unmutes it), and when asked to stop it 'backs off' to belief-6, which is an upward write from -oo. The comment 'lowering: never let a clamp refuse it' (force=True) assumes the desk is where CFS² left it.

**Exploit.** ring_out(1, target_gain_db=-10, step_db=2, dwell_ms=250) (+token) → after a few raises panic() → cfs_status still 'ringout', bus master keeps rising while muted → operator yanks Bus 1 fader to -oo on the desk → feedback_watch_stop() → Bus 1 fader written to -24 dB (belief -18 - 6).

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_bypass.py::test_exploit_cfs_backoff_raises_a_master_the_operator_pulled_down — 'EXPLOIT-3: panic left CFS² running (master reached -18.0 dB while muted); operator set -oo, stop() wrote the bus master back UP to -24.0 dB; report: ring-out Bus 1: master -30.0 -> -24.0 dB (highest -18.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; ABORT: stopped by operator'. a.cfs.active was still True 0.6 s after panic returned ok.

**Proposed fix.** Make every CFS² level write 'never above live': read the fader (cache is push-invalidated) before each raise/back-off; if live < belief - 0.2 dB someone intervened → abort without writing (or write min(live, belief-back)); back-off = min(live, belief - back). server.panic (and Desk.panic) must call cfs.abort('panic') and CFS² should subscribe to desk mute/fader pushes on its bus and abort on any external change.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-bypass-3-repro (commit e289829), through the real server tools on a FakeDesk (tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass3.py, 3 tests, all pass = exploit works).

Code path verified: cfs.py:522 CfsManager subscribes only to 'connection.state' (no desk.panic / mute / fader push listener); server.py:1062-1074 panic() -> desk.panic() (desk.py:1184-1221) mutes outputs and never touches app.cfs; cfs.py:1174-1176 each RAISE writes nxt=min(ses.master_db+step,target) via _write_master -> Desk.set_level(ramp_ms=0) (cfs.py:1115-1124); desk.py:982-995/_move_level 948-980 reads the live 'before' leaf but only uses it for ramp/reporting — an absolute move has no relative guard regardless of force (comment desk.py:961-967), so force=True on back-off is moot; cfs.py:1274-1282 back-off final=max(-90, ses.master_db-back) written absolute with force=True. Nothing compares belief with the live fader.

Observed: (1) ring_out(1,-10,2,250)+token running; at belief -24 srv.panic() -> ok, 24 outputs muted, /bus/01/mix/on=0 on the fake; 0.6 s later a.cfs.active=True, cfs_status mode='ringout', belief -24 -> -20 and the desk fader followed to -20 (raises continue behind the mute). (2) Operator front-panel move: fakedesk.set('/bus/01/mix/fader',0.0) -> desk reads -inf (pushed to our /xremote client); feedback_watch_stop() -> one desk.write of -26.0 to /bus/01/mix/fader (belief -20 - 6) = an UPWARD write from -oo; report 'master -30.0 -> -26.0 dB (highest -20.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; ABORT: stopped by operator'; bus still muted. (3) Without intervention after panic the muted bus is stepped to the target and the run ends DONE 'no feedback detected', desk fader left at -13.0 (target-3) behind the mute, zero notches — whoever unmutes gets +17 dB over the operator's original -30 with none of the ring-out's protection. (4) Narrower no-panic variant: operator pulls Bus 1 to -60 dB mid-run; the very next RAISE writes belief+2 = -22.0, i.e. a +38 dB unramped jump straight back to the level that was howling; stop() then 'backs off' to -28.

Severity: high (agree). Levels stay bounded by the Tier-2-confirmed target/ceiling and panic's mutes are not undone, so not 'critical' by the given scale; but it defeats the documented behaviour that stop/abort lowers the master, overrides the human's corrective action with an upward unramped write, keeps an autonomous writer running after panic, and leaves the desk in a dangerous state (raised, un-notched master parked behind a mute). Minor numeric difference from the claim (-18/-24 vs my -20/-26) is timing only; mechanism identical.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-3-repro @ e289829, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass3.py; run log saved at $SP/reports/verify-bypass-3-repro/run.txt. Output: 'REPRO: panic ok=True muted=24; 0.6 s later cfs.active=True mode=ringout master belief -24.0 -> -20.0; desk fader now -19.97' | 'REPRO: operator set -oo (desk read -inf); belief at stop -20.0; stop ok=True; back-off writes after stop: [-26.0]; desk fader after stop: -25.99; report final_stage=ABORT start=-30.0 end=-26.0 max=-20.0 abort_reason=stopped by operator; summary=ring-out Bus 1: master -30.0 -> -26.0 dB (highest -20.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; ABORT' | 'REPRO-2: after panic the run finished DONE end=-13.0 max=-10.0 ... no feedback detected; DONE; desk fader -13.01, bus muted=True' | 'REPRO-3: operator set -60.0 while belief was -24.0; next CFS² write = -22.0 (desk now -22.0) = a +38.0 dB jump in one unramped step; stop -> desk fader -28.03' | '3 passed in 6.56s'. Code refs: src/x32mcp/cfs.py:522 (only connection.state subscribed), 1115-1124 (_write_master absolute, ramp_ms=0), 1174-1195 (raise from ses.master_db), 1274-1282 (final = ses.master_db - back, force=True); src/x32mcp/server.py:1062-1074 (panic never calls cfs.abort); src/x32mcp/desk.py:948-980 (_move_level: 'before' read but never compared; no relative guard on absolute moves), 1184-1221 (Desk.panic).
>
> *Better fix:* 1) server.panic()/Desk.panic(): call app.cfs.abort('panic') (set abort_reason + wake) BEFORE sending the mutes, and make _finalize_levels skip the back-off write entirely when abort_reason=='panic' (panic already silenced the bus; do not touch the fader). 2) Make every CFS² master write 'never above live': Desk._move_level already has the live `before` in hand pre-write (cache is push-invalidated by /xremote), so add an only_lower/never_above_live flag: if requested > live+0.2 dB on a back-off -> write min(live, requested) or nothing; on a RAISE, if live < belief-0.2 dB (someone intervened) -> raise PolicyError so _run_ringout aborts without writing. This costs no extra round trip. 3) CfsManager should subscribe to desk push/leaf changes for {bus}/mix/fader and {bus}/mix/on (and desk.panic) and abort on any external change to its bus while armed. 4) Preflight's 'bus muted' check should be re-asserted before each RAISE.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain checked end to end; no gap found.

1. Belief-only master tracking. cfs.py:1147-1198 `_run_ringout` computes `nxt = min(ses.master_db + step, target)` and calls `_write_master(ses, nxt)`; cfs.py:1115-1124 `_write_master` -> `Desk.set_level(target, db, ramp_ms=0, force=...)` and returns `res['after_db']`. In desk.py:948-980 `_move_level`, `after_db` is `_db1(written)` where `written = after` = the clamped REQUEST (not a read-back), so `ses.master_db` is purely what CFS² asked for. `set_level` (desk.py:982-995) does read `before = await self._leaf(address)` (live, push-invalidated cache) but uses it only as the ramp start; with `ramp_ms=0` `policy.ramp_steps` (policy.py:439-440) returns `[end]`, a single absolute datagram. Nothing compares the destination with the live value; the ±6 dB relative guard was deliberately removed from absolute moves (desk.py:959-964) and `adjust_level` is the only caller of `check_relative`. During the run CFS² never re-reads the bus fader or `mix/on` (preflight checks mute once, provision.py §Preflight; `_run_ringout`/`_dwell` read nothing).

2. Back-off is absolute from belief and forced. cfs.py:1280-1283 `final = max(FADER_FLOOR_DB, ses.master_db - back)`; `_write_master(ses, final, force=True)` ('lowering: never let a clamp refuse it'). If the live fader is below `master_db - back` (operator pulled it down), this is an upward write. Same path is taken by stop() (cfs.py:587-613 sets abort_reason, wakes, awaits), abort(), MCP cancellation (cfs.py:704-708 -> `_unwind` -> `_finalize_levels`) and any internal error.

3. panic does not reach CFS². server.py:1063-1074 `panic` -> `Desk.panic` (desk.py:1184-1221) writes 24 `mix/on 0` and publishes `desk.panic`; nothing subscribes to it (grep: only the publisher). CfsManager subscribes only to `connection.state` (cfs.py:522) and RTA frames. `App` calls `cfs.abort` only on reconnect (server.py:197) and `cfs.stop` on disconnect (server.py:676). So after panic the session stays armed, keeps raising a muted bus (no acoustic loop -> no detections -> 'target reached'), backs off 3 dB and reports DONE 'no feedback detected', leaving the bus muted at target-3 (default target = ceiling 0 dB -> parked at -3 dB) with no verified notches for whoever unmutes it (unmute is Tier 1, no confirmation).

4. Reachability of concurrent calls is by design, not a test artefact: `feedback_watch_stop`'s docstring (server.py:1674-1677) says it aborts a running ring-out, which is only possible if the client issues it while the `ring_out` tool call (blocking, 'runs for up to several minutes', timeout=None) is in flight; the mcp 2.2 dispatcher spawns each request as its own task (mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py `_dispatch_request` -> `_spawn` -> `tg.start_soon`). panic() is therefore exactly as reachable as feedback_watch_stop. The operator-on-the-console variants (pull fader / press MUTE) need no concurrency at all, and BRIEF.md:90 states the emergency reflex IS physical/X32-Edit, i.e. the expected emergency action is the one CFS² is blind to.

5. Real desk vs FakeDesk: the FakeDesk models a front-panel move as a stored value plus an /xremote push to registered clients, which is what transport.md documents for the real console; my test B shows the Desk cache did see the live -inf (get_bus read -inf) and CFS² still wrote belief-6. On hardware the semantics are identical (OSC fader write is absolute; X32RACK has no physical faders so the operator uses X32-Edit, whose moves are pushed to our client the same way). Muted bus on a real PA = no loop gain = no detections, so scenario A/D end identically ('DONE, no feedback'). Scenario C is worse on hardware than on the fake: the operator pulls the wedge to -oo because it is howling (detector late/missed, e.g. ring outside the 250 Hz-8 kHz window or below threshold, or VERIFY 'passes' because the operator's pull-down produced the decay), and one dwell later CFS² slams the master to belief+step in one unramped datagram, above the level that was already ringing; this repeats every dwell until someone mutes or stops it.

Documented guarantees defeated: BRIEF §4/§5 'abort: back off 6 dB and stop' (back-off must lower), cfs.py module doc 'Every lowering master write ... dropping a level is always safe' (assumption false once the desk is not where CFS² left it), cfs.py:1268-1269 'never cut/override a fader the operator set', and panic as the emergency stop of anything the server is doing. Severity: high (not critical) — every write stays bounded by target/ceiling (≤0 dB bus) and panic's mutes themselves hold; but it leaves the desk in a dangerous state after the emergency action (muted bus parked near target with unverified notches; operator pull-down overridden within one dwell; stop() raising from -oo). Scenario C is the closest to critical (an unrequested, unramped upward jump of a live wedge above its howl point) but requires operator intervention mid-run plus a missed/late detection.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-bypass-3-src (commit b9079d3), tests/integration/test_verify_bypass3.py, run with $SP/runtests.sh ... -s: 4 passed in 12.1 s. Ring-out Bus 1 from -30 dB, step 2 dB, via CfsManager+Desk+FakeDesk.
A (panic): `desk.panic()` at belief -24.0 -> bus.1 in muted list, wire mix/on=0; 0.8 s later cfs.active still True and master_db > -24; fader writes [-28,-26,-24,-22,-20,-18,-16,-14,-12,-10,-13]; report 'master -30.0 -> -13.0 dB (highest -10.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; DONE'; desk left muted at -13.0 dB.
B (operator -oo then stop): fakedesk.set_value(fader,-inf) pushed; `desk.get_bus(1)['fader_db']` read -inf (cache push-invalidated) while belief was -24.0; `cfs.stop()` -> desk.write -30.0 on /bus/01/mix/fader; fakedesk fader = -29.98 dB (raised from -oo); report 'ABORT: stopped by operator', end_master_db -30.0.
C (operator -oo mid-run, no stop): belief -24.0, operator sets -oo; next RAISE 400 ms later put the desk fader at -22.0 (belief+2, single datagram, ramp_ms=0); run then completed DONE.
D (operator presses MUTE mid-run): run continued to target -4.0, parked at -7.0 dB with mix/on still 0 (muted), report 'no feedback detected; DONE'.
Code refs: cfs.py:1115-1124 (_write_master absolute via set_level/set_main_level, returns requested value), cfs.py:1170-1196 (raise from ses.master_db), cfs.py:1280-1283 (final = ses.master_db - back, force=True), cfs.py:522 (only connection.state subscribed), cfs.py:587-613/621-635 (stop/abort -> same BACKOFF path), cfs.py:704-713 (cancel/unexpected -> _unwind -> _finalize_levels), desk.py:948-980 (_move_level: after_db = clamped request, before only feeds ramp_steps), desk.py:982-995 (set_level no live comparison), policy.py:439-440 (ramp_ms<=0 -> [end]), desk.py:1184-1221 + server.py:1063-1074 (panic never touches cfs), server.py:1674-1677 (feedback_watch_stop documented to abort a running ring-out => concurrent tool calls are the intended model), mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:_dispatch_request/_spawn (requests run concurrently). Result notes: $SP/reports/verify-bypass-3-src/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed direction; concretely: (1) In CfsManager._write_master read the live fader first (Desk._leaf/get_bus; the cache is push-invalidated and a miss is one round trip) and enforce 'never above live': if live < ses.master_db - 0.2 dB treat it as external intervention -> set abort_reason='master moved externally' and do NOT write; for back-off write min(live, belief - back) and skip the write entirely when live is already <= that (so stop()/cancel after an operator pull-down or panic writes nothing upward). Also re-check the bus `mix/on` each RAISE/dwell and abort (no back-off write needed) if it was muted externally. (2) server.panic/Desk.panic: set the CFS² abort flag synchronously BEFORE sending the 24 mutes (flag only, no await, so panic stays <1 ms) and mark the session 'panicked' so _finalize_levels skips the fader write (or lowers only). (3) Subscribe CFS² to /xremote pushes for its bus's fader and mix/on and wake+abort on any change it did not author (Desk already publishes desk.write for its own writes, so foreign changes are distinguishable). (4) Report honesty: a run that ended while the bus was muted or after external moves must not say 'DONE, no feedback detected'; flag it ABORT with the reason so nobody trusts the parked level/notches. Add regression tests equivalent to A-D in tests/integration/test_cfs.py.

## D10. [high] connect()/disconnect() are unconfirmed: one call silently re-targets every later write at a different console on the LAN, and a failed connect drops the live desk so panic() is refused
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:645-666 (connect tool), src/x32mcp/server.py:669-683 (disconnect, 'Tier 0'), src/x32mcp/server.py:192-202 (App.connect aborts CFS, re-arms snapshot), src/x32mcp/connection.py:288-350 (conn.connect closes the existing socket BEFORE trying the new host; failure → DISCONNECTED), src/x32mcp/server.py:225-236 (_desk() refuses everything incl. panic while DISCONNECTED/CONNECTING)

**Mechanism.** discover_consoles lists every X32/M32 on the subnet (FOH + monitor desk is a common pairing) and connect(host) switches the single X32Connection with no confirmation and no check against X32_HOST/the current console; all Tier-1 tools then operate the other desk (wedges/IEMs). conn.connect tears the working connection down first, so connect('10.0.0.99') or disconnect() — both unconfirmed — leaves the server detached from the live desk mid-show: panic() returns NOT_CONNECTED until someone reconnects, and a running CFS² session is aborted as a side effect.

**Exploit.** discover_consoles() → connect(<other console>) → set_fader('bus.1', 0, ramp_ms=0) lands on the other desk. connect('127.0.0.1', 1) → CONNECT_FAILED → panic() → NOT_CONNECTED.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_bypass.py::test_connect_retargets_writes_to_another_console (second FakeDesk 'X32-MONITORS'): 'EXPLOIT-5: unconfirmed connect() moved writes to X32-MONITORS ; after a failed connect panic -> NOT_CONNECTED'; other.value('/bus/01/mix/fader') == 0.0.

**Proposed fix.** connect() to a host other than the currently connected console (or X32_HOST) and disconnect() while connected → Tier-2 confirmation naming both consoles; make conn.connect build the new socket and complete /info before closing the old one (swap on success, keep the old desk on failure).

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced every leg of the claim from scratch in worktree verify-bypass-5-repro (commit 75b3d30), tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass5.py, 5/5 passed, against unmodified product code at a408a2a.

(A) Silent re-target. server.py:645-666 `connect` is a plain Tier-0 tool: no _confirm() call, no comparison with the currently connected console or Settings.x32_host, and App.connect (server.py:192-202) just swaps the single X32Connection. With a second FakeDesk 'X32-MONITORS' on the in-memory LAN: discover_consoles(0.5) listed both desks; connect(other) returned ok=True with no requires_confirmation/confirm_token; then set_fader('bus.1', -5, ramp_ms=0) -> ok "Bus 1 'Bus01' fader −10.0 dB → −5.0 dB" and the monitor desk's /bus/01/mix/fader became -5.01 while the FOH desk stayed at -9.98; mute('bus.2') turned bus 2 off on the monitor desk only; panic() muted /main/st/mix/on on the monitor desk and left the FOH main ON. (I pre-set both desks' bus 1 to -10 dB so the write is observable — the claimant's own evidence 'other.value(/bus/01/mix/fader)==0.0' proves nothing by itself because 0.0 dB is the FakeDesk default, but the mechanism is real.)

(B) Failed connect drops the live desk. connection.py:297-298 closes the working socket/tasks before resolving/binding//info for the new host; on /info timeout (connection.py:321-326) or resolve failure (connection.py:395-400) state -> DISCONNECTED and nothing points back at the old host (host fields were already overwritten, loops cancelled, no auto-reconnect). connect('127.0.0.1', 1) -> CONNECT_FAILED, conn.state 'disconnected', panic() -> NOT_CONNECTED ('last error: no /info reply from 127.0.0.1:1'), FOH main still unmuted, still disconnected 1.5 s later, mute('bus.1') also NOT_CONNECTED; only an explicit connect(foh) restores panic. Same with an unresolvable hostname ('no-such-desk.invalid' -> CONNECT_FAILED, state disconnected, panic NOT_CONNECTED). With production defaults (timeout_s 0.5 × 3 attempts, CONNECT_TIMEOUT_S 15) the panic-less window starts at the moment of the call and lasts until someone reconnects.

(C) disconnect() (server.py:669-683) is likewise unconfirmed: ok=True, then panic -> NOT_CONNECTED. Note this exact behaviour is asserted by the shipped suite (test_server_tools.py::test_connection_status_before_and_after_connect), i.e. it is by design, but the design leaves an unconfirmed Tier-0 call able to remove the emergency stop.

(D) CFS side effect: App.connect aborts an active CFS session (server.py:196-197) BEFORE attempting the new connection, so a failed connect('127.0.0.1',1) during feedback_watch(1) left cfs.active False, mode idle, last session '...watch-bus01: ABORT', connection disconnected — feedback protection silently off and the desk unreachable.

Severity: high, as claimed. It does not by itself produce an unbounded/loud change (writes on the wrong desk are still Tier-1 clamped/relative-limited; panic on the wrong desk is silence, and the panic refusal is recoverable by the same client with one connect call), so not critical; but it defeats two documented guarantees — 'wrong strip' at whole-console scale with no confirmation, and 'panic … never blocked' becomes NOT_CONNECTED after a single unconfirmed, failed Tier-0 call that also tears down a running ring-out/watch.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-5-repro (branch wt/verify-bypass-5-repro, commit 75b3d30), file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass5.py; run: runtests.sh <wt> tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass5.py -s -> 5 passed in 4.90s. JSON evidence in $SP/reports/verify-bypass-5-repro/part_{A,B,B2,C,D}.json.
Part A stdout: discover -> [X32-FAKE@127.0.0.1:41000, X32-MONITORS@127.0.0.1:41001]; connect_other -> {ok: True, summary: 'Connected to X32-MONITORS (X32RACK FW 4.06, OSC server V2.07) at 127.0.0.1:41001', requires_confirmation: None}; set_fader -> {ok: True, "Bus 1 'Bus01' fader −10.0 dB → −5.0 dB"}; other_bus1_fader -5.0147, foh_bus1_fader -9.9804; other_bus2_on False, foh_bus2_on True; after panic: other main.st on False, FOH main.st on True.
Part B: bad_connect -> {code: CONNECT_FAILED, 'cannot connect to 127.0.0.1:1: no /info reply from 127.0.0.1:1'}; state_after_bad_connect 'disconnected'; panic -> {code: NOT_CONNECTED, message: 'not connected to a desk: call connect(host) first (last error: no /info reply from 127.0.0.1:1)', state: disconnected}; state_after_wait(1.5 s) 'disconnected'; FOH /main/st/mix/on still True; reconnect + panic ok.
Part B2: connect('no-such-desk.invalid') -> CONNECT_FAILED, state disconnected, panic NOT_CONNECTED ("cannot resolve 'no-such-desk.invalid'").
Part C: disconnect() ok=True without confirmation; panic -> NOT_CONNECTED.
Part D: after setup_ringout_eqs dance + feedback_watch(1) active, connect('127.0.0.1',1) -> CONNECT_FAILED; cfs.active False, mode idle, cfs_status 'CFS² idle (last session …-watch-bus01 on bus 1: ABORT, 0 notch(es))', conn 'disconnected'.
Code refs: server.py:645-666 (connect tool, Tier 0, no _confirm, no host check), server.py:669-683 (disconnect, Tier 0), server.py:192-202 (App.connect: cfs.abort before conn.connect, no rollback), connection.py:297-298 (close() of the live socket before resolve/bind//info), connection.py:321-326 and 395-400 (failure -> DISCONNECTED, old host already overwritten at 300-301), server.py:225-236 (_desk refuses everything incl. panic while DISCONNECTED/CONNECTING).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix, with specifics: (1) connect(host) while state is CONNECTED/DEGRADED and (resolved host,port) != the current console -> route through _confirm('connect', summary naming both consoles 'leave X32-FAKE @a:b, join X32-MONITORS @c:d', payload {from, to}); same for disconnect() while connected (and refuse both in show mode unless confirmed). Optionally pin to Settings.x32_host when set: any other host needs the dance even from DISCONNECTED. (2) Make X32Connection.connect make-before-break: resolve + bind a NEW socket + /info(/xinfo) on it first; only on success cancel the old loops/close the old transport and swap host/console; on any failure leave the old transport, tasks, _host/_port/_console and state untouched and raise — so CONNECT_FAILED never changes what panic() targets. (3) In App.connect, abort CFS only after the new /info succeeded (or stop-with-restore rather than abort), and do desk.invalidate()/snapshot re-arm only on success. (4) Consider letting panic() fall back to a best-effort send to the last-known console address when state is DISCONNECTED but a host is known, flagged delivered='unconfirmed'.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain read end to end and holds with no gap; every link is product code, not FakeDesk behaviour, so a real X32 behaves identically.
(1) Unconfirmed retarget: server.py:644-666 connect() has no Policy involvement at all (no tier lookup, no token, no comparison with conn.status.console or Settings.x32_host); docstring says 'Replaces any existing connection ... Tier 0'. App.connect (server.py:192-202) -> X32Connection.connect (connection.py:288-350) swaps the single socket; Desk/Policy are per-descriptor, not per-console, so every later Tier-1/Tier-2 tool and panic() address whatever console answered /info. discover_consoles (server.py:625-641, connection.py:654+) broadcasts /xinfo to 255.255.255.255 and each local /24, so a FOH+MON pair on one control LAN is enumerated for the model. Policy clamps still apply on the other desk (moves stay Tier-1 bounded), which is why this is not critical; but 'wrong strip' becomes 'wrong console', and panic() then mutes the monitor desk while the FOH desk it was meant for keeps running (reproduced).
(2) Failed connect drops the live desk: connection.py:297-298 `if self._transport is not None or self._tasks: await self.close()` runs BEFORE resolve/bind//info; on /info timeout (connection.py:317-323) _teardown + DISCONNECTED, and _resolve failure (connection.py:393-400) also lands in DISCONNECTED. close() cancelled the heartbeat/watchdog tasks, so no reconnect loop exists afterwards; _host/_host_spelling were overwritten with the bad host and _console=None, so the server no longer knows the original desk (connection_status shows console None, no host). _desk() (server.py:225-236) raises NOT_CONNECTED for DISCONNECTED/CONNECTING and panic() (server.py:1064-1069) goes through _desk(), so panic is refused until an explicit connect(original) — and even Desk.panic's send_raw fallback could not help because the transport is None. Single-desk variant also confirmed: during a desk cutout (DEGRADED, which the watchdog would auto-recover per HANDOVER's PSU-cutout scenario) an LLM calling connect(same host) 'to fix it' converts DEGRADED into permanent DISCONNECTED with no watchdog; when the desk returns the server stays disconnected and panic stays refused.
(3) disconnect() (server.py:669-683) is likewise unconfirmed ('Tier 0'); it does await cfs.stop() first (safe ordering for CFS), then conn.close(); panic refused afterwards by construction.
(4) CFS side effect is worse than the claim states: App.connect calls cfs.abort() (cfs.py:621-635), which for a RINGOUT session only sets abort_reason and wake — it does not await the run (unlike stop(), cfs.py:587-613, which waits _STOP_WAIT_S). conn.connect then closes the socket at its first yield; the woken ring-out reaches _finalize_levels BACKOFF (cfs.py:1274-1290), its _write_master fails with NOT_CONNECTED ('back-off failed'), and because close() goes CONNECTED->DISCONNECTED without a DEGRADED event, _on_connection_event (cfs.py:1296-1304) never starts _restore_master.
