Net: the console we just left keeps its bus master at the highest level the ring-out had pushed it to (no 6 dB abort back-off, no start-level restore, RTA prefs not restored), while the server is now attached to a different console or to nothing. That violates BRIEF §4/§5 'abort: back off 6 dB ... connection loss restores snapshot level'.
Mitigations considered and found insufficient to refute: connect is Tier 0 by design (BRIEF §5 lists it under Phase-1 reads), so this is a design gap rather than a bypass of an implemented check; the connect() envelope and connection_status name the console, but nothing forces a confused/runaway client to read them; an MCP host's per-tool approval UI is outside the stated threat model (server-side policy is the guardrail). Conditions: retarget needs a second X32/M32 reachable on the LAN (common FOH/MON pairing, not this project's single X32 Rack); the drop needs only any non-answering host string or a connect during a cutout — trivially reachable. Severity stays high: wrong-console writes/panic, loss of panic with no auto-recovery after one unconfirmed call, and a ring-out left un-backed-off on the abandoned desk; no unbounded level change (clamps still apply), so not critical.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-5-src, commit 00d717e, tests/integration/test_verify_bypass5.py — 4 passed (runtests.sh, 7.2 s). Output: test_a: `connect(other) -> Connected to X32-MONITORS ... ` ok=True, no requires_confirmation; set_fader('bus.1',-5,ramp_ms=0) -> 'FOH bus1 = -9.98  MON bus1 = -5.01' (write landed on the other desk); panic() ok -> 'FOH main on = True  MON main on = False' (panic muted the wrong console); disconnect() ok with no confirmation, then panic -> NOT_CONNECTED. test_b: connect('127.0.0.1',1) -> CONNECT_FAILED 'no /info reply'; conn.state DISCONNECTED, _transport None, _tasks == [] (no reconnect loop); connection_status console None, no host field; panic -> 'panic failed: not connected to a desk ... (last error: no /info reply from 127.0.0.1:1)'; FOH /main/st/mix/on still True; only an explicit connect(fakedesk) restores panic. test_b2: desk silent -> DEGRADED; connect(same host) -> CONNECT_FAILED -> DISCONNECTED, tasks []; desk un-silenced; 1.5 s later state still 'disconnected', panic NOT_CONNECTED (DEGRADED would have auto-recovered via watchdog). test_c: ring_out(1, target -8, step 1, dwell 150 ms) raised FOH bus1 master -20 -> -17; srv.connect(other) -> ring_out returned final_stage=ABORT abort_reason='reconnect requested' start=-20 max=-17 end=-17 restored=None; FINAL 'FOH bus1 master = -17.0 dB (started -20)  MON bus1 = -39.97 (was -40)': no 6 dB back-off, no restore, FOH /-prefs/rta/source left at BUS01. Code refs: server.py:644-666 (connect, no policy), 669-683 (disconnect Tier 0), 192-202 (App.connect: cfs.abort not awaited to completion, then conn.connect), 225-236 (_desk refuses panic when DISCONNECTED), 1064-1069 (panic via _desk); connection.py:297-298 (close before trying new host), 317-323 and 393-400 (failure -> DISCONNECTED, no tasks), 352-365 (close cancels watchdog, console=None); cfs.py:621-635 (abort only flags RINGOUT), 1274-1290 (back-off write fails -> end_master_db = raised level), 1296-1304 (restore only on DEGRADED, never on close).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix and extend it: (a) connect(host) while CONNECTED/DEGRADED to a console whose resolved address differs from the current one (or from X32_HOST when set) and disconnect() while connected -> Tier-2 confirmation whose action_summary names both consoles (name/model/host) and says panic will be unavailable / which desk later writes will hit; connect to the same address stays Tier 0 (it is the recovery path). (b) X32Connection.connect: resolve, bind a second socket and complete /info+/xinfo against the new host first, then atomically swap transport/console/tasks and close the old one; on any failure leave the existing connection (and its watchdog) untouched and raise. (c) App.connect/disconnect: use `await cfs.stop()` (which awaits the ring-out's BACKOFF/ABORT) instead of fire-and-forget cfs.abort() before touching the socket, so the abandoned desk gets its 6 dB back-off (or start-level restore) while still reachable. (d) Keep the last-good host in App (or fall back to Settings.x32_host) and let panic() attempt a one-shot re-bind + raw mix/on 0 burst to it when state is DISCONNECTED after a failed connect, so 'panic is never blocked' survives a bad connect. (e) Refuse connect() outright while a CFS² ring-out/system run is active unless force-confirmed.

## D11. [high] ring_out_system() with no plan: token bound to {'plan': None}; the stage list is recomputed at redeem time, so buses never shown in the action_summary are rung out (master raised to 0 dB)
*Angle:* tokens · *attacker severity:* critical · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1797-1803 (stages from live validate_ringout_eqs), server.py:1826 (payload = {'plan': _jsonable(plan)} → {'plan': None}), server.py:1836 (cfs_plan=None passed on), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1399-1413 (_system_stages recomputes a third time from the live desk)

**Mechanism.** For plan=None the server derives stages = every bus whose ring-out GEQ validates NOW, renders them into the summary ('1 stage(s) in order: Bus 1'), but binds the token only to {'plan': None}. On the confirmed call the payload trivially matches, stages are recomputed from the live desk, and cfs.ring_out_system(None) recomputes them again. Any bus that started validating inside the 300 s TTL (a separately confirmed setup_ringout_eqs for it — the user agreed to 'set up an EQ', not to a ring-out — or an insert switched on at the console/X32-Edit) is included and its master is stepped to the 0 dB ceiling with open mics. That is a +20 dB raise on a wedge/IEM bus the human never approved: an unconfirmed Tier-2 level change.

**Exploit.** 1) setup_ringout_eqs([1]) → confirm. 2) ring_out_system() → pending, summary names Bus 1 only, token T. 3) setup_ringout_eqs([2]) → confirm (or operator enables the FX5R insert on bus 2 at the desk). 4) ring_out_system(confirm_token=T) → runs Bus 1 AND Bus 2; Bus 2 master goes −20 → 0 dB.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_tokens.py::test_ring_out_system_no_plan_token_executes_stages_not_in_the_summary (line 265) PASSES: pend['stages']==[1] and summary '1 stage(s) in order: Bus 1'; after step 3 the redeemed run printed executed stages: [1, 2] [(1, -2.0, 0.0, -3.0, 'DONE', None), (2, -20.0, 0.0, -3.0, 'DONE', None)] — bus 2 start −20.0, max_master_db 0.0, not skipped.

**Proposed fix.** Bind what executes: payload = {'stages': <the server-computed, normalised stage list>} (bus labels, targets, step/dwell/budget, mics) for BOTH the plan=None and explicit-plan paths, and always pass cfs_plan={'stages': stages} to cfs.ring_out_system so cfs never re-derives the bus list after confirmation. Then a desk change between calls yields BAD_TOKEN instead of extra stages.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-tokens-0-repro (commit da69861), test file tests/integration/test_verify_tokens_0.py, 3 passed in 4.0 s against the real server/policy/cfs code on a FakeDesk + SyntheticRta.

Mechanism verified exactly as claimed: src/x32mcp/server.py:1797-1803 builds `stages` for plan=None from a live validate_ringout_eqs() and renders them into the action_summary, but server.py:1827 binds the token to `payload = {"plan": _jsonable(plan)}` == {'plan': None} (I read a.policy._tokens[T].payload directly: {'plan': None}). On redeem, policy.guard compares {'plan': None}=={'plan': None} -> passes; server recomputes stages from the desk, then passes cfs_plan=None (server.py:1836) so cfs._system_stages (cfs.py:1399-1413) recomputes the bus list a third time from the live desk. Nothing that was shown to the human is bound to the token.

Exploit executed: setup_ringout_eqs([1]) confirmed; mics ch1/2->bus1, ch3->bus2; bus1 master -2 dB, bus2 master -20 dB. ring_out_system() -> pending, stages==[1], summary '1 stage(s) in order: Bus 1', 'Bus 2' absent. Then either (a) a separately confirmed setup_ringout_eqs([2]) or (b) NO MCP call at all — operator sets bus 2 insert to FX5R/on/PRE at the console (GEQ2 already in FX5 from the bus-1 setup) — makes bus 2 validate. Redeeming the ORIGINAL token then ran stages [1, 2]: bus 2 start -20.0 dB, max_master_db 0.0, end -3.0 dB, not skipped; fakedesk /bus/02/mix/fader read back -2.98 afterwards, i.e. a bus the human never approved was ramped +20 dB to the ceiling with an open mic and left +17 dB hotter permanently. Control test: the explicit-plan path IS bound (changing the plan on the second call -> BAD_TOKEN), so the defect is specific to plan=None.

Severity re-assessment: I rate it HIGH rather than the claimed critical. It squarely defeats the documented token-binding guarantee (policy.py docstring: a token 'can only ever execute the request whose summary was shown'), which the rubric lists under high. The resulting level change is real and large (+20 dB ramp, +17 dB persisting on a wedge/IEM bus) but it is the ordinary ring-out ramp: 1 dB steps with dwell, clamped at the 0 dB master ceiling, preflight-gated (the extra bus must have a validated GEQ and open mics) and feedback-detector supervised, during a run the human did consent to happening now — not an unbounded/step jump or a different action class. It also needs an intervening desk change inside the 300 s TTL (device.yaml:738), though that sequence is very natural for an LLM client (sees Bus 2 missing from the pending summary -> 'fixes' it with setup_ringout_eqs([2]) -> redeems the old token) and variant (b) needs no second tool call at all. It meets the literal 'unconfirmed Tier-2 action' wording of critical, so top-of-high / borderline-critical; the lead can adjudicate, but the facts are not in doubt.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-0-repro (branch wt/verify-tokens-0-repro, commit da69861). Test: tests/integration/test_verify_tokens_0.py — run `$SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_verify_tokens_0.py -s -p no:randomly` -> 3 passed in 4.02s. Printed output: `token payload: {'plan': None}`; `executed stages: [(1, -2.0, 0.0, -3.0, 'DONE', None), (2, -20.0, 0.0, -3.0, 'DONE', None)]`; `bus 2 fader now: -2.98142671585083`. Front-panel variant (no second MCP call, operator sets /bus/02/insert sel=FX5R on=1 pos=PRE): `bus 2 validate after panel change: {... 'ok': True ...}`; `executed stages (panel variant): [(1, -2.0, 0.0, None), (2, -20.0, 0.0, None)]`. Control test_explicit_plan_token_is_bound_to_the_plan: second call with an extra stage -> BAD_TOKEN (explicit-plan path is correctly bound). Code refs: src/x32mcp/server.py:1797-1803 (stages from live validate_ringout_eqs, cfs_plan=None), server.py:1827 (payload = {"plan": _jsonable(plan)}), server.py:1836 (a.cfs.ring_out_system(cfs_plan) with cfs_plan None), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1399-1413 (_system_stages re-derives buses from the desk), src/x32mcp/policy.py:347-356 (guard compares stored payload == payload only), device.yaml:738 confirm_token_ttl_s: 300, device.yaml:826-830 step 1 dB / ceiling 0 dB. dwell_ms was monkeypatched to 10 in the test only to shorten the ramp; it does not affect which buses run.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix and would make it slightly stricter: for BOTH paths bind the token to the fully normalised, server-computed stage list that was rendered into the summary — payload = {'stages': [{bus: label, target_gain_db, step_db, dwell_ms, notch_budget, mics/included mic channels, patch_file}]} — and on the confirmed call pass exactly that list as cfs_plan={'stages': stages} so cfs._system_stages never re-derives buses from the live desk after confirmation. For plan=None, still recompute the live-validating set on the second call and compare it to the bound list so a desk change between calls yields BAD_TOKEN (re-run the dance) rather than silently running fewer/more buses. Optionally also include each stage's preflight open-mic list and start master in the bound payload (as single-bus ring_out's summary does) so a mic routed to the bus or a master moved between calls likewise invalidates the token.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap:
1. server.py:1794-1801 — for plan=None the tool derives stages from a LIVE read (_prov.validate_ringout_eqs over buses 1..16+main, provision.py:369-426: ok == FX insert present, GEQ/TEQ type, insert/on, not shared) and sets cfs_plan=None. The human-facing summary (server.py:1828-1832) and the `stages=` extra enumerate exactly that list ("System ring-out of 1 stage(s) in order: Bus 1").
2. server.py:1827 — payload = {"plan": _jsonable(plan)} == {"plan": None}. server._confirm → policy.guard (policy.py:347-360) stores only action + payload; on redeem it compares `stored != dict(payload)` and never compares the summary (tok.summary is kept but unused for validation). So a second call with plan=None always matches, whatever the desk now looks like.
3. On the confirmed call the server recomputes stages again (used only for labels and the timeout) and calls a.cfs.ring_out_system(None) (server.py:1836); cfs._system_stages(None) (cfs.py:1399-1413) re-derives the bus list a third time from the live desk, and ring_out_system (cfs.py:721-770) runs cfs.ring_out on every bus that validates, each with its own preflight. cfs.ring_out (cfs.py:637-720) raises that bus master in step_db steps to min(policy.level_ceiling_db, ringout.master_ceiling_db)=0 dB (device.yaml:725,830), then backs off 3 dB. Nothing between mint and redeem pins the bus list.
Conditions for the extra stage to actually execute (all reachable): within confirm_token_ttl_s=300 s (device.yaml:738) another bus must (i) get a GEQ/TEQ inserted and insert/on — via a separately confirmed setup_ringout_eqs([n]) (whose summary says 'set up an EQ', not 'ring out') OR purely at the console/X32-Edit with no MCP confirmation at all (README principle 5: the engineer works the desk concurrently) — and (ii) pass preflight: bus unmuted, master not -oo and below 0 dB, at least one unmuted physical-input channel sending to it at ≥ −40 dB (provision.py:627-663) — i.e. exactly a live wedge/IEM bus with open mics. The explicit-plan path is NOT affected (payload carries the whole plan → BAD_TOKEN on any edit; verified).
FakeDesk vs real X32: the chain depends only on node reads of /bus/NN/insert, /fx/N/type|par, channel sends and headamp routing — all VERIFIED paths per docs/research and exercised live (HANDOVER §4a/§4b); no FakeDesk-only behaviour is involved, so a real desk behaves identically. On a real IEM bus there is no acoustic loop, so the detector never fires and the master climbs the full way (1 dB / 1.5 s by default) to 0 dB and is left at −3 dB — e.g. +17 dB net into in-ears the human never approved; on a wedge it is deliberately driven into feedback (that is what ring-out does) on a strip nobody was told about.
Severity re-assessment: the raise is bounded (≤ 0 dB ceiling), gradual and under the armed ring detector, and it is disclosed in the report afterwards — but it is a Tier-2 level change of up to ~+20 dB (more from a lower park) on a bus that was never in the action_summary, executed on a token whose documented purpose (policy.guard docstring: 'so it cannot execute anything but what was summarised') is defeated, and route (b) needs no second MCP confirmation of any kind. Per the rubric ('unconfirmed Tier-2 action' / large level change) critical stands; lower bound high.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-tokens-0-src (commit 3937418), tests/integration/test_verify_tokens_0.py — 3 passed (runtests.sh, -s):
A (claimant's route, second confirmed setup_ringout_eqs([2])): pending summary 'System ring-out of 1 stage(s) in order: Bus 1 …', pending stages [{'bus': 1}], stored token payload printed as {'plan': None} action ring_out_system; redeemed run EXECUTED: [(1, -2.0, 0.0, -3.0, 'DONE', None), (2, -20.0, 0.0, -3.0, 'DONE', None)]; fake desk /bus/02/mix/fader after run −2.98 dB (was −20.0).
B (stronger route, no second MCP confirmation): after the pending call only fakedesk.set_value('/bus/02/insert/sel','FX5R') + ('/bus/02/insert/on',True) (front-panel change onto the free B side of the GEQ2 that setup([1]) loaded in slot 5); validate_ringout_eqs([2]).ok became True; policy.pending_confirmations stayed 1; redeem with the original token → EXECUTED: [(1, -2.0, 0.0, -3.0, 'DONE', None), (2, -20.0, 0.0, -3.0, 'DONE', None)], bus 2 fader −2.98 dB (+17 dB net, max_master_db 0.0, not skipped).
C (control): ring_out_system({'stages':[{'bus':1,'dwell_ms':10}]}) → token; redeem with an added {'bus':2} stage → BAD_TOKEN, confirming only the plan=None path is unbound.
Code refs: server.py:1794-1801 (live stage derivation, cfs_plan=None), 1827 (payload={'plan': None}), 1833 (_confirm), 1836 (cfs.ring_out_system(None)); policy.py:347-360 (guard compares payload only, summary ignored); cfs.py:1399-1413 (_system_stages re-derives from desk), cfs.py:674-675 (ceiling/target = 0 dB); device.yaml:738 (TTL 300 s), 725/830 (0 dB ceilings).
>
> *Better fix:* Claimant's fix is correct and sufficient for this finding: in server.ring_out_system bind what will execute — payload = {'stages': normalised stage list (bus label, target_gain_db, step_db, dwell_ms, notch_budget, patch_file, mics)} for BOTH branches, and always call a.cfs.ring_out_system({'stages': stages}) so cfs never re-derives the bus list after confirmation (make cfs.ring_out_system refuse plan=None when invoked from the server, or drop the None branch of _system_stages entirely). A desk change between the two calls then yields BAD_TOKEN instead of extra stages. Two cheap hardenings on top: (1) in Policy.guard also compare the stored action_summary with the summary presented at redeem (tok.summary is already stored but unused) — a generic backstop that catches any live-derived content drifting between mint and redeem for every Tier-2 tool (it would also have caught the sibling issue that ring_out's open-mic list is live-derived and unbound); (2) include the per-stage resolved target_db and start master in the bound payload/summary so the human sees 'Bus 2: −20 → 0 dB' per bus rather than 'default 0 dB'.

## D12. [high] restore_snapshot token binds the snapshot id, not its content: editing the snapshot JSON between calls makes the confirmed 'undo' write arbitrary raw values (Main LR +10 dB, no clamp, no ramp)
*Angle:* tokens · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1247-1257 (payload = {'id': snap.id, 'scope': scope}; preview computed at call 1 only), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1312-1330 (restore writes restore_plan lines with conn.slash — no tier check, no clamp_level, no ramp), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:830-861 (snapshot re-read from disk on every load)

**Mechanism.** Call 1 loads <id>.json, diffs it against the desk and shows 'N setting(s) would change' + preview; the token carries only id+scope. Call 2 re-reads the file from disk and restores whatever it now contains. restore() renders node lines straight from the snapshot values and sends them with '/', bypassing Policy.tier_for/clamp_level/ramps entirely (guarded /main/st/mix/* included). So any change to the file inside the 300 s window executes unseen. The MCP threat model's client (Claude Code / any agent with file tools) can write ~/.x32-mcp/snapshots; so can anyone on the FOH laptop.

**Exploit.** 1) snapshot_desk('safe'). 2) restore_snapshot(id) → 'Restore snapshot …: 0 setting(s) … would change', token T. 3) Edit snapshots/<id>.json: state.sections['/main/st/mix']['mix/fader'] = 10.0. 4) restore_snapshot(id, confirm_token=T) → ok, written>=1; Main LR fader jumps to +10.0 dB in one write (policy main ceiling is 0 dB).

**Evidence.** test_restore_snapshot_token_is_bound_to_the_id_not_the_content (line 193) PASSES: pend['count']==0; after the edit the redeem returned ok and fakedesk.value('/main/st/mix/fader') == 10.0 (±0.1).

**Proposed fix.** Bind the content: include a sha256 of the snapshot file (or of snap.state.to_dict()) and the computed change list hash in the payload, and on call 2 restore from the Snapshot object whose hash matches (or refuse BAD_TOKEN if the file changed). Independently, have Desk.restore clamp fader/send/main levels through Policy.clamp_level (report clamps) so even a legitimate snapshot cannot exceed the configured ceilings without an explicit force.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-tokens-1-repro (commit bbf68e7), test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens_1.py, 4/4 pass against unmodified product code.

Mechanism verified by reading and executing: server.py restore_snapshot (lines ~1240-1268) builds payload = {"id": snap.id, "scope": scope} and hands it to _confirm → Policy.guard (policy.py:347-361), which on redeem only checks action equality and `stored != dict(payload)`; the recomputed call-2 summary/diff is discarded, never compared. SnapshotStore.load (nodes.py:861-880 → _load_path/_read_envelope) re-reads <id>.json from disk on every call, with the id taken from the file's own "id" field, so an edited file keeps its id. Desk.restore (desk.py:1312-1340) renders restore_plan lines from the snapshot values and sends them with conn.slash after acquire_write only — no Policy.tier_for, no clamp_level, no ramp — so guarded /main/st/mix goes out as a single node line.

Observed: preview call returned count==0, action_summary "…0 setting(s) in 0 section(s) would change", token T. After editing state.sections['/main/st/mix']['mix/fader']=10.0 in the JSON, redeem with T returned ok, lines=1, written=1; the only '/' line sent was '/main/st/mix ON +10.0 +0'; FakeDesk /main/st/mix/fader == 10.0 (was ≈0.0); policy.main_fader_max_db == 0.0; the policy.confirmed event still carried the "0 setting(s) would change" summary; no policy.clamped event. Same hole with scope='ch.1' (ch fader → +10, ch ceiling +5). Control: unedited dance writes 0 lines. Boundary: 'latest' re-resolving to a newer snapshot IS refused (BAD_TOKEN) because the resolved id is in the payload — the hole is strictly same-file content (and desk drift between calls).

Severity: high, as claimed. It defeats the documented guarantee in policy.py module doc ("a token can only ever execute the request whose summary the user saw") and produces a +10 dB un-ramped Main LR jump past the 0 dB ceiling, allowed even in show mode. Not rated critical only because it needs an out-of-band write to the snapshot dir (~/.x32-mcp/snapshots) inside the 300 s TTL — reachable for the stated threat model (an agent host with file tools, or anyone on the FOH laptop) but not via MCP tools alone.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-1-repro (branch wt/verify-tokens-1-repro, commit bbf68e7). Test: tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens_1.py — run `$SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens_1.py -s` → 4 passed. Captured output of test_restore_token_bound_to_id_not_content_main_lr_plus_10: SLASH LINES SENT: ['/main/st/mix ON +10.0 +0']; EVENTS: [('policy.confirmed', {'action': 'restore_snapshot', 'summary': 'Restore snapshot 20260921-120001-safe (safe) … (whole desk): 0 setting(s) in 0 section(s) would change'}), ('desk.restore', {'lines': 1, 'written': 1, 'failed': 0, 'scope': None})]; REDEEM RESULT: {"ok": true, "summary": "Restored snapshot …: 1/1 section(s) written in 81 ms", "lines": 1, "written": 1}; MAIN LR before: -0.0098 after: 10.0 policy main ceiling: 0.0. test_restore_token_bound_to_id_not_content_scoped_ch1: 'scope ch.1: 1/1 section(s) written … fader now 10.0'. test_control_unedited_restore_is_noop: written==0. test_latest_alias_reresolution_is_blocked: BAD_TOKEN (id is bound, content is not). Code refs: src/x32mcp/server.py:1247-1259 (payload={'id','scope'}, snap re-loaded each call), src/x32mcp/policy.py:347-361 (guard compares payload only), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:826-880 (SnapshotStore re-reads file; id from envelope), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1312-1340 (restore → conn.slash, acquire_write only, no tier/clamp/ramp), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:1201-1231 (restore_plan renders raw snapshot values).
>
> *Better fix:* Claimed fix is right; concretely: (1) in restore_snapshot put a digest in the payload — sha256 of the snapshot file bytes (or of json.dumps(snap.state.to_dict(), sort_keys=True)) plus optionally a hash of the computed change list — so an edited file yields BAD_TOKEN; better still, cache the loaded Snapshot object keyed by token at call 1 and restore from that object at call 2 instead of re-reading disk. (2) Independently harden Desk.restore: run fader/send/main/bus level fields through Policy.clamp_level (publishing policy.clamped and reporting clamps in the result) and ramp level increases, with an explicit force flag (itself part of the bound payload) for a genuine above-ceiling undo. (3) Optionally write snapshots 0600 and refuse to load a file whose mtime is newer than its recorded 'created' + a small slack, as defence in depth.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain read end to end, no gap:
1) server.py:1247-1257 restore_snapshot: BOTH calls do `snap = a.snapshots.load(id)` (fresh disk read), diff, and build payload = {"id": snap.id, "scope": scope}. Nothing derived from the snapshot's content (state, file mtime/hash, change list) enters the payload. policy.guard (policy.py:347-359) compares only action + that dict, so an edited file with the same envelope id redeems cleanly. Single-use, TTL (device.yaml confirm_token_ttl_s: 300) and action binding all hold; the id IS bound (token for snapshot A refused for B: BAD_TOKEN, my test v4).
2) nodes.py SnapshotStore.load (830-882): re-reads <dir>/*.json on every call and matches on the envelope's "id" field (not the filename); among equal ids the newest 'created' wins, so a drop-in file with the same id also works (my test v3) — no need even to modify the original file.
3) desk.py:1312-1330 Desk.restore: restore_plan(snap.state, live) -> render_node_line -> format_token 'db1' -> format_db(x) verbatim (nodes.py ~408, scales.py:270) -> conn.slash(line). No Policy.tier_for, no clamp_level, no ramp_steps; only acquire_write. The guarded glob /main/st/mix/* (device.yaml:709) and main_fader_max_db: 0 (device.yaml:726) are never consulted. +40 in the file renders as '/main/st/mix ON +40.0 +0' (test v2); only the desk-side fader range bounds it (+10 dB).
4) After call 2 the tool returns ok with 'N/N section(s) written' — the operator who approved '0 setting(s) would change' gets no hint that Main moved.
Real desk vs FakeDesk: identical outcome. `/ ,s` node-style writes with engineering values are CONFIRMED (docs/research/transport.md §6.6, DOC examples `ch/01/mix/fader 3`), the /main/st/mix node format 'ON -oo +0' is verified from a real scene file (scales_params.md:304/615), and Desk.restore itself was exercised live at M5 (HANDOVER.md:116, 3 sections via `/`). The console snaps/clamps out-of-grid values (DOC 4566-4568), so the level is bounded at the fader top, +10 dB — i.e. 10 dB over the configured main ceiling, applied in one unramped step (from -oo if that is where main sat), to a live PA.
Preconditions / threat-model fit: requires write access to snapshot_dir between call 1 and call 2 (<=300 s). No MCP tool mutates an existing snapshot (save() never overwrites; ids are unique), so this is out-of-band relative to the MCP surface. But it is squarely in reach of the documented client: the checked-in .mcp.json/README (README:399-437) set X32MCP_HOME to the repo root, so snapshots/ lives inside the Claude Code agent's own working tree and an Edit/Write needs no network and (in acceptEdits/auto) no prompt — a prompt-injected or jailbroken agent can do steps 1-4 with its ordinary tools, and the human's only checkpoint (the Tier-2 summary) says '0 setting(s) would change'. Caveat that tempers severity: an agent with shell+LAN access could also bypass the MCP entirely with a raw UDP packet, so this is a defence-in-depth gap rather than the only path; still, REVIEW_BRIEF §3 explicitly asks whether a token 'can be made to authorise a different action', and policy.guard's docstring promises 'it cannot execute anything but what was summarised' — that promise is false for restore_snapshot. I keep 'high' (documented token-binding guarantee defeated, loud Main LR move behind a misleading confirmation), not critical, because it needs file access outside the tool surface within the TTL.
Second half of the claim (Desk.restore never clamps) is true but, absent tampering, is previewed and confirmed (test v5: a legitimately 'hot' snapshot restores Main to +10 after a Tier-2 preview) — arguably by design for an undo. Incidental finding from v5: the confirm preview reuses diff phrasing snapshot->live, so it reads "Main LR 'Main' fader +10.0 dB → −∞ dB" for a restore that will RAISE main from −∞ to +10 — the arrow points the wrong way for the action being confirmed, which compounds the 'summary does not describe what will execute' problem.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-1-src (commit 4289b3b), tests/integration/test_verify_tokens1.py, 5 passed in 3.9 s via runtests.sh:
- v1 (exact claim): snapshot_desk('safe'); restore_snapshot(id) -> pending, count==0, '0 setting(s)' in action_summary; edit state.sections['/main/st/mix']['mix/fader']=10.0; restore_snapshot(id, token) -> ok, written=1/1; spied conn.slash lines == ['/main/st/mix ON +10.0 +0'] (exactly one main line, no ramp); fakedesk.value('/main/st/mix/fader') 10.0 (was -0.01).
- v2: file value 40.0 -> line '/main/st/mix ON +40.0 +0' sent verbatim (no server-side clamp); fake desk clamps to 10.0.
- v3: leave original file alone, drop snapshots/zzz-evil.json with the same envelope id and a newer 'created' -> token redeems ok, main -> 10.0 (load() matches envelope id, newest first: nodes.py:852-858,875-877).
- v4 (control): token for snapshot A used with snapshot B's id -> BAD_TOKEN (id binding holds).
- v5 (no tamper): desk main at +10 when snapshotted, then pulled to -oo; restore_snapshot(scope='main.st') preview = "1 setting(s)… would change:\n- Main LR 'Main' fader +10.0 dB → −∞ dB" (arrow backwards); redeem writes +10 in one line.
Code refs: server.py:1247 (load per call), :1251 payload={'id','scope'}, :1260 desk.restore(snap); policy.py:355-359 payload equality only; desk.py:1319-1326 restore_plan -> acquire_write -> conn.slash, no tier/clamp/ramp; nodes.py:408-410 db1 -> format_db verbatim; device.yaml:709 '/main/st/mix/*' guarded, :726 main_fader_max_db 0, :738 TTL 300 s. Real-desk parity: transport.md §6.6 CONFIRMED, scales_params.md:615 '/main/st/mix ON   -oo +0' from a real scene file, HANDOVER.md:116 restore verified live.
>
> *Better fix:* (1) Bind the content, not the name: at call 1 compute h = sha256(canonical JSON of snap.state.to_dict()) (or of the file bytes) and put it in the payload with the id/scope and len(changes); at call 2 recompute from the freshly loaded snapshot and let policy.guard's equality check refuse BAD_TOKEN on mismatch. Simpler and stronger: keep the Snapshot object loaded at call 1 in an App-side dict keyed by the token and restore from THAT object at call 2 (no second disk read), evicting on redeem/expiry. (2) Make SnapshotStore.load match on the filename stem, not the envelope 'id' field, so a drop-in file cannot shadow an existing id. (3) Defence in depth in Desk.restore: run fader/level fields of main/bus/mtx/ch sections through Policy.clamp_level and either clamp-with-report or require force=True (surfaced in the Tier-2 summary as 'N value(s) exceed the configured ceilings') — and ramp upward fader moves larger than relative_limit_db instead of one-shot writes. (4) Fix the preview direction: render restore previews as live → snapshot ('−∞ dB → +10.0 dB'), since describe_changes' snapshot→live phrasing currently shows the opposite of what the confirmed action will do.

## D13. [high] apply_patch_plan(include_source=True) token binds the file path, not the plan: rewriting the patch file between calls repatches channels the user never saw
*Angle:* tokens · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1321-1335 (plan loaded fresh each call; payload = {'file': str(path), 'include_source': True}; summary lists rows from call-1 contents), src/x32mcp/patches.py apply_patch_plan (executes call-2 contents)

**Mechanism.** The summary enumerates 'ch N → SOURCE' from the file as read at call 1; the token carries only the resolved path. At call 2 the file is parsed again and applied as-is (sources are guarded /ch/NN/config/source writes executed with guarded=True). Any edit to patches/<file> in the TTL window — by the agent's own file tools, a runaway export/edit loop, or someone with the folder open — changes what the confirmed call routes: e.g. vocal channels repointed to USB playback / OFF mid-show.

**Exploit.** 1) patches/moved.csv = 'ch16 → AUX1'; apply_patch_plan('moved', include_source=True) → summary 'patch 1 input source(s): ch 16 → AUX1', token T. 2) Overwrite moved.csv with rows ch1→USBL, ch2→USBR, ch3→OFF, ch4→AUX5. 3) apply_patch_plan('moved', include_source=True, confirm_token=T) → ok; ch1..4 sources changed, ch16 untouched.

**Evidence.** test_apply_patch_plan_token_is_bound_to_the_path_not_the_content (line 168) PASSES: applied printed 4 rows (ch1 USBL, ch2 USBR, ch3 OFF, ch4 AUX5, each changed ['name','color','source']); fakedesk /ch/16/config/source still 'IN16', /ch/01 'USBL', /ch/04 'AUX5'.

**Proposed fix.** Put the executed content in the payload: payload = {'file': path, 'include_source': True, 'rows': [(r.channel, r.source) for r in plan.rows if r.source], 'sha256': hash of file bytes}; on call 2 compare and apply the PatchPlan parsed at call 2 only if identical (else BAD_TOKEN). Cheapest: stash the parsed plan keyed by token at call 1 and apply that object at call 2.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-tokens-2-repro (commit 6344229, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens2.py, 5 tests, all pass against unmodified product code at a408a2a).

Code path: src/x32mcp/server.py:1317-1336 apply_patch_plan loads the plan fresh on every call (`plan = _patches.load_patch_plan(path, ...)`, line 1324) and builds the Tier-2 payload as `{"file": str(path), "include_source": True}` (line 1327). policy.Policy.guard (policy.py:347-360) compares only that stored payload to the call-2 payload, so any two calls resolving to the same path are treated as "the same request" regardless of what rows the file holds. The action_summary shown to the user enumerates `ch N -> SOURCE` from the call-1 contents; patches.apply_patch_plan (patches.py:551-597) then executes the call-2 contents via desk.set_source (desk.py:1343-1350, guarded=True /ch/NN/config/source writes). This directly contradicts the documented guarantee in policy.py:22-25 ("a token can only ever execute the request whose summary the user saw").

What I executed: (1) patches/moved.csv = one row ch16->AUX1; call 1 returned pending, summary 'Apply patch plan moved.csv: label 1 channel(s) AND patch 1 input source(s): ch 16 -> AUX1', rows=1. (2) overwrote moved.csv with ch1->USBL, ch2->USBR, ch3->OFF, ch4->AUX5. (3) call 2 with same args + token returned ok=True, 'moved.csv: 4 channel(s) labelled, 0 unchanged, 4 source(s) patched'; FakeDesk /ch/01..04/config/source became USBL/USBR/OFF/AUX5, /ch/16 stayed IN16 — exactly the claimed exploit. Also confirmed it works with show_mode ON (apply_patch_plan is not in SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED; dance proceeds and the swapped content — ch1->OFF — is applied mid-show). TTL window is 300 s (device.yaml:738).

Controls that bound the finding (all executed): a different file name with the token -> BAD_TOKEN (path IS bound); replacing moved.csv by adding moved.yaml between calls -> BAD_TOKEN because _patch_path (server.py:535-551) tries .yaml before .csv so the resolved path changes; the token remains single-use after the swapped apply (reuse -> BAD_TOKEN). So the hole is precisely: same resolved path, different bytes, within 300 s.

Severity: high per the given scale — it defeats the token/payload-binding guarantee for a Tier-2 routing action (channels the user never saw get repointed to USB playback/OFF/other inputs, including during show mode). Not critical: it still needs one confirmation round trip and a file mutation between calls; it is not an unconfirmed Tier-2 action from nothing, nor an unbounded level change by itself. The precondition is realistic under the stated threat model: the LLM client normally has file-edit tools and the intended workflow is edit-plan-then-apply; a confused agent that gets a token, is told 'yes, and also change ch3', edits the CSV and re-calls with the old token applies unreviewed routing with no new confirmation.
>
> *Evidence:* Test file: <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-2-repro/tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens2.py (commit 6344229 on wt/verify-tokens-2-repro). Run: `$SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens2.py -s -p no:randomly` -> 5 passed. Printed output of test_token_bound_to_path_not_content:
SUMMARY@call1: Apply patch plan moved.csv: label 1 channel(s) AND patch 1 input source(s): ch 16 → AUX1
BEFORE: {1: 'IN01', 2: 'IN02', 3: 'IN03', 4: 'IN04', 16: 'IN16'}
RESULT@call2: {'ok': True, 'summary': 'moved.csv: 4 channel(s) labelled, 0 unchanged, 4 source(s) patched', 'rows': 4, 'applied': [{'channel': 1, ... 'changed': ['name', 'source'], 'source': 'USBL'}, {'channel': 2, ... 'source': 'USBR'}, {'channel': 3, ... 'source': 'OFF'}, {'channel': 4, ... 'source': 'AUX5'}], 'unchanged': [], 'failed': []}
AFTER: {1: 'USBL', 2: 'USBR', 3: 'OFF', 4: 'AUX5', 16: 'IN16'}
test_show_mode_variant: show_mode(True) -> apply_patch_plan('moved', include_source=True) still returns requires_confirmation (not SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS); after rewriting moved.csv to ch1->OFF, call 2 with the token -> ok=True, applied ch.1 source OFF; fakedesk /ch/01/config/source == 'OFF'.
Controls: test_control_different_path_is_rejected -> BAD_TOKEN, ch1 untouched; test_control_suffix_swap_changes_path (moved.yaml added between calls) -> BAD_TOKEN; test_token_is_single_use_after_swap -> second reuse BAD_TOKEN.
Source refs: server.py:1324 (fresh load each call), server.py:1327 payload = {"file": str(path), "include_source": True}, server.py:1328-1332 summary built from call-1 rows, server.py:1335 applies call-2 plan; policy.py:353-360 guard compares stored payload == payload only; policy.py:22-25 documented guarantee; patches.py:576,590-593 source writes; desk.py:1349 guarded write; device.yaml:738 confirm_token_ttl_s: 300.
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's fix is right; the minimal robust version: include the executed content in the bound payload, e.g. payload = {"file": str(path), "include_source": True, "sources": [[r.channel, r.source] for r in plan.rows if r.source is not None]} (optionally plus name/color rows or a sha256 of the file bytes). Because guard() compares payload equality, a changed file then yields BAD_TOKEN automatically with no other code changes, and the plan object parsed at call 2 (already in hand at server.py:1324) is what gets applied — so what is applied is provably what was compared. Prefer binding the parsed rows over a raw-bytes hash so whitespace/comment-only edits do not spuriously invalidate, or bind both. Avoid the 'stash the PatchPlan keyed by token' variant unless the stash is swept with the token TTL; binding rows in the payload needs no new state. Same audit should be applied to other file-backed Tier-2 dances (restore_snapshot binds {"id", "scope"} — snapshot files are server-written and id-addressed, lower risk, but a content hash there would close the same class).

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain holds end to end with no gap. server.py:1316-1336 apply_patch_plan: on EVERY call it resolves the path (_patch_path, server.py:535-550) and re-parses the file (_patches.load_patch_plan(path), 1323), then builds payload = {'file': str(path), 'include_source': True} (1326) and a human summary enumerating 'ch N -> SOURCE' from the rows just parsed (1327-1331). _confirm (331-344) -> Policy.guard (policy.py:347-360): with a token it consume_token()s (single-use, TTL 300 s from device.yaml:738, action must match) and then only checks stored_payload == dict(payload). Since the payload carries only the resolved path string and the include_source flag, any file whose path is unchanged compares equal regardless of content; the call-2 summary (built from the new content) is discarded because guard returns the payload, not a PendingConfirmation. Execution then proceeds with the PatchPlan parsed at call 2: patches.apply_patch_plan (patches.py:551-628) iterates plan.rows and for every row whose source differs from the desk calls desk.set_source (desk.py:1343-1350), which writes /ch/NN/config/source with guarded=True (i.e. the Tier-2 gate is deemed already satisfied by the caller). No hash, mtime, row list or parsed-plan object is captured at mint time anywhere (grep: no sha/hash/mtime in server.py/policy.py/patches.py). So the documented guarantee in policy.py:22-26 ('a token can only ever execute the request whose summary the user saw') is defeated: the user saw 'ch 16 -> AUX1', the desk got ch1->USBL, ch2->USBR, ch3->OFF, ch4->AUX5 and ch16 untouched. Additional facts the claimant did not state: (1) show mode does NOT mitigate it - SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED (policy.py:224-235) covers only scene_recall/scene_save/setup_ringout_eqs/ring_out/ring_out_system; my second test repatched ch13 (lead vocal) -> OFF with show_mode ON. (2) The MCP surface itself cannot author malicious content: the only file writer reachable through tools is export_patch_plan (server.py:1340-1352), which writes the desk's current names/colours/sources, so an export between the calls makes the confirmed apply a no-op (safe direction). The exploit therefore needs an out-of-band writer inside the 300 s window: the LLM client's own file tools (the documented primary deployment is Claude Code launched in this repo via the checked-in .mcp.json, README.md:399-421, which has Write/Edit on patches/), a runaway edit loop, or a human with the file open. The most plausible non-malicious path is a 'confused' agent: user sees the summary, asks for a tweak, agent edits patches/<file> and re-calls with the SAME token believing the confirmation still covers it -> an unreviewed routing plan executes. (3) Real desk vs FakeDesk: the flaw is entirely host-side (token/payload comparison and file re-read); the resulting writes are ordinary ch_source enum sets on /ch/NN/config/source that a real X32 accepts exactly as the FakeDesk does (verified enum, scales_params.md 11.3), so a real console behaves identically - vocal channels repointed to USB playback/OFF are heard immediately. apply_patch_plan's read-before-write (only differing values written) does not help because the new rows differ from the desk. Severity: high, not critical - it defeats the documented token-binding guarantee and produces an unreviewed Tier-2 routing change (wrong strips), but it requires a legitimately minted token for that same file plus a second capability outside the MCP tool surface within the TTL; the level consequence is bounded by existing channel trims/faders rather than being an unbounded gain write. The claimant's fix is right; cheapest robust form: include a content digest (sha256 of the file bytes, or the canonical [(channel,name,color,source)] row list) in the payload so guard's equality check fails with BAD_TOKEN on any edit, or stash the parsed PatchPlan keyed by token at call 1 and apply that object at call 2.
>
> *Evidence:* Reproduced independently in worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-2-src (commit b45edc8), test file tests/integration/test_verify_tokens2_src.py, run via runtests.sh -> '2 passed in 1.37s'. Test 1 output: SUMMARY1: 'Apply patch plan moved.csv: label 1 channel(s) AND patch 1 input source(s): ch 16 -> AUX1'; BEFORE {1:'IN01',2:'IN02',3:'IN03',4:'IN04',16:'IN16'}; file rewritten; second call with the same token -> DONE ok=True 'moved.csv: 4 channel(s) labelled, 0 unchanged, 4 source(s) patched' applied=[(1,'USBL',['name','source']),(2,'USBR',[...,'source']),(3,'OFF',[...,'source']),(4,'AUX5',[...,'source'])]; AFTER {1:'USBL',2:'USBR',3:'OFF',4:'AUX5',16:'IN16'}. Test 2 (show_mode(True) first; blocked list printed = ['ring_out','ring_out_system','scene_recall','scene_save','setup_ringout_eqs']): token minted for 'ch 16 -> AUX1', file rewritten to '13,Vox Lead,RD,OFF', confirm with same token -> ok=True '1 source(s) patched', fakedesk /ch/13/config/source == 'OFF'. Code refs: server.py:1323 (plan re-loaded each call), 1326 (payload = {'file': str(path), 'include_source': True}), 1332-1335 (guard then apply call-2 plan); policy.py:347-360 (guard compares stored payload == payload only), policy.py:22-26 (documented guarantee), policy.py:224-235 + show_mode summary (apply_patch_plan not blocked); patches.py:551-598 (rows from the call-2 plan, desk.set_source per differing row); desk.py:1349 (_write_value(..., guarded=True)); device.yaml:738 confirm_token_ttl_s: 300; server.py:1340-1352 export_patch_plan writes desk truth only (no MCP-side way to author malicious content); README.md:399-421 checked-in .mcp.json => Claude Code in-repo is the primary client (has file-write tools on patches/).
>
> *Better fix:* In server.py apply_patch_plan, bind the token to the content, not the reference: payload = {'file': str(path), 'include_source': True, 'digest': hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()} (or 'rows': [[r.channel, r.name, r.color, r.source] for r in plan.rows]) so Policy.guard's stored != payload check raises BAD_TOKEN after any edit and the user is shown the new summary. Alternatively stash the parsed PatchPlan in the _Token (or an App-side dict keyed by token) at mint time and apply that object on redemption, ignoring the file at call 2. Either is a ~3-line change; the digest form keeps policy.py generic. Consider the same treatment for ring_out(patch_file=...) if it derives targets from a patch file under a token, and optionally add apply_patch_plan(include_source=True) to SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED since re-routing inputs mid-show is never an accident-safe action.

## D14. [high] Show mode's ±3 dB guard (and the 6 dB relative limit) only covers adjust_*: set_fader/set_send take any absolute value (inf -> ceiling) with ramp_ms=0, force=true is a free tool argument that voids adjust_* as well, and the limit is per call
*Angle:* clamps · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/desk.py:948-965 (_move_level: relative guard deliberately removed from absolute moves 2026-09-20, force unused there), desk.py:997-1013 (adjust_level is the only check_relative caller), src/x32mcp/policy.py:285-303 (relative_limit_db 3 in show mode; force returns early), src/x32mcp/server.py:879-913,946-978 (set_fader/adjust_fader/set_send/adjust_send expose force: bool and pass float(db) — float('inf') accepted), server.py:1270-1282 + README.md:206,633-636 (documented claim 'relative moves limited to ±3 dB (accident protection during a performance)')

**Mechanism.** In show mode Policy only tightens relative_limit_db, which is consulted solely by Desk.adjust_level -> policy.check_relative. Desk.set_level/_move_level clamps to the family ceiling and ramps, but ramp_ms=0 is a legal argument (single step) and +inf passes policy._num (only NaN is rejected) and clamps to the ceiling, so an absolute move from -oo to +5 dB (channel) / 0 dB (send into a wedge, bus, matrix, DCA) is one immediate write with show mode on and force=false. For adjust_*, force=True is an ordinary boolean parameter on the tool schema; check_relative returns before comparing, by design 'even in show mode'. The limit is also per call with no time window: two un-forced adjust_fader(+3) calls complete in ~50 ms (+6 dB in show mode), and nothing stops a loop of them at 50 writes/s. Net effect: against the stated threat model (confused/runaway LLM) show mode constrains nothing about levels except an un-forced single adjust_* call.

**Exploit.** show_mode(true); set_fader('ch.1', db=inf (or 999), ramp_ms=0) -> -oo to +5.0 dB in 1 step; set_send('ch.3', 1, inf, ramp_ms=0) -> -oo to 0 dB into Bus 1 in 1 step; adjust_fader('ch.2', 200, ramp_ms=0, force=true) -> +5 dB in 1 step; adjust_fader('ch.4', 3, ramp_ms=0) twice back-to-back -> +6 dB in <1 s with no force.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_clamps.py::test_exploit_show_mode_only_limits_adjust_not_set_or_force PASSES: with show mode on, adjust_fader('ch.1', 6) -> RELATIVE_TOO_LARGE (guard works), then set_fader('ch.1', inf, ramp_ms=0) -> ok, before '-oo', after_db 5.0, steps 1, clamped.limit 5.0; adjust_fader('ch.2', 200, force=True, ramp_ms=0) -> ok after_db 5.0 steps 1; set_send('ch.3', 1, inf, ramp_ms=0) -> ok after_db 0.0 steps 1; two adjust_fader('ch.4', 3) -> -10 -> -4.0 dB in <1 s; fakedesk confirms /ch/01 and /ch/02 faders at +5.0 and /ch/03/mix/01/level at 0.0.

**Proposed fix.** In show mode make Policy govern absolute moves too: in Desk._move_level, when policy.show_mode and the move is upward, call policy.check_relative(after - before_or_-90, force=force) (keeps the M5 'bring a send up from -oo' convenience outside show mode) and impose a minimum ramp for upward moves (e.g. ms = max(ms, ramp_default_ms) when after > before). Refuse force=True while show mode is on (PolicyError SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS 'turn show mode off to force'), or drop force from the Tier-1 tool signatures and keep it desk-internal (CFS back-off already uses set_level directly). Optionally make check_relative cumulative per address over a short window (e.g. 6 dB per 2 s) so back-to-back calls cannot stack.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-clamps-1-repro (commit a0299a6), test tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_clamps1.py (2 tests, both PASS against FakeDesk + App + server tools). Every element of the claim happens exactly as stated, and one element is slightly worse than claimed:

1. Code path is as described. policy.py:286-303: show mode only changes relative_limit_db (3 vs 6) and check_relative returns early on force. The only caller of check_relative for levels is Desk.adjust_level (desk.py:1004). Desk._move_level (desk.py:948-980) accepts `force` but never uses it and applies only clamp_level (family ceiling) + ramp; the comment at desk.py:957-962 records the deliberate removal of the relative guard from absolute moves (2026-09-20). policy._num/desk._num reject only NaN, so +inf passes and clamp_level maps it to the ceiling (policy.py:259-266). server.py set_fader/set_send/adjust_* (879-913, 946-978) expose ramp_ms 0..60000 and force: bool as ordinary tool args and do float(db).

2. Executed results with show_mode(True), no token, Tier 1:
 - control: adjust_fader('ch.1', 6, ramp_ms=0) and (3.5) -> RELATIVE_TOO_LARGE limit_db 3 (guard works for un-forced adjust).
 - set_fader('ch.1', inf, ramp_ms=0) -> ok, before '-oo', after_db 5.0, steps 1, exactly 1 desk write, clamped.limit 5.0; fakedesk /ch/01/mix/fader = +5.0.
 - set_fader('ch.5', 999.0, ramp_ms=0) -> identical (JSON-reachable value).
 - set_fader('ch.6', 999.0) with the default ramp -> -oo to +5.0 in 15 steps/282 ms — still allowed in show mode, just glides.
 - adjust_fader('ch.2', 200, ramp_ms=0, force=True) -> ok, -oo -> +5.0 in 1 write (requested 110 = -90+200, clamped).
 - set_send('ch.3', 1, inf, ramp_ms=0) -> ok, -oo -> 0.0 dB into Bus 1 in 1 write; fakedesk /ch/03/mix/01/level = 0.0.
 - two un-forced adjust_fader('ch.4', +3, ramp_ms=0) back-to-back -> -10 -> -7 -> -4 dB, elapsed 0.000 s (rate-limiter bucket holds 50 tokens, so no wait).
 - stronger than claimed: a loop of 33 un-forced adjust_fader('ch.4', +3, ramp_ms=0) calls walked the fader from -oo to +5.0 dB in 0.011 s with show mode on and force never set — nothing (relative limit, show mode, rate limiter) tripped, because the limit is per call with no cumulative window and 33 writes fit inside the 50-token burst.
 - MCP argument layer (srv.server.call_tool, i.e. FastMCP/pydantic validation): db=float('inf'), db='Infinity' (string) and db=1e9 are all accepted and each produced -oo -> +5.0 on the fake desk; so 'inf' is reachable from a real client, not just from Python.
 - negative controls hold: NaN -> BAD_ARGUMENT; set_fader('main.st', 999) -> GUARDED (main still needs the Tier-2 tool).

3. Severity re-assessment: not critical — every move is bounded by the family ceiling (+5 dB ch/aux/fxrtn/DCA, 0 dB bus/mtx/send) and main LR/mono stay Tier 2, so the change is bounded, and panic is unaffected. But it is high: the user-facing promise of show mode (README:206, 515, 633-636; show_mode tool docstring server.py:1273 'relative moves are limited to ±3 dB (accident protection during a performance)') is defeated for levels in three independent ways against the stated threat model (confused/runaway LLM): (a) any absolute set_* jumps -oo -> ceiling instantly with ramp_ms=0 and no force; (b) force is a free boolean on the Tier-1 schema; (c) even WITHOUT force and WITHOUT absolute sets, stacking un-forced ±3 dB adjust calls reaches the ceiling in ~10 ms. Caveat for the record: (a) and (b) are explicit, documented design decisions (README:574-579 says the relative limit is adjust_* only; DESIGN.md:450 and policy.py:29-31 say force bypasses even in show mode), so those parts are 'design is weak vs. threat model' rather than an implementation bug; (c) is not documented anywhere and is the clearest defeat of the ±3 dB guarantee as an operator would understand it.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-clamps-1-repro @ a0299a6; test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_clamps1.py::test_show_mode_guard_scope and ::test_inf_through_mcp_argument_validation — `runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_clamps1.py -s` -> 2 passed. Printed RESULTS: set_fader_inf {ok:true, before:'-oo', after_db:5.0, steps:1, ramp_ms:0, clamped:{requested:'+oo', limit:5.0}, writes:1}; set_fader_999 {after_db:5.0, steps:1, writes:1}; set_fader_999_default_ramp {after_db:5.0, steps:15, ramp_ms:300, elapsed_ms:282}; adjust_fader_force {before:'-oo', after_db:5.0, steps:1, requested 110, writes:1}; set_send_inf {before:'-oo', after_db:0.0, steps:1, send_to:1, writes:1}; two_adjusts {a:[-10,-7], b:[-7,-4], elapsed_s:0.0}; loop_to_ceiling {calls:33, elapsed_s:0.011, final:4.995}. MCPRESULTS via srv.server.call_tool: py_inf -> 'Ch 7 fader -oo dB → +5.0 dB (clamped…, requested +oo dB)'; str 'Infinity' -> same on ch.8; 1e9 -> same on ch.9; fakedesk faders 7/8/9 all 4.995. Code refs: policy.py:156-163 (_num rejects only NaN), 254-272 (clamp_level: inf -> ceiling), 286-303 (relative_limit_db/check_relative, force early-return); desk.py:948-980 (_move_level: force unused, no relative check, comment 957-962), 986-997 (set_level), 999-1013 (adjust_level sole check_relative caller); server.py:879-895/898-911 (set_fader/adjust_fader force+ramp_ms args), 946-978 (set_send/adjust_send), 1270-1282 (show_mode docstring/summary); README.md:206, 515, 574-579, 633-636; device.yaml:729-733 (relative_max_db 6/3, ramp_default_ms 300, writes_per_second 50). Result JSON: $SP/reports/verify-clamps-1-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix direction; concretely and minimally: (1) in Desk._move_level, when policy.show_mode is on and after > (before_db or -90), call policy.check_relative(after - max(before_db_or_-90, -90), force=force) so absolute upward moves obey ±3 dB in show mode while the M5 'bring a send up from -oo' convenience survives outside show mode; (2) in show mode enforce a minimum ramp for upward moves (ms = max(ms, ramp_default_ms)) so ramp_ms=0 cannot produce a step; (3) make Policy.check_relative refuse force while show mode is on (SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS 'turn show mode off to force') — CFS back-off in cfs.py should then call a desk-internal path (or pass an internal flag) rather than the public force, since README:724 relies on forced back-offs during a ring-out, but ring-outs are already refused in show mode so there is no conflict; (4) the piece the original fix lists as optional should be mandatory: add a per-address cumulative window to check_relative (e.g. track sum of upward deltas per address over the last 2-3 s and refuse beyond relative_limit_db), otherwise (1)-(3) are bypassed by N back-to-back +3 dB calls exactly as the 33-call loop shows. Also reject non-finite db at the tool boundary (server.py float(db) -> BAD_ARGUMENT for ±inf) so '+oo requested' never appears as a legitimate request.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain read end to end; no gap found. (1) Show mode's only level effect is Policy.relative_limit_db (policy.py:285-288, device.yaml:729-730 → 6/3 dB); its only consumer on a write path is Desk.adjust_level → policy.check_relative (desk.py:1005; grep confirms no other caller — cfs.py:660 and server.py:1819 only read the number to validate a ring-out step size). Desk._write (desk.py:868-884) consults tier, snapshot-before-write and the rate limiter, never show mode. (2) Absolute path: server.set_fader/set_send (server.py:879-896, 946-961) do float(db) and _int_arg(ramp_ms,0,60000) → Desk.set_level → desk._num (desk.py:170-178, rejects only NaN/bool/non-number, +inf passes) → _move_level (desk.py:948-978): tier check, policy.clamp_level (policy.py:253-272: inf>limit → ceiling + Clamped), the relative guard is explicitly absent (comment desk.py:957-963, 'Jim's call, 2026-09-20'), `force` is accepted and unused, ramp_steps(before, after, 0) → [after] (policy.py:437-439 'ms<=0 → [end]') → one _write. So -oo→+5 dB (ch/auxin/fxrtn/dca) or -oo→0 dB (bus/mtx master, any send) is a single OSC set with show mode on and force=false. (3) force: check_relative returns before comparing when force=True (policy.py:293-294), documented as intentional 'even in show mode' (policy.py:29-31, DESIGN.md:450 DECISION, README:633-636); force is a plain bool on four Tier-1 tool schemas whose only control is the docstring 'force=true only on explicit user request' — i.e. prompt-enforced, contradicting DESIGN principle 7 ('policy is enforced in code, not by prompt'). (4) Per-call: check_relative has no memory/window; the error text itself says 'single-call limit'. desk.py:20-23 justifies keeping the guard on adjust_level because 'that is where a typo or a runaway loop does damage', but a runaway loop of un-forced +3 dB calls is not stopped: I drove ch.4 from -40 to the +5 ceiling with 16 consecutive adjust_fader(+3, ramp_ms=0) calls in 85 ms with show mode ON (rate limiter irrelevant: 1 write per call, 50/s budget). Wire reachability: mcp's jsonrpc_message_adapter.validate_json accepts `Infinity` and `1e999` as db (→ inf), and 999 behaves identically anyway. Real desk vs FakeDesk: identical — clamps, ramps and the relative guard are all client-side; ramp_ms=0 emits exactly one `/ch/NN/mix/fader ,f` (or `mix/NN/level`) UDP set which the X32 applies instantly (HANDOVER §4a verified set_fader clamp and instantaneous fader response live; motor fader slams). Each adjust call adds one read RTT (~ms on LAN), so the 16-call climb would take ~0.1-0.3 s on hardware, still far inside a second. What does NOT hold in the claim: minor — DCA ceiling is +5 (policy.py:249-251, ch ceiling), not 0 dB as the mechanism text lists; and 'defeats a documented guarantee' is overstated: every element (absolute moves unlimited, force bypasses in show mode, single-call limit) is the deliberate, documented post-M5 design (README:633-636, set_fader/set_send docstrings server.py:881-885/948-951, policy.py:29-31, DESIGN.md:450, BRIEF.md:5 header, HANDOVER.md:125 defect #3). Severity re-assessment: not critical — every path still clamps to the family ceiling (+5 ch/dca, 0 bus/mtx/send; verified with inf on ch, bus, mtx, dca, send), main LR/mono remain Tier-2 (set_fader('main.st') → GUARDED even here), panic is unaffected, so the change is bounded, never unbounded. I keep it at high rather than medium because (a) REVIEW_BRIEF §3 asks exactly 'can show_mode be circumvented / by argument choice' and the answer is yes, trivially, by tool choice (set_* instead of adjust_*), by argument (force=true, ramp_ms=0) or by repetition; (b) the operator-facing promise 'accident protection during a performance' (server.py:1273, README:206) is functionally empty for levels — with show mode on, a confused/runaway client can put any channel at +5 dB and any wedge/IEM send at 0 dB in one un-ramped write, which on a live stage with an open vocal mic is a realistic instant-feedback/hearing event; (c) the one guard that exists is enforced by docstring, not code. It is a design gap acknowledged in the docs rather than an implementation bug, so the remedy is a show-mode-scoped design change, not a revert of M5 fix #3.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-clamps-1-src, commit e19e83f, tests/integration/test_verify_clamps_1.py — 5 passed (runtests.sh … tests/integration/test_verify_clamps_1.py -s). With show_mode(True) (relative_limit_db==3.0): adjust_fader('ch.1',6,ramp_ms=0) → RELATIVE_TOO_LARGE (guard present); set_fader('ch.1', inf|999|5.0, ramp_ms=0) from -oo → ok, before '-oo', after_db 5.0, steps 1, ramp_ms 0, desk.write_count +1 per call, fakedesk /ch/01/mix/fader == 5.0; set_fader('ch.1',999) with default ramp → after_db 5.0, steps 15 (ramp changes onset only). set_send('ch.3',1,inf,ramp_ms=0) → -oo→0.0 dB steps 1, fakedesk /ch/03/mix/01/level == 0.0; set_fader('bus.1'|'mtx.1', 999, ramp_ms=0) → 0.0 dB steps 1; set_fader('dca.1',999,ramp_ms=0) → +5.0 steps 1; set_fader('main.st',0) → GUARDED. adjust_fader('ch.2',200,ramp_ms=0,force=True) → after_db 5.0 steps 1; adjust_send('ch.2',5,200,ramp_ms=0,force=True) → 0.0 steps 1; fakedesk confirms +5.0 / 0.0. Loop: 'REPRO: -40 -> +5 dB via 16 un-forced adjust_fader(+3) calls in 85 ms, show mode ON', fakedesk /ch/04/mix/fader == 5.0. Bounds that hold: inf on ch→5.0 (clamped.limit 5.0), bus→0.0, send→0.0; NaN → BAD_ARGUMENT. Wire check (repo .venv): mcp.types jsonrpc adapter validate_json accepts "db":Infinity and "db":1e999 → float inf, "db":999 → 999. Code refs: policy.py:285-303 (relative_limit_db/check_relative force early-return), policy.py:437-439 (ramp_ms<=0 → single step), desk.py:948-978 (_move_level: clamp only, force unused, comment dated 2026-09-20), desk.py:1005 (sole check_relative caller), desk.py:868-884 (_write: no show-mode check), server.py:879-913/946-978 (force: bool, float(db), ramp_ms 0 allowed), server.py:1270-1282 + README.md:206 ('accident protection during a performance'), README.md:633-636 + DESIGN.md:450 + policy.py:29-31 + BRIEF.md:5 + HANDOVER.md:125 (documented as intentional).
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's first fix (apply check_relative(after - before_or_-90) to absolute upward moves in show mode and refuse force in show mode) re-creates M5 defect #3 exactly when it hurts most: mid-set, 'more kick in Tony's ears' from a send resting at -oo/-84 dB becomes impossible without leaving show mode. Prefer code-enforced intent instead of a boolean: (1) In show mode, any UPWARD level move (absolute or relative, fader or send) whose destination exceeds current+relative_max_db_show_mode goes through the existing Tier-2 dance — Desk/_move_level raises NEEDS_CONFIRMATION and the server tools call policy.guard('level_jump', summary 'Ch 3 send→Bus 1 -oo → -12.0 dB', payload{address,to_db,ramp_ms}, confirm_token) — so the user, not the model, authorises the jump; drop `force` from the four Tier-1 tool signatures (keep it desk-internal for CFS back-off/restore). Downward moves never gated. (2) Impose a minimum ramp on upward moves in show mode (ms = max(ms, ramp_default_ms, ~100 ms per dB of rise)) so even a confirmed jump has an audible onset and panic() has time to land; also have panic() cancel in-flight ramps. (3) Make the relative allowance cumulative per address in show mode (e.g. net rise ≤ relative_max_db_show_mode per address per 5-10 s sliding window, tracked in Policy keyed by address) so un-forced +3 dB calls cannot be stacked at 50/s. (4) Optionally a show-mode send ceiling below 0 dB (e.g. -6) since 0 dB into a wedge from an open vocal mic is the classic feedback trigger. Update server.py:1273/README:206 to state precisely what show mode bounds.

## D15. [high] panic() does not abort a running CFS2 ring-out (or in-flight fader ramps): the ring-out keeps raising the now-muted bus, hears no rings, reaches the 0 dB target and parks the wedge at -3 dB with final_stage DONE
*Angle:* clamps · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1220 (panic: mutes outputs only; self._ramps untouched, no CFS hook), src/x32mcp/server.py:1062-1074 (panic tool calls desk.panic() only, never app.cfs.abort), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1147-1198 (_run_ringout raises while no detection is pending; never re-reads mix/on), cfs.py:1254-1292 (_finalize_levels backs off safety_margin 3 dB from the max and reports DONE), src/x32mcp/provision.py:647 (bus-muted is only a PREFLIGHT blocker), desk.py:897-946 (ramp tasks survive panic)

**Mechanism.** panic() writes mix/on=0 on main/bus/mtx and returns; the CfsManager session is independent state driven by RTA frames and its own raise loop. With the bus muted the acoustic loop is open, the detector sees the calibrated floor, ses.pending stays empty, so _run_ringout executes nxt=min(master+step, target) every dwell until 'target reached' (target defaults to the 0 dB bus ceiling), then _finalize_levels writes master-3 dB (force=True, so not even show mode/relative limit can refuse it) and records final_stage DONE, aborted False, notches []. The operator who un-mutes after the emergency opens a wedge sitting 17 dB (from a -20 dB start) above where it was, at a gain that was never rung out, i.e. straight into feedback; the report and cfs_status claim a successful ring-out. Same class: a long set_fader ramp (up to 60 s) started before panic keeps writing the channel up to its ceiling behind the muted outputs (panic is not blocked by it — verified — but does not cancel it).

**Exploit.** ring_out(bus=1, step_db=1, dwell_ms=1500) confirmed and running (or ring_out_system); user shouts stop -> LLM calls panic() (the documented emergency verb) instead of feedback_watch_stop -> outputs mute, tool returns ok; ring-out continues for ~30 s to 0 dB and ends DONE at -3 dB; later unmute('bus.1') / restore -> wedge live at -3 dB. Ramp variant: set_fader('ch.6', 5, ramp_ms=60000) then panic(); channel still arrives at +5.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_clamps.py::test_exploit_panic_does_not_stop_a_ring_out_which_then_parks_the_muted_bus_at_target PASSES: GEQ provisioned on bus 1, mics routed, master -20 dB; desk.panic() fired from the first RAISE stage event (count 24); ring_out(1, step_db=1, dwell_ms=60) then returned final_stage 'DONE', aborted False, start_master_db -20.0, max_master_db 0.0, end_master_db -3.0, notches []; fakedesk /bus/01/mix/on == 0 (still muted) and /bus/01/mix/fader == -3.0. ::test_probe_ramp_ms_bounds_and_panic_during_a_long_ramp PASSES: panic returned in <0.5 s during a 1.5 s ramp (not blocked) and the ramp then completed to +5.0 (mid-ramp value <4.0, final 5.0, main mix/on still 0).

**Proposed fix.** Make panic authoritative over automation: in server.panic (or Desk.panic via a registered callback) call `await app.cfs.abort('panic')` (ring-out: back-off/ABORT; watch: stop) BEFORE muting, and cancel every task in Desk._ramps (report them as superseded). In cfs._run_ringout, before each raise re-read `{bus}/mix/on` (cheap leaf read, cache already invalidated by panic's _invalidate_address) and set abort_reason 'bus muted during run' when it is 0; also subscribe the session to desk.panic events. _finalize_levels for an abort caused by mute/panic should restore start_master_db (like the connection-loss path) rather than park at max-6.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-clamps-2-repro (commit d905328), tests/integration/test_verify_clamps2_repro.py, 3/3 pass against HEAD a408a2a code.

Static check of the claim's mechanism: server.py:1062-1074 panic tool calls only desk.panic(); desk.py:1184-1220 panic() writes mix/on=0 on main/bus/mtx, publishes 'desk.panic', never touches self._ramps and has no CFS hook; nothing in src/ subscribes to 'desk.panic' (grep: only the docstring at desk.py:47 and the publish). cfs.py:1147-1198 _run_ringout loops on ses.pending/abort_reason/target only and never re-reads {bus}/mix/on; provision.py:646-647 checks 'muted' only at preflight. cfs.py:1254-1292 _finalize_levels with reason 'target reached' and no abort backs off safety_margin_db (3) from the max with force=True and records DONE. The server instructions (server.py:128-129) explicitly tell the model to use panic() 'whenever the user says stop / kill it / mute everything', so during a ring-out the documented emergency verb is exactly the one that does not stop the automation (cfs.stop/abort via feedback_watch_stop is the only path that does).

Dynamic results (see evidence): (1) CfsManager-level: panic fired from the first RAISE event muted 24 outputs in 0.2 ms; the ring-out then made 20 further /bus/01/mix/fader writes (-18 ... 0.0, then -3.0), returned final_stage DONE, aborted False, abort_reason None, max_master_db 0.0, end_master_db -3.0, notches []; fake desk /bus/01/mix/on == 0 and fader == -2.98. (2) Full MCP tool surface: setup_ringout_eqs + ring_out token dance, srv.panic() called while srv.ring_out(...confirm_token) was in flight -> panic ok 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted'; ring_out tool returned ok 'master -20.0 -> -3.0 dB (highest 0.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; DONE'; cfs_status reports the session DONE; srv.unmute('bus.1') (Tier 1, no confirmation) then opened the bus live at -3 dB, 17 dB above where the operator had it, at a gain that was never actually rung out (loop was open while 'measuring'). (3) Ramp variant: set_level(ch.6, +5 dB, ramp_ms=1500) started from -20; panic at t=0.4 s returned in 0.3 ms (not blocked, mid value -14.65); ramp then completed to +4.995 (superseded False) behind the muted mains.

Severity: I agree with 'high', not critical. Panic itself still silences the room and is not delayed; the level the ring-out reaches is bounded by the confirmed target/0 dB bus ceiling, so there is no unbounded or unconfirmed level change. But the documented emergency stop leaves autonomous writes running, produces a false 'DONE / no feedback' report, and parks a muted wedge hot so that the natural one-step Tier-1 recovery (unmute) lands straight in likely feedback — 'leaves the desk in a dangerous state after an emergency' on the task's scale. Reachability caveat (does not change the verdict): the server is stdio/single-client and the ring_out tool awaits the whole run inline (_tool(timeout=None), no detached task), so the scenario needs the client to issue panic while the ring_out request is still pending (concurrent requests, or a client that timed out/abandoned the 30 s+ ring_out call without sending an MCP cancel); a client that does send notifications/cancelled would instead hit the CancelledError->_unwind back-off path at cfs.py:706-710. ring_out_system self-limits after panic because the next stage's preflight sees the muted bus, but the current stage behaves exactly as above.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-clamps-2-repro (branch wt/verify-clamps-2-repro, commit d905328). Test file: tests/integration/test_verify_clamps2_repro.py. Run: `$SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_verify_clamps2_repro.py -s -p no:randomly` -> 3 passed in 5.63s.
