Code refs verified: server.py:1064-1074 (panic tool -> desk.panic() only), server.py:128-129 (instructions: use panic on 'stop'), desk.py:1184-1220 (panic: mix/on 0, no _ramps cancel, no cfs hook), desk.py:897-946 (ramp task keyed by address, only a newer ramp cancels it), cfs.py:1147-1198 (_run_ringout never reads mix/on), cfs.py:1254-1292 (_finalize_levels: force=True back-off of safety_margin_db, DONE), provision.py:646-647 (muted is a preflight-only blocker), cfs.py:587-636 (stop/abort are the only abort entry points; not wired to panic).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix; concretely: (1) server.panic: `await asyncio.shield(app.cfs.abort('panic'))` (non-blocking: abort() just sets abort_reason + wake, the awaiting ring_out call does the back-off) before desk.panic(), and have Desk.panic cancel every task in self._ramps (their _ramp callers already report superseded=True). (2) cfs._finalize_levels: when abort_reason is a panic/mute, write start_master_db (force=True) instead of max-6, mirroring the connection-loss restore, so the muted wedge returns to the operator's level. (3) Defence in depth in _run_ringout: before each raise read `{prefix}/mix/on` via desk._leaf (panic already invalidated the cache) and abort with 'bus muted during run' if 0 — this also covers a front-panel/X32-Edit mute mid-run, which panic wiring alone would not. (4) Update the server instructions to say panic also aborts CFS²/ramps so the model does not follow it with feedback_watch_stop expecting a second back-off.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain read end to end, every link holds:
(1) server.py:1063-1074 panic tool -> only desk.panic(); it never touches app.cfs (contrast App.connect server.py:196-197 and App.close, which DO call cfs.abort/close). (2) desk.py:1184-1220 Desk.panic: acquire_write(panic=True), conn.set mix/on 0 on main/bus/mtx, _invalidate_address, publish 'desk.panic' event, return. self._ramps is never consulted; there is no callback/registry for automation. (3) cfs.py:522: CfsManager subscribes only to 'connection.state'; nothing subscribes to 'desk.panic'. (4) cfs.py:1147-1198 _run_ringout loops on ses.pending / ses.abort_reason / master vs target only; the only desk reads during the run are RTA frames (cfs.py:1016-1048) and the fader read-back of its own writes; {bus}/mix/on is read exactly once, in provision.preflight (provision.py:636,647) BEFORE the session opens. (5) Raise writes are _write_master(force=False) -> Desk.set_level: +step_db (<= relative_max 6, ring_out refuses larger steps, cfs.py:661) at <=50 writes/s -> policy passes every one; target = min(bus_fader_max_db 0, ringout.master_ceiling_db 0) = 0 dB by default (server.py:1757, device.yaml:725,830). (6) With no detections the loop ends 'target reached' and _finalize_levels (cfs.py:1274-1292) writes master-3 dB with force=True and records DONE, aborted False; _finish saves a report claiming a completed ring-out. (7) The 'no detections' step is physics, not FakeDesk: fakedesk.py:44 says its RTA is deliberately NOT coupled to bus fader/mute, and the claimant's run had no ring injected, so its notches==[] proves nothing about detection. On a real X32, panic's mix/on 0 silences every POST/+M-tapped output (research fx_routing_scenes.md §11 option 1; factory OUT taps POST per general knowledge, UNCONFIRMED) -> wedge silent -> acoustic loop open -> the detector (growth/persistence over a calibrated floor) sees only mic ambient on the bus tap and reports nothing while /meters/15 frames keep streaming, so no watchdog fires. If the wedge were PRE-tapped, panic would not silence it at all (a different, worse problem) and the bus master would not drive it either. So a real desk behaves as claimed for the normal POST-tapped monitor path.
Reachability of 'panic during ring_out': the ring_out tool blocks for the whole run (server.py:1775-1779), so panic must arrive as a concurrent MCP request. mcp 2.2's JSON-RPC dispatcher spawns every incoming request in its task group (site-packages/mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:670-686), and the product's OWN design requires this concurrency: feedback_watch_stop is documented as 'abort a running ring-out' (server.py:1673-1676, cfs.stop cfs.py:587-613) and cfs_status as live status — both are only meaningful as concurrent calls while ring_out is pending; INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:128) tell the model to use panic 'whenever the user says stop / kill it'. Whether a given client (Claude Code) lets the model take a turn mid-tool is client-specific (Esc there cancels the request -> CancelledError -> _unwind back-off, the safe path), but the gig concept is a voice/phone client and any async-tool agent can do it. More importantly the same end state needs NO concurrency: README:621-626 says the reflex at a gig is X32-Edit/the physical mute — an engineer muting the bus on the console mid-run is a third-party mix/on write that _run_ringout equally never sees (reproduced). 
Consequence: bus left muted (CFS never unmutes) at -3 dB = +17 dB over the engineer's -20 dB start, zero notches, report/cfs_status say DONE. The exit panic's own docstring prescribes ('Unmute afterwards with unmute') is Tier 1 for a bus (device.yaml:256; server.py:929-935) — no token — and opens the wedge at a gain that, by the premise of running a ring-out, rings. Ramp variant also holds (desk.py:897-921: ramp task keyed by address, only a newer ramp on the same address cancels it) but is weaker on its own: the ramp's destination was already policy-approved as an absolute move.
Corrections to the claim: 'restore -> wedge live at -3 dB' is wrong — restore_snapshot of the SNAPSHOT-stage or auto-pre-write dump puts the fader back to -20 (it is the safe exit); the dangerous exit is unmute. Also note that merely wiring panic -> cfs.abort is insufficient if panic lands late: the ABORT path parks at max-6 (still +14 dB here), so a mute/panic-caused abort must restore start_master_db.
Severity: HIGH (agree). Not critical: outputs are genuinely silenced, the level is bounded by the 0 dB bus ceiling and was the confirmed ring_out target; but it defeats the purpose of the emergency verb for the server's own automation, leaves the desk in a trap state after the emergency, and files a false 'DONE' report — and the one-call Tier-1 unmute that panic's docstring recommends converts it into feedback. Borders on critical for that reason.
>
> *Evidence:* Own repro, worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-clamps-2-src, commit ce782b6, tests/integration/test_verify_panic_vs_ringout.py — 5 passed in 13.6 s, all at the MCP-tool level (make_app + srv.* with the real confirmation dance, GEQ provisioned via setup_ringout_eqs, ch1/ch2 sends -20, bus 1 master -20, SyntheticRta injected as frame source):
- test_control_the_documented_stop_verb_aborts_a_running_ring_out: srv.feedback_watch_stop() fired concurrently at the 1st RAISE -> report final_stage ABORT, max_master -19.0, end_master -25.0 ('stopped by operator'), fader on fake desk -25. The stop verb works concurrently; so concurrency is the designed operating mode.
- test_panic_does_not_abort_the_ring_out_which_walks_the_muted_bus_to_target: ring injected (2400 Hz, 20 dB/s from -45) at RAISE#1, srv.panic() at RAISE#2 (ring killed on the desk.panic event to model the open acoustic loop the FakeDesk does not model): panic ok count 24; run continued 18 more RAISE stages -> ('RAISE', 0.0), ('BACKOFF', 0.0), ('DONE', -3.0); report ok True, final_stage DONE, aborted False, abort_reason None, start -20.0, max 0.0, end -3.0, notches []; fakedesk /bus/01/mix/on == 0 and fader -3.0; cfs_status idle/DONE; then srv.unmute('bus.1') -> ok, no requires_confirmation -> /bus/01/mix/on == 1, fader -2.98 dB, GEQ notches [].
- test_control_same_ring_without_panic_is_found_and_notched: identical ring, no panic -> detected at master -11.0, notch band 22 (2.5 kHz) to -9 dB, run ends at -17.0 (ABORT not tamed in the synthetic model) — i.e. the ring the muted run sailed past.
- test_operator_mutes_the_bus_on_the_console_mid_run_same_outcome_no_concurrency_needed: fakedesk.set_value('/bus/01/mix/on', False) at RAISE#1 (third-party write) -> DONE, max 0.0, end -3.0, notches []; get_strip: "Bus 1 'Bus01': fader −3.0 dB, MUTED, … insert FX5L on".
- test_panic_does_not_cancel_an_in_flight_fader_ramp: set_fader('ch.6', 5.0, ramp_ms=1500) as a task, srv.panic() after 0.4 s returned in ~0 ms with main mix/on 0; ch.6 mid-ramp -12.3 dB, ramp completed to +5.0, superseded False, Desk._ramps empty.
Source anchors: server.py:1063-1074 (panic tool), server.py:196-197 (connect DOES abort cfs — the hook exists, panic just doesn't use it), desk.py:1184-1220 (panic body), desk.py:897-921 (_ramp keyed cancel only), cfs.py:522 (only connection.state subscribed), cfs.py:1147-1198 (_run_ringout, no mix/on read), cfs.py:1254-1292 (_finalize_levels DONE=max-3 force=True; ABORT=max-6), provision.py:636-647 (muted = preflight-only blocker, 'CFS² never unmutes'), device.yaml:256 (bus mix/on tier 1), fakedesk.py:39-44 (RTA not coupled to bus mute), docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md:702-706 (panic silences POST/+M taps only; factory POST UNCONFIRMED), README.md:621-626 (reflex is the physical mute; 'Unmute afterwards with unmute').
>
> *Better fix:* 1) server.panic (server.py:1064): `if a.cfs.active: await asyncio.wait_for(a.cfs.abort('panic'), 1.0)` BEFORE desk.panic() (never let it delay the mutes: shield/timeout and continue on error), and have Desk.panic cancel every task in self._ramps (report them superseded). 2) cfs._run_ringout: before each raise read `{prefix}/mix/on` via desk._leaf (cache already invalidated by panic; a console mute arrives via /xremote) and set abort_reason='bus muted during run' when muted; additionally subscribe the manager to 'desk.panic' and to desk.write/xremote events on the session bus's mix/on so a mute between dwell ticks wakes the loop immediately (ses.wake.set()). 3) _finalize_levels: for abort reasons {panic, bus muted, stopped by operator before first detection} write start_master_db (as the connection-loss path does) instead of max-abort_backoff_db — a late panic otherwise still parks the muted bus +14 dB up; record final_stage ABORT and put 'NOT rung out — master restored' in the report summary. 4) preflight's 'muted' check should also run at ARM (re-read, not cached) to close the window between the confirm call and the run.

## D16. [high] CFS² ring_out ignores panic(): it keeps RAISING the bus master to the 0 dB ceiling after the panic and reports DONE
*Angle:* panic · *attacker severity:* critical · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/cfs.py:522 (CfsManager subscribes only to connection.state; nothing listens for desk.panic), cfs.py:621-635 (abort() exists but nobody calls it on panic), cfs.py:1147-1198 (_run_ringout loop: exit conditions are target/budget/abort_reason only; never re-reads mix/on), cfs.py:1115-1124 (_write_master → Desk.set_level ramp_ms=0), src/x32mcp/server.py:1062-1075 (panic tool calls desk.panic() only; no a.cfs.abort), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1220 (panic publishes desk.panic but touches nothing else), docs/research/meters.md:398/449 (RTA taps pre/post-EQ = before fader and mute)

**Mechanism.** panic() writes /bus/NN/mix/on 0 and returns. The ring-out state machine holds its own ses.master_db and raises /bus/NN/mix/fader by step_db every dwell; it neither subscribes to desk.panic nor checks the bus mute, and preflight's 'bus is muted' blocker (provision.py:646) only runs before the session. With the bus muted the acoustic loop is open, the RTA (tapped pre-fader) shows no ring, so no detection ever interrupts the RAISE loop: it climbs to min(policy ceiling, ringout.master_ceiling_db)=0 dB, declares 'target reached', backs off safety_margin 3 dB and finishes DONE with 0 notches — the bus is parked at -3 dB, 17 dB above where the operator had it, with none of its feedback modes treated, and the tool result says ok/DONE. Un-muting that bus (operator on the surface, or the model via Tier-1 unmute, see the latch finding) is an instant howl. Additionally, while ring_out runs the model is blocked inside that tool call (typical MCP clients serialize calls) and the dashboard is read-only, so during a ring-out there is no software panic path at all; server-side the dispatcher is concurrent (verified) so a second client/call would get through — and then be ignored by CFS as shown.

**Exploit.** setup_ringout_eqs([1]) (confirm) → ring_out(1, target_gain_db=0, step_db=1, dwell_ms=1500) (confirm; runs for minutes) → operator says 'kill it' → panic() (from a concurrent request) → ok 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted' → ring_out continues: cfs_status shows mode=ringout stage=RAISE master climbing; ~20 dwells later ring_out returns ok 'master -20.0 -> -3.0 dB (highest 0.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; DONE' → unmute('bus.1') (Tier 1, no token) → mics into wedges at -3 dB master.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_panic.py::test_B1_ring_out_keeps_raising_bus_master_after_panic and ::test_B2_server_panic_tool_does_not_abort_cfs PASS on HEAD code. B1 (reports/redteam-panic/B1_ring_out_after_panic.json): panic at master -17 dB → 17 further RAISE writes after the panic (-16,-15,…,0.0 at +1.05 s, then -3.0), report final_stage DONE, aborted false, max_master_db 0.0, end_master_db -3.0, notches [], fake /bus/01/mix/on=0, fader -2.98. B2 via MCP tools: cfs_status 0.3 s after the panic tool returned = {mode: ringout, stage: RAISE, master_db: -14.0}; ring_out tool returned ok, max_master_db 0.0, bus parked -3.0. With the fix sketch (14f408a) test_panic_epoch.py::test_ring_out_is_aborted_by_panic_and_the_master_goes_back_to_start passes: ABORT 'panic() called…', max_master_db ≤ -16, end_master_db -20.0, bus still muted, CFS idle.

**Proposed fix.** Make panic an event CFS obeys: CfsManager subscribes to desk.panic (as it does to connection.state) → _stop_system('panic'); for a RINGOUT session set ses.panicked/abort_reason, wake the dwell and the VERIFY wait; in _finalize_levels a panicked session writes the START master back (forced, lowering) instead of the abort_backoff arithmetic from the raised level, and ends ABORT; a WATCH session stops. Also have server.panic() call a.cfs.abort('panic') as belt-and-braces, and consider a dashboard panic button so a software panic exists while the model is blocked in ring_out. Implemented and tested in the worktree (cfs.py _on_panic_event + _finalize_levels branch).

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (critical)** — Reproduced from scratch on HEAD a408a2a in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-panic-0-repro (commit b48681c, tests/integration/test_verify_panic_0.py; results in $SP/reports/verify-panic-0-repro/*.json). Static reading matches the claim exactly: CfsManager.__init__ (cfs.py:522) subscribes only to 'connection.state'; grep of src/ for 'desk.panic' finds only the publisher (desk.py:1214) — no subscriber anywhere; server.panic (server.py:1064-1074) calls desk.panic() and nothing else; _run_ringout (cfs.py:1147-1198) exits only on target/budget/clamp/abort_reason and never re-reads /bus/NN/mix/on; _write_master (cfs.py:1115-1124) → Desk.set_level(force=False, ramp_ms=0) which has no mute check; preflight's 'bus is muted' blocker (provision.py:646) runs once before ARM. Dashboard is GET-only and ignores inbound WS messages (webui.py:310, 355), so it offers no panic/abort path either. Dynamic results: (A) direct CfsManager.ring_out(1, target 0 dB, step 1, dwell 60 ms) from -20 dB with Desk.panic() fired at the RAISE to -17 dB: panic returned 24 outputs muted (bus.1 included, fake /bus/01/mix/on=0), then 17 further RAISE writes after the panic (-16,-15,…,0.0 at +1.04 s) plus the BACKOFF write -3.0; report final_stage DONE, aborted false, abort_reason null, max_master_db 0.0, end_master_db -3.0, notches [], fake fader -2.98 dB with the bus still muted; stage list is 20×RAISE, BACKOFF, DONE with no HOLD/ABORT. (B) via the MCP tools: setup_ringout_eqs dance, ring_out(1, target 0, step 1, dwell 100) dance run as a task, concurrent srv.panic() → ok 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted in 0.2 ms (sent)'; cfs_status just before = {ringout, RAISE, -17.0}, 0.35 s after the panic tool returned = {ringout, RAISE, -14.0} (still climbing); ring_out tool returned 1.8 s after the panic with ok=true, summary 'ring-out Bus 1: master -20.0 -> -3.0 dB (highest 0.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; DONE'; then srv.unmute('bus.1') succeeded with no confirmation (Tier 1) leaving bus 1 live at -2.98 dB, 17 dB above where the operator had it, with zero feedback modes treated. (C) control: calling the existing cfs.abort('panic') right after desk.panic() does stop the raise immediately (max_master_db -17, ABORT 'panic') but the HEAD back-off arithmetic parks the bus at raised-6 dB (-23 here; a late panic at e.g. -4 dB would park it at -10, still 10 dB above the operator's setting), confirming the claimant's point that a panicked session should write the START master back rather than use abort_backoff_db. Caveat on one sub-point: the 'RTA tapped pre-fader shows no ring while muted, so nothing interrupts the climb' argument is physically sound (RTA taps pre/post bus EQ, upstream of fader/mute per meters.md:398/427; a muted bus opens the acoustic loop) but the FakeDesk does not model acoustics, so that part is reasoning, not measurement — it does not change the outcome: with or without detections the loop never consults panic. Severity: I keep critical. panic() is the documented 'stop / kill it' path and its own docstring tells the model to 'Unmute afterwards with unmute'; the autonomous Tier-2 procedure keeps running behind the mute, drives the master to the 0 dB ceiling, reports success, and the documented Tier-1 recovery step (unmute, no token) then delivers a +17 dB jump into wedges with open mics — a loud level change reachable with no further confirmation, i.e. panic is defeated in effect. During the run a serialized MCP client has no software stop at all (model blocked in ring_out, dashboard read-only).
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-panic-0-repro @ b48681c; test file tests/integration/test_verify_panic_0.py: test_A_direct_desk_panic_mid_ringout PASS, test_B_server_panic_tool_during_ring_out_tool PASS, test_C_what_abort_would_do_if_panic_called_it PASS (3 passed, ~5 s, run via $SP/runtests.sh <wt> tests/integration/test_verify_panic_0.py -s). A_direct_panic.json: panic_result muted 24 keys incl bus.1, master_db_at_panic -17.0, fader_writes_before_panic [-19,-18,-17], fader_writes_after_panic 18 entries [(0.005,-16.0)…(1.039,0.0),(1.099,-3.0)], report {final_stage: DONE, aborted: false, abort_reason: null, start -20.0, max 0.0, end -3.0, target 0.0, notches [], connection_lost false}, fake_bus01_mix_on 0, fake_bus01_fader -2.981, fake_main_st_on 0, cfs_mode_after idle. B_server_tools.json: panic_tool {ok true, 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted in 0.2 ms (sent)'}, cfs_status_before_panic {ringout, RAISE, -17.0}, cfs_status_0.35s_after_panic {ringout, RAISE, -14.0}, ring_out_tool_result {ok true, summary 'ring-out Bus 1: master -20.0 -> -3.0 dB (highest 0.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; DONE', final_stage DONE, aborted false, max 0.0, end -3.0, notches []}, ring_out_returned_s_after_panic 1.81, fake_bus01_mix_on 0, fake fader -2.981; unmute_bus1 {ok true, 'Bus 1 'Bus01' unmuted', requires_confirmation null}, fake_bus01_mix_on_after_unmute 1, fader still -2.981. C_abort_control.json: report {ABORT, aborted true, abort_reason 'panic', max_master_db -17.0, end_master_db -23.0}, fader_writes_after_panic [-23.0]. Code refs: cfs.py:522 (only connection.state subscribed), cfs.py:621-635 abort() never invoked by panic, cfs.py:1147-1198 loop conditions, cfs.py:1115-1124 _write_master, cfs.py:1260-1290 _finalize_levels back-off from raised level, server.py:1062-1074 panic tool, desk.py:1184-1220 Desk.panic publishes desk.panic only, provision.py:646 muted blocker preflight-only, webui.py:310/355 read-only dashboard.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the claimant's fix and would layer it: (1) CfsManager subscribes to 'desk.panic' alongside 'connection.state' and treats it like a connection loss for a RINGOUT session — set ses.abort_reason='panic', ses.wake.set(), ses.frame_event.set(), and in _finalize_levels a panicked session force-writes start_master_db (lowering, force=True) and ends ABORT, instead of max-abort_backoff_db (test C shows the HEAD abort path parks the bus at raised-6, which after a late panic is still well above the operator's level); a WATCH session just stops; a SYSTEM run gets _stop_system('panic'). (2) server.panic() also awaits a.cfs.abort('panic') (cheap, and covers a CfsManager built on a different EventBus). (3) Defence in depth inside the loop: _run_ringout re-reads the bus mix/on (cache is invalidated by panic) before each RAISE and aborts if the bus went muted by any route (front panel, X32-Edit, another client), since a muted bus silently defeats the whole measurement. (4) Give the dashboard a POST /panic (or WS message) so a software panic exists while a serialized MCP client is blocked inside ring_out.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain checked end to end on HEAD a408a2a; it has no gap.
(1) Desk.panic() (src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1220) only sends `<prefix>/mix/on 0` to main/mono/bus/mtx, invalidates those cache entries and publishes `desk.panic`. It sets no latch/epoch anywhere in Policy or Desk.
(2) CfsManager subscribes to exactly one event type, `connection.state` (cfs.py:522); grep confirms no other `subscribe(` on the EventBus and no read of `mix/on`/`muted` anywhere in cfs.py after preflight. The server `panic` tool (server.py:1062-1075) calls desk.panic() only — `a.cfs.abort` is called solely from App.connect (server.py:197) and cfs.stop() from disconnect (server.py:676) and feedback_watch_stop (server.py:1678).
(3) `_run_ringout` (cfs.py ~1143-1197) exits only on: pending detection not tamed, `ses.master_db >= target`, `nc.spent`, a PolicyError/DeskError from the raise, a no-progress clamp, or `ses.abort_reason` — none of which panic touches. Each iteration calls `_write_master` → Desk.set_level(ramp_ms=0) → `_move_level` → `_ramp` → `_write`, which checks tier (bus fader is Tier 1), pre-write snapshot, and the token bucket only; panic consumed no tokens (policy.py:378), so nothing refuses the next +1 dB.
(4) No detection can rescue it: on a real X32 the RTA taps pre/post-EQ of the bus (docs/research/meters.md:398 `/-prefs/rta/pos` 0 Pre EQ / 1 Post EQ), i.e. before fader and mute, so with mix/on=0 the wedges are silent, the acoustic loop is open, and the mic sum the RTA still sees cannot regenerate — no ring grows, detector stays quiet, RAISE runs to min(policy bus ceiling 0, ringout.master_ceiling_db 0)=0 dB (device.yaml:725,830). The FakeDesk reaches the same outcome for a different reason (fakedesk.py:44: fader/mute deliberately not coupled to ring growth; rings only appear when injected), so the FakeDesk repro is faithful to the real-desk result here.
(5) `_finalize_levels` (cfs.py ~1255-1290) with abort_reason None and master raised → BACKOFF by safety_margin_db 3 → writes −3 dB (forced), final_stage DONE; report summary 'master -20.0 -> -3.0 dB (highest 0.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; DONE', tool envelope ok:true. Bus left muted at −3 dB, 17 dB above the operator's −20, with zero feedback modes treated; preflight's 'bus is muted' blocker (provision.py:646) only runs before the session.
(6) The same blind spot applies to the operator's documented reflex (BRIEF.md:90: emergencies go via X32-Edit/physical, not the conversational path): a surface MUTE of the bus mid-ring-out is a third-party /xremote push that CFS never looks at — reproduced identically (V2). The claimant's fix sketch (subscribe to desk.panic) does not cover this variant.
Reachability caveats (why I rate HIGH, not CRITICAL): (a) panic's own guarantee — outputs silenced — holds; the bus stays muted and the fader excursion is silent and bounded by the Tier-2-confirmed target/0 dB ceiling; sound only happens on a later unmute (operator on the surface, or the model via Tier-1 `unmute`, no token). (b) The server is stdio-only (server.py:1949), one client; the MCP dispatcher does run requests concurrently (mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:684-686 start_soon per request) so a concurrent panic is transport-possible, but a Claude client cannot issue panic while blocked in ring_out without the user first pressing Esc — and Esc sends notifications/cancelled, which this SDK applies in 'interrupt' mode (jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:82-84) → CancelledError → cfs.ring_out `except CancelledError` → `_unwind` → backs off (V3: aborted at −12 → parked −18, ABORT). So the literal 'kill it → panic() from a concurrent request' path needs an SDK/multi-request client; the surface-mute path needs nothing special. (c) feedback_watch_stop (Tier 1) reached the same way DOES abort the ring-out (V4), so a software abort exists — but the panic tool docstring steers the model to panic for 'stop / kill it', and panic is the one that CFS ignores.
Net: a documented emergency action (panic, or the physical mute it stands in for) does not stop the autonomous gain-raising state machine; the run completes 'ok/DONE, no feedback detected' and leaves an untreated monitor bus parked 3 dB under full scale behind a mute. That defeats the abort semantics DESIGN.md:619 promises for ring_out ('restores start level on abort') in the one situation that matters most and leaves the desk in a dangerous state after an emergency → high. Not critical because panic still silences, the level is bounded and confirmed, and a further human/model unmute is required for anything to be heard.
>

On CancelledError/_unwind, back off to start_master_db rather than raised−6 when no notch was placed. Consider a dashboard STOP/PANIC button (webui is currently read-only) so a software abort exists while the single stdio client is blocked inside ring_out.

## D17. [high] panic() does not abort a running CFS² session: the ring-out keeps raising the (muted) bus to its target and reports DONE
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1062-1074 (panic tool → desk.panic() only), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1220 (publishes desk.panic; nothing subscribes), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:522 (CfsManager subscribes only to connection.state), cfs.py:1147-1197 (loop has no notion of panic/mute)

**Mechanism.** panic() mutes mix/on of main/bus/mtx and returns. The ring-out session is untouched: preflight's 'bus muted' blocker is only evaluated before arming, the fader writes do not care about mix/on, the detector now hears silence so no detection ever interrupts the loop, and the run raises the muted bus all the way to target, backs off 3 dB and reports DONE. The operator (or the LLM told 'ok unmute') then un-mutes a bus sitting 10-20 dB hotter than before the run — straight back into the feedback that triggered the panic, louder.

**Exploit.** ring_out(1, target_gain_db=-3) confirmed and running from -20 dB; user shouts 'kill it' → LLM calls panic() (succeeds, 24 outputs muted) → ring_out continues ≈13 more raises → DONE at -3, parked at -6 dB, bus still muted → unmute('bus.1') → +14 dB relative to the pre-run level with the ring un-notched.

**Evidence.** test_x3b_panic_does_not_stop_ringout_which_keeps_raising_the_muted_bus (CfsManager + Desk.panic) and test_x6b_panic_and_show_mode_tools_do_not_stop_a_running_ring_out (through srv.panic()/srv.show_mode tools after a confirmed srv.ring_out): observed /bus/01/mix/on == 0 after panic AND report final_stage DONE, aborted False, max_master_db -3.0, end_master_db -6.0, ≥ 10 RAISE stages after the panic, fake fader -6.0 (was -20.0). Both PASSED.

**Proposed fix.** In the panic tool: after desk.panic() returns, if a.cfs.active schedule a.cfs.abort('panic') (create_task — must not delay or be able to fail the panic). In CfsManager subscribe to 'desk.panic' and abort any session; for a ring-out aborted by panic restore start_master_db (the _restore_master path) rather than max−6, so un-muting returns to the operator's level.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-2-repro (commit 1ad6ce1, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs2.py, 3 passed).

Code path (HEAD a408a2a): the panic tool (src/x32mcp/server.py:1062-1074) only calls desk.panic(); Desk.panic (src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1220) writes 24× mix/on=0 and publishes 'desk.panic', but nothing in cfs.py subscribes to it — CfsManager.__init__ subscribes only to 'connection.state' (cfs.py:522; the only other EventBus subscriber is webui). _run_ringout (cfs.py:1147-1197) loops on `ses.abort_reason is None`, which is set only by stop()/abort()/_lose_connection/policy refusal; _write_master (cfs.py:1115-1124) goes through desk.set_level, which does not look at mix/on; preflight's 'bus muted' blocker (provision.py:646) is evaluated once before arming. show_mode is likewise only checked at entry (cfs.py:648 / server.py:1746) and each 1 dB raise is inside the ±3 dB show-mode clamp, so show_mode(True) mid-run does nothing either.

Test A (CfsManager.ring_out(1, target -3, step 1, dwell 60 ms) from -20 dB; Desk.panic() fired from the RAISE stage at -18 dB): panic returned count 24 incl. bus.1 in 0.2 ms; zero cfs.abort events; 15 further RAISE stages after the panic; fader writes -19…-3 then back-off -6; report final_stage DONE, aborted False, max_master_db -3.0, end_master_db -6.0, notches []; fake desk afterwards /bus/01/mix/on == 0, /main/st/mix/on == 0, /bus/01/mix/fader == -6.0 (was -20.0).
Test B (real tools after make_app: setup_ringout_eqs dance, srv.ring_out dance, confirmed srv.ring_out running as a concurrent request, then srv.panic() + srv.show_mode(True)): panic ok 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted'; show_mode ON with ring_out listed as refused; cfs_status immediately after still mode 'ringout', stage RAISE, master -18; ring_out tool then returned ok, 'master -20.0 -> -6.0 dB (highest -3.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; DONE'; 15 raises after panic; fader -6.0; srv.unmute('bus.1') (Tier 1, no confirmation) succeeded, was_muted True, mix/on back to 1 — i.e. the bus comes back un-ramped 14 dB above the pre-run level with no notches cut.
Test C (contrast): cancelling the in-flight ring_out task (what an MCP notifications/cancelled does) → _unwind, ABORT, fader -24 (max -18 − 6); cfs.stop() (the feedback_watch_stop tool) → ABORT, end = max − 6. So the stop paths exist, but panic is not one of them, and the server instructions (server.py:128-129) explicitly tell the LLM to use panic() 'whenever the user says stop / kill it'.

Conditions: requires panic() to arrive as a second request while the confirmed ring_out request is still in flight. That is protocol-legal and executed — the mcp 2.2 dispatcher runs each inbound request in its own task (server/runner.py start_soon), so any client that sends a tool call without first cancelling the pending one (agent loops with parallel tool calls, a second client on HTTP transport, or simply an SDK client) hits it. A client that instead cancels the pending ring_out gets the safe _unwind path (Test C). This is the claim's own stated scenario, so CONFIRMED rather than PARTIAL.

Severity: high, as claimed. Panic itself still silences all 24 outputs (not 'defeats panic' → not critical), and the raise is bounded by the user-confirmed target/0 dB ceiling. But it defeats the documented expectation that 'stop / kill it' (→ panic per the server instructions) halts autonomous writes, the run reports a misleading DONE 'no feedback detected', and it leaves the desk in a dangerous post-error state: a muted monitor bus parked at target−3 dB (here +14 dB over the level at which feedback already occurred), un-notched because the mute broke the acoustic loop, one Tier-1 un-ramped unmute away from a loud feedback onset.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-2-repro (branch wt/verify-cfs-2-repro, commit 1ad6ce1). Test file: tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs2.py — test_A_panic_mid_ringout_cfsmanager, test_B_panic_and_show_mode_tools_mid_ringout, test_C_contrast_client_cancel_and_feedback_watch_stop_do_abort: '3 passed in 4.86s' via $SP/runtests.sh. JSON observations dumped to $SP/reports/verify-cfs-2-repro/test_A.json, test_B.json, test_C.json.
Key observed values — Test A: panic_result {count:24, muted incl 'bus.1', elapsed_ms 0.2, delivered 'sent'}; cfs_abort_events []; stage_names PREFLIGHT,SNAPSHOT,ARM, 17×RAISE, BACKOFF, DONE; raises_after_panic 15; fader_writes [-19..-3, -6]; report {final_stage 'DONE', aborted false, abort_reason null, start -20.0, max -3.0, end -6.0, notches []}; bus1_mix_on_after_run 0; main_st_mix_on_after_run 0; bus1_fader_after_run -5.99. Test B: panic_tool ok 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted in 0.2 ms (sent)'; show_mode_tool ok show_mode true 'refused: ring_out, ...'; cfs_status_right_after_panic mode 'ringout' master -18.0 stage RAISE; ring_out_tool ok summary 'ring-out Bus 1: master -20.0 -> -6.0 dB (highest -3.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; DONE', aborted false; raises_after_panic 15; bus1_fader_after_run -5.99; unmute_tool ok was_muted true; bus1_mix_on_after_unmute 1. Test C: cancel → mode idle, stage ABORT, fader -24.04; stop() → stopped true, final_stage ABORT, max -18.0, end -24.0.
Source refs: server.py:1062-1074 (panic tool → desk.panic only), server.py:128-129 (instructions: panic on 'stop / kill it'), desk.py:1184-1220 (publishes desk.panic; no CFS hook), cfs.py:522 (only 'connection.state' subscribed), cfs.py:1147-1197 (_run_ringout loop keyed on ses.abort_reason), cfs.py:1115-1124 (_write_master ignores mute), provision.py:646-647 (muted-bus blocker only at preflight), cfs.py:648/server.py:1746 (show mode checked only at entry); mcp SDK server/runner.py:684-686 (requests dispatched concurrently via start_soon).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix, with specifics: (1) in the panic tool, after desk.panic() returns, `if a.cfs.active: asyncio.get_running_loop().create_task(a.cfs.abort('panic'))` — fire-and-forget so panic latency/robustness is untouched (abort() only sets abort_reason + wake, it cannot raise into panic). (2) Belt-and-braces: CfsManager subscribes to 'desk.panic' (types={'connection.state','desk.panic'}) and aborts any live session / flags a system run stopped, so a panic issued through Desk by any caller (dashboard later, ring_out_system between stages) also stops it. (3) For an abort whose reason is panic, finalize to start_master_db (reuse the _restore_master/lowering force path via _write_master(ses, ses.start_master_db, force=True)) instead of max−abort_backoff_db, and mark the report ABORT 'panic' — so a later unmute returns the bus to the operator's own level. (4) Optionally make Policy.show_mode a property setter that publishes an event CfsManager also treats as abort('show mode engaged'), or have _run_ringout re-check self._policy.show_mode each iteration. (5) Consider having _run_ringout read mix/on of the target before each raise and abort if the bus went muted (covers a front-panel/X32-Edit mute too, which is the documented gig reflex).

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain read end to end, no gap found:
1. server.py:1062-1074 panic tool → only desk.panic(); it never touches a.cfs (contrast: disconnect at server.py:667-683 calls a.cfs.stop() first, App.reconnect at server.py:196 calls a.cfs.abort()).
2. desk.py:1184-1220 Desk.panic sends mix/on 0 to main/bus/mtx via acquire_write(panic=True) and publishes 'desk.panic'. grep of src/: the only subscribers to the EventBus are webui (display), Desk ('write') and CfsManager, which subscribes ONLY to {'connection.state'} (cfs.py:522). No panic latch exists in Policy (policy.py:375-379 panic just returns).
3. cfs.py:1147-1197 _run_ringout loops on `reason is None and ses.abort_reason is None`; per iteration it only checks pending detections, master>=target, budget, then _write_master → Desk.set_level(bus, +1 dB, force=False) (cfs.py:1115-1124). Nothing reads mix/on; the 'bus is muted' check lives only in provision.preflight (provision.py:636-647), evaluated once before arming (cfs.py:670 / server.py:1754). A +1 dB step passes the relative clamp (6 dB, 3 dB in show mode) and the 0 dB bus ceiling, so no PolicyError ever fires.
4. After panic the acoustic loop bus→output→wedge→mic is broken (bus output muted), so no ring can form; the RTA tap (/‑prefs/rta/pos = pre/post EQ, meters.md §5) is upstream of the mute so on a real desk the detector sees un-ringing ambient, on the fake the SyntheticRta floor — either way no HOLD, so the loop raises every dwell until 'target reached', then _finalize_levels (cfs.py:1262-1296) backs off safety_margin_db=3 (device.yaml:828) and stamps DONE. Report summary literally reads 'no feedback detected; DONE'.
5. X32 mix/fader and mix/on are independent parameters; a real desk accepts fader writes on a muted bus exactly like FakeDesk, and read-backs succeed, so the real desk behaves identically (arguably worse: guaranteed zero detections). Nothing autonomously un-mutes; the hazard materialises on the follow-up the panic docstring itself prescribes ('Unmute afterwards with unmute') — unmute('bus.N') is Tier 1, unconfirmed, unramped → bus comes back at target−3 dB (here −6 vs −20 pre-run, +14 dB) with the rings that provoked the 'kill it' un-notched and a report certifying the bus clean.
Reachability condition (the one caveat): the panic request must be dispatched while the ring_out request is still in flight on the same stdio server. The MCP SDK (mcp 2.2 jsonrpc_dispatcher) dispatches inbound requests concurrently, and the project's own surface presumes mid-run calls (feedback_watch_stop 'abort a running ring-out', cfs_status live, disconnect stops CFS), and INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:128-129) route 'stop / kill it' to panic(), so a client that can issue a call mid-run will hit this. With stock Claude Code the user must press Esc first, which sends notifications/cancelled → CancelledError → cfs._unwind (back-off max−6, session cleared) before panic runs — a different, safer ordering (my 4th test). Clients/agent loops that time out the long call without cancelling, run tools asynchronously, or share the server, produce the claimed ordering. Severity stays HIGH, not critical: panic does silence all 24 outputs and nothing re-opens them by itself, and the raise is bounded by the user-confirmed target ≤ 0 dB; but the emergency stop leaves an autonomous fader-raising loop running for up to minutes, voids the ring-out's core guarantee (raise only under a listening detector, report what was found) with an affirmatively false 'no feedback detected; DONE', and parks the muted bus up to target−3 dB behind a Tier-1 unmute — 'dangerous state after an emergency'. Same gap exists for show_mode(true) mid-run (checked only at entry, cfs.py:651) and for a WATCH session (harmless there).
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-2-src (commit 043b29c), tests/integration/test_verify_panic_vs_ringout.py, all through the server tool functions after make_app(FakeDesk, SyntheticRta floor-only): (1) test_panic_tool_mid_ringout_leaves_session_raising_the_muted_bus_then_tier1_unmute — setup_ringout_eqs(token) → ring_out(1, target −3, step 1, dwell 80 ms, token) as a task from −20 dB → at master −17 call srv.panic(): ok, count 24, 'bus.1' in muted, fake /bus/01/mix/on == 0, a.cfs.active True, mode 'ringout'; task result: ok, final_stage DONE, aborted False, abort_reason None, max_master_db −3.0, end_master_db −6.0, notches [], 14 RAISE stages above −17 after the panic, fake fader −6.0 (was −20.0), mix/on still 0, summary 'ring-out Bus 1: master -20.0 -> -6.0 dB (highest -3.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; DONE' (no mention of mute/panic); then srv.unmute('bus.1'): ok with no requires_confirmation → mix/on 1, fader −6.0. PASSED. (2) control test_control_feedback_watch_stop_mid_ringout_does_abort: same run, feedback_watch_stop at −17 → ABORT 'stopped by operator', max −17, end −23 (max−6). PASSED. (3) control test_control_disconnect_mid_ringout_does_abort: disconnect → cfs_stopped.stopped True, ABORT, max −17. PASSED. (4) test_cancelled_ringout_call_unwinds...: task.cancel() (what a compliant client's Esc/timeout does) → CancelledError, session cleared, fader −23 (max−6), later panic() finds nothing to abort. PASSED. 4 passed in 5.8 s. Source refs: server.py:1062-1074, 667-683, 196, 128-129; desk.py:1184-1220; cfs.py:522, 620-634, 651, 670, 1115-1124, 1147-1197, 1262-1296; provision.py:636-647; policy.py:375-379; device.yaml:828-830; docs/research/meters.md §5 (rta/pos is an EQ tap, upstream of mix/on).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the direction; tighten it: (a) make CfsManager subscribe to 'desk.panic' (cfs.py:522, alongside connection.state) and on it set ses.abort_reason='panic' + ses.wake.set() synchronously in the callback (no await, cannot delay or fail panic; covers every panic caller, not just the tool), and _stop_system('panic'); the panic tool can additionally report cfs_aborted in its envelope. (b) For an abort caused by panic, finalize to min(start_master_db, max−abort_backoff) via the forced Desk write (or the _restore_master raw path if the Desk write is refused) so un-muting returns to the operator's own level, and put 'ABORT: panic — bus left muted at X dB, rings after <level> untested' in the report summary the LLM reads. (c) Defence in depth in _run_ringout: before each RAISE re-read the target's mix/on and mix/fader (one GET per dwell) and abort if the bus is muted or the fader no longer matches ses.master_db (also closes the operator-pull-down override). (d) Re-check policy.show_mode inside the loop (or have show_mode(True) call cfs.abort) since it is likewise only checked at entry (cfs.py:651). (e) INSTRUCTIONS/panic docstring: during a ring-out 'stop/kill it' = panic() AND feedback_watch_stop(); never unmute a bus a ring-out touched without re-reading its fader.

## D18. [high] GEQ 'existing' gains: unreadable pars become 0.0 and gains are read once — CFS² writes −3 dB over an operator's −15/−12 dB cut (a +12/+9 dB boost at a feedback frequency)
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/desk.py:676 (get_fx: `par = secs.get('/fx/N/par') or {}` — a missing/timed-out par section is tolerated, all 64 tokens None), src/x32mcp/provision.py:336 (_side_gains: `0.0 if b is None`), provision.py:413-420 (bands all 0.0 → flat=True, ok, no 'already carries cuts' warning), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:886-889 (existing built once at preflight; None entries dropped → controller/writer assume 0 dB), cfs.py:413 (validate_notch(self.gains.get(band, 0.0), new) — belief, not the desk), detector.py:568

**Mechanism.** Two routes to the same wrong belief 'band is at 0 dB': (a) the single /node reply for /fx/N/par is lost at preflight (3 UDP attempts; get_fx only _require()s the /fx/N head) → validation reports a FLAT usable GEQ; (b) the value is read once at preflight and never refreshed, so any front-panel/X32-Edit GEQ move during a watch (which can run for a whole show) is invisible. On the next detection plan() computes 0+(−3) = −3, validate_notch(0, −3) passes (cut, ≥ −9), Desk.set_geq_band writes raw 0.4: the physical band goes from −15 (or −12) to −3 dB — the 'cuts only' machine has boosted a known feedback frequency by 12 (9) dB. Same path turns a band already at −9 into −3 (+6).

**Exploit.** (a) Operator rang the room out by hand earlier (2.5 kHz at −15 on the bus GEQ); LLM calls feedback_watch(1); the /fx/5/par reply is dropped → 'GEQ FX5L (FX 5A) … existing_cuts []'; the howl returns at 2.5 kHz → server writes /fx/5/par/22 = −3 dB → +12 dB at the ringing frequency. (b) During a watch the engineer pulls 2.5 kHz to −12 on the desk; next detection there → −3 written over it.

**Evidence.** test_x2_lost_par_reply_at_preflight_makes_cfs_raise_a_deep_cut_by_12db: fake par/22 preset to −15 dB (validate shows −15 when the reply arrives); conn.node made to raise RequestTimeout for 'fx/5/par' during feedback_watch → observed preflight ok, existing_cuts [], no 'already carries' warning; note injected → first set_geq_band write gain_db −3.0 to /fx/5/par/22, fake band now −3.0 dB (was −15). test_x2b_operator_deepens_band_mid_watch_then_cfs_raises_it: band set to −12 mid-watch → CFS writes −3.0, fake reads −3.0. Both PASSED.

**Proposed fix.** (1) get_fx: for GEQ/TEQ types _require the /fx/N/par section (missing → DeskError TIMEOUT) and _side_gains must keep None; validate_ringout_eqs adds blocker 'GEQ gains unreadable' when any band is None. (2) _DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain: read the band's current dB from the desk (Desk._section('/fx/N/par'), cache is dropped by /xremote pushes and own writes; one leaf GET otherwise) and validate_notch(actual, new); if actual < new (someone cut deeper) adopt `actual` into controller/writer state and skip the write; if actual > 0 (boost appeared) refuse. (3) Optionally re-sync NotchController gains from that read.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced both routes from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-5-repro (branch wt/verify-cfs-5-repro, commit 3fac494), test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs5.py, 3 tests, all PASS against unmodified product code (full suite: 914 passed; the single failure is test_settings_defaults_and_env asserting the checkout dir is named 'x32-mcp', a worktree artefact).

Code path verified by reading: desk.py:674-678 get_fx reads [/fx/N, /fx/N/par] via _read_sections; only the head is _require()d, `par = secs.get('/fx/N/par') or {}` so a None (timed-out OR unparsable, desk.py:291-300 _parse) par section yields 64 None tokens -> _geq_token_db(None)=None -> bands_db=[None]*31. provision.py:336 _side_gains maps None->0.0, so validate_ringout_eqs (provision.py:413-421) computes flat=True, ok=True, notches=[]; preflight (provision.py:657-660) therefore emits no 'GEQ already carries cuts' warning. cfs.py:886 builds `existing` once from pf.geq.bands_db and hands it to NotchController (detector.py:529-536) and _DeskGeqWriter (cfs.py:407); nothing in cfs.py ever re-reads /fx/N/par or listens to the /xremote push (desk.py:282 _on_push only invalidates the Desk read cache). On a detection NotchController._apply (detector.py:567-569) uses its belief `self._gains.get(band, 0.0)` -> plans 0+(-3)=-3, validate_notch(0,-3) passes (policy.py:405-421 checks belief, not desk), _DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain (cfs.py:413-414) re-validates against the same belief and Desk.set_geq_band (desk.py:1403-1431) writes raw 0.4 unconditionally (explicitly 'Not a notch policy check').

Observed (a): fake /fx/5/par/22 preset to -15 dB; control validate_ringout_eqs shows bands_db[21]==-15, flat False, preflight warns 'already carries cuts'. Then conn.node made to raise RequestTimeout for 'fx/5/par' only -> feedback_watch(1) returned preflight ok=True, geq flat=True, bands_db 31x0.0, warnings [], existing_cuts []. Open-loop ring injected at 2400 Hz -> first desk.write event {'address': '/fx/5/par/22', 'value': {'gain_db': -3.0, 'band': 22, 'side': 'A'}, 'tier': 1}; fake band 22 raw 0.4 = -3.0 dB (was 0.0 = -15 dB): a +12 dB physical move at the ringing frequency by the 'cuts-only' machine. Observed (b): watch armed on a flat GEQ, operator sets band 22 to -12 dB mid-watch (push delivered; desk.get_fx(5) itself reads -12.0), ring at 2400 Hz -> CFS writes gain_db -3.0 to /fx/5/par/22, fake reads -3.0 (+9 dB). Control (c): with the -15 read correctly at preflight, existing_cuts==[22] and the same ring produces zero writes at par/22 (band left at -15) — so the hazard is exactly the wrong 0 dB belief.

Conditions/realism: route (a) needs every attempt of the one /fx/N/par /node request to fail (timeout: retries+1 = 3 attempts at 0.5 s in production; or an unparsable/truncated reply, which _parse also maps to None) while the sibling /fx/N head answers — unusual but reachable on a lossy link, and the failure is silent and reported as a healthy flat GEQ. Route (b) needs no fault at all: any front-panel/X32-Edit GEQ move on the ring-out GEQ during a watch (which is designed to run for a whole show) is invisible to CFS; this is an ordinary live-sound situation. The physical result defeats the documented 'cuts only / only deepens' guarantee and raises gain by up to 12 dB at a frequency currently ringing, which can itself provoke runaway feedback. I rate it high per the rubric (documented guarantee defeated; bounded to one 1/3-octave band and to <=0 dB absolute, so not 'unbounded level change'); an argument for critical exists because the boost lands precisely on an active feedback frequency, but I keep the claimant's high as the honest rubric reading. All cited file:line references in the claim check out (desk.py:676, provision.py:336, provision.py:413-420, cfs.py:886-889, cfs.py:413, detector.py:568).
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-5-repro (commit 3fac494). Test: tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs5.py — run `$SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs5.py -s -p no:randomly` -> 3 passed in 4.27 s. Captured stdout: 
(a) test_a_lost_par_reply_at_preflight_then_cfs_raises_deep_cut: 'preflight ok: True warnings: [] existing_cuts: []' / 'preflight geq: {ok: True, flat: True, bands_db: [0.0 x31], notches: [], reasons: []}' / 'first GEQ write: {address: /fx/5/par/22, value: {gain_db: -3.0, band: 22, side: A}, tier: 1, tool: set_geq_band}' / 'fake band 22 now raw 0.40000 = -3.0 dB (was -15 dB)'. Setup: provision bus1->FX5L GEQ2, fakedesk.set_value('/fx/5/par/22', -15.0), control validate_ringout_eqs bands_db[21]==-15.0 & preflight warning 'already carries cuts' present; then monkeypatch conn.node to raise RequestTimeout for path 'fx/5/par' (hits>=1), CfsManager(frames=open-loop SyntheticRta), feedback_watch(1), rta.inject_ring(2400, 20 dB/s, -34).
(b) test_b_operator_deepens_band_mid_watch_then_cfs_raises_it: 'desk.get_fx band 22 reads: -12.0' then 'first GEQ write: {address: /fx/5/par/22, value: {gain_db: -3.0 ...}}' / 'fake band 22 now raw 0.4 = -3.0 dB (was -12 dB)'.
(c) test_c_control_when_par_reply_arrives_the_deep_band_is_left_alone: existing_cuts bands == [22], 'GEQ writes with correct belief: []', fake par/22 still raw 0.0 (-15 dB) after 1.5 s of ringing.
Code: desk.py:676 `par = secs.get(paths[1]) or {}`; desk.py:291-300 _parse -> None on missing/unparsable line; provision.py:336 `bands = [0.0 if b is None else float(b) ...]`; provision.py:420 flat computed from those zeros; provision.py:657 warning only when `geq.ok and not geq.flat`; cfs.py:886 `existing = {i+1: float(g) for ... if g is not None}` built once; cfs.py:413 `validate_notch(self.gains.get(band, 0.0), gain_db)`; detector.py:567-568 `current = self._gains.get(band, 0.0); new = max(notch_max_db, current + notch_step_db)`; desk.py:282 _on_push only invalidates cache (no CFS resync); desk.py:1403-1431 set_geq_band writes raw without comparing to the desk's current value. Notes JSON: $SP/reports/verify-cfs-5-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the claimant's fix; concretely: (1) desk.py get_fx: when head type is a GEQ/TEQ, `_require(secs, '/fx/N/par')` (missing -> DeskError TIMEOUT so preflight fails loudly) and keep None per band; provision._side_gains must not coerce None->0.0, and validate_ringout_eqs should add reason 'GEQ gains unreadable (no /fx/N/par reply)' if any band is None (flat must be False when unknown). (2) Make the write path read-before-write: in _DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain (or Desk.set_geq_band given a `expect_current_db`), fetch the band's actual dB via Desk._section('/fx/N/par') (cheap: cache is hot unless a push/own write dropped it, else one /node round trip ~10 ms) and call validate_notch(actual, new); if actual < new-eps (someone cut deeper) adopt actual into writer.gains and NotchController._gains and skip/return None; if actual > 0 (a boost appeared) refuse and warn. (3) Cheaper continuous variant: have CfsManager subscribe to conn.on_update for '/fx/{slot}/par/*' during a session and resync nc/writer gains from pushed values, so front-panel moves are tracked without extra reads; keep (2) as the authoritative guard since pushes can be lost.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Tried to refute by walking both routes end to end; no gap found, and a wire-level reproduction (FakeDesk itself not answering `/node fx/5/par`, so X32Connection's real retry loop ran) plus a control test confirm it.

Causal chain, route (a) — lost par reply:
1. provision.preflight (provision.py:627) → validate_ringout_eqs → _FxCache.get → Desk.get_fx (desk.py:670-701). get_fx reads [/fx/5, /fx/5/par] via _read_sections → conn.node_many, which maps a RequestTimeout (after timeout_s×(retries+1) = 3×0.5 s in production, connection.py:747-770/556-571) to None and never raises. get_fx `_require`s only the /fx/5 head (desk.py:675); `par = secs.get('/fx/5/par') or {}` (desk.py:676) → 64 None tokens → geq.a.bands_db = [None]*31, master None. A failed read is not cached, but nothing re-reads par before the first notch (set_geq_band only reads the /fx/5 head, desk.py:1418).
2. provision._side_gains (provision.py:329-339) maps None→0.0, so bands=[0.0]*31; validate_ringout_eqs adds no reason, cuts={} , flat=True (master None counts as flat, provision.py:419) → GeqStatus ok. preflight therefore emits no 'GEQ already carries cuts' warning (provision.py:657-660).
3. cfs._open_session (cfs.py:886) builds existing={1..31: 0.0}; the `if g is not None` filter is dead because _side_gains already substituted 0.0. NotchController._gains and _DeskGeqWriter.gains both hold 0.0 for band 22; cfs.py never reads GEQ gains again (only line 886; no /xremote hook — Desk._on_push only drops Desk's read cache).
4. Detection at ~2.4-2.5 kHz → band_for_freq→22 → plan(): no notches, _can_deepen(0.0 > -9) True → _apply: new=max(-9, 0-3)=-3, validate_notch(0,-3) passes (detector.py:568-571) → writer.set_band_gain validates against self.gains (belief 0.0, cfs.py:413) → Desk.set_geq_band writes absolute raw 0.4 to /fx/5/par/22 (desk.py:1403-1432 → _write: tier 1, no relative clamp, no desk read). Physical band -15 → -3 dB = +12 dB at the ringing frequency. Had validate_notch been given the desk value it raises BOOST_FORBIDDEN (policy.py:412) — verified.
Control test proves the mechanism is specifically the unread/stale value: when the par reply arrives, band 22 at -15 is 'existing', _can_deepen(-15>-9) is False and NO write happens.

Route (b) — stale after preflight: identical from step 3 on. Any change to the ring-out GEQ after preflight (front panel / X32-Edit, or LLM-reachable: recall_scene, restore_snapshot, setup_ringout_eqs are not refused while a watch/ring-out is armed — only the RTA-source move is, server.py:1504) is invisible to the controller; next detection at that band writes belief+(-3) over it: -12→-3 (+9), -9→-3 (+6). Reproduced for -12 and -9 via fakedesk.set_value (which also emits the /xremote push; it only invalidated Desk's cache).

Real desk vs FakeDesk: nothing fake-specific in the chain. The real X32 answers `/node fx/5/par` with 64 display-unit tokens (fx_routing_scenes.md §1.5/§2.2), pushes /fx/5/par/NN changes over /xremote exactly as the fake does, and applies an absolute `/fx/5/par/22 ,f 0.4` immediately; Desk/CFS code paths are the same. Route (a) on hardware needs every attempt of that one exchange lost while ~60 neighbouring /node reads in the same preflight burst (get_strip, get_inserts across all strips at concurrency 16, /fx/5 head — all _require'd) succeed: rare on wired LAN, plausible on Wi-Fi (DOC warns burst /node traffic drops datagrams, transport.md:149/649); in DEGRADED state only one attempt is made but then the _require'd reads would usually fail first. It is a fail-open design: an unreadable GEQ is reported as a healthy FLAT GEQ. Additional hardware-only exposure: /node GEQ token formatting is listed UNCONFIRMED (fx_routing_scenes.md:745); any token float() cannot parse also becomes None→0.0 silently (desk.py:704-712 + provision.py:336), which would void the existing-cuts protection on every run. Route (b) is trivially reachable during a watch that runs for a whole show and behaves identically on a real desk.

Severity: high (not critical): it defeats the documented 'CFS² only cuts / only deepens' guarantee (policy.py:405-416, checked against belief not the desk) and raises a band that is ringing at that moment by up to +12 dB, but the change is bounded (band never above -3 dB, one 1/3-octave band, at most one write per detection/cooldown). Route (b)'s reachability (ordinary operator behaviour, or an LLM restoring a snapshot/scene mid-watch) is what keeps it firmly at high rather than medium.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-5-src (branch wt/verify-cfs-5-src, commit cec127d), tests/integration/test_verify_cfs5.py — 4 passed (runtests.sh … tests/integration/test_verify_cfs5.py):
- test_control_deep_cut_visible_at_preflight_is_never_raised: par read OK, band 22 preset -15 → preflight warning 'GEQ already carries cuts', existing_cuts=[22]; injected note 2.4 kHz -6 dB for 3 s → zero set_geq_band writes, fake band still -15.0. PASSED.
- test_a_lost_par_reply_reports_flat_geq_and_first_notch_raises_band_by_12db: FakeDesk /node handler made silent for 'fx/5/par' (wire level). Observed: handler hit retries+1 times (real X32Connection retry loop), preflight ok, pf.geq.ok True, flat True, bands_db == [0.0]*31, master_db None, notches [], no 'already carries' warning; feedback_watch armed with existing_cuts []; ses.nc.gains[22]==0.0, ses.writer.gains[22]==0.0; note injected → first desk.write event tool=set_geq_band address /fx/5/par/22 gain_db -3.0 tier 1; fakedesk raw → -3.0 dB (was -15.0); policy.validate_notch(-15.0,-3.0) raises PolicyError. PASSED.
- test_b_operator_cut_to_minus12_mid_watch_is_overwritten_with_minus3: after arming, fakedesk.set_value(/fx/5/par/22, -12) (push sent); nc/writer gains still 0.0; note → write -3.0, fake band -3.0. PASSED.
- test_b2_band_at_minus9_from_operator_mid_watch_becomes_minus3: -9 → -3 written. PASSED.
Code refs: desk.py:675-676 (head required, par tolerated), desk.py:704-712 (_geq_token_db None on unparsable), provision.py:336 (None→0.0), provision.py:413-420 (no reason; flat with master None), provision.py:657-660 (warning only if not flat), cfs.py:886 (existing built once; None filter dead), cfs.py:413 and detector.py:544/568-571 (belief-based validate/_can_deepen), desk.py:1403-1432 (absolute write, no read-back), connection.py:556-571 (node_many swallows RequestTimeout → None), server.py:1504 (only RTA move is refused while armed; recall_scene/restore_snapshot/setup_ringout_eqs are not). Not executed: the LLM-driven variant of (b) via restore_snapshot/recall_scene during a watch (reasoned from the absence of a CFS-busy guard), and real-desk token-format failure (UNCONFIRMED item in fx_routing_scenes.md:745).
>
> *Better fix:* The proposed fix is right in direction; tighten it: (1) Fail closed on unreadable gains: Desk.get_fx should _require('/fx/N/par') when the head type is a GEQ/TEQ (DeskError TIMEOUT → preflight blocker), _side_gains must keep None, and validate_ringout_eqs must add a reason 'GEQ gains unreadable (bands …)' when any of the 31 bands or the master is None — never coerce to 0.0. (2) Make the desk, not belief, the reference at write time: in _DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain read the band's current dB (Desk._leaf/_section of /fx/N/par — cheap, cache is push-invalidated; one /node otherwise), then validate_notch(actual, new); if actual < new-eps (someone cut deeper) adopt actual into writer.gains and NotchController._gains and skip the write; if actual > eps (a boost appeared) or unreadable, refuse and surface a warning/abort rather than write. (3) Keep the controller in sync cheaply between detections: subscribe CfsManager to Desk/connection push events for /fx/{slot}/par/* of the armed slot/side and update nc._gains/writer.gains (or mark the band 'dirty' to force the read in (2)). (4) Refuse recall_scene/restore_snapshot/setup_ringout_eqs/load into the armed FX slot while CFS² is WATCH/RINGOUT (same BUSY guard as the RTA-source move), or re-run GEQ validation and re-seed the controller after them. (5) Optionally have set_geq_band itself enforce cuts-only for CFS callers by reading the current par and rejecting new > current, so the guarantee lives at the Desk boundary like the fader clamps do.

## D19. [high] F2b: panic() does not abort a running ring_out — the muted bus master is driven to the 0 dB ceiling and parked at −3 dB with no notches; a Tier-1 unmute then opens a hot wedge
*Angle:* llm-surface · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1062-1074 (panic does not touch app.cfs), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1115-1124 (_write_master keeps raising via Desk.set_level), cfs.py:1174-1197 (RAISE loop: no detections → next step until target), cfs.py:621-635 (abort exists but nothing calls it on panic), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1015-1024 (set_mute on bus is Tier 1), server.py:1067 (panic docstring: 'Unmute afterwards with unmute')

**Mechanism.** panic mutes the bus output (mix/on=0) which breaks the acoustic loop, so the detector sees no ring; the ring-out state machine interprets silence as headroom and steps the bus master up every dwell until min(ceiling 0 dB, target), then DONE backs off safety_margin 3 dB and writes a 'successful' report with zero notches. The bus is left muted at −3 dB (it started at e.g. −12 dB). Re-opening it is a Tier-1 unmute('bus.1') with no confirmation (only main mix/on is guarded), i.e. the model can undo panic on 22 of the 24 outputs by itself, now 9 dB hotter than before and with none of the feedback frequencies notched.

**Exploit.** setup_ringout_eqs([1]) (confirmed); ring_out(1, step_db=3, dwell_ms=150) → token → ring_out(…, confirm_token) starts raising from −12 dB; panic() → ok; ring_out returns ok, final_stage DONE, max_master_db 0.0, notches []; /bus/01/mix/fader == −3.0, mix/on == 0; unmute('bus.1') → ok, bus live at −3 dB.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_llm_surface.py::test_F2b_ring_out_keeps_raising_a_panicked_bus PASSES: asserts a.cfs.active is still True after panic, report final_stage=='DONE', aborted False, max_master_db==0.0, notches==[], fakedesk '/bus/01/mix/fader'≈−3.0 (was −12.0), mix/on 0 → after unmute('bus.1') mix/on 1 at −3.0 dB.

**Proposed fix.** server.panic (or Desk.panic via an event) must call CfsManager.abort('panic') BEFORE muting so the abort path restores the starting master (cfs already has _restore_master); additionally treat 'bus muted / mix/on pushed to 0' observed via /xremote as an abort condition inside the RAISE loop; consider making unmute of bus/mtx after a panic require the Tier-2 dance (see F2a latch).

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-llm-surface-2-repro (commit a711f71, tests/integration/test_verify_f2b_repro.py, 4/4 pass) against the real server.py tools on FakeDesk + injected SyntheticRta.

Code path verified by reading: server.py:1062-1074 panic() only calls desk.panic(); Desk.panic (desk.py:1184-1221) fires mix/on 0 on main/bus/mtx and publishes 'desk.panic' but nothing consumes that event — CfsManager subscribes only to 'connection.state' (cfs.py:522) and CfsManager.abort() (cfs.py:621-635) is called only from close()/shutdown (cfs.py:788) and reconnect (server.py:197). The RAISE loop (cfs.py ~1160-1210) treats 'no pending detection' as headroom and steps the master by step_db each dwell until target (default = ceiling 0 dB), then _finalize_levels backs off safety_margin_db 3 (cfs.py:1274-1291) → DONE. _write_master → Desk.set_level on the bus fader never looks at mix/on. Preflight (provision.py:646-647) refuses to START a ring-out on a muted bus ('CFS² never unmutes'), showing the design intent that a muted bus must not be rung out — but a mute arriving mid-run (panic) is invisible to the session. Bus mix/on is Tier 1 (device.yaml blk_mix_full line 214; only main mix/on is Tier 2 at line 262), so server.unmute('bus.1') (server.py:929-935 → desk.set_mute desk.py:1015-1024) needs no token, and panic's own docstring (server.py:1067) tells the model to 'Unmute afterwards with unmute'.

Observed: ring_out(1, step_db=3, dwell_ms=150) confirmed and running from −12 dB; panic() → ok, 24 muted, /bus/01/mix/on==0; a.cfs.active stays True, cfs_status mode=='ringout'; the run completes ok with final_stage DONE, aborted False, start −12.0, max_master_db 0.0, end −3.0, notches []; fakedesk /bus/01/mix/fader ≈ −2.98, mix/on 0. unmute('bus.1') → ok with no requires_confirmation; bus live at −3 dB (+9 dB vs operator setting, zero notches). Magnitude variant: start −30 dB → still driven to 0.0 and parked at −3.0; unmute lands +27 dB above the operator's level — bounded only by the 0 dB bus ceiling. Physical-mechanism variant (a ring growing at panic, removed when the mute breaks the loop) behaves identically: raising resumes to the ceiling. Control: feedback_watch_stop during the same run DOES abort (ABORT, max −9, end −15), so the abort machinery works; panic simply is not wired to it.

Severity: high (as claimed). Panic itself still silences the outputs (not 'defeated' acutely), but it leaves the desk in a dangerous latent state after the emergency: an autonomous loop keeps writing fader raises to a panicked output for seconds-to-minutes, parks it up to ceiling−3 dB with none of the feedback frequencies cut, files a 'successful' report, and the documented recovery step (Tier-1 unmute, or the operator's physical unmute at FOH mid-run) re-opens the wedge far hotter than before straight into the feedback it was being rung out for. Borderline critical given the +27 dB case, but the loud change requires the follow-up unmute, so high is the honest rating.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-2-repro (branch wt/verify-llm-surface-2-repro, commit a711f71). Test file: tests/integration/test_verify_f2b_repro.py — 4 passed in 6.73 s via $SP/runtests.sh.
- test_F2b_panic_does_not_abort_ring_out_and_bus_is_raised_to_ceiling: printed REPORT {'final_stage': 'DONE', 'aborted': False, 'abort_reason': None, 'start_master_db': -12.0, 'max_master_db': 0.0, 'end_master_db': -3.0, 'notches': []}; fader at panic -9.0, fader after run -2.98, mix/on after run False; asserts a.cfs.active True after panic, cfs_status mode 'ringout', unmute('bus.1') ok without token → mix/on 1 at −3.0 dB.
- test_F2b_variant_ring_in_progress_vanishes_with_the_mute_and_raising_resumes: same REPORT (DONE, max 0.0, end −3.0, notches []), mix/on False.
- test_F2b_magnitude_is_bounded_only_by_the_ceiling: start −30.0 → max 0.0, end −3.0, DONE; unmute → live at −3.0 (+27 dB).
- test_F2b_control_feedback_watch_stop_does_abort: REPORT {'final_stage': 'ABORT', 'aborted': True, 'abort_reason': 'stopped by operator', 'max_master_db': -9.0, 'end_master_db': -15.0}.
Code refs: server.py:1062-1074 (panic → desk.panic only), desk.py:1184-1221 (publishes desk.panic; no consumer), cfs.py:522 (only connection.state subscribed), cfs.py:621-635 (abort; callers only cfs.py:788 shutdown, server.py:197 reconnect), cfs.py RAISE loop ~1160-1210 + _finalize_levels 1274-1291, provision.py:646-647 (preflight blocks muted bus at start only), device.yaml:214 vs 262 (bus mix/on Tier 1, main Tier 2), server.py:929-935 unmute Tier 1, server.py:1067 docstring 'Unmute afterwards with unmute'.
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's fix direction is right but one detail is off: CfsManager.abort() does NOT restore the starting master — it takes the BACKOFF path (current − abort_backoff_db 6 dB, cfs.py:1274-1276), so wiring panic→abort('panic') alone would still leave a bus panicked late in the run parked well above its start (e.g. −30 start, panicked at −6 → parked at −12, +18 dB). _restore_master (raw send of start_master_db) is only used on connection loss. Recommended: (1) server.panic/Desk.panic first calls cfs.abort with a dedicated reason that takes the restore-to-start path (end = min(start_master_db, master−backoff)), written with force/raw so it can never be refused, then mutes; or have CfsManager subscribe to 'desk.panic'. (2) In the RAISE loop, read back mix/on of the target each dwell (or watch /xremote) and abort-with-restore if the bus was muted by anyone (front panel, X32-Edit, panic) — silence must never be read as headroom. (3) Consider latching bus/mtx unmute behind the Tier-2 dance while a panic latch is set, and drop 'Unmute afterwards with unmute' from the panic docstring in favour of restore_snapshot/operator action.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain read end to end, no gap found:
1. server.panic (src/x32mcp/server.py:1062-1074) calls only desk.panic(); Desk.panic (desk.py:1184-1220) fire-and-forgets `<prefix>/mix/on 0` for main st/m, 16 buses, 6 mtx and publishes `desk.panic`. Nothing touches app.cfs. CfsManager subscribes only to `connection.state` (cfs.py:522); grep for panic in cfs.py finds docstring mentions only. Contrast: the connect tool DOES abort a running session (server.py:196-197 `if self.cfs.active: await self.cfs.abort("reconnect requested")`), so the hook pattern exists and was simply omitted for panic.
2. The ring_out tool awaits cfs.ring_out (server.py:1773-1776); MCP requests are dispatched as independent tasks (mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:606 `_spawn(self._handle_request, ...)`), and there is no tool-level lock (_tool wrapper server.py:312-328), so a concurrent panic() is server-reachable. More importantly, BRIEF.md:90 documents the operator's emergency reflex as X32-Edit/front panel — a physical bus mute — which produces the identical state (variant B below), so client concurrency is not even required.
3. _run_ringout (cfs.py:1146-1197): each iteration pops a pending detection (none once the acoustic loop is open), checks master>=target, else `_write_master(ses, nxt)` → Desk.set_level(bus, nxt, ramp_ms=0, force=False) (cfs.py:1115-1124 → desk.py:982-996 → _move_level 948-978). _move_level has no relative guard (removed 2026-09-20 per the comment), only the 0 dB family ceiling; step 3 dB ≤ relative_limit 6 so ring_out accepts it; nothing on this path reads mix/on or any panic latch (none exists in Policy: acquire_write(panic=True) just returns, policy.py:375-379). So RAISE continues every dwell until target = min(bus_fader_max_db 0, master_ceiling_db 0) = 0 dB (device.yaml:725, 830).
4. _finalize_levels (cfs.py:1254-1292): abort_reason None and master raised → back = safety_margin_db 3 → forced write to −3 dB, final_stage DONE, aborted False. cfs never writes mix/on, so the bus stays muted at −3 dB.
5. unmute('bus.N') → Desk.set_mute → _write with tier_for('/bus/01/mix/on') = Tier 1 (device.yaml bus family inherits blk_mix_full tier 1; guarded globs 708-721 cover only /main/st|m/mix/*), no token; and panic's own docstring (server.py:1065-1067) tells the model to "Unmute afterwards with unmute". 22 of the 24 panicked outputs (16 bus + 6 mtx) are re-openable this way.
Real desk vs FakeDesk: the FakeDesk has no acoustics, so 'panic breaks the loop' had to be modelled (stop_ring at panic time in variant C); on a real X32 the bus mute silences POST-tapped outputs (desk.py:1187, HANDOVER §4a: 24/24 verified muted), the wedge goes quiet, a live ring decays within the RTA decay time, and the RTA (tapped pre/post bus EQ, i.e. pre-fader/pre-mute per meters.md §5 /-stat/rtasource 48..63/146..161) keeps showing only the mic sum with no regeneration → no detections (or at worst spurious ambient ones that get notched) → the machine reads silence as headroom exactly as on the fake. VERIFY (cfs.py:1222-1251) only checks that the band dropped ≥ decay_verify_db, so a panic/mute landing during VERIFY is credited as a successful notch (variant C: VERIFY ok=True after panic, then RAISE −6/−3/0). A wedge fed from a PRE-tapped (no +M) output would not even be silenced by panic — separate known limitation, makes this worse not better.
Severity: high, not critical. The level is bounded by the Tier-2-confirmed target (0 dB) minus 3 dB and the output stays muted until a further action; panic's mutes themselves are never undone by CFS². But it defeats the documented guarantees that an emergency stop makes the desk safe and that 'nothing may leave the manager armed and the master raised' (cfs.py:41-44, 711-714): autonomous writes continue after panic, the report says DONE with 0 (or falsely credited) notches, and the desk is left with a wedge 9 dB hotter than the operator set it, un-notched, one unconfirmed Tier-1 call (explicitly recommended by the panic docstring) from an instant howl.
Note on the proposed fix: CfsManager.abort() does NOT restore the starting master — it takes the BACKOFF path with abort_backoff_db 6 from the level reached (cfs.py:1274-1276; variant D: abort at −6 → end −12 only because 6 dB happened to equal the climb). _restore_master (cfs.py:1325-1359) is wired only to the connection-loss path.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-2-src, commit 94cfc5a, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_f2b.py — 4 passed in 6.9 s via runtests.sh:
(A) setup_ringout_eqs([1,2]) confirmed; bus 1 master −12 dB; ring_out(1, step_db=3, dwell_ms=150) token → run; panic() → ok count 24, /bus/01/mix/on == 0; a.cfs.active still True; zero cfs.abort events; desk.write events on /bus/01/mix/fader with ts AFTER the desk.panic event: [-6.0, -3.0, 0.0, -3.0]; report final_stage DONE, aborted False, start −12.0, max_master_db 0.0, end_master_db −3.0, notches []; fakedesk fader ≈ −3.0, mix/on 0; unmute('bus.1') → ok with no requires_confirmation → mix/on 1 at −3.0 dB; set_main_mute('st', False) by contrast returns requires_confirmation.
(B) same run but the mute is applied on the desk itself (fakedesk.set_value('/bus/01/mix/on',0), the X32-Edit/front-panel reflex): identical DONE, max 0.0, end −3.0, still muted — a fix that only hooks the panic tool misses this.
(C) dwell 400 ms; inject_ring(2400 Hz, 40 dB/s, −30 dB) → HOLD/NOTCH at master −9; panic() then stop_ring (models the opened acoustic loop); stage timeline: PREFLIGHT, SNAPSHOT −12, ARM, RAISE −9, HOLD, NOTCH, VERIFY(ok=True, after panic), RAISE −6, RAISE −3, RAISE 0, BACKOFF, DONE −3; report DONE, aborted False, 1 notch (2.5 kHz −3 dB) credited although it was the panic that killed the ring.
(D) control: a.cfs.abort('panic') when master reached −6 → final_stage ABORT, end_master_db = max_master_db − 6 (abort_backoff_db), i.e. back-off from the reached level, not a restore of start_master_db.
Source anchors: server.py:1062-1074 (panic tool, no cfs call) vs server.py:196-197 (connect aborts cfs); cfs.py:522 (only connection.state subscribed); cfs.py:1146-1197 RAISE loop; cfs.py:1115-1124 _write_master; desk.py:948-996 _move_level/set_level (no mute/panic awareness, no relative guard); cfs.py:1254-1292 BACKOFF/DONE; desk.py:1015-1024 set_mute Tier 1; device.yaml:708-721 guarded globs (main only), 725/828-830 ceilings/margins; mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:606 per-request task spawn.
>
> *Better fix:* (1) In CfsManager.__init__ subscribe to `desk.panic` (alongside `connection.state`, cfs.py:522) and on it set abort_reason='panic', wake the session, and restore the master to start_master_db (a forced lowering Desk.set_level / set_main_level write, falling back to the raw _restore_master loop) rather than abort()'s 6 dB back-off from the reached level; also have server.panic/App call cfs.abort before desk.panic as belt-and-braces (mirrors server.py:196-197 for connect). (2) Make the RAISE loop and VERIFY observe the desk: before each raise and before crediting a VERIFY pass, read `<prefix>/mix/on` and the master fader (cache-invalidated / via xremote echo); if the bus was muted or the fader moved by someone else since the last write, abort and restore start — this covers the documented physical-mute reflex (variant B) and refuses to credit a decay that coincides with a mute (variant C). (3) Flag the report ('bus muted during run — result invalid, master restored') so the model does not read DONE/0 notches as success, and consider latching bus/mtx unmute behind the Tier-2 dance for outputs muted by panic() until the operator clears it.

## D20. [high] F3: absolute set_fader / set_send / DCA moves have no relative limit and accept ramp_ms=0, in show mode too — −oo→+5 dB (channels, DCAs) or −oo→0 dB (bus masters, sends into IEM buses) in one unramped write; INSTRUCTIONS tells the model the ±6/±3 dB limit applies
*Angle:* llm-surface · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/desk.py:948-978 (_move_level: 'No relative guard on an ABSOLUTE move', comment 959-964), desk.py:20-23 (module doc), src/x32mcp/server.py:879-896 & 946-961 (docstrings: 'size of the move is NOT limited … force is accepted but not needed'), server.py:114-118 (INSTRUCTIONS: 'Tier 1 mix moves (set_fader … set_send …) … limited to +-6 dB per call (+-3 dB in show mode)'), docs/BRIEF.md:63 ('single-call relative change ≤ ±6 dB'), src/x32mcp/policy.py:243-252 (DCA gets the +5 channel ceiling although device.yaml:272 declares clamp max 0 for /dca/N/fader)

**Mechanism.** check_relative is only called from Desk.adjust_level (desk.py:1005). set_level/_move_level clamp to the family ceiling and ramp, but ramp_ms=0 is accepted (server _int_arg 0..60000) so the 'shaped' part is optional, and show mode's ±3 dB tightening never engages. adjust_fader's guard is therefore decorative for a confused model: set_fader(target, current+N) does the same move unguarded. A DCA at +5 dB adds +5 to every member channel on top of their own faders. This is a deliberate post-M5 decision, but it removes the only magnitude guard BRIEF §4 promised for Tier 1, and the model-facing INSTRUCTIONS still claim the guard exists.

**Exploit.** show_mode(true); adjust_fader('ch.5', +4) → RELATIVE_TOO_LARGE (limit 3); set_fader('ch.5', 5, ramp_ms=0) → ok, before '-oo', after 5.0, steps 1; set_fader('bus.3', 0, ramp_ms=0) → ok from -oo; set_send('ch.5', 3, 0, ramp_ms=0) → ok from -oo; set_fader('dca.1', 5, ramp_ms=0) → ok after_db 5.0 clamped None; 32× set_fader('ch.N', 5, ramp_ms=0) concurrently → all ok within milliseconds.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_llm_surface.py::test_F3_absolute_moves_ignore_the_relative_limit_even_in_show_mode PASSES (fakedesk /ch/05/mix/fader 5.0, /bus/03/mix/fader 0.0, /ch/05/mix/03/level 0.0, all single-step, show mode on); test_F11_edge_probes prints 'dca +5: ok True, after_db 5.0, clamped None'.

**Proposed fix.** Keep absolute moves convenient but bounded: apply check_relative to upward absolute moves whose start is above a 'silence' threshold (e.g. > −60 dB), or require force for any upward absolute move > relative_limit_db while show_mode is on; enforce a minimum ramp (e.g. ≥100 ms per 6 dB) for upward moves regardless of ramp_ms; honour device.yaml's dca clamp (0 dB) in Policy.level_ceiling_db; correct INSTRUCTIONS/BRIEF so the model is not told a guard exists where it does not.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-llm-surface-4-repro (branch wt/verify-llm-surface-4-repro, commit 1c81d2e) with tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_f3.py against FakeDesk+App via the real server tools; both tests PASS and every element of the claim holds.

Mechanism verified in code: Policy.check_relative is called only from Desk.adjust_level (src/x32mcp/desk.py:1005).
