Desk.set_level -> _move_level (desk.py:948-978) takes `force` but never uses it; it only applies policy.clamp_level (family ceiling) and policy.ramp_steps, and ramp_ms=0 is accepted (desk.py:965 `_int(ramp_ms,'ramp_ms',0,60000)`, server.py set_fader/set_send `_int_arg(ramp_ms,'ramp_ms',0,60_000)`), which yields a single step. Show mode only changes Policy.relative_limit_db (policy.py:296-299), which absolute moves never consult. Policy.level_ceiling_db (policy.py:243-252) returns ch_fader_max_db (+5) for family 'dca' even though device.yaml:274 declares `fader: {... clamp: {max: 0}}` for /dca/N/fader; descriptor.py:15-18 states clamps are NOT applied by to_raw and are left to policy, and policy ignores the yaml value (policy.py:13-14 module doc deliberately gives DCA the channel ceiling) — so the yaml clamp is dead config and the two sources disagree.

What actually happens with show_mode(true): adjust_fader('ch.5', +4) -> RELATIVE_TOO_LARGE limit_db 3 (guard live); set_fader('ch.5', 5, ramp_ms=0) from -oo -> ok, steps 1, ramp_ms 0, clamped None, exactly one OSC write (/ch/05/mix/fader 0.8749), desk at +5.0 dB, elapsed 0.4 ms; set_fader('bus.3', 0, ramp_ms=0) from -oo -> ok, 1 write, desk 0.0 dB (requesting +10 is clamped to 0 and reported); set_send('ch.5', 3, 0, ramp_ms=0) from -oo -> ok, 1 write, /ch/05/mix/03/level = 0.0 dB; set_fader('dca.1', 5, ramp_ms=0) from -oo -> ok, after_db 5.0, clamped None, 1 write, /dca/1/fader = +5.0 while fakedesk.spec('/dca/1/fader').clamp_max == 0.0 and policy.level_ceiling_db(dca) == 5.0; set_fader('ch.5', cur-20) then set_fader('ch.5', +5) -> two 20 dB single-write swings, no force, show mode on; 32 concurrent set_fader('ch.N', 5, ramp_ms=0) from -oo -> all ok, all 32 desk faders at +5.0, total 5.6 ms (fits the 50-token write bucket, no RATE_LIMITED). With the default ramp (no ramp_ms) the -oo -> +5 move is ramped over 300 ms but its size is still unguarded. INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:114-118) still tells the model 'Tier 1 mix moves (set_fader, ... set_send, ...) are ... limited to +-6 dB per call (+-3 dB in show mode); a larger move needs force=true' and docs/BRIEF.md:63 lists 'single-call relative change <= +-6 dB' as a Tier-1 clamp.

Severity assessment: kept at HIGH. Nuance the claimant already acknowledged and I confirm: this is an intentional post-M5 decision, correctly documented in README.md:265-269/575-580, HANDOVER.md:107/125, desk.py:20-23 and the set_fader/set_send tool docstrings (server.py:879-884, 946-950: 'size of the move is NOT limited ... force is accepted but not needed'), and the destination stays bounded by the family ceiling (+5 ch/aux/fxrtn/DCA, 0 bus/mtx/send; mains remain Tier 2), so it is not 'unbounded' and not critical. But under the stated threat model (confused/runaway LLM) it removes the only magnitude and speed guards for Tier 1 level writes: show mode — documented as accident protection during a performance — provides zero protection against set_fader/set_send, ramping is opt-out via ramp_ms=0, force is decorative, adjust_*'s guard is trivially sidestepped by set_fader(target, current+N), a DCA can be slammed to +5 (adding +5 dB to every member on top of their own +5 ceiling, contradicting device.yaml's declared 0 dB DCA clamp), and 32 open channels can go -oo -> +5 dB in ~6 ms with no confirmation. The model-facing INSTRUCTIONS and BRIEF §4 promise a guard that does not exist, which is exactly the 'defeats a documented guarantee (show mode / relative limit)' bar for high.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-4-repro (commit 1c81d2e). Test: tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_f3.py::test_f3_absolute_moves_unbounded_by_relative_limit_in_show_mode and ::test_f3_default_ramp_still_no_size_guard -> '2 passed in 1.28s' via $SP/runtests.sh. Results JSON: $SP/reports/verify-llm-surface-4-repro/f3_results.json. Key observed values (show_mode true, relative_limit_db 3.0 throughout): adjust_fader ch.5 +4 -> {code: RELATIVE_TOO_LARGE, limit_db: 3.0}; set_fader ch.5 +5 ramp0 -> {ok: true, before: '-oo', after_db: 5.0, steps: 1, ramp_ms: 0, clamped: null, elapsed_ms: 0.4, desk: 4.995, writes: 1}; set_fader bus.3 0 ramp0 -> {ok: true, before: '-oo', after_db: 0.0, steps: 1, writes: 1, desk: -0.01}; set_fader bus.3 +10 -> clamped {limit: 0.0}; set_send ch.5->bus3 0 ramp0 -> {ok: true, before: '-oo', after_db: 0.0, steps: 1, writes: 1, desk: 0.0}; set_fader dca.1 +5 ramp0 -> {ok: true, before: '-oo', after_db: 5.0, steps: 1, clamped: null, desk: 4.995, raw write /dca/1/fader 0.87488}; dca_fader_yaml_clamp_max: 0.0 vs policy_dca_ceiling: 5.0; 20 dB down/up swings on ch.5 in show mode, no force -> ok, steps 1 each; mass 32ch -oo->+5 ramp0 concurrent -> {all_ok: true, elapsed_ms: 5.6, desk_levels_min/max: 4.995, errors: []}; INSTRUCTIONS sentence captured verbatim: 'Tier 1 mix moves (set_fader, adjust_fader, mute/unmute, set_send, ...) are clamped (...), ramped (default 300 ms) and limited to +-6 dB per call (+-3 dB in show mode); a larger move needs force=true'. Code refs: desk.py:1005 (only check_relative call), desk.py:948-978 (_move_level, force unused, comment 959-964), desk.py:965 (ramp_ms 0 allowed), policy.py:243-252 (DCA -> ch ceiling), policy.py:296-310 (relative limit/show mode), device.yaml:274 (dca fader clamp max 0, never enforced), descriptor.py:15-18 (to_raw does not apply clamps), server.py:114-118 (INSTRUCTIONS), server.py:879-884 & 946-950 (docstrings say NOT limited), docs/BRIEF.md:63, README.md:575-580 (documents adjust-only guard).
>
> *Better fix:* Keep 'bring a send/fader up from silence' working but restore a guard a runaway model cannot opt out of: (1) in _move_level, when the move is upward and show_mode is on, require force (or apply check_relative against max(before,-60 dB)) for any rise > relative_limit_db — show mode is exactly when an unrequested -oo->+5 is dangerous; outside show mode at least apply it when `before` is above a silence threshold (e.g. > -60 dB) so set_fader(x, current+N) cannot sidestep adjust_fader. (2) Enforce a minimum ramp for upward moves regardless of ramp_ms (e.g. ms = max(ms, 25 ms per dB of rise above relative_limit_db)), so ramp_ms=0 cannot turn a 95 dB rise into one datagram; downward moves and moves to -oo may stay instant. (3) Make Policy.level_ceiling_db derive from the descriptor's ParamSpec.clamp_max for the family's fader/send spec (single source of truth) instead of hard-coded per-family constants — that makes device.yaml:274's DCA 0 dB ceiling real (or consciously change the yaml to +5 and say so); add a test that every `clamp:` in device.yaml is either enforced or reported. (4) Optionally add an aggregate guard: N strips raised by > X dB within T seconds -> require force/confirmation, which is what catches the 32-channel slam. (5) Correct INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:114-118) and BRIEF §4 so the model is told the truth: the ±6/±3 dB limit is adjust_fader/adjust_send only, and what (if anything) bounds set_fader/set_send in show mode.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — I tried to refute the claim by tracing every absolute-level path end to end and could not: the causal chain has no gap.

1. Code path. server.py:879-896 set_fader and :946-961 set_send accept ramp_ms via _int_arg(0..60000) (server.py:395-401) and call Desk.set_level/set_send -> desk.py:982-995 set_level -> desk.py:948-978 _move_level. _move_level does: tier_for (refuses only Tier-2 addresses), policy.clamp_level (family ceiling + -90 floor), ensure_pre_write_snapshot, policy.ramp_steps, _ramp -> _write (acquire_write rate limiter). There is NO call to policy.check_relative anywhere on this path; grep confirms check_relative has exactly one caller, desk.py:1005 in adjust_level. The `force` parameter of set_level/_move_level is accepted and never read. The comment at desk.py:959-964 and the module doc desk.py:20-23 state this is deliberate ("No relative guard on an ABSOLUTE move (Jim's call, 2026-09-20, after M5)"), and HANDOVER.md:107,125 lists "the +-6 dB guard applied to absolute moves" as a defect that was removed. So the behaviour is intentional, not accidental.

2. Show mode. Policy.show_mode affects only relative_limit_db (policy.py:277-280, read solely by check_relative) and check_show_mode_allows (scene/ring-out actions). Since set_* never calls check_relative, show mode has zero effect on any absolute level move. The show_mode tool docstring (server.py:1273-1274), INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:114-118: "Tier 1 mix moves (set_fader, adjust_fader, ... set_send ...) are ... limited to +-6 dB per call (+-3 dB in show mode)"), adjust_fader's docstring (server.py:902-903: "same clamps and 6 dB/3 dB relative limit as set_fader") and BRIEF.md:63 ("single-call relative change <= +-6 dB" for Tier-1 fader/send levels; show_mode "tightens relative clamps to +-3 dB") all tell the model/operator a guard exists that the code does not apply to set_fader/set_send. For the stated threat model (confused/runaway LLM) adjust_*'s guard is decorative: set_fader(target, current+N) performs the refused move with no force and no token.

3. ramp_ms=0. Accepted by design (DESIGN.md §10 ramp_steps "0 -> [to_db]", policy.py:437-439), producing a single write. But this is not the crux: I also showed the DEFAULT 300 ms ramp takes ch.7 from -oo to +5 dB (15 steps, 281 ms) — a 95 dB rise in 0.3 s is a slam either way. The ramp shapes a move; it never bounds it. So the finding stands even if ramp_ms=0 were removed.

4. DCA ceiling. policy.py level_ceiling_db (the `return self.ch_fader_max_db  # ch, auxin, fxrtn, dca` branch, module doc policy.py:13-15) gives DCA the +5 dB channel ceiling, while device.yaml:274 declares `/dca/N/fader clamp: {max: 0}`. descriptor.py:15-18 documents that ParamSpec.to_raw never applies yaml clamps (policy is supposed to), and nothing in desk.py consults ParamSpec.clamp_max — verified: spec.clamp_max==0.0 yet policy ceiling==5.0 and set_fader('dca.1', 10) lands at +5.0. A DCA at +5 adds +5 dB to every member on top of the member's own +5 ceiling, i.e. the declared device clamp is silently widened.

5. Real desk vs FakeDesk. Everything involved (relative check, ramping, rate limiting, clamps) is client-side; the console applies an OSC fader float immediately (HANDOVER M5 confirms set_fader +9 -> +5.0 on hardware and that ramps are produced by the client and merely 'glide' on the panel). A real X32 receiving one `/ch/05/mix/fader ,f 0.8776` or `/bus/03/mix/fader ,f 0.75` datagram jumps the DSP gain and motor fader at once, so the hardware behaves exactly as the FakeDesk did; UDP loss could only make the write not happen. 48 writes in <0.1 s sit inside the 50-token bucket, so the rate limiter does not intervene on hardware either (up to ~100 such writes/s before RATE_LIMITED).

Severity re-assessment: HIGH is right. Not critical — the destination is bounded by the Tier-1 ceilings (+5 dB ch/aux/fxrtn/DCA, 0 dB bus/mtx/sends), main LR/mono remain GUARDED (Tier-1 set_fader('main.st') is refused), panic is unaffected, and Tier 1 is unconfirmed by design. But it defeats two documented guarantees named in the rubric — the BRIEF §4 / INSTRUCTIONS single-call +-6 dB limit for fader/send levels, and show mode's +-3 dB tightening — completely and by mere tool choice, with the model-facing INSTRUCTIONS actively asserting the guard exists. Practical consequence: with show mode on, an LLM can take every channel to +5, every bus/IEM master to 0 and any send into an IEM bus from off to 0 dB in one datagram each, ~50/s, no force, no token. The DCA sub-point on its own would be medium (declared clamp widened by 5 dB).
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-4-src (branch wt/verify-llm-surface-4-src, commit 8cbe3b7), test file tests/integration/test_verify_f3.py — 4 passed in 1.88 s against FakeDesk via server tools after make_app:
- V1 (show_mode on, relative_limit_db==3.0): adjust_fader('ch.5',+4,ramp_ms=0) -> RELATIVE_TOO_LARGE; adjust_send('ch.5',3,+4) -> RELATIVE_TOO_LARGE; then set_fader('ch.5',5,ramp_ms=0) -> ok before '-oo' after_db 5.0 steps 1 ramp_ms 0 clamped None; set_fader('bus.3',0,ramp_ms=0) -> ok -oo->0.0 steps 1; set_send('ch.5',3,0,ramp_ms=0) -> ok -oo->0.0 steps 1; set_fader('dca.1',5,ramp_ms=0) -> ok -oo->5.0 steps 1 clamped None. EventBus 'write' log shows exactly one datagram per address: {'/ch/05/mix/fader':1,'/bus/03/mix/fader':1,'/ch/05/mix/03/level':1,'/dca/1/fader':1}; fakedesk.value confirms 5.0/0.0/0.0/5.0. Equivalence: ch.6 at -20 dB, adjust_fader(+20) refused, set_fader('ch.6',0,ramp_ms=0) ok -20->0 in 1 step.
- V2: 32x set_fader('ch.N',5,ramp_ms=0) concurrently in show mode -> all ok in 0.083 s, all faders read 5.0 on the fake; then 16x set_fader('bus.N',0,ramp_ms=0) -> 16 ok, no RATE_LIMITED.
- V3: show mode on, set_fader('ch.7',5) with DEFAULT ramp -> ok -oo->+5.0, 15 steps, ramp_ms 300, elapsed 280.9 ms (ramp is not a magnitude guard).
- V4: descriptor ParamSpec for /dca/1/fader has clamp_max==0.0 (device.yaml:274) while policy.level_ceiling_db(Target('dca',1))==5.0; set_fader('dca.1',10,ramp_ms=0) -> after_db 5.0, clamped.limit 5.0.
Source refs: desk.py:948-978 (_move_level, no check_relative; force unused; comment 959-964), desk.py:1005 (sole check_relative caller, adjust_level), desk.py:20-23 (module doc), policy.py:277-291 (relative_limit_db/check_relative — only consumer of show-mode tightening), policy.py:429-446 (ramp_steps ms<=0 -> [end]), server.py:395-401 (_int_arg 0..60000), server.py:879-896/946-961 (tool docstrings 'size of the move is NOT limited'), server.py:902-903 (adjust_fader docstring claims set_fader has the limit), server.py:114-118 + :618 (INSTRUCTIONS sent to the MCP client claim +-6/+-3 applies to set_fader/set_send), server.py:1273-1274 (show_mode docstring), docs/BRIEF.md:63, docs/HANDOVER.md:107,125 (removal was deliberate), descriptor.py:15-18 (yaml clamps not applied by to_raw), device.yaml:274 (dca clamp max 0) vs policy level_ceiling_db dca->ch_fader_max_db 5. Results note: $SP/reports/verify-llm-surface-4-src/f3.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Keep the M5 usability win (a send/fader can come up from off without force) but restore a real bound for the runaway-LLM case: (1) in _move_level, when the move is UPWARD and the start is audible (before_db finite and > ~-60 dB), apply check_relative(after-before, force) — this preserves 'bring it up from silence' while stopping set_fader(current+20); (2) while show_mode is on, require force for ANY upward absolute move larger than relative_limit_db regardless of start level (show mode is exactly when an unrequested -oo->+5 matters); (3) enforce a minimum upward slew independent of ramp_ms (e.g. ramp_ms floor of ~50 ms per dB of rise above -40 dB, so -oo->+5 cannot be a single datagram) — downward moves and panic stay instant; (4) make Policy.level_ceiling_db honour the descriptor's ParamSpec.clamp_max when present (DCA -> 0 dB as device.yaml declares) or change device.yaml to +5 so the two agree, and add a test that every clamp: in device.yaml matches the policy ceiling for that family; (5) fix the model-facing text: INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:114-118), adjust_fader/adjust_send docstrings and BRIEF §4 must say exactly which tools carry the relative limit, and show_mode's summary should state what it does and does not restrict. Optionally count large upward absolute moves per minute and refuse past a small budget (a loop detector), since the token bucket limits packet rate, not loudness.

## D21. [high] F5: apply_patch_plan(include_source=True) binds its token to the file PATH, not to the routing shown — swapping the file between the two calls repatches all 32 inputs under a token whose summary said 'ch 16 → AUX1' (summary also truncates at 12 rows)
*Angle:* llm-surface · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1314-1336 (payload = {'file': str(path), 'include_source': True}; plan re-loaded from disk on the second call at 1323; summary shows rows[:12] + ' …'), src/x32mcp/policy.py:347-361 (guard compares payload dicts only), server.py:31-34 (module doc claims 'a token can only execute what its action_summary described')

**Mechanism.** The confirmed payload contains only the filename. On the confirming call the YAML/CSV is parsed again and whatever sources it now holds are written through desk.set_source (guarded=True, no further check). Any actor able to write the file inside the 300 s TTL (the same agent via a filesystem tool, export_patch_plan, another MCP server, an editor autosave) changes what the token executes. Independently, plans with >12 source rows are never fully shown to the user.

**Exploit.** write patches/tonight.csv with one row (16,Talkback,OFF,AUX1); apply_patch_plan('tonight', include_source=true) → token, summary 'patch 1 input source(s): ch 16 → AUX1'; overwrite tonight.csv with 32 rows source=OFF; apply_patch_plan('tonight', include_source=true, confirm_token) → ok, 32 sources changed, every input on the desk now OFF (silent band) — or equally IN→wrong preamp with phantom, etc.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_llm_surface.py::test_F5_patch_plan_token_is_not_bound_to_the_routing_it_described PASSES: fakedesk /ch/{01,08,16,32}/config/source all 'OFF' after confirming the 1-row token; done['applied'] shows 32 rows with 'source' changed.

**Proposed fix.** Put the concrete source map in the token payload (e.g. payload={'file':…, 'sources': {ch: token,…}} or a SHA-256 of the parsed plan) and on the confirming call apply exactly the stored map / refuse if the file's hash changed; list every source change in action_summary (no '…' truncation for routing).

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-llm-surface-6-repro (branch wt/verify-llm-surface-6-repro, commit b68724f), test file tests/integration/test_verify_f5_patch_token.py, 4/4 pass against unmodified HEAD a408a2a code on the FakeDesk.

Mechanism verified in the code: server.py:1322-1323 resolves and parses the file on EVERY call; server.py:1326 builds payload = {"file": str(path), "include_source": True} — nothing derived from the parsed rows; server.py:1327-1331 builds the human summary from rows[:12] + " …"; server.py:1332 → _confirm → policy.guard (policy.py:347-361), whose only binding check is `stored != dict(payload)` (policy.py:356). Since the payload is identical for any content at the same path, the token redeems, and server.py:1335 then applies whatever plan was loaded on the confirming call through patches.apply_patch_plan → desk.set_source(..., guarded=True) (desk.py:1349) for every row, with no further check. This contradicts the module's own stated guarantee (server.py:31-34: "the token payload is the normalised request … so a token can only execute what its action_summary described") and is inconsistent with the other Tier-2 tools, which do bind concrete content (recall_scene index server.py:1135, setup_ringout_eqs concrete buses/type_loads/inserts server.py:1594, ring_out_system full plan server.py:1827).

What I executed: (1) main exploit — 1-row tonight.csv (16,Talkback,OFF,AUX1) → token with action_summary "…patch 1 input source(s): ch 16 → AUX1", rows=1; rewrite same path with 32 rows source=OFF; confirm with the token → ok:true, rows 32, writes 32, "32 source(s) patched", fakedesk /ch/01../ch/32 config/source all "OFF" (whole input stage silent mid-show; equally any IN/AUX re-route). (2) control — token minted for a.csv is refused (BAD_TOKEN) for b.csv, proving the binding is exactly the path string. (3) truncation — a 32-row plan's action_summary lists ch 1..12 then "…"; ch 13-32 never shown to the user. (4) tools-only variant — no external filesystem access needed: Tier-0 export_patch_plan("tonight.csv") overwrites the same file inside the TTL with the desk's live 32 sources; after a transient front-panel change (ch5→AUX3, then reverted to IN05), confirming the stale 'ch 16 → AUX1' token re-patched ch 5 to AUX3 and did NOT patch ch 16 at all — the token executed something disjoint from its summary.

Severity: high per the given scale — it defeats the documented token↔payload binding guarantee for a Tier-2 routing action (31 unconfirmed source changes executed under one confirmation). Not critical: it needs the file to change between the two calls (reachable for an agent with a file-write tool, an editor autosave, or this server's own export_patch_plan, but still a precondition), and source re-patching is show-breaking/silencing rather than an inherently unbounded level increase (though routing a hot line source into a high-gain vocal channel is plausible).
>
> *Evidence:* Test run ($SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_verify_f5_patch_token.py -s): "4 passed in 1.89s". Captured output:
ACTION_SUMMARY: Apply patch plan tonight.csv: label 1 channel(s) AND patch 1 input source(s): ch 16 → AUX1
BEFORE: {1: 'IN01', 8: 'IN08', 16: 'IN16', 32: 'IN32'}
DONE: {"ok": true, "summary": "tonight.csv: 32 channel(s) labelled, 0 unchanged, 32 source(s) patched", "rows": 32, "writes": 32, "failed": []}
AFTER: {1: 'OFF', 2: 'OFF', … 32: 'OFF'} (all 32)
BIG SUMMARY: … patch 32 input source(s): ch 1 → OFF, … ch 12 → OFF …   (ch 13-32 omitted)
export variant: "ch5 after: AUX3 ch16 after: IN16" after confirming the token whose summary said only 'ch 16 → AUX1'.
Control: token for patches/a.csv presented with file 'b' → error.code BAD_TOKEN, ch16 source unchanged (IN16).
Code refs: src/x32mcp/server.py:1322-1336 (re-load + path-only payload + rows[:12] truncation), server.py:31-34 (doc claim), server.py:331-344 (_confirm), src/x32mcp/policy.py:347-361 (guard: stored != dict(payload) only), src/x32mcp/patches.py:551-596 (apply loop → desk.set_source), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1343-1350 (set_source guarded=True). Repro test: <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-6-repro/tests/integration/test_verify_f5_patch_token.py (commit b68724f).
>
> *Better fix:* Bind the token to the routing, not the filename: on the first call compute the concrete source map from the parsed plan (e.g. payload = {"file": str(path), "include_source": True, "sources": {str(r.channel): r.source for r in rows}} or a SHA-256 of path.read_bytes()/the normalised rows) and on the confirming call either (a) apply exactly the stored map via patches.apply_patch_plan(desk, plan, include_source=True, channels=stored) after asserting the re-parsed rows equal the stored map, or (b) refuse with BAD_TOKEN/"plan changed since confirmation" if the hash differs. Also list every source change in action_summary for routing (drop the rows[:12] '…' truncation, or refuse include_source plans whose summary would be truncated), so the human confirms what will actually be written. Optionally diff against the desk at mint time and summarise only the sources that will change, binding that diff.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (high)** — Causal chain, end to end, holds with no gap:
1. server.py:1316-1336 apply_patch_plan: `path = _patch_path(file)` then `plan = _patches.load_patch_plan(path, ...)` runs on EVERY call (first and confirming). With include_source the token payload is exactly `{"file": str(path), "include_source": True}` (1326) — no row content, no hash, no mtime. action_summary lists `rows[:12]` + " …" (1328-1331).
2. server.py:331-345 _confirm → policy.py:347-361 Policy.guard: with a token it calls consume_token(expected_action) then `if stored != dict(payload): BAD_TOKEN`. Both payloads are {file, include_source}; identical as long as the same path string resolves → passes. Nothing else inspects the plan.
3. server.py:1335 → patches.py:551-604 apply_patch_plan(desk, plan, include_source=True): for every row whose source differs from the freshly dumped /ch/NN/config it calls desk.set_source(t, source) (587) → desk.py:1343-1350 _write_value(..., guarded=True) → _write (871-884): tier check is satisfied by guarded=True, then snapshot, rate limiter (50/s, 32 writes trivially fit), conn.set. No per-row confirmation, no cap on row count, not in SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED (policy.py:75, check_show_mode_allows 224-235) so it also works during the show.
4. So whatever the file holds at confirm time is what is written; the user was shown (and the token was 'bound to') only the filename. This contradicts the module doc server.py:31-34 ('the token payload is the normalised request … so a token can only execute what its action_summary described') and guard's own docstring ('cannot execute anything but what was summarised'); every other Tier-2 tool binds the concrete request (set_channel_config {ch,source,link} 1388, setup_ringout_eqs concrete plan 1594, ring_out_system plan 1827) — apply_patch_plan is the outlier.
Attempted refutations that failed: (a) path change is caught — yes, deleting tonight.csv and creating tonight.yaml changes str(path) → BAD_TOKEN (my test v3), but in-place overwrite keeps the path; (b) 'only this server's tools' — true that the server's only file writer, export_patch_plan, writes the desk's own state so an export between the calls makes the confirm a source no-op (my test v4); the exploit therefore needs a writer outside this server. But the documented deployments are Claude Code (README 399-424, .mcp.json checked in, X32MCP_HOME = repo root = the agent's writable project dir, patches/ inside it) and Claude Desktop; a confused/jailbroken agent in Claude Code has Write/Edit on patches/*.csv by construction, and patch plans are human/agent-authored data files by design (patches/README.md). An operator editing the plan in an editor while the model holds a token produces the same mismatch non-maliciously. TTL is 300 s (device.yaml:738), a wide window. (c) FakeDesk vs real desk: /ch/NN/config/source int 0..64 is VERIFIED (docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md §4.9, scales_params.md §11.3); a real X32 repatches the input instantly, so the effect is at least as real on hardware (all 32 inputs OFF = band goes silent mid-show; or all → In17/USB/Bus = whatever is on that source appears on 32 open strips at their current fader/gain — potentially loud/feedback). patches.py itself is untested on hardware (HANDOVER.md:76) but that does not affect the write path, which is the ordinary Desk._write.
Severity: high per the rubric — defeats the documented token-binding guarantee for a Tier-2 routing action (what executes ≠ what was summarised/confirmed), reachable in the documented Claude Code deployment, effective in show mode, up to 32 routing writes under one token. Not 'critical' because a valid user-issued confirmation for the same action is still required and the server's own tool surface cannot weaponise it alone (needs an external file write). The 12-row summary truncation is a real but lesser (medium/low) sub-issue: count is shown ('patch 32 input source(s)') but rows 13+ are never displayed.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-6-src (commit c936a1c), tests/integration/test_verify_f5.py, 7 passed against FakeDesk via real tools (`await srv.apply_patch_plan(...)`):
- v1: 1-row file (16,Talkback,OFF,AUX1) → token, action_summary contains 'patch 1 input source(s): ch 16'; overwrite same path with 32 rows source=OFF; confirm → ok; fakedesk /ch/01..32/config/source: before IN01..IN32, after all 'OFF'; done['applied'] has 32 rows with 'source' in changed.
- v2: same swap but to In17 → ch 1,8,16,32 sources all 'IN17' (wrong-preamp/loud variant lands).
- v3 (control): delete .csv, create tonight.yaml → str(path) differs → BAD_TOKEN (binding is to the path string only).
- v4 (control): export_patch_plan('tonight.csv') between the calls → confirm ok but 0 source writes, desk unchanged (server's own writer cannot weaponise it; external writer required).
- v5: 32-row plan summary = 'patch 32 input source(s): ch 1 → OFF … ch 12 → OFF …', 'ch 13' absent.
- v6 (control): token remains single-use (second confirm BAD_TOKEN).
- v7: identical swap succeeds with show_mode(True) — apply_patch_plan not in SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED (policy.py:75).
Code refs: server.py:1322-1336 (payload={'file','include_source'}, plan reloaded each call, rows[:12]+' …'), server.py:331-345 _confirm, policy.py:347-361 guard compares payload dicts only, patches.py:575-590 set_source per differing row with no further check, desk.py:1343-1350/871-884 guarded write path, device.yaml:738 TTL 300 s, README.md:399-424 Claude Code deployment with patches/ in the agent's workspace, docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md:431-433 config/source VERIFIED on real X32.
>
> *Better fix:* Bind the token to the concrete routing, not the filename: on the first call compute `sources = {r.channel: r.source for r in plan.rows if r.source is not None}` (or sha256 of dumps_patch_plan(plan)) and put it in the payload; on the confirming call either (a) re-load, recompute, and let guard's payload compare fail with BAD_TOKEN + 'file changed since confirmation' if it differs, or better (b) apply exactly the stored map from the consumed payload (pass channels/sources to patches.apply_patch_plan) so the file is not re-read at all. List every `ch N → SRC` in action_summary (32 short items is fine; routing must never be elided) and consider adding apply_patch_plan/include_source to SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED (bulk re-routing mid-show is the same class as scene recall). Same pattern should be audited for restore_snapshot (payload {id, scope} → snapshot JSON file on disk) and ring_out(patch_file=…).

## D22. [medium] show_mode and the relative-move limit are self-service: show_mode(false) needs no token, force=true is a free argument, set_fader/set_send have no relative limit and ramp_ms=0 is allowed (DCA/bus -oo→ceiling in one datagram)
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1270-1282 (show_mode tool, 'Tier 0'), src/x32mcp/policy.py:290-305 (check_relative: force bypasses, also in show mode), src/x32mcp/desk.py:959-965 (_move_level: relative guard removed for absolute moves), src/x32mcp/server.py:881-913 (ramp_ms 0..60000, force passthrough), src/x32mcp/policy.py:238-252 (DCA ceiling = channel ceiling +5), device.yaml (/main/st/grp exists: Main LR can be a DCA member)

**Mechanism.** BRIEF §4 promises 'single-call relative change ≤ ±6 dB (±3 in show mode)' and show-mode blocking of scene/ring-out actions, but (a) show_mode(False) is an unconfirmed tool so a confused/jailbroken client simply switches it off before recall_scene; (b) adjust_fader(force=True) is honoured on the model's say-so; (c) set_fader/set_send skip check_relative entirely by design and accept ramp_ms=0, so any channel/aux/fxrtn/DCA goes -oo→+5 dB and any bus/matrix -oo→0 dB in a single unramped write; at 50 writes/s a runaway loop repositions the whole desk in ~1 s. A DCA whose members include Main LR (X32 allows it; /main/st/grp/dca is in the descriptor) moves the main output level without the Tier-2 token.

**Exploit.** show_mode(true) → recall_scene(1) refused SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS → show_mode(false) (ok, no token) → recall_scene(1) → token → recall_scene(1, token) recalled. adjust_fader('ch.1', +60, ramp_ms=0, force=true) → +5 dB. set_fader('dca.1', 10, ramp_ms=0) from -oo → +5.0 dB, steps=1.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_bypass.py::test_show_mode_is_self_service — 'EXPLOIT-4: show_mode(False) needs no token; … Ch 1 'Kick' fader 0.0 dB → +5.0 dB (+60.0 dB; clamped…) | DCA 1 'DCA1' fader -oo dB → +5.0 dB (clamped to the +5.0 dB limit, requested +10.0 dB)', scene 1 recalled after the self-service toggle. DCA→Main coupling not modelled by FakeDesk (semantic claim from the descriptor/X32 behaviour).

**Proposed fix.** Make show_mode(False) a Tier-2 confirmation (turning protection ON stays free); in show mode ignore force; re-apply a (larger, e.g. 20 dB) relative ceiling or a mandatory minimum ramp to absolute moves whose start is finite and above -60 dB, and cap upward ramp_ms=0 jumps; give DCA its own ceiling (0 dB) and treat a DCA that contains main.st/main.m (read /main/*/grp/dca) as guarded.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Every executable element of the claim reproduces exactly against HEAD a408a2a in my own worktree (verify-bypass-4-repro @ 78d9006, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass4.py, 12 passed): (a) show_mode(False) is an unconfirmed Tier-0 call (server.py:1270-1282) — after SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS the client flips it off with ok:true and no requires_confirmation, and recall_scene then proceeds through its normal Tier-2 dance; the refusal text itself (policy.py:233 'refused until show_mode(false)') tells a helpful-but-confused model how to lift the block. (b) adjust_fader(force=True) is honoured on the caller's word (policy.py:293-296 returns before the limit check), including in show mode: ch.2 -oo→+5.0 dB (+95 dB) in one write with the 3 dB show-mode limit active. (c) set_fader/set_send never call check_relative (desk.py:959-965 comment; only adjust_level at desk.py:1005 does) and accept ramp_ms=0, so with NO force ch/auxin/fxrtn/dca go -oo→+5.0 dB and bus/mtx/sends -oo→0.0 dB in exactly one datagram (steps=1, write_count delta 1), identically in show mode. (d) A loop over 64 strips (32 ch, 8 auxin, 8 DCA, 16 bus) put all of them from -oo to their ceiling in 0.36 s with zero RATE_LIMITED (token bucket capacity 50 + refill). (e) policy.level_ceiling_db gives DCA the channel ceiling (+5 dB, policy.py:246) although a DCA is a group offset; the descriptor does carry main grp/dca (device.yaml:648, tier 2), and with /main/st/grp/dca=1 set on the fake the DCA move is still an unconfirmed Tier-1 write. What does NOT hold / narrows it: (1) These are documented, deliberately-tested design decisions, not defeated guarantees — BRIEF.md header lines 3-7 says the ±6 dB guard now applies only to relative moves; README lines 487, 515, 575-580, 633-636 and DESIGN.md:450 ('DECISION: force bypasses relative limit even in show mode') state the current contract; the project's own tests assert it (test_server_tools.py:201-203 forced -9 dB, :370 free show_mode(False)). So under the given rubric this is not 'high = defeats a documented guarantee'; it is the documented guarantee being weak against the stated threat model (confused/jailbroken/runaway LLM): show mode is advisory, force is prompt-enforced rather than code-enforced (contradicting README:8/119 'enforced in code, not prompt'), and the only code-enforced bound on any Tier-1 level move is the family ceiling. (2) Main LR/mono are NOT reachable this way: set_fader/adjust_fader(force) on main.st → GUARDED, set_main_fader → requires_confirmation. (3) The 'DCA moves Main without a token' angle needs an operator/scene-set precondition (Main assigned to a DCA — grp/* is Tier 2 and no MCP tool writes it) and FakeDesk does not model DCA→member coupling, so that sub-claim is semantic only (not executed). All resulting levels are bounded (+5 dB inputs/DCA, 0 dB buses/matrices/sends), hence not 'critical'; but a DCA or bus master parked at -oo (standard between-songs practice) slamming to ceiling in one unramped datagram with no force and in show mode is a real hearing/feedback hazard the relative-limit rule was originally meant to catch, and a runaway loop repositions the whole desk in well under a second — medium.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-4-repro commit 78d9006, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass4.py; run: runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass4.py -s → 12 passed. Printed: '(a) show_mode(False) -> ok=True requires_confirmation=None; scene 0 -> 1 after self-service toggle (recall still needed its own Tier-2 token)'; '(b) adjust_fader(ch.1,+60,ramp 0,force) -> "Ch 1 \'Ch01\' fader 0.0 dB → +5.0 dB (+60.0 dB; clamped to the +5.0 dB limit, requested +60.0 dB)"; steps=1 writes=1 desk=4.995'; '(b) SHOW MODE: adjust_fader(ch.2,+95,ramp 0,force) -> "Ch 2 \'Ch02\' fader -oo dB → +5.0 dB (+95.0 dB)"'; '(c) set_fader(dca.1,+10,ramp 0) no force -> "DCA 1 \'DCA1\' fader -oo dB → +5.0 dB (clamped …)"; steps=1 writes=1 desk=4.995' (same for ch.3, auxin.1, fxrtn.1; bus.1/mtx.1 → 0.0 dB; all identical in SHOW MODE); '(c) set_send(ch.1->bus3,+10,ramp 0) -> "Bus 3 \'Bus03\' send from Ch 1 \'Ch01\' -oo dB → 0.0 dB …"; steps=1 writes=1'; '(c\') main.st via set_fader/adjust_fader -> GUARDED; set_main_fader -> requires_confirmation (Tier 2 intact)'; '(d) 64 strips -oo->ceiling with ramp 0, no force: ok=64 rate_limited=0 at_ceiling=64 in 0.36 s'; '(e) DCA fader ceiling=+5.0 dB, main ceiling=+0.0 dB; descriptor main grp/dca param: True; fakedesk /main/st/grp/dca=0; tier(/main/st/grp/dca)=2; FakeDesk does not model DCA->member level coupling … with /main/st/grp/dca=1 on the desk, set_fader(dca.1,+10,ramp 0) -> "DCA 1 \'DCA1\' fader -oo dB → +5.0 dB" (no token asked)'. Code refs: server.py:1270-1282 (show_mode tool, no _confirm), policy.py:229-235 (SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS message names the escape hatch), policy.py:290-305 (check_relative force early-return), desk.py:959-965 + 1005 (relative guard only in adjust_level), server.py:881-896/948-960 (ramp_ms 0..60000, force passthrough), policy.py:238-247 (dca → ch_fader_max_db), device.yaml:648 (main grp/dca). Docs establishing this is by design: BRIEF.md:3-7, README.md:487,515,575-580,633-636, DESIGN.md:450, tests/integration/test_server_tools.py:201-203,370. Notes JSON: $SP/reports/verify-bypass-4-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Keep the M5 usability decision (absolute set from silence works) but re-add code-enforced bounds that do not depend on the model's honesty: (1) make show_mode(False) a Tier-2 confirmation (turning it ON stays free) and drop the 'until show_mode(false)' hint from the SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS text; (2) in show mode ignore force (or require a token for force) — an explicit-intent override should not be a free boolean during a performance; (3) for absolute set_fader/set_send impose a mandatory minimum ramp (e.g. ≥300 ms, ≥1 s in show mode) whenever the upward move exceeds the relative limit, rather than allowing ramp_ms=0 — this preserves 'kick in Tony's ears from -84 dB' while removing the one-datagram slam; (4) give DCA its own ceiling (dca_fader_max_db: 0) since it is a group offset stacked on member faders, and treat a DCA as guarded when any /main/*/grp/dca bit names it (read once at connect / after scene recall); (5) optionally a per-second aggregate 'level-up budget' in Policy (sum of positive dB deltas across all strips) so a runaway loop is throttled by something other than the 50 writes/s bucket.

> **Verifier 2: PARTIAL (medium)** — Every MECHANISM in the claim is real and I reproduced each one end to end on the FakeDesk (worktree verify-bypass-4-src, tests/integration/test_verify_bypass4.py, 5 passed). But three of the four sub-claims describe behaviour that is the current, explicitly documented design, not a defeated guarantee, so the 'high = defeats a documented guarantee' framing does not hold; what survives is a medium design weakness plus two genuine inconsistencies.

(a) show_mode(False) unguarded — TRUE: server.py:1270-1282 sets a.policy.show_mode with no guard; DESIGN §19 fixes the signature as show_mode(on: bool) with no confirm_token; README:515 lists it T0 and README:636 says 'Switch it on at doors, off for changeovers' (i.e. the model is the intended actor). No document promises that show mode resists the model; README:206/633-636 and policy.py:27-30 frame it as accident protection. So no documented guarantee is defeated. Note also that show_mode(False) recalls nothing by itself: recall_scene still runs the Tier-2 dance (server.py:1130 check, then _confirm), which a jailbroken client can self-serve exactly as easily — making show_mode Tier-2 adds a round trip, not a barrier, under that threat model. The one genuinely bad detail I found: the SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS text (policy.py:231-235) reads 'refused until show_mode(false)', i.e. the refusal names its own off-switch to a confused client. Real desk: identical (pure server-side state).

(b) force=True honoured on the model's say-so — TRUE: policy.py:290-295 returns before the limit when force, also in show mode; this is a recorded DECISION (DESIGN §10 'force bypasses relative limit even in show mode', README:580-583, 635). It is enforcement-by-instruction (server.py:117-118 instructions string), which sits awkwardly with 'enforced in code, not by prompting', but given (c) it adds no capability: adjust_fader(+60, force) == set_fader(+5). Destination is still ceiling-bounded (+5 ch / 0 bus/send).

(c) set_fader/set_send have no magnitude guard and ramp_ms=0 → one datagram — TRUE: desk.py:958-964 removed check_relative from _move_level deliberately after M5 defect #3 (HANDOVER §4a 'The ±6 dB guard applied to absolute moves, so a send could never come up from off'; README:265-269, 487, 575-579 document 'The move size is not limited — the ceiling bounds it'); ramp_steps(ms<=0) → [to_db] (policy.py:439-440); server accepts ramp_ms 0..60000 (server.py:888). The 'documented guarantee' the claimant cites (BRIEF §4 'single-call relative change ≤ ±6 dB') is the superseded original brief; the live README says the opposite. Genuine residue: the MCP instructions string still tells the model set_fader/set_send are 'limited to +-6 dB per call' (server.py:114-118) — stale vs code. ramp_ms=0 is not materially more dangerous to hearing than the 300 ms default glide to the same ceiling; the safety bound the owner chose is the ceiling. Real desk: identical (X32 applies a fader float immediately).

(d) DCA — TRUE and this is the part with real substance: policy.py:252 gives dca the CHANNEL ceiling (+5) while device.yaml:274 declares dca fader clamp {max: 0} 'group master; 0 dB ceiling' — descriptor clamps are never consulted by Policy.clamp_level (descriptor.py:15-18 says policy should report them; it does not), so the descriptor author's 0 dB intent is dead config (README:573 meanwhile says DCA ≤ +5). Effect: per-strip ceilings stack through a DCA (member ch at +5 + DCA at +5 = +10 dB effective; bus 0 + DCA +5 = +5), i.e. a clamp CAN be widened by argument choice when a DCA exists. DCA→Main: /main/st/grp/dca and /main/m/grp/dca exist (research scales_params.md:608/619, fx_routing_scenes.md:480-488 VERIFIED, FW 4.06+; Jim's desk is 4.13), so on a real desk a DCA that contains Main LR moves the main output on a Tier-1 set_fader('dca.N') with no token. Preconditions: the operator must already have assigned Main to a DCA — the server cannot do it on a Tier-1 path (/*/grp/* is guarded; verified GUARDED in test D) — and FakeDesk does not model DCA gain coupling, so the audible effect is inference, not reproduction. That is 'unusual but reachable' → medium, not critical.

Throughput: worse than claimed — the token bucket starts full (capacity = 50), so 56 full-scale unramped slams (32 ch → +5, 16 bus → 0, 8 DCA → +5) all succeeded in 0.14 s with zero RATE_LIMITED (test E), not '~1 s'.

Net: CONFIRMED mechanics, REFUTED 'defeats documented guarantee' for (a)(b)(c) (they are the documented design; only BRIEF §4 and the instructions string are stale), CONFIRMED-with-preconditions for (d). Overall PARTIAL, severity medium: the guardrail against a confused/runaway client for level moves is the family ceiling alone, DCA stacking widens that ceiling, a DCA containing Main is an unconfirmed main-level path on real hardware, and the show-mode refusal text advertises its own bypass.
>
> *Evidence:* Ran $SP/runtests.sh <wt> tests/integration/test_verify_bypass4.py -s → 5 passed (commit 1a568f7 on wt/verify-bypass-4-src). Output: A: 'show_mode(False) unguarded; SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS message = show mode is on: scene_recall is refused until show_mode(false)'; recall_scene then needed and accepted a fresh token. B: show mode ON, adjust_fader('ch.1', +4, ramp_ms=0) → RELATIVE_TOO_LARGE, adjust_fader('ch.1', +95, ramp_ms=0, force=True) → 'Ch 1 fader -oo dB → +5.0 dB (+95.0 dB)', steps=1, desk.write_count delta 1, wire value 5.0. C (show mode ON, no force): set_fader ch.2 -oo→+5.0 (clamped, req +10) steps=1/1 write; ch.3 −10.0→+5.0 steps=1; bus.1 -oo→0.0; mtx.1 -oo→0.0; set_send ch.1→bus1 -oo→0.0; dca.1 -oo→+5.0 '[descriptor clamp_max=0.0]'; fakedesk /dca/1/fader == 5.0; set_fader('main.st') → GUARDED. D: policy.tier_for('/main/st/grp/dca') == 2, desk._write_value(main.st, 'grp/dca') raises GUARDED. E: '56 full-scale slams: ok=56 rate_limited=0 in 0.14 s; channels now at +5: 32/32'. Code refs: server.py:1270-1282 (show_mode, no guard), policy.py:231-235 (message names show_mode(false)), policy.py:290-295 (force early-return), desk.py:958-966 (no relative guard on absolute moves, ramp_ms 0..60000), policy.py:439-440 (ms<=0 → [end]), policy.py:243-252 (dca → ch_fader_max_db), device.yaml:274 (dca fader clamp max 0, unused), device.yaml:718 + research fx_routing_scenes.md:480-488 (/main/st/grp/dca exists, guarded), README:487,515,573-583,633-636 and HANDOVER §4a defect 3 (all of a/b/c documented as intended), server.py:114-118 (instructions still claim ±6 dB on set_fader/set_send — stale). DCA→Main audible coupling NOT reproducible on FakeDesk (no DCA gain model); real X32 FW ≥4.06 would honour it if the operator assigned Main to that DCA.
>
> *Better fix:* Respect the post-M5 'bring up from off' decision rather than re-imposing ±6 dB on absolute moves: (1) Policy.level_ceiling_db should take min(family ceiling, ParamSpec.clamp_max) so device.yaml's dca fader max 0 (and any future per-param clamp) is actually enforced — today descriptor clamps are dead config; give DCA 0 dB. (2) In set_level for family dca, read /main/st/grp/dca and /main/m/grp/dca (cached) and treat an upward DCA move whose bit is set there as GUARDED (route through the set_main_fader dance). (3) Asymmetric slam guard on absolute upward moves only: if before_db is finite and > −40 dB (strip in use) and after−before > +12 dB, require force; if before ≤ −40 dB (bring-up-from-off) allow it but impose a minimum ramp (ignore ramp_ms < 300 for upward jumps > 12 dB). This keeps 'kick in Tony's ears at −12' working and catches '−10 → +5 in one datagram'. (4) Reword SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS to 'ask the operator to end show mode' instead of naming the tool; optionally make show_mode(False) Tier-2 (on stays free) — cheap, and it at least forces a visible round trip for a confused client, though it is no barrier to a jailbroken one. (5) Separate bucket capacity from refill rate (e.g. capacity 10, 50/s) so a runaway loop cannot land 50 full-scale writes in 100 ms. (6) Fix the stale text: BRIEF §4 and the MCP instructions string (server.py:114-118) still promise ±6 dB per call on set_fader/set_send.

## D23. [medium] restore_snapshot's confirmation summary hides the dangerous part and reads in the wrong direction; the token binds the snapshot id, not the plan
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1248-1257 (preview = describe_changes(changes[:15]), payload = {id, scope}), src/x32mcp/server.py:1210-1212 (_diff = diff_states(snap.state, live) → before=snapshot, after=live), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:1125-1160 (diff order = node sweep order: ch first, main/headamp/config last), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1312-1341 (restore writes main mix, headamp gain+phantom, routing, inserts, mute groups with no tier checks)

**Mechanism.** The Tier-2 gate is only as good as the summary the human sees. For a whole-desk restore the preview lists the first 15 changes in sweep order — always channel names/faders — so 'Main LR fader -40 → 0 dB', 'Main LR unmuted', 128 head-amp gain/phantom lines, output routing and insert changes never appear, only a count. Each line is also rendered snapshot→live ('Ch 1 fader 0.0 dB → −10.0 dB') i.e. the opposite of what the restore will do, so a main-level line, if it were shown, would read as a cut when it is a +40 dB raise. The token payload is {id, scope}: if the snapshot file is edited between the two calls (client with a filesystem tool, or another process) the confirmed token executes different lines, and restore lines can carry any descriptor node incl. Tier-2 params.

**Exploit.** snapshot_desk → main fader drifts to -40, 32 channel names/faders drift → restore_snapshot(id) → summary: '88 setting(s) … would change: - Ch 1 name … - Ch 1 fader 0.0 dB → −10.0 dB … (15 lines)' with no mention of Main LR/Bus; confirm → main jumps -40 → 0 dB unramped (single '/' line) and every output mix/on is rewritten.

**Evidence.** test_exploit_panic_defeated_by_inflight_restore prints the pending envelope: 'RESTORE PREVIEW: count 88 sections 88 | mentions Main LR: False | mentions Bus: False' followed by 15 channel lines in snapshot→live direction; after confirmation the fake's main fader went -40 → 0.0 dB.

**Proposed fix.** Build the preview from the restore plan, not the diff: sort by hazard (main/mtx/bus mix, headamp, config/routing, inserts, sources first), render live → snapshot, always list every main/headamp/routing change explicitly plus per-family counts, and flag upward level moves > 6 dB; bind the token to a hash of the rendered plan lines (recompute on the second call → BAD_TOKEN if the file or scope resolution changed); optionally ramp main/bus fader lines instead of a single '/' write.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced all three parts independently in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-bypass-6-repro (commit 63dd0a7, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass6.py, 2 passed) against the FakeDesk through the real server tools.

(1) Preview hides the hazardous lines. server.py:1248-1250 builds preview = describe_changes(changes[:15]) where changes come from diff_states() in descriptor sweep order (nodes.py:1125-1147: d.nodes() iterates ch first; main/headamp/config last). After snapshot_desk, dropping Main LR to -40 dB and muting it, changing headamp/000 gain+phantom, bus 1 fader, and 32 channel names/faders, restore_snapshot(id) returned action_summary '69 setting(s) in 67 section(s) would change:' followed by 15 lines that are exclusively Ch 1..Ch 8 name/fader, then '… 54 more'. 'Main', 'Bus' and headamp never appear in the text the human is asked to approve.

(2) Direction is backwards. server.py:1210-1212 _diff = diff_states(snap.state, live) so before=snapshot, after=live; the rendered line was "Ch 1 'drift1' fader 0.0 dB → −10.0 dB" while the restore actually moves the fader −10 → 0. A main line, had it been shown, would read '0.0 dB → −40 dB' for what is a +40 dB raise.

(3) After confirming, Desk.restore (desk.py:1312-1341) wrote 67 conn.slash lines with no clamp/tier checks; the fake's Main LR went −40 dB muted → 0.0 dB unmuted via ONE node line '/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0' (unramped level+unmute in a single write).

(4) Token binds {id, scope}, not the plan (server.py:1251 payload). With a token minted for a preview of exactly '1 setting(s) in 1 section(s): Ch 1 name', editing the snapshot JSON on disk between the two calls (SnapshotStore.load re-reads the file, nodes.py:848-870) and then confirming executed ['/ch/01/config "Ch01" 1 RD 1', '/main/st/mix ON +10.0 +0', '/headamp/000 +60.0 ON']: Main LR to +10 dB (above policy main_fader_max_db=0, device.yaml:726 — restore lines are never clamped), head-amp 000 gain 24 → 60 dB and phantom switched ON, none of which was summarised. policy.guard's docstring promise (policy.py:350-352, 'cannot execute anything but what was summarised') does not hold for restore_snapshot.

Nuance: the claim's 'every output mix/on is rewritten' is scenario-dependent — restore_plan (nodes.py:1200-1226) only writes sections that differ (67 lines here); it is true only when all outputs differ (e.g. after panic). Line references in the claim are accurate.

Severity kept at medium: the whole-desk restore is still behind a Tier-2 confirmation and the count/'… N more' is shown, so this weakens rather than removes the gate in ordinary use; the file-edit TOCTOU variant executes an unsummarised, unclamped main/headamp change under a valid token (arguably high), but it needs out-of-band write access to the snapshot dir between the two calls — unusual but reachable for an LLM client that also holds a filesystem tool.
>
> *Evidence:* Test output (runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass6.py -s): 
test_preview_hides_main_and_reads_backwards -> 'RESTORE PREVIEW: count 69 sections 67 | mentions Main: False | mentions Bus: False | mentions head amp/Headamp: False'; preview lines '- Ch 1 'drift1' name 'Ch01' → 'drift1'', '- Ch 1 'drift1' fader 0.0 dB → −10.0 dB' … Ch 8, '… 54 more'; after confirm: 'AFTER RESTORE main fader: -0.0098 main on: True', 'MAIN LINES WRITTEN: ['/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0']', 'HEADAMP LINES: ['/headamp/000 +24.0 OFF']', 'TOTAL LINES: 67' (fake main had been set to -40 dB, mix/on False before the restore).
test_token_binds_id_not_plan_file_edit_between_calls -> 'PREVIEW: … 1 setting(s) in 1 section(s) would change: - Ch 1 'drift' name 'Ch01' → 'drift''; snapshot file edited (/main/st/mix mix/fader=10.0, /headamp/000 gain=60 phantom=true); confirm with the same token -> 'RESULT: Restored snapshot …: 3/3 section(s) written', 'MAIN: -0.0098 -> 10.0 | headamp 000 gain: 60.0 phantom: True', 'LINES: ['/ch/01/config "Ch01" 1 RD 1', '/main/st/mix ON +10.0 +0', '/headamp/000 +60.0 ON']'. Code: server.py:1210-1212 (_diff before=snap after=live), server.py:1248-1257 (preview=changes[:15], payload={id,scope}), nodes.py:1125-1160 (sweep-order diff, describe_changes), nodes.py:848-870 (load re-reads file), desk.py:1312-1341 (restore writes plan lines via conn.slash, no clamps/tier checks), device.yaml:726 main_fader_max_db: 0. Files: <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-6-repro/tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_bypass6.py; notes at $SP/reports/verify-bypass-6-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Compute the restore plan once on the first call (restore_plan(snap.state, live)), render it live → snapshot, hazard-sorted (main/mtx/bus mix, headamp gain/phantom, config/routing/inserts/sources first, each always listed explicitly with per-family counts, upward level moves > relative_max_db flagged), and bind the token to a SHA-256 of the rendered plan lines plus snap.path mtime/size; on the second call recompute and raise BAD_TOKEN on mismatch. Additionally pass main/bus/mtx fader values in restore lines through policy.clamp_level (or ramp them via Desk.ramp instead of a single '/' line) so an edited or corrupt snapshot cannot exceed main_fader_max_db, and refuse headamp gain increases / phantom-on in a restore unless scope names headamp explicitly.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain verified end to end, no gap:
(1) Preview content/order: server.py:1247-1250 computes changes = diff_states(snap.state, live) (server.py:1210-1212) and preview = describe_changes(changes[:15]). diff_states (nodes.py:1125-1147) iterates d.nodes() in sweep order, which device.yaml:578-660 defines as ch (config, delay, preamp, gate, dyn, insert, eq, mix, sends, grp) -> auxin -> fxrtn -> bus -> mtx -> main -> dca -> headamp -> fx -> config(routing/mute groups/links). describe_changes (nodes.py:1150-1157) only regroups by strip in first-seen order, so the slice keeps ch.1..ch.N. Any whole-desk restore with >=15 differing channel leaves (the normal case against the session-start auto-snapshot) therefore never lists Main LR/M, bus/mtx masters, head-amp gain/phantom, routing, inserts or mute groups; only the total count hints at them.
(2) Direction: diff_states(a=snapshot, b=live) builds Change(before=snapshot, after=live) and _describe renders 'before → after' (nodes.py:1068-1108). The restore summary says 'N setting(s) would change:' followed by those lines, so 'Ch 1 fader 0.0 dB → −10.0 dB' reads as the restore lowering ch1 when it will raise it to 0.0. This rendering is correct for diff_snapshot ('changes since snapshot') but inverted for restore, which reuses it unchanged.
(3) Execution: Desk.restore (desk.py:1312-1341) re-dumps live, builds restore_plan(snap.state, live) (nodes.py:1201-1227; families incl. main, headamp, config; only show/stat/prefs/action excluded, nodes.py:111) and writes each section as one '/' node line via conn.slash under the rate limiter only: no tier check, no clamp_level (main_fader_max_db 0 not applied), no ramp. transport.md §6.6 (CONFIRMED) says a real X32 applies '/ ,s "/main/st/mix ON +0.0 +0"' immediately in engineering units, and HANDOVER M5 verified restore '/' writes on hardware, so a real desk behaves exactly like the FakeDesk here: main jumps in one step and output mix/on is rewritten (un-muting a panic).
(4) Token binding: payload = {"id": snap.id, "scope": scope} (server.py:1251); Policy.guard (policy.py:347-360) compares only that dict. SnapshotStore.load re-reads the JSON from disk on every call with no content hash (nodes.py:829-870), and Desk.restore recomputes the plan from the desk at execute time, so within the 300 s TTL (device.yaml:738) both a snapshot-file edit and desk drift change what the confirmed token executes; the second call does not even compare its freshly computed count against the previewed one. Counter-check in the code's favour: id='latest' is resolved to snap.id before binding, so a newer snapshot between calls correctly yields BAD_TOKEN (verified).
Severity stays medium: the Tier-2 gate itself holds (confirmation required, single-use, action+id+scope bound, show-mode check), so this is not an unconfirmed Tier-2 action; but the human-facing summary systematically hides the highest-hazard lines and inverts the arrows under ordinary conditions, and the executed plan is not what was previewed when the file or desk changes inside the TTL. The file-edit vector needs write access to ./snapshots (an LLM client that also has a filesystem MCP server, or another process) — reachable but not default. Combined with restore bypassing clamps/ramps, a crafted or stale snapshot can produce an unramped main raise (demonstrated +10 dB, above the policy ceiling) behind a preview that shows only channel names.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-bypass-6-src, commit dba3676, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify6.py — 4 passed (runtests.sh, FakeDesk via in-memory UDP shim).
test_preview_hides_main_and_reads_snapshot_to_live: snapshot at main 0 dB; then on the fake surface main.st fader -> -40 dB and mix/on -> off, headamp/000 phantom -> on, headamp/001 gain -> 40, ch1-16 renamed + faders -10. restore_snapshot(id) pending envelope: count 36, sections 35; action_summary = 'Restore snapshot …-t0 (t0) … (whole desk): 36 setting(s) in 35 section(s) would change:' + 15 lines all of the form "- Ch 1 'X1' name 'Ch01' → 'X1'" / "- Ch 1 'X1' fader 0.0 dB → −10.0 dB" … "- Ch 8 'X8' name 'Ch08' → 'X8'" + '… 21 more'. 'Main LR' in summary: False; 'phantom'/'Head' in summary: False. Arrows read snapshot→live. After confirming with the token: fake main/st/mix/fader -40 → -0.01 dB (one '/' line, no ramp), main/st/mix/on False → True, headamp/000 phantom True → False, headamp/001 gain 40 → 24.
test_token_binds_id_not_content_file_edit_between_calls: preview count 1 (ch1 name only, no 'Main'); edited <snapshot>.json state.sections['/main/st/mix']['mix/fader'] 0.0 → 10.0 between calls; same token accepted; result '2/2 section(s) written'; fake main fader = +10.0 dB (policy main_fader_max_db is 0; restore applies no clamp).
test_desk_drift_between_calls_executes_different_plan: preview count 1; main pulled to -40 on the surface after the preview; confirm wrote 2 lines and main went back to 0.0 dB — a line the human never saw.
test_latest_alias_rebinds_on_new_snapshot (counter-check): token minted for restore_snapshot('latest'), new snapshot taken, second call → BAD_TOKEN (payload carries resolved snap.id) — this part of the gate is sound.
Code refs: server.py:1210-1212 (_diff = diff_states(snap.state, live)), server.py:1247-1257 (changes[:15] preview, payload {id, scope}, _confirm), nodes.py:1125-1157 (diff order = d.nodes() sweep; describe_changes groups by first-seen strip), device.yaml:578-660 (sweep order ch … main … headamp … config), nodes.py:1068-1108 (label 'before → after'), desk.py:1312-1341 (restore: restore_plan + conn.slash per line, acquire_write only), nodes.py:111 & 1201-1227 (restore includes main/headamp/config families), nodes.py:829-870 (snapshot load re-reads file, no integrity hash), policy.py:347-360 (guard compares payload dict only), device.yaml:738 (TTL 300 s), docs/research/transport.md §6.6 + HANDOVER.md:116 (real desk applies '/' node writes immediately; restore verified on hardware).
>
> *Better fix:* Build the confirmation from the restore plan, not the diff, and bind to it: (a) in restore_snapshot compute lines = restore_plan(snap.state, live) once, render each as live → snapshot English, sort hazard-first (main/mtx/bus mix on+fader, dca, headamp gain/phantom, config routing/links/mute groups, inserts, sources, then channel mix, then the rest), always enumerate every main/bus/mtx/dca/headamp/config line regardless of the 15-line cap, add per-family counts and a header flag for any output un-mute or upward fader/gain move > 6 dB and any value above the policy ceilings; (b) payload = {id, scope, plan_sha256: sha256('\n'.join(lines))} and pass the precomputed lines into Desk.restore so the executed plan is byte-identical to the confirmed one; on the second call recompute and return PLAN_CHANGED (re-preview) if the digest differs (desk drift or file edit) instead of silently executing more lines; (c) store a content sha256 in the snapshot envelope at save and verify on load (tamper/torn-file detection); (d) in Desk.restore route main/bus/mtx/dca 'mix' sections through the existing ramp + clamp_level path (or at minimum clamp mix/fader to the policy ceiling and refuse > ceiling without an explicit force flag) rather than a single '/' line, and write output mix/on last so a restore cannot un-panic before levels are sane.

## D24. [medium] ring_out's token does not bind what the human was asked to confirm (the open-mic list / preflight state)
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1754-1773 (payload = bus,target,step,dwell,budget,patch_file; summary lists pf.included_mics), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:637-690 (ring_out re-runs preflight and uses whatever it finds), src/x32mcp/policy.py:347-362 (guard compares payload only)

**Mechanism.** The Tier-2 summary says 'open mics: Ch 1, Ch 2 … Nobody may play' and BRIEF §5 makes confirming the open-mic list the purpose of the gate, but the minted payload omits the mic list, GEQ slot/side and master level. On the token call preflight runs again and the session arms on the current routing, so a mic opened/routed after the summary (or a different GEQ insert) is rung out under a confirmation that never mentioned it. The 300 s TTL widens the window.

**Exploit.** ring_out(1, …) → summary 'open mics: Ch 1, Ch 2' + token → ch 3's send to bus 1 is opened → ring_out(1, …, confirm_token) runs with included mics [1,2,3].

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_bypass.py::test_ring_out_token_does_not_bind_the_confirmed_mic_list — 'TOKEN-10: confirmed mics [1, 2]; the run used [1, 2, 3] -> DONE'.

**Proposed fix.** Include pf.included channel list, geq.sel/fx_slot/side and round(master_db,1) in the ring_out (and ring_out_system per-stage) payload so any change between summary and execution yields BAD_TOKEN and a fresh summary.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-bypass-7-repro (commit 7680936), file tests/integration/test_verify_bypass7.py, 7/7 pass against unmodified product code.

Mechanism confirmed exactly as claimed. server.py:1754-1757 builds the ring_out payload from arguments only {bus, target_gain_db, step_db, dwell_ms, notch_budget, patch_file}; the summary at 1759-1765 additionally renders pf.master, pf.geq.insert.sel and pf.included_mics, and _confirm() at 1771 hands only the payload to policy.guard (policy.py:347-362), which compares action+payload. On the token call server.ring_out re-runs preflight (1752) and then cfs.ring_out re-runs it again (cfs.py:658) and arms on whatever the desk shows now. device.yaml:738 sets confirm_token_ttl_s=300 (BRIEF §4 says 60 s), so the human's 'open mics: Ch 1, Ch 2' confirmation stays redeemable for 5 minutes against any routing.

What I executed (FakeDesk + App + SyntheticRta, setup_ringout_eqs([1,2]) FX5L/FX5R, ch1/ch2 -> bus1 at -20, bus1 master -20):
 A) ring_out(1,-19,dwell 10) -> summary 'open mics: Ch 1, Ch 2', preflight.included=[1,2]; open ch3 send to bus1 (-10 dB); same-args token call -> ok, DONE, report preflight.included=[1,2,3]. Exactly the claimed exploit.
 B) Stronger: after the summary drop ch1/ch2 sends to -90 and open ch5 at 0 dB -> token call runs DONE with included=[5]; none of the confirmed mics were in the run.
 C) Master not bound: summary 'from -20.0 dB to -19.0 dB'; move bus1 master to -30 -> token redeems, run climbs -30 -> -19 (11 x 1 dB steps instead of the 1 step shown). Still capped at the confirmed target/ceiling, so bounded.
 D) GEQ not bound: summary 'on GEQ FX5L'; load GEQ2 into FX6 and repoint bus1 insert to FX6L -> validate ok, token redeems, session arms on FX 6A (report geq={fx_slot:6, sel:FX6L}).
 D2) Boundary: a change that makes preflight FAIL (insert -> FX5R, shared with bus 2) is refused PREFLIGHT_FAILED - but because server.ring_out runs preflight BEFORE policy.guard, the token is NOT consumed; after putting the insert back (and opening ch4) the same token still redeems and runs with included=[1,2,4].
 E) Control: changing a bound argument (notch_budget 6->7) with the token -> BAD_TOKEN, so argument binding itself is intact.
 F) ring_out_system: plan stage 'mics':[1,2] IS in the payload, but cfs.py:680-684 only warns about MISSING expected mics; an extra ch3 opened after the summary runs with included=[1,2,3] and no warning.

Severity: I agree with MEDIUM, not high. The documented token guarantees (single-use, action binding, argument/payload binding, TTL) all hold; what is unbound is observed desk state, which is a property shared by every Tier-2 tool here (tokens bind the request, not the world). The level trajectory stays bounded by the confirmed target and the descriptor ceiling, cuts-only/-9 dB/-3 dB step/budget are unaffected, the second preflight still enforces every blocker (muted bus, -oo master, no mics, invalid/shared GEQ), and in D the 'other' GEQ is still the one genuinely inserted on the confirmed bus. The weakened guarantee is the tool's own docstring/BRIEF §5 purpose ('call again ... after the user has confirmed the open-mic list'): within a 300 s window a mic (or playback channel with a preamp source) routed to the bus after the summary is driven up to the target with the master, under a confirmation that never named it. Reachable by a confused LLM (ring_out -> set_send -> ring_out with token) or by ordinary stage activity during the TTL. Note the proposed fix only narrows the window to the run itself: nothing re-checks routing during the multi-minute climb either.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-7-repro, commit 7680936, tests/integration/test_verify_bypass7.py — `runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_verify_bypass7.py -s` -> 7 passed. Printed results: RESULT-A: {'ok': True, 'final_stage': 'DONE', 'aborted': False} confirmed mics [1, 2]; run used [1, 2, 3] | RESULT-B: {'ok': True, 'final_stage': 'DONE'} confirmed mics [1, 2]; run used [5] | RESULT-C: {'ok': True, 'final_stage': 'DONE', 'start_master_db': -30.0, 'max_master_db': -19.0, 'end_master_db': -22.0} (summary had said 'from −20.0 dB') | VALIDATE-D: {'ok': True, 'insert': {'sel': 'FX6L', 'fx_slot': 6, 'side': 'A', 'fx_type': 'GEQ2'}} RESULT-D: {'ok': True, 'final_stage': 'DONE', 'geq': {'fx_slot': 6, 'side': 'A', 'sel': 'FX6L'}} (summary had said 'on GEQ FX5L') | RESULT-D2: insert->FX5R gives PREFLIGHT_FAILED, token survives (preflight at server.py:1752 precedes guard at 1771), later redeems with included [1, 2, 4] | test_E: notch_budget changed -> BAD_TOKEN (argument binding intact) | RESULT-F (ring_out_system, plan mics [1,2]): {'final_stage': 'DONE', 'warnings': None} included [1, 2, 3]. Code refs: src/x32mcp/server.py:1752 (first preflight), 1754-1757 (payload w/o mics/geq/master), 1759-1765 (summary renders pf.master, ins.sel, pf.included_mics), 1771 (_confirm), 331-345 (_confirm -> policy.guard); src/x32mcp/policy.py:347-362 (guard compares dict(payload) only); src/x32mcp/cfs.py:658 (ring_out re-runs preflight and arms on current state), 680-684 (_expected_mics -> warning for missing only); device.yaml:738 confirm_token_ttl_s: 300.
>
> *Better fix:* Bind a digest of the preflight facts the summary shows into the payload on BOTH calls: payload['preflight'] = {'included': [m.ch for m in pf.included_mics], 'geq': (ins.fx_slot, ins.side, ins.sel), 'master_db': round(pf.master_db, 1), 'muted': pf.muted}. Since server.ring_out already recomputes pf before _confirm on the token call, any drift yields BAD_TOKEN + a fresh summary for free. Then pass that pf (or the included list as a hard _expected_mics that BLOCKS on extra/missing mics rather than warns) into cfs.ring_out so the third preflight at cfs.py:658 cannot diverge from the confirmed one; do the same per stage in ring_out_system/cfs.ring_out_system (treat plan 'mics' as binding: refuse the stage if included != mics). Also move the token consumption ahead of, or make it independent of, the PREFLIGHT_FAILED path only if you want failed attempts to burn the token (D2); and consider restoring the BRIEF's 60 s TTL for ring_out specifically. Residual: routing changes during the run itself remain undetected; a cheap mitigation is to re-run discover_mics at each RAISE step and abort on a new included channel.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap:
1. server.py ring_out (lines ~1745-1771): on EVERY call (with or without token) it re-runs `_prov.preflight()`, then builds `payload = {bus, target_gain_db, step_db, dwell_ms, notch_budget, patch_file}` and a `summary` that additionally embeds pf.master (start level), the GEQ insert `ins.sel` (e.g. FX5L) and `pf.included_mics`. Only `payload` goes to `policy.guard`.
2. policy.py guard (347-362) / consume_token (330-346): redeems on `stored == dict(payload)` and matching action; the stored summary is never compared. So the token binds the call ARGUMENTS, not the desk-derived state the human was shown. guard's own docstring promises the token "cannot execute anything but what was summarised" — for ring_out that promise is only true for the argument part of the summary.
3. After redemption, cfs.py ring_out (637-690) runs preflight a THIRD time and `_open_session(...)` arms on whatever mics/GEQ/master it finds now; `_expected_mics` is only passed by ring_out_system and only produces a warning for MISSING planned mics, never for extra ones (688-692).
4. The change between summary and redeem is reachable by the stated threat model without any human or front-panel action: set_send is Tier 1, unconfirmed, and explicitly unlimited in move size (server.py ~948 docstring), unmute is Tier 1; no CFS/Desk lock prevents routing writes to the bus under test before (or during) a run. TTL is 300 s (device.yaml:738).
5. Real desk vs FakeDesk: nothing here depends on the fake. The LLM path writes through Desk (cache invalidated on our own writes, desk.py:17-18); a front-panel change on a real X32 is pushed via /xremote and drops the cache section, and read_cache_ttl_s=2 s is far below any human confirmation round trip, so the re-run preflight on a real console sees the new routing exactly as the fake does. The run completing to DONE needs the synthetic RTA, but the token acceptance and arming do not.
What it does NOT do (why it is not high): every WRITE the token authorises stays fully bound and bounded — bus, target (min(target_gain_db, fixed descriptor ceiling 0 dB)), step (≤ relative limit), dwell, budget; cuts-only/−9 dB/−3 dB/budget are untouched; master raised above target between summary and redeem is refused BAD_ARGUMENT (cfs.py 668-675, reproduced); a swapped GEQ insert is re-validated (bus-under-test only, not shared/stereo) before it is cut, so no wrong-strip write. The mic list is never an input to any write; it only describes the acoustic loop, which a token can never bind anyway (a mic can be walked in front of the wedge, or set_send called 1 s after redeem). So the defect is consent-integrity of the one human gate on the autonomous ring-out (the tool docstring literally says "call again … after the user has confirmed the open-mic list"), not a level/hearing hazard: medium at the low end, matching the rubric's "guarantee weakened under reachable conditions". Two aggravating details the claim did not mention: (a) the GEQ actually cut can differ from the one named (test B: summary said FX5L, cuts went to FX6 side A) — a different write destination than summarised, and setup_ringout_eqs (server.py ~1594) shows the codebase does bind derived write targets elsewhere; (b) ring_out_system binds plan.mics in its payload yet still rings out extra mics silently (test E: plan mics [1,2] → included [1,2,3], warnings []). Also minor: on the token call a now-failing preflight raises PREFLIGHT_FAILED before guard, so the token is not burnt and stays redeemable for the rest of its 300 s.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-7-src, commit 790dca0, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify7.py — 5 passed (runtests.sh … -s):
A (LLM-reachable path, Tier-1 tools only): ring_out(1,target −29,step 1,dwell 10) → pending, preflight.included == [1,2], summary "open mics: Ch 1 'Ch01', Ch 2 'Ch02'"; then srv.set_send("ch.3",1,−10,ramp_ms=0) ok + srv.unmute("ch.3") ok (no token); ring_out(…,confirm_token) → ok, report preflight.included == [1,2,3], final_stage DONE. Output: "A: token minted for mics [1, 2] -> run: True [1, 2, 3] DONE None".
B (GEQ insert swapped FX5L→FX6L holding GEQ2, front panel): validate ok {'sel':'FX6L','fx_slot':6,'side':'A'}; token minted with summary "on GEQ FX5L" redeems → ok, report geq {'fx_slot': 6, 'side': 'A', 'sel': 'FX6L'}, DONE.
C (master raised −30→−22 after summary "from −30.0 dB to −20.0 dB"): redeems, start_master_db −22.0, max −20.0, end −23.0 (target still bound); C2 master raised to −15 (above target) → BAD_ARGUMENT (refused).
D (control): changing target_gain_db or notch_budget on the token call → BAD_TOKEN (argument binding intact).
E (ring_out_system plan with mics [1,2], then set_send ch.3→bus1): redeems, stage preflight.included [1,2,3], warnings [] — plan mics are in the payload but extras are neither refused nor warned.
Code refs: server.py 1755 (preflight re-run before _confirm), 1760-1763 payload, 1764-1770 summary incl. pf.master/ins.sel/included_mics, 1771 _confirm; policy.py 347-362 guard compares payload only; cfs.py 665 third preflight, 686-692 expected_mics warns on missing only; device.yaml 738 confirm_token_ttl_s: 300; server.py ~948 set_send Tier 1 "size of the move is NOT limited".
>
> *Better fix:* Bind the desk-derived facts that the summary asserts and that determine where/what the run touches: add to the ring_out payload `included_mics: [m.ch …]`, `geq: {fx_slot, side, sel}` and `start_master_db: round(pf.master_db,1)` (server.py ~1760); since preflight is already re-run on the token call, any drift then yields BAD_TOKEN + a fresh summary at zero extra cost. For ring_out_system, compute per-stage preflights on the first call and bind the same triple per stage (or at minimum make cfs.ring_out treat mics present-but-not-in `_expected_mics` as a warning/abort, cfs.py 688-692, so extras are surfaced, not just missing ones). To cover the run window (which payload binding cannot), have CfsController refuse/abort when discover_mics for the armed bus changes mid-session, or have Desk reject Tier-1 send/mute writes that target the bus under test while a RINGOUT session is armed. Optionally consume the token before the PREFLIGHT_FAILED raise so a failed attempt burns it.

## D25. [medium] ring_out_system stage with target_gain_db=NaN passes the token check (NaN→None) and wedges CFS² permanently: session left armed in RINGOUT after the tool errors; stop() cannot clear it
*Angle:* tokens · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1806-1822 (stage normalisation validates notch_budget/dwell/step but not target_gain_db), server.py:247-257 (_jsonable NaN→None so {'target_gain_db': NaN} == {'target_gain_db': None} in the payload), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:675 (target = min(nan, ceiling) = nan; 676 'target <= master' is False), cfs.py:694-699 (the try/except that resets self._ses covers _snapshot_stage/_stage/_arm but NOT the log.info at 698-699 whose format_db(ses.target_db) raises ScaleError), cfs.py:587-613 (stop() waits _STOP_WAIT_S=30 s for ses.done that nobody will set and never clears self._ses), scales.py:277

**Mechanism.** NaN reaches the tool (mcp 2.2 stdio: jsonrpc_message_adapter.validate_json parses NaN literals — verified). The payload maps NaN to None, so a NaN plan redeems a token minted for the None/default plan (and vice versa). cfs.ring_out opens the session, snapshots, publishes ARM, arms the RTA/detector, leaves the lock, then log.info(...format_db(nan)...) raises ScaleError outside any handler: self._ses stays set with mode RINGOUT/stage ARM, no _run_ringout loop exists to honour abort_reason, ses.done is never set. ring_out_system's except clause (CfsError/DeskError/PolicyError) does not catch it; the tool returns BAD_ARGUMENT. From then on feedback_watch/ring_out → BUSY, get_rta(target) → BUSY, feedback_watch_stop blocks 30 s and reports 'Stopped' while mode stays 'ringout'; App.close blocks 30 s. The show loses its feedback guard until the server is restarted. (The bus master is not raised — the loop never ran.)

**Exploit.** 1) setup GEQs; ring_out_system({'stages':[{'bus':1,'target_gain_db':None,'dwell_ms':10}]}) → token T. 2) ring_out_system({'stages':[{'bus':1,'target_gain_db':NaN,'dwell_ms':10}]}, confirm_token=T) → token ACCEPTED (payloads equal), run fails 'NaN is not a valid dB value'. 3) cfs_status → mode 'ringout', stage 'ARM'; feedback_watch(2) → BUSY; feedback_watch_stop → waits 30 s, says Stopped, mode still 'ringout'. (Step 1 can equally be the NaN plan itself: its first call mints fine because the system summary does not format targets.)

**Evidence.** test_ring_out_system_stage_with_nan_target (line 323) PASSES: printed nan stage: {ok:false, error BAD_ARGUMENT 'NaN is not a valid dB value'}; cfs after: ringout ARM bus1 master -3.998; feedback_watch(2) BUSY; stop: {'stopped': True, 'mode': 'ringout', ...}; cfs after stop: ringout. First unpatched run of the test took 33 s (the 30 s _STOP_WAIT_S in close()). Separate check: `types.jsonrpc_message_adapter`-equivalent TypeAdapter(JSONRPCMessage).validate_json('{..."target_gain_db":NaN...}') parsed to nan.

**Proposed fix.** (a) Validate every stage's target_gain_db in server.ring_out_system and in cfs.ring_out with math.isfinite (reject NaN/inf as BAD_ARGUMENT before anything is armed); make _jsonable raise on NaN inside confirmation payloads instead of mapping to None. (b) In cfs.ring_out move the log.info inside the armed try-block or wrap everything after _open_session in try/except BaseException → _unwind/disarm + self._ses=None + ses.done.set(), so no exception can leave a session armed without its loop. (c) stop(): if the ring-out task is not running (done never set within the wait), force-disarm and clear self._ses.
