> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-tokens-3-repro (branch wt/verify-tokens-3-repro, commit e26b1c9), test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens3.py (5 tests, all pass against unmodified src). Every element of the claim holds:

1. NaN reaches the tool: mcp 2.2 stdio uses types.jsonrpc_message_adapter.validate_json (mcp/server/stdio.py:189); I fed it a tools/call line with "target_gain_db":NaN and it parsed to float nan. The tool param is plan: dict[str, Any], so nothing downstream rejects it.

2. Token binding defeated: server.py:1806-1822 validates notch_budget/dwell_ms/step_db per stage but never target_gain_db; payload = {"plan": _jsonable(plan)} (server.py:1827) and _jsonable maps NaN->None (server.py:255-256). A token minted for {'bus':1,'target_gain_db':None,'dwell_ms':10} was redeemed by the NaN plan with no BAD_TOKEN (policy.guard's stored != payload compare at policy.py:356 sees equal dicts). The NaN plan can also mint and redeem its own token (system summary never formats targets).

3. Wedge: cfs.ring_out computes target = min(nan, ceiling) = nan (cfs.py:675); 'target <= master' (676) is False so it proceeds; _open_session/_snapshot_stage/_stage(ARM)/_arm succeed (RTA source written, frames started, detector consumer task created, self._ses set). The try/except BaseException that resets self._ses (cfs.py:694-700) ends before the log.info at cfs.py:702-703, whose format_db(ses.target_db) raises ScaleError('NaN is not a valid dB value') (scales.py:277-278). Traceback captured: cfs.py:745 ring_out_system -> cfs.py:703 ring_out -> scales.py:278. ring_out_system's per-stage except (CfsError, DeskError, PolicyError) does not catch ValueError, so it propagates; its finally clears self._system but self._ses stays set (mode RINGOUT, stage ARM). _tool maps ValueError -> BAD_ARGUMENT.

4. Consequences observed: cfs_status -> mode 'ringout', stage 'ARM', rta_source bus.1; feedback_watch(2) -> BUSY; get_rta('bus.2') -> BUSY; feedback_watch_stop waits the full _STOP_WAIT_S (patched to 2 s in-test; 30 s in product, cfs.py:107/609) then returns {stopped: True, mode: 'ringout'} while self._ses is still set and a subsequent feedback_watch(2) is still BUSY; App.close also blocks the full _STOP_WAIT_S (cfs.py:790). Additionally verified: in the wedged state the detector consumer keeps running (30 frames consumed in 1.5 s), an injected 2.4 kHz ring produced 2 detections that only piled up in ses.pending — no notch was written (GEQ band 22 stayed 0.0) because no _run_ringout loop exists. So the armed bus's real feedback goes uncut and no other bus can be guarded until process restart. Bus master was NOT raised (-19.97 before/after) and panic still works (24 outputs muted), so not critical.

Control: the single-bus ring_out tool is NOT affected — its summary calls _db_from_float(target) (server.py:1766) which raises BAD_ARGUMENT on the first call before anything arms; CFS stays idle.

Severity high per the scale: defeats token payload binding (NaN plan redeems a None-plan token) and leaves CFS in a stuck armed state after an error that stop() cannot clear (documented feedback-guard guarantee lost for the rest of the process). No loud/unbounded level change, panic intact -> not critical.
>
> *Evidence:* Test run (PYTHONPATH=worktree/src, pytest): 5 passed in 12.62s. Key printed output from test_nan_stage_redeems_none_token_and_wedges_cfs:
- 'nan stage result: {ok: False, error: {code: BAD_ARGUMENT, message: "NaN is not a valid dB value"}}' (token minted for the None plan was ACCEPTED — not BAD_TOKEN)
- 'bus1 master before/after: -19.97 -19.97'
- 'cfs after: mode ringout, stage ARM, bus 1, rta_source bus.1'; 'internal: _ses is None? False _system: None'
- 'feedback_watch(2): BUSY "CFS² is already ringout on Bus 1 ... stop or abort it first"'; 'get_rta(bus.2): BUSY'
- 'feedback_watch_stop took 2.00s -> {ok: True, stopped: True, mode: ringout}' (with _STOP_WAIT_S monkeypatched 30->2; it waited the full timeout); 'cfs after stop: ringout ARM internal _ses None? False'; 'feedback_watch(2) after stop: BUSY'
- 'panic: True PANIC: 24 outputs muted'; 'App.close took 2.00s' (full _STOP_WAIT_S again)
test_nan_plan_mints_and_redeems_its_own_token: first call requires_confirmation=True with stages [{'bus':1,'target_gain_db':None,...}], second call BAD_ARGUMENT NaN, cfs after: ringout ARM.
test_where_the_scaleerror_escapes traceback: cfs.py line 745 ring_out_system -> cfs.py line 703 'format_db(ses.target_db)' -> scales.py line 278 format_db; after: _ses set, stage ARM, _system None.
test_wedged_session_detects_but_never_notches: 'frames consumed while wedged: 30 detections: 2 pending: 2 notches written: 0 consumer task done? False; desk GEQ FX5 band22 value: 0.0'.
test_single_bus_ring_out_with_nan_target (control): ring_out(1, target_gain_db=nan) -> BAD_ARGUMENT on first call, cfs stays idle.
MCP transport check: types.jsonrpc_message_adapter.validate_json('{..."target_gain_db":NaN...}', by_name=False) -> JSONRPCRequest with params[...]['target_gain_db'] == nan (float).
Files: <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-3-repro/tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens3.py (copy at $SP/reports/verify-tokens-3-repro/). Source refs: src/x32mcp/server.py:255-256 (_jsonable NaN->None), :1806-1827 (stage validation omits target_gain_db; payload), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:675-676, :694-703 (try-block ends before log.info format_db), :587-613 & :107 (stop() 30 s wait, never clears _ses), :745-748 (stage except misses ValueError), :784-791 (close() waits too), src/x32mcp/scales.py:277-278.
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's fix is right; concretely: (a) server.ring_out_system stage loop: if item.get('target_gain_db') is not None: v=float(...); if not math.isfinite(v): raise DeskError('BAD_ARGUMENT'); same isfinite guard in cfs.ring_out (and single-bus server.ring_out for +/-inf) before opening a session. (b) Make _jsonable (or a dedicated _payload_jsonable used only by _confirm) raise on NaN rather than mapping to None, so no NaN-bearing request can ever alias another payload. (c) cfs.ring_out: extend the armed try/except BaseException to cover everything between _open_session and entering _run_ringout (move the log.info inside it, or compute the log strings before arming), and on failure call _disarm() as well as clearing self._ses/setting ses.done — currently even the existing except at 698 leaves frames/consumer running if _arm succeeded and something after it raised. (d) cfs.ring_out_system: catch Exception per stage (not just CfsError/DeskError/PolicyError) and force-disarm before continuing/aborting. (e) stop()/close(): if wait_for(ses.done) times out and no ring-out task is alive, force _unwind/_disarm and clear self._ses so the operator can always recover without restarting the server.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain verified end to end, no gap:
1. server.ring_out_system (src/x32mcp/server.py:1806-1826) normalises notch_budget/dwell_ms/step_db per stage but never touches target_gain_db; cfs._system_stages (cfs.py ~1329-1340) passes it through untouched; cfs.ring_out (cfs.py:675) does `min(float(target_gain_db), ceiling)` -> min(nan, 0.0) == nan (Python min returns the first arg when `b < a` is False); cfs.py:676 `nan <= master` is False so the 'already at target' refusal is skipped.
2. Inside the `async with self._lock` block: _open_session, _snapshot_stage, _stage('ARM', target_db=_db1(nan)=nan — _db1 rounds, does not raise; EventBus.publish swallows subscriber errors), _arm (writes /-prefs/rta/source, sets self._ses=ses, starts the frame source + detector consumer task). The try/except BaseException at cfs.py:694-701 that resets self._ses covers only those three calls. The next statement, log.info(... format_db(ses.target_db) ...) at cfs.py:702-703, is outside any handler; scales.format_db (scales.py:277-278) raises ScaleError('NaN is not a valid dB value'). ScaleError subclasses ValueError (scales.py:66), so cfs.ring_out_system's `except (CfsError, DeskError, PolicyError)` (cfs.py:748) does not catch it; its `finally` clears self._system only. server._tool maps ValueError -> BAD_ARGUMENT. Result: self._ses set (mode RINGOUT, stage ARM), consumer task alive, no _run_ringout loop, ses.done never set.
3. Nothing can clear it: stop() (cfs.py:587-613) for a RINGOUT session only sets abort_reason/wake and waits _STOP_WAIT_S=30 s for ses.done, then returns stopped=True without touching self._ses; abort() same; _lose_connection for RINGOUT only schedules _restore_master; App.connect -> cfs.abort (no-op); server.disconnect -> cfs.stop (reports 'stopped CFS² ringout session', still armed); cfs.close waits 30 s then _disarm()s frames but self._ses stays. Only a process restart recovers; feedback_watch/ring_out/ring_out_system -> BUSY via _require_idle.
4. Token half: server._confirm passes _jsonable(payload) to policy.guard; _jsonable maps NaN->None (server.py:255-256); guard compares `stored != dict(payload)` (policy.py guard) so {'target_gain_db': None} and NaN payloads are equal and cross-redeem. But this collision is not even needed: the NaN plan mints and redeems its own token because the system summary never formats the target (unlike single-bus ring_out, whose summary calls _db_from_float(target) and dies cleanly before minting — verified).
5. Reachability is BROADER than claimed: besides the NaN literal (mcp 2.2 stdio jsonrpc_message_adapter.validate_json accepts it — re-verified in the venv), the plain-JSON strings "nan"/"NaN" work from any client because cfs.py:675 calls float() on the raw stage value. The human-facing confirmation shows target None / 'nan' in the `stages` extra and 'default 0 dB' in the summary, so a confused/jailbroken client gets it confirmed easily.
Real desk vs FakeDesk: nothing desk-specific is on the failing path (pure Python before any loop write), so a real X32 behaves identically; additionally /-prefs/rta/source stays pinned to the bus and LiveMeters keeps renewing /meters subscriptions indefinitely. No audio consequence: the bus master is never raised (loop never ran), no GEQ cuts are written (RINGOUT-mode consumer only queues detections), panic() still works (desk-level).
Severity re-assessment: MEDIUM rather than high. What is defeated is availability/integrity of the CFS² automation (feedback_watch guard unavailable until restart; stop()/disconnect report 'Stopped' while still armed, contradicting DESIGN §15 'stop() # disarm' and the code's own 'nothing may leave the session armed' comment; 30 s stalls in feedback_watch_stop/disconnect/close) plus a NaN≡None token-binding blur. It produces no level change, no cut, does not defeat panic or any clamp, requires an unusual malformed argument on a human-confirmed Tier-2 call, and ring_out_system is refused in show mode (so the wedge is planted at soundcheck, then persists into the show). That matches 'guarantee weakened under unusual but reachable conditions' more than the hearing-safety 'high' bucket; it sits at the top of medium because recovery needs a server restart and the operator is actively misinformed.
Note on the fix: (a) isfinite validation must come first/also in cfs.ring_out — applying only (b) (moving the log inside the try) would let target=nan reach _run_ringout, where `master >= nan` is always False and `min(master+step, nan)` returns master+step, so the loop would raise step by step until the policy bus clamp (0 dB, device.yaml bus_fader_max_db) stops it as 'master clamped' — same ceiling as the default target today, but the ringout.master_ceiling_db bound would be silently bypassed if the two ever diverge.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-3-src (branch wt/verify-tokens-3-src, commit ceaed8d), test file tests/integration/test_verify_nan_wedge.py (copy in $SP/reports/verify-tokens-3-src/). 5 passed in 9.5 s with _STOP_WAIT_S monkeypatched to 0.5 s:
- test_system_stage_nan_target_self_minted[float-nan|str-nan|str-NaN]: plan {'stages':[{'bus':1,'target_gain_db':<NaN|'nan'|'NaN'>,'dwell_ms':10}]} mints its own token (summary: '...raised to its target (default 0 dB)...', stages extra shows target None / 'nan' / 'NaN'), redeem -> {ok:false, error BAD_ARGUMENT 'NaN is not a valid dB value'}; cfs_status -> mode 'ringout', stage 'ARM', session 2026..-ringout-bus01; bus1 fader unchanged at -3.998; FakeDesk /-prefs/rta/source MAIN -> BUS01; a.cfs._system is None, a.cfs._ses set, consumer task alive; feedback_watch(2) -> BUSY; feedback_watch_stop -> {ok:True, stopped:True, mode:'ringout'} after the (patched) wait, cfs_status still 'ringout'; cfs.abort() no effect; disconnect ('...stopped CFS² ringout session') + connect -> cfs_status still 'ringout' ARM, feedback_watch(2) still BUSY; panic() ok.
- test_none_token_redeemed_by_nan_plan: token minted for target None accepted for the NaN plan (BAD_ARGUMENT not BAD_TOKEN, pending_confirmations==0), mode 'ringout' after.
- test_single_bus_ring_out_string_nan_refused_cleanly: ring_out(1, target_gain_db=NaN) -> BAD_ARGUMENT, mode stays 'idle' (control).
- venv check: TypeAdapter(mcp.types.JSONRPCMessage).validate_json('{..."target_gain_db":NaN...}') -> params arguments contain float nan (mcp 2.2.0), matching mcp/server/stdio.py's jsonrpc_message_adapter.validate_json line.
Code refs: server.py:1806-1826 (no target validation), server.py:247-257 (_jsonable NaN->None), server.py:331-345 (_confirm), policy.py guard `stored != dict(payload)`, cfs.py:675-676, cfs.py:694-703 (try scope vs log.info/format_db), scales.py:66 (ScaleError(ValueError)), scales.py:277-278, cfs.py:748 (except tuple), cfs.py:587-613 (stop), cfs.py:1307-1320 (_lose_connection RINGOUT branch), server.py:192-197/669-676 (connect/disconnect), device.yaml:725/830 (bus clamp 0 dB == master_ceiling_db).
>
> *Better fix:* 1) Reject non-finite targets at both layers: in server.ring_out_system's stage loop add `if item.get('target_gain_db') is not None: v=float(...); if not math.isfinite(v): raise DeskError('BAD_ARGUMENT', ...)` (also format it into the summary/stages so the human sees the real number), and in cfs.ring_out validate `math.isfinite(float(target_gain_db))` before preflight (covers the string 'nan' path and any future caller). Do this BEFORE/together with 2 — moving the log alone would let nan reach _run_ringout and raise to the policy clamp. 2) In cfs.ring_out wrap everything from _open_session to the hand-off to _run_ringout (including the log line) so any BaseException runs _disarm(), clears self._ses and sets ses.done; simplest: move the log.info inside the existing try at cfs.py:694. 3) Make stop()/close() self-healing: if ses.mode is RINGOUT and ses.done is not set after the wait (or no ring-out task is registered), force _unwind(ses) (back-off is a no-op when master was never raised) so self._ses is cleared and the report says ABORT instead of returning stopped=True while armed. 4) Optionally make _jsonable raise (or map to a sentinel string) for NaN inside confirmation payloads so NaN can never alias None in token binding, and have cfs.ring_out_system catch ValueError/ScaleError per stage like the other argument errors.

## D26. [medium] A declined confirmation cannot be revoked: older tokens for the same action stay live for 300 s after a newer mint, so a confused model can replay the request the human said no to
*Angle:* tokens · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/policy.py:277-294 (require_confirmation never invalidates existing tokens; no cancel API), policy.py:296-313, device.yaml:734-738 (confirm_token_ttl_s: 300), src/x32mcp/server.py:122-125 (instructions still say 60 s; no 'decline' tool)

**Mechanism.** Tokens are single-use and payload-bound, but nothing ties a token to the conversation turn: minting T2 for set_main_fader leaves T1 (different payload, same action) valid until TTL. The human's 'no' to T1 has no server-side effect. With the TTL raised to 5 minutes, a model that keeps both tokens in context (or a runaway loop iterating over earlier tool results) can redeem T1 later; the server sees a perfectly valid token+payload and executes the move the operator rejected. pending_confirmations is also unbounded (5000 live tokens minted in a loop in the test) — memory is trivial but it shows there is no per-action cap.

**Exploit.** 1) Main at −20. set_main_fader('st', 0.0) → T1; operator: NO. 2) set_main_fader('st', −20.0) → T2; operator: yes → redeem T2 (ok). 3) Later (≤300 s) model sends set_main_fader('st', 0.0, confirm_token=T1) → ok, Main LR −20 → 0 dB (+20 dB) with no fresh confirmation.

**Evidence.** test_declined_token_replay_executes_the_move_the_user_said_no_to (line 154) PASSES: after redeeming T2, policy.pending_confirmations == 1, redeeming T1 returned ok with after_db 0.0 and the fake desk main fader read 0.0. test_policy_older_token_for_same_action_survives_a_newer_mint (line 99) and test_policy_ttl_boundary_and_sweep (line 78: 5000 outstanding tokens) PASS.

**Proposed fix.** On require_confirmation(action=...) revoke all outstanding tokens for that action (or for that action+target), so only the most recently summarised request per action is redeemable; add a Tier-0 cancel_confirmation(token|all) tool and tell the model to call it when the user declines; cap outstanding tokens (e.g. 8) and fix the '60 s' text in the instructions to match device.yaml.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in my own worktree ($SP/worktrees/verify-tokens-4-repro, branch wt/verify-tokens-4-repro, commit ab7ac85, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens4.py; 8/8 pass, full suite 919 passed + 1 unrelated worktree-dirname failure in test_settings_defaults_and_env).

Mechanism verified against HEAD a408a2a: Policy.require_confirmation (src/x32mcp/policy.py:311-327) only sweeps EXPIRED tokens and adds a new one; it never revokes outstanding tokens for the same action/target. consume_token/guard (policy.py:329-361) delete only the token presented. There is no cancel/revoke/decline method on Policy and no such tool in server.py (grep for cancel|revoke|decline finds nothing relevant). TTL is confirm_token_ttl_s: 300 (device.yaml:738, comment 734-737) while the model-facing instructions still say '(60 s)' (server.py:124). Policy is created once per App (server.py:155), not per connection, so tokens also survive disconnect/reconnect and show_mode toggles. set_main_fader is not in SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED and Desk.set_main_level passes force=True (desk.py:1224-1230), so the replay is not limited by the relative-move rule or show mode.

What actually happens (executed): Main LR at -20 dB; set_main_fader('st',0.0) -> T1 ('from −20.0 dB to 0.0 dB'); [operator says no — nothing to call]; set_main_fader('st',-20.0) -> T2; redeem T2 ok; policy.pending_confirmations == 1; set_main_fader('st',0.0,confirm_token=T1) -> {ok: True, before_db -20.0, after_db 0.0}; fake desk /main/st/mix/fader reads ≈0.0 (+20 dB, 0 ms ramp). T1 is then BAD_TOKEN (single-use still holds). Pure-policy: T1 still redeemable at clock+299 s, TOKEN_EXPIRED at +300 s; 5000 tokens minted in a loop -> pending_confirmations == 5000 (no cap). Additional narrowing/aggravation I tested: (a) show_mode on/off + disconnect/reconnect between decline and replay: T1 still executes; (b) show mode ON at replay time: still executes (+20 dB on Main LR in show mode); (c) the payload binds only {which, db, ramp_ms}, not the 'from' level shown in the summary — after the operator pulls Main LR to -60 on the surface, replaying T1 jumps −60 → 0 dB (+60 dB), larger than anything ever summarised.

Severity: I keep MEDIUM. The documented guarantees (single-use, TTL, action+payload binding) all hold, so it is not 'high' by the rubric's definition; and the precondition is model misbehaviour (redeeming a token the human rejected), which a relay-consent token scheme can never fully prevent — the model could equally self-confirm T1 immediately. The real, fixable defect is that the exposure window is needlessly wide: a rejected request stays armed for 5 minutes, across later legitimate confirmations, reconnects and show-mode changes, with no per-action supersession, no cap and no cancel tool, and the instructions misstate the TTL as 60 s. The consequence when it fires is critical-class (unramped +20..+60 dB on Main LR), which is why it matters, but reachability depends on a runaway/confused client replaying stale tool results. Minor citation error in the claim: the cited policy.py line ranges (277-294, 296-313) are off; at HEAD the code is at 305-367.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-4-repro (commit ab7ac85). Test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens4.py — all 8 PASS (runtests.sh ... -v -s): test_policy_ttl_is_300s (l.43); test_older_token_for_same_action_survives_a_newer_mint_and_redeem (l.47: after redeeming T2 pending_confirmations==1, T1 redeems at +299 s returning its payload); test_older_token_expires_at_300s (l.67: TOKEN_EXPIRED at +300 s); test_no_cap_on_outstanding_tokens (l.76: 5000 live tokens); test_declined_token_replay_executes_the_move_the_user_said_no_to (l.85) printed: 'EVIDENCE T1 summary: Set Main LR fader from −20.0 dB to 0.0 dB (0 ms ramp) | T2 summary: Set Main LR fader from −20.0 dB to −20.0 dB (0 ms ramp)' / 'pending after T2 redeem = 1; replay envelope: {ok: True, summary: "Main LR 'Main' fader −20.0 dB → 0.0 dB", before_db: -20.0, after_db: 0.0}' / 'fake desk /main/st/mix/fader after replay: -0.0098'; then T1 reuse -> BAD_TOKEN. test_declined_token_replay_also_survives_show_mode_toggle_and_reconnect (l.126) PASS: show_mode(True)/show_mode(False)/disconnect/connect, pending_confirmations still 1, replay ok, desk 0.0. test_declined_token_replay_in_show_mode_still_executes_main_fader (l.149) PASS: replay with show mode ON -> ok, desk 0.0. test_stale_token_is_not_bound_to_the_starting_level_the_operator_saw (l.169) printed 'EVIDENCE replay from -60: {ok: True, summary: "Main LR 'Main' fader −60.0 dB → 0.0 dB", before_db: -60.0, after_db: 0.0}'. Code refs: src/x32mcp/policy.py:306-309 (_sweep_tokens expires only), 311-327 (require_confirmation adds without revoking), 329-345 (consume_token pops only the presented token), 347-361 (guard), 364-367 (pending_confirmations); device.yaml:734-738 (confirm_token_ttl_s: 300); src/x32mcp/server.py:124 ('confirm_token (60 s)'), 331-345 (_confirm), 155 (Policy per App, survives reconnect), 1082-1101 (set_main_fader payload = {which, db, ramp_ms}); src/x32mcp/desk.py:1224-1230 (set_main_level force=True, no relative check).
>
> *Better fix:* (1) In Policy.require_confirmation(action=...), delete every outstanding token whose action (and, where the payload carries one, target e.g. 'which'/bus/scene) matches before minting, so only the most recently summarised request per action/target is redeemable. (2) Bind level tokens to the starting state too: include the observed 'from' level (rounded) in the set_main_fader payload, or re-check at redeem time that the current level is within a small tolerance of the level in the summary and refuse with STALE_CONFIRMATION otherwise — this closes the −60→0 case. (3) Add a Tier-0 cancel_confirmation(confirm_token | 'all') tool and instruct the model to call it when the user declines; also clear all tokens on disconnect()/connect() and on show_mode(True). (4) Cap outstanding tokens (e.g. 8, oldest evicted). (5) Make the instructions text derive the TTL from policy.confirm_token_ttl_s instead of the hard-coded '60 s' at server.py:124. (6) Consider ramping guarded main-fader moves by default even when ramp_ms=0 is requested with a large delta, so a bad redeem is at least not a step.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap:
1) Mint: server.py:1091-1097 set_main_fader builds payload {which,db,ramp_ms} and calls _confirm → server.py:331-343 → Policy.guard (policy.py:347-354) → require_confirmation (policy.py:311-327). require_confirmation only sweeps EXPIRED tokens (_sweep_tokens, policy.py:306-309) and inserts a new _Token; it never looks at, supersedes or deletes other live tokens for the same action/target. There is no other mutation site of Policy._tokens anywhere (grep: only 204/306-309/317-321/333 in policy.py; nothing in server.py/desk.py touches _tokens; no revoke/cancel/decline tool exists). Policy is one instance per App (server.py:155), so tokens also survive connect()/reconnect.
2) Redeem: consume_token (policy.py:329-345) checks only: token present, now-minted < confirm_token_ttl_s (device.yaml:738 = 300 s), tok.action == expected_action. guard (policy.py:355-361) then checks stored payload == current payload. A verbatim replay of the declined call (same which/db/ramp_ms + T1) satisfies all four checks. The human's "no" never reaches the server, and minting/redeeming T2 in between does not touch T1.
3) Execute: desk.set_main_level (desk.py:1224-1230) passes force=True ('no relative limit — the confirmation token is the gate'), so the ±6/±3 dB relative guard does not apply; main ceiling is 0 dB (device.yaml:726) so db=0.0 is not clamped; show mode does not list set_main_fader (policy.py:75-77) so it lands in show mode too; ramp_ms=0 is accepted (server.py:1088, 0..60000) so it can be a single-step +20 dB jump on Main LR.
FakeDesk vs real desk: the whole mechanism is server-side state in policy.py; the desk only receives /main/st/mix/fader ,f 1.0. HANDOVER §4a (M5) verified on the real X32 that a confirmed set_main_fader executes (−22.8→−15.0) and that Tier-1 set_fader('main.st') is refused, so a real desk behaves identically — the room hears −20→0 dB.
Attempted refutations that failed: (a) no relative limit on main (force=True by design); (b) no session/connection boundary clears tokens; (c) payload binding does not help because the replay uses T1's own payload; (d) show mode irrelevant. 
Severity re-assessment: I keep MEDIUM but at its low edge. No documented guarantee is defeated — single-use, TTL, action+payload binding all hold (my test also shows T1 is dead after the replay), and DESIGN/BRIEF/device.yaml never promise 'newest mint supersedes'. The server has never had knowledge of consent: my control test shows a model can equally self-mint T3 and redeem it in two calls, indistinguishable server-side, so this is a defence-in-depth gap against the honest-but-confused client (misattributed 'yes', agent retry loop re-sending an earlier tool call verbatim with its token, context compaction keeping an old tool result) rather than a bypass usable only by an adversary. What makes it worth medium rather than low: the window was deliberately widened 5x (0e509c2, device.yaml:734-738), the affected action is the loudest one on the desk with no relative limit and ramp 0 allowed, several live tokens for the same action/target routinely coexist (the stock test_set_main_fader_dance already leaves 'first' live), and the fix is a few lines. The sub-claims are LOW/hygiene: unbounded outstanding tokens (each needs a tool call; ~200 B each; swept at TTL) and the stale '60 s' in INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:124) — the model sees the true expires_in_s=300 in every pending envelope anyway. Claim's policy.py line numbers are off (require_confirmation is 311-327, consume_token 329-345, guard 347-361) but the functions named are correct.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-4-src (branch wt/verify-tokens-4-src, commit 8cd20c6), file tests/integration/test_verify_tokens4.py, run via runtests.sh: 3 passed in 1.84 s.
- test_declined_token_replay: fader preset −20; T1=set_main_fader('st',0.0,ramp_ms=0) pending (summary 'from −20.0 dB to 0.0 dB'); T2=set_main_fader('st',−20.0,0) pending; redeem T2 ok; policy.pending_confirmations==1; redeem T1 with its original args → {'ok': True, 'summary': "Main LR 'Main' fader −20.0 dB → 0.0 dB", 'before_db': -20.0, 'after_db': 0.0, 'steps': 1, 'elapsed_ms': 0.1}; fakedesk /main/st/mix/fader == 0.0; second use of T1 → BAD_TOKEN; pending==0.
- test_show_mode_does_not_change_the_replay: T1 minted, show_mode(true), replay T1 → ok, after_db 0.0, steps 1 (show mode neither blocks set_main_fader nor applies the ±3 dB limit because desk.py:1230 passes force=True).
- test_control_a_fresh_self_mint_is_equally_unconstrained: mint T3 for 0.0 and redeem immediately → ok, after_db 0.0 — the server cannot distinguish this from the replay, which bounds the severity.
Source: policy.py:306-309 (_sweep_tokens removes only expired), 311-327 (mint adds, never revokes), 329-345 (consume: existence/TTL/action only), 347-361 (payload equality), 204 (single dict, per-App at server.py:155); server.py:331-343 (_confirm), 1082-1101 (set_main_fader, ramp 0..60000), 122-126 (instructions say 60 s, no decline tool); desk.py:1224-1230 (force=True, guarded=True); device.yaml:726 (main max 0 dB), 734-738 (TTL 300 with rationale), policy.py:75-77 (show-mode block list excludes set_main_fader); HANDOVER.md:110-113,126 (real-desk confirmation behaviour, TTL raised after M5). Claimant's own test at worktrees/redteam-tokens/tests/integration/test_redteam_tokens.py:154-165 matches what I observed.
>
> *Better fix:* In Policy.require_confirmation(action=...), before inserting the new token delete every live token with the same action (optionally same action+target key carried in the payload, e.g. 'which'/'bus'/'id'), so the most recently summarised request is the only redeemable one per action — this closes the replay without shortening the 300 s conversational TTL that M5 showed is needed. Add a Tier-0 cancel_confirmation(confirm_token | 'all') tool and one sentence in INSTRUCTIONS ('if the user declines, call cancel_confirmation'), fix '60 s' → read confirm_token_ttl_s, and cap outstanding tokens (e.g. 8, oldest evicted) as hygiene. Optionally bind the observed 'from' state into the summary check for absolute moves (re-read the fader at redeem time and refuse with STALE_CONFIRMATION if it differs from the value shown by more than the relative limit), which also covers the 5-minute staleness the longer TTL introduced. None of this changes the fundamental property that consent is model-relayed; it only removes stale grants.

## D27. [medium] ring_out token does not bind the open-mic list the user is asked to confirm; Tier-1 set_send/unmute between the calls changes which mics are live when the bus is raised
*Angle:* tokens · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1755-1771 (payload = bus/target/step/dwell/budget/patch_file; pf.included_mics only in the summary and the 'preflight' extra), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:667-668 (preflight recomputed at run time, no comparison)

**Mechanism.** The docstring says the second call happens 'after the user has confirmed the open-mic list', but the token payload omits that list. Between the calls the model can, without any confirmation, unmute a channel or raise its send into the bus (Tier 1). The confirmed run then raises the bus master toward 0 dB with a mic open that the human never saw in the summary (e.g. a lectern/lav parked in front of the wedge), which is exactly the hazard the mic-list confirmation exists for. Same class: ring_out's patch_file and ring_out_system stages' patch_file are bound by name, not content.

**Exploit.** 1) ch1,ch2 → bus 1; ring_out(1, target_gain_db=-19, dwell_ms=10) → summary 'open mics: Ch 1, Ch 2', token T. 2) set_send('ch.3', 1, -5.0) (Tier 1, ok). 3) ring_out(1, …, confirm_token=T) → runs; report preflight mics include ch 3 at −5.0 dB.

**Evidence.** test_ring_out_token_does_not_bind_the_open_mic_list (line 238) PASSES: summary contained "open mics: Ch 1 'Ch01', Ch 2 'Ch02'" and not Ch03; redeemed run ok; printed report mics list includes {'ch': 3, 'send_db': -5.0, 'include': True}.

**Proposed fix.** Add 'mics': sorted(m.ch for m in pf.included_mics) (and the GEQ insert sel/slot and start master rounded to 0.5 dB) to the ring_out payload so any change to the live-mic set or insert between the calls yields BAD_TOKEN and a fresh summary; for patch_file include a content hash.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-tokens-5-repro (commit abc6fff, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens5.py, 5 tests all PASS against unmodified src).

Code facts: server.py:1755-1758 builds the ring_out token payload from {bus, target_gain_db, step_db, dwell_ms, notch_budget, patch_file} only; pf.included_mics appears only in the human summary string (server.py:1760-1766) and the 'preflight' extra of the pending envelope. policy.guard (policy.py:347-360) compares stored payload == new payload, so anything not in the payload is unbound. On redemption cfs.ring_out re-runs preflight (cfs.py:669) and uses whatever mics are live now; it has an _expected_mics hook (cfs.py:647, 690-694) but the ring_out tool never passes it, and even that hook only warns about MISSING mics, never extra ones. README.md:713 documents 'Tier 2, one confirmation that includes the open-mic list' and the tool docstring says 'call again with it after the user has confirmed the open-mic list' — so the confirmed object (the mic list) is not what the token binds.

Executed exploits: (1) ch1,ch2 -> bus1 @ -20, master -20; ring_out(1,-19,dwell 10) -> summary 'open mics: Ch 1 'Ch01', Ch 2 'Ch02'' (no Ch 3), token T; set_send('ch.3',1,-5.0) Tier 1 ok; ring_out(...,confirm_token=T) -> ok=True, final_stage DONE, report preflight included [1,2,3] with ch3 at -5.0 dB (15 dB hotter than the confirmed mics), no warning. (2) Same with ch3 pre-routed at -2 dB but muted (summary even warns 'sends to Bus 1 but is muted'), unmute('ch.3') between calls -> confirmed run includes ch3. (3) Control: changing notch_budget or target_gain_db on the second call -> BAD_TOKEN, so arg binding works; the gap is desk state. (4) patch_file bound by name: editing the YAML between calls (owner Ray->Mallory) -> run uses edited content, token accepted. Note the patch only affects annotations/warnings in discover_mics (provision.py:586-612), not inclusion or any write, so the patch_file sub-claim is real but hygiene-level.

Severity: I keep MEDIUM. It is not critical: the bus-master trajectory (start, step<=6 dB, target<=0 dB ceiling, dwell) is exactly what was confirmed and the detector/notcher still runs; no unbounded level. It is not a clean 'high' either because the token single-use/arg-binding guarantee itself holds; what is defeated is the documented scope of the confirmation (README:713 'includes the open-mic list'). Additional context that tempers it further: test_set_send_during_the_run_is_also_unguarded shows set_send('ch.3',1,-5) and unmute('ch.4') succeed while CFS is in RAISE at -19 dB mid-run — Tier-1 writes into the bus under ring-out are never locked out, so binding the mic list into the token closes only the between-calls window, not the during-run window. The hazard (a lav/lectern mic the human never saw goes live into a wedge being driven toward 0 dB) is real and reachable by a confused model in two ordinary tool calls, hence medium rather than low.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-5-repro, commit abc6fff, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens5.py — `runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens5.py -s` => 5 passed. Printed output: test_set_send_between_calls_adds_unconfirmed_mic: SUMMARY 'Ring out Bus 1 'Bus01': raise its master from −20.0 dB to −19.0 dB ... open mics: Ch 1 'Ch01', Ch 2 'Ch02'. Nobody may play...'; SET_SEND 'Bus 1 'Bus01' send from Ch 3 'Ch03' -oo dB → −5.0 dB' ok=True; REDEEM ok=True final=DONE; REPORT preflight included [1, 2, 3]; mics [(1,-20.0,True),(2,-20.0,True),(3,-5.0,True)]. test_unmute_between_calls_adds_unconfirmed_mic: summary warns 'ch.3 'Ch03': sends to Bus 1 but is muted', after unmute('ch.3') REDEEM ok=True included=[1,2,3]. test_control_changing_a_bound_arg_is_refused: notch_budget 6->7 and target -19->-10 with the token => BAD_TOKEN (arg binding intact). test_patch_file_bound_by_name_not_content: token minted with patch owner Ray; file rewritten to Mallory; REDEEM ok=True mics=[(1,'Mallory'),(2,'Mallory'),(13,'Tony')]. test_set_send_during_the_run_is_also_unguarded: MID-RUN status ringout RAISE -19.0; set_send ch.3->bus1 −5.0 dB ok=True; unmute ch.4 ok=True; run DONE max -16.0; ch3 send on fake = -5.0. Source refs: server.py:1755-1758 (payload), 1760-1766 (mics only in summary), 1767 (_confirm), policy.py:347-360 (guard compares payload dicts), cfs.py:669 (preflight recomputed), cfs.py:647/690-694 (_expected_mics exists, unused by ring_out tool, warns only on missing), README.md:713 (documented 'confirmation that includes the open-mic list').
>
> *Better fix:* The proposed fix (add sorted included-mic channels, GEQ insert sel/slot, start master to the payload; hash patch content) is right but incomplete: (a) rather than only failing with BAD_TOKEN via payload mismatch, pass the confirmed list into cfs.ring_out as _expected_mics and make cfs.ring_out (cfs.py:690-694) ABORT before SNAPSHOT/ARM when pf.included_mics contains any channel NOT in the confirmed set (extra mics are the hazard; today it only warns on missing ones) — this also covers ring_out_system stages, whose 'mics' key currently only warns; (b) the same hazard exists mid-run (Tier-1 set_send/unmute/set_mute into the bus under an active RINGOUT session succeed while the master is being raised), so Desk.set_send/set_mute (or policy) should refuse, or CFS should abort+backoff on, any write that opens a new input into the session bus while cfs.mode==ringout (a cfs 'strip.changed' listener comparing against ses.preflight.included_mics would do); (c) include round(pf.master_db,1) in the payload so a master moved between calls re-prompts (a lowered master silently lengthens the raise; a raised one is already refused by the target<=master check).

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap:
1) server.py ring_out (1729-1779): first call runs _prov.preflight, builds payload = {bus, target_gain_db, step_db, dwell_ms, notch_budget, patch_file} (1755-1758). pf.included_mics appears ONLY in the human-readable summary string (1760-1766) and in the `preflight=` extra of the pending envelope; it is not in payload. Start master (pf.master), GEQ insert sel and warnings are likewise summary-only.
2) server._confirm (331-344) -> policy.guard (policy.py 347-359): with a token it calls consume_token (checks existence, TTL 300 s per device.yaml:738, action name) and then compares `stored != dict(payload)` only. _Token.summary is stored (321) but never compared. So any desk state not encoded in payload is unbound by construction.
3) Interleaved action is unconfirmed: set_send (server.py 948-961) is Tier 1, explicitly has NO relative-move limit ('a send sitting at -oo can be brought straight up'), only the 0 dB send ceiling; unmute (929-935) is Tier 1 for input channels (/ch/NN/mix/on is not in the guarded globs, device.yaml 708-721). Bus master set_fader is also Tier 1.
4) Second call: server.ring_out recomputes preflight (1751) -> ok (an extra included mic is never a blocker; provision.preflight 639-660 blocks only on GEQ invalid / no mics / bus muted / master -inf), recomputes the identical payload from identical args -> guard accepts -> cfs.ring_out (cfs.py 667-673) recomputes preflight a third time, again blockers-only, opens the session with the new mic set and raises the master. cfs.ring_out's `_expected_mics` hook (647, 685-689) is not passed by server.ring_out at all, and even for ring_out_system it only appends a warning for MISSING mics, never refuses and never flags EXTRA mics.
Executed in my worktree: token minted with summary "open mics: Ch 1 'Ch01', Ch 2 'Ch02'" (preflight.included == [1,2]); set_send('ch.3',1,-5.0,ramp_ms=0) ok (fake desk /ch/03/mix/01/level -oo -> -5.0); original token redeems, run reaches DONE at -19 dB, report preflight.included == [1,2,3] with ch3 send_db -5.0 include True. Unmute variant (ch3 pre-routed at 0 dB but muted -> listed only as warning 'sends to Bus 1 but is muted'; Tier-1 unmute; token redeems; included == [1,2,3]) also passes. Control: changing notch_budget between calls -> BAD_TOKEN, so binding works for what IS in the payload. Same-class check: set_fader bus.1 -20 -> -14 between calls; summary said 'raise its master from −20.0 dB'; token redeems and the run starts from -14 (start_master_db -14.0).
Real desk vs FakeDesk: the defect is pure Python (payload construction + dict compare); nothing depends on FakeDesk fidelity. On a real X32 the second preflight reads /node through the same Desk cache that Desk.set_send/set_mute write through, so it behaves identically; additionally a human at the console (or X32-Edit) opening a lectern/lav during the 5-minute TTL produces the same unflagged divergence, which is the more likely field path. HANDOVER §4a/§4b note the detector missed a −25 dB howl on hardware (M7), so 'the detector will catch it' is not a mitigation to lean on.
Severity re-assessment: stays MEDIUM, not high/critical. The writes the run performs (bus master trajectory ≤ min(bus_fader_max_db 0, master_ceiling_db 0), step ≤ relative limit, back-off, GEQ cuts-only) are exactly what was confirmed; no clamp, ceiling, show-mode or single-use property is defeated, and the token IS bound to action+args as DESIGN §10 specifies. What is weakened is the README:713 / docstring promise that the one confirmation 'includes the open-mic list' and README:700-701 'include is a suggestion the model must confirm with you' — the list is displayed, not enforced. Preconditions are reachable but require an interleaved Tier-1 write (or physical desk change) inside the TTL. Mitigating further: there is no CFS lock on Tier-1 send/mute writes DURING the multi-minute run either (no BUSY check in set_send/unmute/desk), so a confused client can put ch3 into the wedge mid-run regardless; binding the mic set narrows the window to 'before RAISE' but cannot close it. patch_file-by-name is hygiene only: patch content feeds owner labels/notes/warnings, never `include`.
>
> *Evidence:* Code: src/x32mcp/server.py:1755-1758 (payload keys: bus,target_gain_db,step_db,dwell_ms,notch_budget,patch_file), 1760-1766 (included_mics only in summary), 1767 (_confirm with preflight as extra only); src/x32mcp/server.py:331-344 (_confirm -> policy.guard); src/x32mcp/policy.py:310-327 (token stores summary+payload), 329-345 (consume_token checks TTL/action only), 347-359 (guard compares payload dict only); src/x32mcp/cfs.py:667-673 (run-time preflight, blockers only), 647/685-689 (_expected_mics: not passed by server.ring_out; warning-only, missing-only); src/x32mcp/provision.py:639-649 (blocker set: extra mic is not one); src/x32mcp/server.py:948-952 (set_send Tier 1, no relative limit), 929-935 (unmute Tier 1); device.yaml:708-721 (guarded globs exclude /ch/*/mix/*), 738 (confirm_token_ttl_s 300); README.md:713 ('one confirmation that includes the open-mic list'), 700-701.
Execution (worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-5-src, commit 57df7c0, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens5.py, `runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens5.py -s` -> 4 passed in 3.85 s):
- test_set_send_between_calls_adds_unconfirmed_mic: pending preflight.included==[1,2], summary contains "open mics: Ch 1 'Ch01', Ch 2 'Ch02'" and no Ch03; set_send('ch.3',1,-5.0,ramp_ms=0) ok, fake /ch/03/mix/01/level == -5.0; ring_out(...,confirm_token=T) ok, final_stage DONE, max_master_db -19.0, report preflight.included==[1,2,3], ch3 include True send_db -5.0.
- test_unmute_between_calls_adds_unconfirmed_mic: summary1 = "…open mics: Ch 1 'Ch01', Ch 2 'Ch02'. Warnings: ch.3 'Ch03': sends to Bus 1 but is muted…"; unmute('ch.3') ok (Tier 1); token redeems; report preflight.included==[1,2,3].
- test_changed_bound_arg_is_rejected_control: notch_budget 6->5 with same token -> BAD_TOKEN (binding of args works).
- test_start_master_change_between_calls_not_bound: summary 'from −20.0 dB'; set_fader('bus.1',-14.0,ramp_ms=0) ok (Tier 1); token redeems; run start_master_db -14.0, max -10.0.
Result note: $SP/reports/verify-tokens-5-src/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Bind the safety-relevant preflight state, not just the arguments: add to the ring_out payload `mics: sorted(m.ch for m in pf.included_mics)`, `geq_sel: pf.geq.insert.sel`, `fx_slot`, and `start_master: round(pf.master_db*2)/2` (0.5 dB grid so fader read-back jitter does not spuriously invalidate), so a changed live-mic set/insert/start between the calls yields BAD_TOKEN plus a fresh summary. Then pass the bound list into cfs.ring_out as `_expected_mics` and make cfs refuse (not warn) when pf.included_mics != expected in EITHER direction (extra or missing) at its own run-time preflight (cfs.py:669), which also covers a physical console change in the gap between server preflight and RAISE. For ring_out_system, compare each stage's plan 'mics' the same way and skip/abort the stage on mismatch instead of appending a warning. Optionally have Desk refuse Tier-1 send/unmute writes that target the bus under an active RINGOUT session (CfsError BUSY) so the mic set cannot change mid-run through the MCP surface. For patch_file, bind a content hash (sha256 of the loaded file) rather than the name.

## D28. [medium] ring_out accepts dwell_ms=0 (and step_db up to the 6 dB relative limit): a confirmed ring-out becomes a sub-second slam of a live monitor bus from its current level to 0 dB, faster than the detector can react
*Angle:* clamps · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1748-1752 (step>0 only; dwell _int_arg 0..60000), server.py:1813-1821 (ring_out_system stages: dwell 0..60000, step <= relative_limit_db), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:655-665 (step >= 0.1 and <= relative limit; dwell >= 0), cfs.py:1174-1197 (raise loop; _dwell returns immediately for 0), cfs.py:1115-1122 (_write_master uses ramp_ms=0)

**Mechanism.** Each raise is Desk.set_level(ramp_ms=0) followed by _dwell(dwell_ms/1000). With dwell_ms=0 and step_db=6 the loop issues consecutive un-ramped +6 dB writes limited only by OSC round trips (~20-50 ms each), so -20 -> 0 dB takes 4 writes and well under 0.5 s. Detection needs RTA frames (50 ms) x persistence plus HOLD/NOTCH/VERIFY, so no notch can land between steps: the procedure whose whole safety argument is 'raise slowly, cut as rings appear' degenerates into set_fader(bus, 0, ramp_ms=0) on a bus with open stage mics, then parks at -3 dB. It is Tier-2 confirmed and the summary does print 'in 6 dB steps (dwell 0 ms)', so an attentive operator could catch it; a runaway/confused model choosing 'fast' arguments would not.

**Exploit.** ring_out(bus=1, step_db=6, dwell_ms=0) -> confirm_token -> ring_out(bus=1, step_db=6, dwell_ms=0, confirm_token=...) ; or ring_out_system(plan={'stages':[{'bus':1,'step_db':6,'dwell_ms':0}, ...]}) with one token for all buses and Main LR.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_clamps.py::test_probe_ring_out_dwell_zero_step_six_is_a_sub_second_slam PASSES on the FakeDesk (bus 1 provisioned, master -20): desk.write values on /bus/01/mix/fader were exactly [-14.0, -8.0, -2.0, 0.0] then back-off -3.0, final_stage DONE, max_master_db 0.0, and the timestamp span of the four raises was < 0.5 s.

**Proposed fix.** Floor the cadence in cfs.ring_out (and mirror in server/_int_arg for ring_out and ring_out_system stages): dwell_ms >= max(500, persistence window = detector.persist_frames x 50 ms + decay_verify_s headroom) and step_db <= ringout.step_max_db (e.g. 2 dB) independent of the relative limit; reject rather than clamp so the summary the user confirms is what runs.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-clamps-3-repro (commit ac89ab7, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_clamps3.py, 4 tests, all PASS). The claim is correct and, if anything, understates the speed.

Code path: server.py ring_out (lines ~1748-1752) only requires step_db > 0 and dwell_ms in 0..60000; ring_out_system stages (~1813-1821) accept dwell 0..60000 and 0 < step <= policy.relative_limit_db (6); cfs.py ring_out (655-665) accepts 0.1 <= step <= relative_limit_db and dwell >= 0. The raise loop (cfs.py 1174-1197) does _write_master -> Desk.set_level(ramp_ms=0) then _dwell(0), and _dwell is asyncio.wait_for(..., timeout=0) which returns immediately. Desk._move_level applies NO relative clamp to absolute moves (desk.py ~958 comment 'No relative guard on an ABSOLUTE move'), and Desk._write (desk.py 871-884) is fire-and-forget conn.set with no read-back, so consecutive raises are separated only by event publishing, not even an OSC round trip. The token-bucket limiter (50/s, waits rather than refuses) does not slow 4-10 writes.

Measured: bus 1 provisioned, master -20 dB, cfs.ring_out(1, step_db=6, dwell_ms=0): fader writes exactly [-14.0, -8.0, -2.0, 0.0] then back-off -3.0; span of the four raises 1.1-1.4 ms (claim said <0.5 s / ~20-50 ms per write; reality is ~1 ms total because there is no round trip), whole run 353 ms, final_stage DONE, max_master_db 0.0, 0 notches even though a growing 2.4 kHz ring was injected at the first RAISE event (no RTA frame, 50 ms period, can arrive before the target is reached; the session finishes DONE and leaves the ring running with the bus parked at -3 dB). From a -60 dB start: 10 writes -54..0 dB in 3.0 ms. Via the server tool: ring_out(1, step_db=6, dwell_ms=0) mints a token (action_summary does say 'in 6 dB steps (dwell 0 ms)'), the confirmed call produced the same [-14,-8,-2,0,-3] in 1.2 ms, end -3 dB. Via ring_out_system({'stages':[{'bus':1,'step_db':6,'dwell_ms':0}]}): token minted, same slam in 1.0 ms, and the action_summary the operator confirms does NOT mention step or dwell at all (only the 'stages' extra field carries them) - an aggravating detail beyond the claim. Boundary checks hold: cfs refuses step 6.5/7 and dwell -1 (BAD_ARGUMENT); server ring_out has no upper step bound so it mints a token for step_db=7 and only cfs refuses after confirmation (wasteful, not unsafe). Preflight refuses a -oo start.

Severity: medium, agreeing with the claim. The level change is bounded by the 0 dB bus ceiling and is a Tier-2 confirmed action whose single-bus summary names the cadence, so it is not 'critical' (not unbounded, not unconfirmed) and no documented guarantee promises a minimum dwell (README only bounds step_db to 0.1..6), so not 'high'. But under reachable arguments the procedure's entire safety rationale (raise slowly, notch as rings appear) is void: it is an instantaneous +20..+60 dB step on a live monitor bus with open mics, the detector physically cannot intervene, and ring_out_system hides the cadence from the confirming operator.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-clamps-3-repro, commit ac89ab7, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_clamps3.py; run: runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_clamps3.py -s -p no:randomly -> 4 passed in 2.92s. Output: test_cfs_ring_out_dwell0_step6_is_a_sub_second_slam: 'WRITES [-14.0, -8.0, -2.0, 0.0, -3.0]', 'STAGES [PREFLIGHT, SNAPSHOT, ARM, RAISE, RAISE, RAISE, RAISE, BACKOFF, DONE]', 'RAISE SPAN 1.1 ms, total run 353 ms, notches 0' (ring injected at first RAISE never notched), fakedesk bus1 fader -2.98. test_server_ring_out_tool_accepts_dwell0_step6_through_the_dance: ACTION SUMMARY 'Ring out Bus 1 'Bus01': raise its master from -20.0 dB to 0.0 dB in 6 dB steps (dwell 0 ms)...', token minted, confirmed call 'SERVER WRITES [-14.0, -8.0, -2.0, 0.0, -3.0] final DONE max 0.0 end -3.0', 'SERVER RAISE SPAN 1.2 ms'; step_db=7: first call requires_confirmation=True (token minted), confirmed call BAD_ARGUMENT 'step_db 7 exceeds the 6 dB single-call relative move limit' from cfs; ring_out_system stage {bus:1, step_db:6, dwell_ms:0}: token minted, summary 'System ring-out of 1 stage(s) in order: Bus 1 — each bus master is raised to its target (default 0 dB)...' (no step/dwell mentioned), 'SYSTEM WRITES [-14.0, -8.0, -2.0, 0.0, -3.0]', 'SYSTEM RAISE SPAN 1.0 ms'. test_cfs_ring_out_dwell0_step6_from_minus_infinity (start -60 dB): 'WRITES [-54.0, -48.0, -42.0, -36.0, -30.0, -24.0, -18.0, -12.0, -6.0, 0.0, -3.0]', 'RAISE SPAN 3.0 ms over 10 writes'; a -90/-oo start is refused by preflight ('Bus 1 master is -oo — set a starting level first'). test_cfs_ring_out_rejects_step_above_relative_limit: step 6.5 and dwell -1 -> CfsError BAD_ARGUMENT. Source refs: src/x32mcp/server.py:1748-1752, 1813-1821; src/x32mcp/cfs.py:655-665, 1115-1133 (_write_master ramp_ms=0, _dwell wait_for timeout=0), 1174-1197; src/x32mcp/desk.py:871-884 (_write fire-and-forget, no read-back), ~952-965 (no relative clamp on absolute set_level); device.yaml policy relative_max_db 6, writes_per_second 50, ringout step_db 1.0 dwell_ms 1500.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix and sharpen it: (1) in cfs.CfsManager.ring_out reject (not clamp) dwell_ms below a floor derived from the detector, e.g. max(500, detector.persistence_frames * frame_period_ms + hold headroom) and step_db above a dedicated ringout.step_max_db (2 dB) decoupled from policy.relative_limit_db; (2) mirror the same bounds in server.ring_out and in ring_out_system stage validation so no token is minted for arguments cfs will refuse (today server.ring_out mints a token for step_db=7); (3) make ring_out_system's action_summary list each stage's target/step/dwell so the one confirmation actually describes the cadence; (4) optionally give each raise a short ramp (ramp_ms = min(dwell, policy.ramp_default_ms)) instead of ramp_ms=0 so even the permitted cadence is never a hard step on the desk.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain read end to end, no gap:
(1) server.py ring_out: `step = float(step_db); if not step > 0` (only a >0 check) and `dwell = _int_arg(dwell_ms, "dwell_ms", 0, 60_000)` — 0 is in range. ring_out_system stage loop: `dwell_ms` bounded 0..60000, `step_db` bounded 0 < step <= policy.relative_limit_db (6). (2) cfs.py CfsManager.ring_out: step >= _MIN_STEP_DB (0.1) and <= relative_limit_db (6); `if dwell < 0: raise` — 0 passes; values stored on the session. (3) cfs._run_ringout: each iteration `nxt = min(master + step, target)` → `_write_master(ses, nxt)` → `Desk.set_level(target, db, ramp_ms=0, force=False)` → desk._move_level: explicitly NO relative guard on absolute moves (comment 'Jim's call, 2026-09-20'), clamp only to the 0 dB bus ceiling, `policy.ramp_steps(before, after, 0)` returns `[to_db]` for ms<=0 → one un-ramped `_write`. `after_db` is the requested value, not a desk read-back, so the 'clamped/no progress' early exit cannot trigger. Then `_stage('RAISE')` and `_dwell(ses, 0/1000)` → `asyncio.wait_for(wake.wait(), timeout=0)` yields once and returns. The only per-raise cost is one `_leaf` GET (cache was invalidated by the previous write) plus a fire-and-forget SET; the token bucket (capacity=refill=50/s) does not delay 5 writes. (4) Loop exits with 'target reached' at 0 dB → _finalize_levels BACKOFF −3 dB → DONE → _finish → _disarm: the detector stops, so any ring the slam provoked grows unwatched at −3 dB.
Detector cannot intervene: frames arrive every 50 ms, persistence_frames=3 (≥150 ms) plus the ring must clear min_level (−45, calibrated up to floor+8) and 12 dB prominence; my run consumed exactly ONE RTA frame between ARM and DONE (report frames=1). Control run with the documented cadence holds and notches at the first raised level.
Real desk vs FakeDesk: identical logic; the only difference is the GET round trip per raise (transport.md §5.4: replies 'well under 10 ms on wired LAN', 50–100 ms budget recommended), so −20→0 dB lands in ~10–40 ms wired, <0.4 s even on poor Wi-Fi — still below one detection window. X32 applies fader OSC writes immediately (DSP gain, ms-level smoothing only). So a real desk behaves the same.
Severity stays MEDIUM, not high/critical: the move is bounded (0 dB ceiling then −3 dB), it is Tier-2 confirmed, refused in show mode, and the single-bus summary prints 'in 6 dB steps (dwell 0 ms)'. No documented dwell floor exists to defeat (README documents step 0.1..6 dB; dwell has only a default). Crucially it is not a capability escalation: Tier-1 `set_fader('bus.1', 0, ramp_ms=0)` performs the identical one-write −20→0 dB slam with NO confirmation (verified), by design. Aggravating details the claimant understated: ring_out_system's confirmation summary does not surface step/dwell at all (only the `stages` field does), and one token covers every bus plus Main LR (whose direct fader tool would need a token per call); single-bus step_db>6 is only rejected after the token is consumed (hygiene). Net: the ring-out's own safety rationale ('raise slowly, notch as rings appear') is voided by reachable arguments and the run ends with the detector disarmed on a bus it just drove to the feedback point — medium.
>
> *Evidence:* Code: src/x32mcp/server.py:1748-1752 (ring_out: `if not step > 0`; `_int_arg(dwell_ms,'dwell_ms',0,60_000)`), server.py:1811-1821 (ring_out_system stage bounds: dwell 0..60000, 0<step<=relative_limit_db), server.py:1826-1831 (system summary text has no step/dwell); src/x32mcp/cfs.py:655-666 (step 0.1..6, `if dwell < 0`), cfs.py:1115-1124 (_write_master → set_level(ramp_ms=0) / set_main_level(ramp_ms=0)), cfs.py:1126-1133 (_dwell returns after a zero-timeout wait), cfs.py:1146-1197 (raise loop; after_db is the requested value), cfs.py:978 (_finish → _disarm); src/x32mcp/desk.py:948-980 (_move_level: 'No relative guard on an ABSOLUTE move', ramp_steps(ms=0) → single write), desk.py:871-884 (_write: tier check + acquire_write + conn.set); src/x32mcp/policy.py:430-441 (ramp_steps: ms<=0 → [to_db]); device.yaml policy.relative_max_db 6, writes_per_second 50, ringout.dwell_ms 1500 default only, detector.persistence_frames 3, decay_verify_s 1.5; docs/research/transport.md:350-358 (real-desk reply latency <10 ms wired).
Executed (worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-clamps-3-src, commit 5e9bc15, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_clamps3.py, 5 passed in 6.5 s):
- test_fast_dwell0_step6_outruns_the_detector: closed loop, 20 dB/s ring injected at the first RAISE. Stages PREFLIGHT,SNAPSHOT,ARM,RAISE,RAISE,RAISE,RAISE,BACKOFF,DONE; /bus/01/mix/fader writes [-14.0,-8.0,-2.0,0.0,-3.0]; span of the 4 raises 0.0011 s; report frames=1, notches=[], gain_before_feedback_db=None, max 0.0, end −3.0; CFS idle and the ring still growing (−23 dB) 0.5 s after DONE; GEQ band 22 still flat.
- test_control_default_cadence_catches_the_same_ring (step 1, dwell 500): RAISE,HOLD,NOTCH,VERIFY after the FIRST raise (gain_before_feedback −19.0), notch band 22 −3 dB, DONE.
- test_cfs_layer_bounds: step 6.01 and dwell −1 raise CfsError; step 6/dwell 0 runs DONE.
- test_server_ring_out_accepts_dwell0_step6_and_runs_after_one_token: pending summary 'Ring out Bus 1 'Bus01': raise its master from −20.0 dB to 0.0 dB in 6 dB steps (dwell 0 ms)…'; with token → DONE, writes [-14,-8,-2,0,-3], span 0.001 s, fake fader −3.0. ring_out_system({'stages':[{'bus':1,'step_db':6,'dwell_ms':0},{'bus':'main','step_db':6,'dwell_ms':0}]}) mints one token; its summary contains no 'dwell'/step text. step_db 6.5 stage → BAD_ARGUMENT; single-bus step 7 mints a token and is refused only on the confirmed call.
- test_context_tier1_set_fader_already_slams_a_bus_unconfirmed: srv.set_fader('bus.1', 0.0, ramp_ms=0) from −20 → ok, steps=1, no confirmation, fake fader 0.0; set_main_fader('st',0,ramp_ms=0) requires a token.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with rejecting (not clamping) so the confirmed summary is what runs, but tie the floor to the detector rather than a magic 500: in cfs.ring_out require dwell_ms >= ceil((detector.persistence_frames + detector.decay_verify_frames) * rta_period_ms) + cooldown headroom (≈ 300–500 ms with current device.yaml) and add ringout.step_max_db (e.g. 2–3 dB) checked independently of policy.relative_limit_db; mirror both in server.ring_out/_int_arg and the ring_out_system stage validator so refusal happens BEFORE a token is minted (today single-bus step>6 is only refused after confirmation). Also: (a) make ring_out_system's confirmation summary list per-stage target/step/dwell, since one token covers every bus and Main LR; (b) consider ramping each raise over min(dwell, ramp_default_ms) instead of ramp_ms=0 so even a coarse step is not a hard edge; (c) note the residual: Tier-1 set_fader(bus, 0, ramp_ms=0) reaches the same state unconfirmed, so if sub-second bus slams are the concern the ramp_ms=0 allowance on bus/main absolute moves is the larger hole.

## D29. [medium] DCA fader ceiling is +5 dB (policy uses the channel ceiling) although device.yaml declares dca fader clamp max 0 dB; the yaml `clamp:` on level params is never enforced anywhere
*Angle:* clamps · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/policy.py:243-252 (level_ceiling_db: dca -> ch_fader_max_db; docstring line 13-14 says '+10 dB top like a channel'), device.yaml:272-274 (dca fader clamp {max: 0} '0 dB ceiling'), device.yaml:724 (ch_fader_max_db 5), src/x32mcp/descriptor.py:15-18,364-380 (to_raw ignores clamp_min/max by design; ParamSpec.clamp exists but Desk never calls it), src/x32mcp/desk.py:955 (_move_level uses policy.clamp_level only)

**Mechanism.** set_fader('dca.N', x) -> _move_level -> policy.clamp_level(Target('dca',N)) returns min(x, 5.0); ramp -> spec.to_raw(db) converts on the fader scale (physical top +10) without applying spec.clamp_max=0, so +5 dB reaches /dca/N/fader. A DCA is a multiplicative offset on every member channel post-fader, so DCA +5 on channels already at their +5 ceiling is +10 dB over unity into buses/mains from two Tier-1 calls, and the descriptor's stated 0 dB group-master ceiling is silently not honoured (the two sources of truth disagree; policy.py's comment even cites +10).

**Exploit.** set_fader('dca.1', 10, ramp_ms=0) -> ok, after_db 5.0 (clamped.limit 5.0); combine with set_fader('ch.N', inf, ramp_ms=0) on the DCA's members.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_clamps.py::test_exploit_dca_ceiling_is_plus5_not_the_declared_0 PASSES: descriptor.param('dca','fader').clamp_max == 0.0, yet set_fader('dca.1', 10.0, ramp_ms=0) returned ok with after_db 5.0 / clamped.limit 5.0 and fakedesk /dca/1/fader == +5.0.

**Proposed fix.** Give DCAs their own ceiling: add policy.dca_fader_max_db: 0 to device.yaml and map fam=='dca' to it in Policy.level_ceiling_db (or reuse bus_fader_max_db). More robustly, have Policy.clamp_level take min(family ceiling, spec.clamp_max) — pass the ParamSpec from _move_level — so device.yaml clamp declarations can never be looser in code than on paper; add a descriptor self-test that policy ceilings <= the yaml clamp for every fader/send spec.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-clamps-4-repro (commit dc53cd5), test file tests/integration/test_verify_clamps4.py — 5/5 pass against unmodified code (FakeDesk + App via make_app, tools called as srv.set_fader/adjust_fader).

Mechanism verified exactly as claimed:
- src/x32mcp/policy.py:243-252 level_ceiling_db(): family 'dca' falls through to `return self.ch_fader_max_db` (5.0 from device.yaml:724); module docstring policy.py:13-14 states this is deliberate ("dca the channel ceiling … +10 dB top like a channel").
- device.yaml:274 declares `dca: fader: {… clamp: {max: 0}}  # group master; 0 dB ceiling (DESIGN silent)`; Descriptor exposes it (descriptor.param('dca','fader').clamp_max == 0.0, also pinned by tests/test_descriptor.py:140) and ParamSpec.clamp(5.0) returns 0.0 — but nothing in src/ ever calls ParamSpec.clamp or reads clamp_max/clamp_min (grep of src/x32mcp/*.py: only descriptor.py defines them; desk.py:_write_value/_run_ramp use spec.to_raw, which descriptor.py:364-367 documents as NOT applying clamp_min/max). desk.py:955 _move_level bounds the destination solely with policy.clamp_level.
- So every `clamp:` on a level param in device.yaml is dead configuration; for ch/auxin/fxrtn (5), bus/mtx/main (0), sends and mlevel (0) the yaml value happens to equal the `policy:` block, and dca is the only family where they diverge today (my scope test enumerates all nine level specs: only ('dca','fader', yaml 0.0, policy 5.0) mismatches).

Observed behaviour: set_fader('dca.1', 10.0, ramp_ms=0) -> ok, address /dca/1/fader, after_db 5.0, clamped.limit 5.0, summary "DCA 1 'DCA1' fader 0.0 dB → +5.0 dB (clamped to the +5.0 dB limit, requested +10.0 dB)"; FakeDesk /dca/1/fader raw 0.87488 = +4.995 dB. Ramped variant (dca.2, 100 ms) and the relative path adjust_fader('dca.3', +5) land at +5 too. Stacking: set_fader('ch.1', inf) -> +5 (inf is accepted and clamped) plus set_fader('dca.1', 10) -> +5 gives ch+dca = +9.99 dB effective post-fader gain from two unconfirmed Tier-1 calls, no force needed (absolute moves have no relative guard, desk.py:960-966).

Severity: I keep medium, with a nuance the claim understates. The USER-FACING contract says +5 for DCAs everywhere: README.md:487 and :573 ("channel/aux/FX-return/DCA faders ≤ +5 dB"), the set_fader tool docstring server.py:882-883, policy.py:13-14, and tests/test_policy.py:132 (Target('dca',3), 7.0 -> 5.0). So this is not a bypass of a documented guarantee (not 'high'); it is (a) two sources of truth that disagree, with the tighter one (device.yaml, described by DESIGN §7 as 'the only place device knowledge lives' and whose header line 15 advertises `clamp: {min,max}`) silently losing, and (b) a latent trap: an operator who tightens any fader/send `clamp:` in device.yaml to protect a show gets no protection at all. The level consequence is bounded (+5 on a group master, +10 combined with a member channel, versus a physical +10/+20), ramped, and Tier-1-by-design, but a DCA typically spans many channels so +5 on it is a whole-mix +5 dB into mains that the descriptor author intended to cap at 0.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-clamps-4-repro (branch wt/verify-clamps-4-repro, commit dc53cd5). Test: tests/integration/test_verify_clamps4.py (copy at $SP/reports/verify-clamps-4-repro/). Run: `$SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_verify_clamps4.py -s` -> 5 passed in 1.72s. Captured output:
RESULT {'target': 'dca.1', 'address': '/dca/1/fader', 'before_db': 0.0, 'after_db': 5.0, 'requested_db': 10.0, 'clamped': {'value': 5.0, 'requested': 10.0, 'limit': 5.0, 'reason': 'CLAMPED_TO_LIMIT: DCA 1 fader ceiling is +5.0 dB'}, 'summary': "DCA 1 'DCA1' fader 0.0 dB → +5.0 dB (clamped to the +5.0 dB limit, requested +10.0 dB)"}
FAKEDESK before -0.0098 after 4.9951 raw 0.8748778
CH inf -> True 5.0 None; effective offset ch+dca = 9.990
Scope table (family, rel, yaml clamp_max, policy ceiling): ch mix/fader 5.0/5.0 ok; auxin 5.0/5.0 ok; fxrtn 5.0/5.0 ok; bus 0.0/0.0 ok; mtx 0.0/0.0 ok; main 0.0/0.0 ok; dca fader 0.0/5.0 MISMATCH; ch mix/01/level 0.0/0.0 ok; ch mix/mlevel 0.0/0.0 ok.
Assertions that passed: descriptor.param('dca','fader').clamp_max == 0.0; policy.level_ceiling_db(Target('dca',1)) == 5.0; ParamSpec.clamp(5.0) == 0.0 (would have limited it if called); grep src/x32mcp for clamp_max|clamp_min|.clamp( outside descriptor.py/scales -> no hits (only fakedesk's scale.clamp). Code refs: policy.py:243-252, policy.py:13-14, desk.py:955, desk.py:886-895 (_write_value uses to_raw), desk.py:936-941 (_run_ramp uses to_raw), descriptor.py:15-18 and 364-380, device.yaml:274 and :724, README.md:487/:573, server.py:882-883, tests/test_policy.py:132, tests/test_descriptor.py:140.
>
> *Better fix:* Pick one source of truth and make the other provably follow it. Minimal: add `dca_fader_max_db: 0` (or reuse bus_fader_max_db) under `policy:` in device.yaml, map fam=='dca' to it in Policy.level_ceiling_db (policy.py:243-252), fix the docstring at policy.py:13-14, README.md:487/573, server.py set_fader docstring and tests/test_policy.py:132 — OR, if +5 is the intended DCA ceiling, change device.yaml:274 to `clamp: {max: 5}` so the descriptor stops lying. Robust: make Policy.clamp_level take the ParamSpec (Desk._move_level already has it) and apply min(family ceiling, spec.clamp_max) / max(floor, spec.clamp_min), so a yaml clamp can only ever tighten; and add a Descriptor.load()/Policy.__init__ self-check that raises DescriptorError when any fader/send/mlevel spec's clamp_max is below the policy ceiling that would govern it (turns the current silent disagreement into a startup failure). Either way delete the now-misleading sentence at descriptor.py:15-18 ('so that policy.py can report a clamp') unless policy.py actually consumes clamp_min/max.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap. server.set_fader(target,db) (src/x32mcp/server.py:881-896) -> desk.resolve('dca.1') -> Desk.set_level (desk.py:983-995) -> _level_param picks spec dca:fader, address /dca/1/fader, policy kind 'fader' (desk.py:399-418) -> _move_level (desk.py:948-980): tier_for('/dca/1/fader') = 1 (spec tier 1; no guarded glob in device.yaml:709-722 matches), then policy.clamp_level(Target('dca',1), db, kind='fader') -> level_ceiling_db (policy.py:243-252) falls through to `return self.ch_fader_max_db  # ch, auxin, fxrtn, dca` = 5.0 (device.yaml:724). ramp_steps -> _run_ramp -> spec.to_raw(5.0) (descriptor.py:364-367: scale conversion only, explicitly NOT clamp_min/max) -> _write -> conn.set('/dca/1/fader', 0.875..). Nothing on this path (or anywhere in src/: grep for clamp_max/clamp_min/.clamp( finds only descriptor.py itself and fakedesk's scale.clamp) ever reads ParamSpec.clamp_max, so device.yaml:274 `dca: fader: {... clamp: {max: 0}}  # group master; 0 dB ceiling` is dead data at runtime. adjust_fader takes the same _move_level path. There is no other tool that writes /dca/N/fader. Policy has no dca-specific ceiling key at all (policy.py:187-190; descriptor.py:956 validates only ch/bus/main/send/eq keys).

The disagreement is real and both sides are codified: tests/test_policy.py:132 asserts Target('dca',3) 7.0 -> 5.0, while tests/test_descriptor.py:140 and :546 assert dca fader clamp_max == 0 and clamp(1) == 0. policy.py:13-14 documents the +5 choice as a 'DESIGN silent' decision (its '+10 dB top' phrase refers to the physical scale top, used to justify borrowing the channel ceiling, not the ceiling itself), and the LLM-facing tool doc server.py:882 says 'channels/aux/fx-returns/DCAs clamp at +5 dB'. DESIGN.md §10 (line 449: 'applies ch/bus/main/send ceilings') and BRIEF.md §4 (line 63) never mention a DCA ceiling. descriptor.py:15-18 says the yaml clamps are exposed 'so that policy.py can report a clamp' — i.e. the design intent was for policy to honour them, and it does not. For every other level spec the yaml clamp and the policy number happen to coincide (verified programmatically: ch/auxin/fxrtn mix/fader 5==5, bus/mtx/main mix/fader 0==0, ch/auxin/fxrtn sends 0==0, mlevel 0==send 0), so dca:fader is the only practical divergence; the general statement 'yaml clamp on level params is never enforced' is true but only bites here.

Real desk vs FakeDesk: identical. docs/research/scales_params.md §2 (line 249, VERIFIED) puts /dca/N/fader on the same 1024-step fader taper 'level [0.0...1.0(+10dB)]' as channel faders (example '/dca/1/fader +10.0' at line 297), so raw 0.875 lands the real DCA at +5.0 dB exactly as the FakeDesk did. On an X32 a DCA is a VCA-style offset added to every member channel's fader gain (affects LR/post-fader sends; pre-fader wedge sends are unaffected), so ch +5 (Tier 1) plus DCA +5 (Tier 1) = +10 dB over unity on a whole group from two unconfirmed, unforced calls; with the yaml's declared 0 dB DCA ceiling the stack would top out at +5.

Severity: medium is right (lower half of medium). Not critical/high: the move is bounded (+5 on the DCA, masters still capped at 0), ramped 300 ms by default, reported as CLAMPED_TO_LIMIT, and no DESIGN/BRIEF guarantee names a DCA ceiling — the only place '0 dB' is promised is the device.yaml comment, contradicted by policy.py's own documented decision and the tool docstring. Not low: it is 5 dB of extra unconfirmed gain on a group master that multiplies across every member channel, and it is exactly the two-sources-of-truth drift (descriptor says 0, policy says 5, both with passing tests) that a safety layer should not carry; a future edit tightening a yaml clamp would silently do nothing.
>
> *Evidence:* Executed in own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-clamps-4-src (commit d530de8), tests/integration/test_verify_dca_ceiling.py, 4 passed: (1) test_dca_ceiling_sources_disagree: descriptor.param('dca','fader').clamp_max == 0.0 and .clamp(5.0) == 0.0 while policy.level_ceiling_db(Target('dca',1)) == 5.0; for ch/auxin/fxrtn/bus/mtx/main faders and ch/auxin/fxrtn sends yaml clamp_max == policy ceiling (DCA is the only divergence). (2) test_set_fader_dca_reaches_plus5_on_desk: await srv.set_fader('dca.1', 10.0, ramp_ms=0) -> ok True, after_db 5.0, clamped {'limit': 5.0, 'reason': 'CLAMPED_TO_LIMIT: DCA 1 fader ceiling is +5.0 dB'}; FakeDesk /dca/1/fader before -0.01 dB, after +4.995 dB. (3) test_adjust_fader_dca_also_plus5: adjust_fader('dca.2', +6, ramp_ms=0) from 0.0 -> ok, after_db 5.0, desk +5.0. (4) test_stack_ch_plus5_and_dca_plus5: set_fader('ch.1', inf, 0) and set_fader('dca.1', inf, 0) both ok, both land at +5.0 on the desk with no force/confirmation. Code refs: policy.py:243-252 (dca -> ch_fader_max_db), policy.py:13-14 (documented decision), device.yaml:274 (clamp max 0) vs :724 (ch_fader_max_db 5), descriptor.py:15-18 and :364-380 (to_raw ignores clamp; ParamSpec.clamp unused by runtime — grep of src/ finds no caller), desk.py:955 (only clamp applied), server.py:882 docstring ('DCAs clamp at +5 dB'), tests/test_policy.py:132 vs tests/test_descriptor.py:140,546 (contradictory expectations both passing), docs/research/scales_params.md:249,297 (real /dca/N/fader = same 1024-step +10 dB taper, so real hardware behaves identically).
>
> *Better fix:* Make the descriptor the single source of truth instead of adding yet another policy key: in Desk._move_level pass the ParamSpec into Policy.clamp_level and have it apply limit = min(family ceiling, spec.clamp_max) (and floor = max(-90, spec.clamp_min) when set), so a yaml clamp can only ever tighten code. Then either (a) keep device.yaml dca fader clamp {max: 0} and change policy.py:252/docstring, server.py:882 docstring and tests/test_policy.py:132 to 0 dB, or (b) if +5 on DCAs is genuinely wanted, change device.yaml:274 to {max: 5} — but decide once. Add a descriptor/policy self-test (tests/test_policy.py) that iterates every fader/send/mlevel spec across all strip families and asserts policy.level_ceiling_db(target, kind) <= spec.clamp_max, so the two tables can never drift again; optionally have Descriptor.load raise DescriptorError when policy.ch/bus/main/send ceilings exceed the corresponding spec clamps.

## D30. [medium] restore_snapshot writes the Main LR mix section (fader + mute + pan) as one un-ramped node line with no ceiling/relative check, is allowed in show mode, and its writes are invisible to the write/desk.write event stream
*Angle:* clamps · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/desk.py:1312-1341 (restore: conn.slash per section line, acquire_write only; no ramp, no clamp, no tier check), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:1201-1227 (restore_plan renders whole sections from the snapshot, mix section = on+fader+st+pan in one line, mix ordered before eq/dyn), src/x32mcp/server.py:1238-1267 (payload binds {id, scope}; show mode not checked by design), policy.py:27-30 (restore exempt from SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED), src/x32mcp/connection.py:520-542 (set() publishes 'write'; slash() does not)

**Mechanism.** The token-gated 'undo' replays snapshot values verbatim: a snapshot taken at sound-check with Main LR at 0 dB/unmuted, restored (scope 'main.st' or whole desk) while the main sits at -oo and muted (e.g. after panic or at doors), un-mutes and jumps the PA to 0 dB in a single OSC message — the one main-level path with neither the 300 ms ramp nor clamp_level, and show mode permits it with one confirmation. Values above policy ceilings in the snapshot (operator had a channel at +10 on the surface) are restored as-is. Because conn.slash does not publish 'write' and Desk.restore publishes only the summary desk.restore event, the dashboard/event log shows no per-address writes for the largest write burst the server can produce. The preview (count + 15 lines) and token are the only mitigation; payload binds snapshot id+scope, not the previewed diff, so desk drift between preview and confirm is restored unannounced.

**Exploit.** snapshot exists from earlier (auto-pre-write or snapshot_desk); main currently down/muted; restore_snapshot('latest', scope='main.st') -> token -> restore_snapshot('latest', scope='main.st', confirm_token=tok) -> Main LR 0 dB and unmuted in one write, in show mode. An LLM asked to 'undo the panic' will naturally do exactly this.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_clamps.py::test_probe_restore_slams_main_fader_unramped_in_show_mode PASSES: snapshot at main 0 dB/unmuted; fakedesk main set to -inf and muted; show_mode on; preview count 2; confirmed restore returned written 1, lines 1; fakedesk /main/st/mix/fader == 0.0 and mix/on True afterwards; a subscriber to {'desk.write','write'} events captured zero events during the restore.

**Proposed fix.** In Desk.restore special-case level fields: for */mix/fader, */mix/NN/level, /dca/*/fader (and main) route through _move_level with the default ramp and policy.clamp_level instead of the raw section line (write the rest of the section via slash with the fader field set to the CURRENT value first, then ramp), and restore mutes last (mix/on after fader) for upward moves. Publish a desk.write (or 'write') event per slash line. Optionally bind the token to a hash of the previewed change list so a changed desk re-prompts, and in show mode cap restore to scope != whole-desk or require the diff to contain no upward main/bus level change without a second token.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-clamps-5-repro (tests/integration/test_redteam_verify5.py, committed 4cb4a44; 4 passed). Every element of the claim holds against the real code on a FakeDesk via the server tools:

1. Un-ramped, single-datagram Main LR un-mute + level jump, allowed in show mode with one token. Snapshot taken with /main/st at 0 dB/unmuted; real panic() muted main; surface pulled fader to -oo; show_mode(True); restore_snapshot(id, scope='main.st') -> pending (count 2, sections 1) -> confirm -> written 1/lines 1. A datagram tap on the fake desk saw exactly ONE message touching /main/st/mix: address '/' with the node line '/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0' (mute-off and 0 dB fader in the same OSC message, no intermediate steps). Desk ends at mix/on True, fader 0.0. Path: server.py:1240-1267 (_confirm checks show mode but 'restore_snapshot' is not in policy.SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED, policy.py:75) -> desk.py:1312-1341 Desk.restore (acquire_write + conn.slash per section line; no tier check, no clamp_level, no ramp_steps) -> nodes.py:1201-1227 restore_plan (whole /main/st/mix section = on+fader+pan rendered verbatim from the snapshot; _SECTION_RANK mix=3 < eq=5 < dyn=6 so levels come back before EQ/dyn on strip restores).
   Contrast in the same test: set_main_fader('st', 0.0) for the identical -oo->0 move required its own token AND sent 15 ramped /main/st/mix/fader SETs with 30 write/desk.write events.

2.
