No clamp: with the operator having had ch1 and Main LR at +10 dB on the surface at snapshot time, set_fader('ch.1', 10) clamps to +5 and set_main_fader('st', 10) clamps to 0 (both report clamped), but a confirmed whole-desk restore_snapshot writes ch1 = +10.0 (> ch_fader_max_db 5) and main = +10.0 (> main_fader_max_db 0) verbatim.

3. Invisible to the write stream: subscriber on {'write','desk.write','desk.restore','policy.confirmed'} during the restore captured only policy.confirmed and one desk.restore {lines:1, written:1}; zero 'write'/'desk.write' events (connection.py:520-530 set() publishes 'write'; slash() at 542-554 does not; Desk.restore publishes only the summary). This contradicts DESIGN.md §13 'Every write publishes events.publish("write", ...)'.

4. Token binds {id, scope}, not the previewed diff: preview with only the mute differing (count 1, preview text has no 'fader'); operator then pulls main to -oo; confirming the same token executes the never-previewed -oo -> 0 dB jump with the un-mute, no re-prompt. (What IS enforced, also tested: a main.st token cannot run a whole-desk restore -> BAD_TOKEN, and the token is burned.)

Additional aggravation found while reproducing (not in the claim): the confirmation preview prints the diff in snapshot->live direction (server.py:1249 uses _diff = diff_states(snap.state, live), Change.before=snapshot, after=live, nodes.py:1144), under the heading '... setting(s) would change:'. The operator is shown "- Main LR 'Main' unmuted → muted / fader 0.0 dB → −∞ dB" for an action that will UNMUTE and raise the PA to 0 dB — the sole safety gate describes the opposite of what will happen.

Severity: kept at medium. It is a confirmed Tier-2 action (token required, single-use, bound to id+scope, refused on scope swap), restore-as-undo in show mode and bit-faithful section replay are explicit design decisions (DESIGN.md restore_plan comment, policy.py docstring), and the restored values are ones the desk verifiably held earlier. It does not defeat panic or run unconfirmed. But it is the one main-level path with neither the 300 ms ramp nor clamp_level, it breaks the documented 'every write publishes write' guarantee (dashboard/event log blind to the largest burst the server emits), the token does not cover desk drift after preview, and the preview direction is inverted — so the human confirmation, the only mitigation, is weaker than it looks. An LLM told to 'undo the panic' will naturally reach for exactly this call.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-clamps-5-repro (branch wt/verify-clamps-5-repro, commit 4cb4a44). Test file: tests/integration/test_redteam_verify5.py; run log: $SP/reports/verify-clamps-5-repro/run.txt -> '4 passed in 3.15s'.
Captured output (run with -s):
 test_restore_slams_main_unramped_in_show_mode_and_is_invisible: PREVIEW: 2 1 "- Main LR 'Main' unmuted → muted\n- Main LR 'Main' fader 0.0 dB → −∞ dB"; RESULT: 'Restored snapshot ...-soundcheck scope main.st: 1/1 section(s) written in 3 ms'; MAIN WRITES: [('/', ['/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0'])]; EVENTS: [('policy.confirmed', {...}), ('desk.restore', {'lines': 1, 'written': 1, 'failed': 0, 'scope': 'main.st'})] (no 'write'/'desk.write'); afterwards fakedesk /main/st/mix/on == True, fader == 0.0, show_mode still True. SANCTIONED PATH (set_main_fader st 0.0 from -oo): 15 fader SETs, 30 write events, steps reported 15.
 test_restore_writes_levels_above_policy_ceilings: set_fader ch.1 10 -> after_db 5.0 clamped; set_main_fader st 10 -> after_db 0.0 clamped; then whole-desk restore_snapshot (token) -> 'after restore: ch1 10.0 main 10.0'.
 test_token_binds_id_and_scope_not_the_previewed_diff: PREVIEW: 1 "- Main LR 'Main' unmuted → muted"; main fader pulled to -inf after preview; confirm with same token -> ok, written 1; desk main on True, fader 0.0.
 test_token_reuse_and_scope_swap_are_refused: main.st token used for scope=None -> BAD_TOKEN; reuse -> BAD_TOKEN; desk unchanged.
Code refs: src/x32mcp/desk.py:1312-1341 (restore: acquire_write + conn.slash, no clamp/ramp/tier), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:112-115 (_SECTION_RANK mix before eq/dyn), 1201-1227 (restore_plan renders full section from snapshot), src/x32mcp/server.py:1240-1267 (payload={'id','scope'}; preview = describe_changes(diff_states(snap, live)) i.e. snapshot→live direction under 'would change'), src/x32mcp/policy.py:75 (SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED excludes restore_snapshot), src/x32mcp/connection.py:520-530 vs 542-554 (set publishes 'write', slash does not); DESIGN.md line 588 'Every write publishes events.publish("write", …)'.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix (route */mix/fader, */mix/NN/level, /dca/*/fader, /main/*/mix/fader through _move_level with ramp + policy.clamp_level, write mix/on last on upward moves, publish a desk.write per slash line, bind the token to a hash of the previewed change list). Two additions: (1) fix the preview direction in restore_snapshot — render live→snapshot (swap diff_states args or invert Change before/after for the restore summary) so 'would change' describes what the write will do; today it shows the exact opposite for the main un-mute/raise. (2) In show mode, if the plan contains an upward main/bus/mtx level change or a main un-mute, either refuse without scope or surface it as a separate highlighted line in the summary ('WILL UNMUTE Main LR and raise it −∞ → 0.0 dB, un-ramped') and require the diff-hash-bound token, so drift between preview and confirm re-prompts.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain checked link by link; no gap found.

1. Entry: server.py:1240-1260 restore_snapshot → _confirm("restore_snapshot", summary, payload={id, scope}, token). _confirm (server.py:331-345) calls policy.check_show_mode_allows("restore_snapshot"); SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED (policy.py:66-68) = {scene_recall, scene_save, setup_ringout_eqs, ring_out, ring_out_system} — restore is deliberately exempt (policy.py module doc lines 27-30, DESIGN.md §10 line 455, tool docstring "Allowed in show mode", and tests/integration/test_server_tools.py:386-389 asserts it). policy.guard (policy.py:347-361) binds the token to action + payload {id, scope} only; the change list is computed for the summary but is not part of the bound payload, so desk drift between preview and confirm is not detected.
2. Executor: Desk.restore (desk.py:1312-1341): ensure_pre_write_snapshot → dump → nodes.restore_plan → per line: policy.acquire_write() then conn.slash(line). No tier_for, no clamp_level, no ramp_steps, no check_relative anywhere on this path (by design: DESIGN §13 lists restore under "Tier 2 executors (server does the confirmation dance; these just execute)" and describes it as bit-faithful node-line replay).
3. restore_plan (nodes.py:1201-1227) emits one line per differing SECTION rendered from the snapshot values; device.yaml:643 defines /main/st/mix = [mix/on, mix/fader, mix/pan] (claim said on+fader+st+pan — it is on+fader+pan; immaterial). So mute state and fader travel in ONE datagram: observed line '/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0'. _SECTION_RANK puts mix(3) before eq/dyn; families ordered ch, auxin, fxrtn, bus, mtx, main… so in a whole-desk restore the main line comes late but is still one un-ramped write.
4. Events: connection.set (connection.py:520-530) publishes 'write'; connection.slash (542-555) publishes nothing; Desk._write publishes 'desk.write' (desk.py:884) but restore never calls _write — it publishes only the aggregate 'desk.restore {snapshot, lines, written, failed, scope}' (desk.py:1336). DESIGN §13 line 597 states "Every write publishes events.publish('write', …)", so the per-address audit gap is a real deviation.
5. Ceilings: snapshot values are replayed verbatim, so a main or channel fader captured at +10 dB (hardware max) is restored to +10, which set_main_fader (clamp main_fader_max_db 0) and set_fader (ch max +5) can never produce. Values are bounded by a genuine prior desk state (operator-origin, ≤ +10 dB), not LLM-chosen — this is why it is not critical.
6. Real desk vs FakeDesk: the '/ ,s "<path> v1 v2 …"' multi-field write and its verbatim echo are DOC-CONFIRMED (docs/research/transport.md §6.6, DOC 4560-4593, e.g. 'ch/01 newname 10 CY 1'), and HANDOVER.md M5 (line 116) verified restore on the real X32: "restore wrote 3 sections in 9 ms, diff afterwards clean". An OSC fader/mute set on the X32 takes effect immediately (no desk-side slew; the M5 ramp test shows the desk follows each step), so a real console un-mutes and jumps Main LR to the snapshot level within one datagram exactly as the fake does — with a live PA the consequence is worse, not better.

Severity context (why medium, not high/critical): it is a confirmed Tier-2 action (token single-use, action+payload bound, TTL) and the level it lands on is a value the desk genuinely held earlier. Also 'un-ramped main jump with one token' is not unique to restore: set_main_fader accepts ramp_ms=0 (verified: -oo→0 dB in 1 step with one token), and after panic() (which only writes mix/on) set_main_mute(False) is a one-token full-level un-mute. What IS unique to restore and weakens documented guarantees: (a) fader+un-mute coalesced into one instantaneous write, (b) no ceiling (main +10 reachable), (c) preview capped at 15 lines in ch-first order so a whole-desk restore with ≥15 channel changes hides the Main LR lines behind "… N more" (verified: 18 changes, Main absent from action_summary), (d) drift after preview restored unannounced (verified), (e) zero per-address write events. Additional observation strengthening the claim: the preview reuses diff_states(snap, live) so its arrows read snapshot→current ("Main LR 'Main' unmuted → muted", "fader 0.0 dB → −∞ dB") under the header "would change:" — the opposite direction of what the restore will do, so an operator skimming it sees 'gets quieter' and approves a write that un-mutes and raises the PA to 0 dB. Not by itself a policy bypass, hence medium stands.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-clamps-5-src (branch wt/verify-clamps-5-src, commit 7b86719), tests/integration/test_verify_restore_main.py — 5 passed; JSON records in $SP/reports/verify-clamps-5-src/.
v1 (exploit, show mode on, scope main.st): snapshot at 0 dB/unmuted; fakedesk main set to -inf + muted; pending count=2, action_summary = "…scope main.st: 2 setting(s) in 1 section(s) would change:\n- Main LR 'Main' unmuted → muted\n- Main LR 'Main' fader 0.0 dB → −∞ dB" (arrows reversed w.r.t. the action); confirmed call → result lines=1 written=1 in 2.3 ms; captured conn.slash line = ['/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0']; desk after: fader -0.01 dB, on=True; events captured with types {desk.write, write, desk.restore, policy.confirmed} = only policy.confirmed + desk.restore{lines:1,written:1} — zero write/desk.write.
v2 (above ceiling): main and ch1 at +10 on the surface at snapshot; set_main_fader(10, ramp_ms=0)+token → after_db 0.0 clamped limit 0.0; set_fader(ch.1, 10) → 5.0; whole-desk restore → slash lines ['/ch/01/mix ON +10.0 ON +0 OFF -oo', '/main/st/mix ON +10.0 +0'], desk after main=10.0, ch1=10.0.
v3 (drift): whole-desk preview count=1 ("Ch 1 fader 0.0 → −6.0", no Main mentioned); main pulled to -inf+muted after the preview; confirm with the same token → lines=2 incl. '/main/st/mix ON 0.0 +0'; main 0 dB unmuted, never shown to the user.
v4 (preview cap): 16 channel faders moved + main -inf/muted → count=18, action_summary lists Ch1..Ch15 then "… 3 more", 'Main LR' absent; confirmed restore → main 0 dB unmuted.
v5 (context): set_main_fader('st', 0.0, ramp_ms=0) with one token → steps=1, ramp_ms=0, main -inf→0 in one write, desk.write event present.
Code refs: server.py:331-345 (_confirm), 1240-1260 (payload={id,scope}); policy.py:66-68 (SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED), 347-361 (guard binds payload only); desk.py:1312-1341 (restore: acquire_write + conn.slash only; event desk.restore at 1336); nodes.py:1201-1227 (restore_plan), 112-115 (_SECTION_RANK), 1125-1130/1068-1110 (diff direction before=snapshot, after=live); device.yaml:643 (/main/st/mix = on fader pan), 726 (main_fader_max_db 0); connection.py:520-530 vs 542-555 (set publishes 'write', slash does not); docs/research/transport.md §6.6 + HANDOVER.md:116 (real-desk '/' multi-field write verified).
>
> *Better fix:* Keep restore as the show-mode undo but make the main/bus output sections safe and visible: (1) in Desk.restore, split any */mix (and /dca/N) section whose target fader/level rises: write the section via slash with the fader token replaced by the CURRENT live value, then move the level through _move_level (default ramp, policy.clamp_level unless an explicit allow_above_ceiling flag is confirmed), and for main/bus/mtx write mix/on (un-mute) AFTER the fader ramp; downward moves and mutes can stay verbatim. (2) Publish a desk.write (address=section path, value=line, tool='restore_snapshot', tier=max tier of its fields) per slash line so the dashboard/event log shows the burst. (3) Bind the token to a digest of the planned lines (or at least of the set of sections + any main/bus/mtx mix/on→ON or level-increase), recompute at confirm time and re-prompt with BAD_TOKEN/'desk changed since preview' if it differs. (4) Fix the preview: render restore changes as live→snapshot (what WILL happen), sort output-master (main/mtx/bus) level increases and un-mutes to the top of the 15-line preview and flag them ("⚠ Main LR −∞ → 0.0 dB and UN-MUTE"), never truncating them. (5) In show mode, require scope != whole-desk or a second token when the plan un-mutes/raises main or a bus master.

## D31. [medium] panic() cancels nothing: fader ramps (up to 60 s, any number, incl. a confirmed Main LR ramp) and writes queued behind the pre-write snapshot keep executing after it — an in-flight unmute lands after the panic
*Angle:* panic · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/desk.py:222 (self._ramps), desk.py:897-946 (_ramp/_run_ramp: per-address tasks, cancelled only by a newer move on the same address or Desk.close), desk.py:965 + server.py:888,905,954,971,1088 (ramp_ms accepted up to 60 000), desk.py:871-884 (_write: awaits ensure_pre_write_snapshot — a full ~2100-node dump on the session's first write — and acquire_write, then sends unconditionally), desk.py:1184-1220 (panic: no ramp cancellation, no epoch), desk.py:239-248 (close() is the only place ramps are cancelled)

**Mechanism.** panic() only emits 24 mix/on=0 datagrams. Desk._ramps tasks started before it continue stepping /…/mix/fader every 20 ms until ramp_ms (≤60 s) elapses, so bus/main/channel levels keep climbing under the mute; the operator or model un-mutes into a strip far hotter than at panic time (and channel ramps are not even under a muted master if an output is PRE-tapped). Separately, a Tier-1 write that is parked inside ensure_pre_write_snapshot (seconds on a real desk, README:622) or sleeping in acquire_write resumes after the panic and is sent: an unmute('bus.N') issued just before the panic re-opens that bus just after it. There is no generation counter that in-flight writes compare against.

**Exploit.** (a) set_fader('bus.1', 0, ramp_ms=60000) ×N buses (Tier 1, absolute moves have no relative limit) or a confirmed set_main_fader('st', 0, ramp_ms=60000) → panic() → ok → faders keep rising for up to a minute; operator un-mutes on the surface after a few seconds believing the system is stopped. (b) first write of the session: unmute('bus.1') (dump starts) → panic() → ok 24 muted → unmute completes → Bus 1 open again, tool says ok 'Bus 1 unmuted'.

**Evidence.** All on HEAD code: A1 (A1_bus_ramp_after_panic.json): after panic, ramps_alive ['/bus/01/mix/fader'], 129 fader writes over 2.56 s, bus 1 -34.3 → 0.0 dB while mix/on stayed 0. A2: same for /main/st/mix/fader via the token dance (-34.3 → 0.0 after panic). A4: three concurrent ramp_ms=60000 bus ramps all alive 1 s after panic and still climbing at 2 s (-58.6 → -57.5). A3 (A3_inflight_unmute_after_panic.json): event order ['panic', 'write /bus/01/mix/on={muted: False}'], unmute ok, /bus/01/mix/on=1 after the panic. With the fix sketch: test_panic_epoch.py::test_panic_cancels_ramps_and_refuses_the_move (ramps_cancelled 2, both moves return PANICKED, faders frozen at panic level ±1 step, a fresh deliberate set_fader afterwards succeeds) and ::test_inflight_first_write_is_dropped_by_panic (unmute → PANICKED, bus stays muted) pass; suite otherwise green.

**Proposed fix.** In Desk.panic(): bump self._panic_epoch and task.cancel() every entry of self._ramps before sending (no await needed). In _write(): capture the epoch on entry (or accept it from the ramp that started earlier), re-check after ensure_pre_write_snapshot and after acquire_write, raise DeskError('PANICKED', …) if it changed; _move_level checks after its snapshot wait; _ramp raises PANICKED when its task was cancelled by a panic (vs. superseded by a newer move). Lowering/cut writes issued AFTER the panic (CFS back-off, notches, a deliberate new move) are unaffected because they capture the new epoch. Optionally cap ramp_ms (e.g. 10 s) — a 60 s glide has no live-mixing use and widens every race. Implemented in worktree desk.py.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch on unmodified HEAD (a408a2a) in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-panic-2-repro (commit 20b3de2, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic2.py, 6/6 pass; JSON results in $SP/reports/verify-panic-2-repro/). Code path is exactly as claimed: Desk.panic() (src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1220) only loops 24 `conn.set(<prefix>/mix/on, 0)`; it never touches self._ramps (desk.py:222) and there is no generation/epoch that _write (desk.py:871-884) or _run_ramp (desk.py:922-946) re-checks. Ramp tasks are cancelled only by a newer ramp on the same address (desk.py:900-903) or Desk.close() (desk.py:239-248). ramp_ms is accepted up to 60 000 by _move_level (desk.py:965) and every level tool (server.py:888/905/954/971/1088). _write awaits ensure_pre_write_snapshot() and policy.acquire_write() (≤1 s sleep) and then sends unconditionally, so a write already past the tier check when panic fires is delivered after it. The race is transport-reachable, not just in-process: the MCP JSON-RPC dispatcher spawns every tools/call in its own task (mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:606 `_spawn(self._handle_request, …)`), so a panic() request sent while a set_fader/set_main_fader call is still pending runs concurrently with it — and 'panic while a long fade is running' is precisely when an operator says "stop".
Severity: I keep HIGH, not critical. Panic's own documented guarantee (24 output mutes) holds — mix/on stayed 0 on every output in every run and the level rise is bounded by the family ceilings (bus/main 0 dB, ch +5 dB), so it is not an unbounded/loud change by itself and does not unmute the mains. But it leaves the desk in a dangerous state after an emergency stop: the server keeps emitting up to 60 s of fader writes (130 writes in 2.6 s here) after reporting 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted', drives strips to their ceiling under the mute (−52 → 0 dB), fights an operator who pulls the fader down on the surface (−80 dB yanked back to −34 dB within 300 ms, then on to 0 dB), and an unmute that was in flight at panic time re-opens one output tens of ms to seconds after the panic with the tool still answering ok 'Bus 1 unmuted (was not muted)'. The in-flight-unmute variant needs a narrow window (session's first write parked in the dump — seconds on a real desk per README:628 — or a write sleeping ≤1 s in the rate limiter), so on its own it would be medium; the surviving-ramp behaviour needs nothing unusual (any ramp longer than the panic latency) and is the part that earns high.
>
> *Evidence:* All against HEAD code + FakeDesk via the in-memory UDP shim; run: $SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic2.py -s → 6 passed (full suite 917 passed, the 1 failure is test_settings_defaults_and_env asserting the checkout dir is named 'x32-mcp', a worktree-name artefact).
A1 test_A1_bus_ramp_keeps_climbing_after_panic (Desk API): set_level('bus.1', 0, ramp_ms=3000) started at −60 dB, panic() 0.4 s in → count 24, elapsed 0.2 ms; desk._ramps alive right after panic ['/bus/01/mix/fader'] and still alive 1 s later; fader −52.0 dB at panic → −31.5 dB 1 s after → −0.01 dB final; 130 desk.write events after the panic event; ramp result superseded=False, after_db 0.0, steps 150, elapsed 2981 ms; /bus/01/mix/on stayed 0.
A4 test_A4_three_60s_ramps_survive_panic: three set_level(bus.1..3, 0, ramp_ms=60000); after panic all three tasks alive at +1 s and +2 s, faders −59.52 → −57.49 dB and still climbing (would run ~60 s; cancelled by the test).
A2 test_A2_confirmed_main_ramp_survives_panic_tool (server tools after make_app): set_main_fader('st', 0, ramp_ms=3000) → requires_confirmation → same call with confirm_token as a task; srv.panic() 0.4 s in → ok 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted in 0.2 ms (sent)'; app.desk._ramps alive ['/main/st/mix/fader']; Main LR −52.0 dB at panic → −31.5 dB 1 s later → −0.01 dB final; the move returns ok=True 'Main LR fader −60.0 dB → 0.0 dB (ramped 3000 ms)', superseded False; /main/st/mix/on still 0.
A3 test_A3_inflight_first_write_unmute_lands_after_panic (server tools, fresh session, snapshot_before_write True): srv.unmute('bus.1') task started, 50 ms later pre_write_snapshot still None (dump in progress) → srv.panic() ok at t=0.050 s, /bus/01/mix/on == 0 right after; unmute returns at t=0.088 s ok=True 'Bus 1 'Bus01' unmuted (was not muted)'; /bus/01/mix/on == 1 final; event order ['panic','write'].
A3b test_A3b_unmute_sleeping_in_rate_limiter_lands_after_panic (Desk API, bucket drained so acquire_write sleeps ≈0.9 s): set_mute('bus.2', False) pending → panic → mix/on 0 → unmute completes → mix/on 1; order ['panic','write'].
A5 test_A5_surviving_ramp_overrides_front_panel_pulldown: 0.5 s after panic the operator sets /bus/01/mix/fader to −80 dB on the (fake) surface; 300 ms later the surviving ramp has written it back to −33.6 dB and it ends at 0.0 dB (superseded False).
File refs: desk.py:222 (_ramps), 239-248 (close = only cancel site), 871-884 (_write: snapshot → acquire_write → unconditional conn.set), 897-920 (_ramp), 922-946 (_run_ramp), 954-983 (_move_level, ramp_ms ≤ 60 000 at 965), 1184-1220 (panic: no cancel, no epoch), 1224-1230 (set_main_level → same _move_level); server.py:881-896/1082-1101 (set_fader/set_main_fader await the whole ramp inside the tool call, ramp_ms 0..60000), 1064-1075 (panic tool); mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:606 (concurrent request tasks).
>
> *Better fix:* The proposed fix is the right shape: in Desk.panic(), before the mute loop, increment a self._panic_epoch and task.cancel() every live entry in self._ramps (no await — panic must stay sub-ms); in _write() capture the epoch before `await ensure_pre_write_snapshot()` and re-check it after that await and after `acquire_write()`, raising DeskError('PANICKED', …) instead of sending; _ramp() should distinguish 'cancelled by panic' (epoch changed → raise PANICKED so the tool reports the level it stopped at) from 'superseded by a newer move'. Additions worth making at the same time: (1) set_mute/set_main_mute read `was` before the parked write, so after the fix the PANICKED error (not a stale 'was not muted') is what the model sees; (2) have panic also publish an event CFS listens to so an in-progress ring-out step-up/back-off ramp (cfs.py drives the bus/main fader itself via send_raw in _restore_master) is aborted rather than resumed; (3) cap ramp_ms well below 60 s (e.g. 10 s) — a minute-long glide has no live use and is the multiplier on every one of these races; (4) optionally make panic re-assert the 24 mutes once ~100 ms later (cheap, covers any write that slipped between the tier check and the send on a slow desk).

> **Verifier 2: PARTIAL (medium)** — MECHANISM — holds exactly as stated, on HEAD a408a2a. Desk.panic() (desk.py:1184-1220) does acquire_write(panic=True) (returns without yielding, policy.py:378) then 24 synchronous conn.set() datagrams (connection.py:520-530/_send:733) and nothing else: it never touches self._ramps (desk.py:222), and Desk.close() (desk.py:239-248) plus 'a newer ramp on the same address' (desk.py:897-901) are the only cancellation sites. _run_ramp (desk.py:922-946) walks precomputed steps and calls _write() per step; _write (desk.py:871-884) re-checks only tier, snapshot and the token bucket, none of which change after a panic, so every remaining step is sent. Likewise a write parked in ensure_pre_write_snapshot (desk.py:846-862, awaits a full dump under _snapshot_lock, which panic does not take) or sleeping in acquire_write (policy.py:375-392, ≤ max_wait_s=1 s) resumes and sends unconditionally; set_mute also does resolve()+_leaf() round-trips before the write, so any concurrently in-flight unmute lands after a panic that was dispatched behind it. No generation counter exists. A real X32 behaves identically: everything here is client-side asyncio task lifetime; the desk just applies datagrams in arrival order (motor fader keeps gliding under the mute; a mix/on 1 arriving after the 24 mix/on 0 re-opens that bus). ramp_ms up to 60 000 is accepted (desk.py:965, server.py:888/905/954/971/1088) and _bounded_level's budget (server.py:429-461: steps*per_step*2.5+15 s) never cuts a ramp short.

WHERE THE CLAIM IS OVERSTATED (why PARTIAL, medium not high):
1. Precondition is a CONCURRENT, UN-CANCELLED request. Every level tool awaits its ramp to completion inside the tool call (_move_level→_ramp awaits the task, desk.py:907-909; server.py:896). A sequential MCP client (Claude Desktop, the documented deployment, README/BRIEF:90) cannot issue panic() while its own set_fader is pending; if the user aborts or the client times the call out, SDK clients send notifications/cancelled, the mcp 2.2 dispatcher default peer_cancel_mode='interrupt' cancels the handler scope (jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:80-84,623-628), CancelledError reaches _ramp's await and it task.cancel()s the ramp (desk.py:910-912) — verified (V2: ramp dead, fader frozen). So exploit (a) needs a client that dispatches tool calls in parallel or abandons requests without cancelling (Agent-SDK loops, the HANDOVER §4a Python driver, custom clients). The server is built to accept that (FastMCP start_soon per request; the supersede logic exists precisely for concurrent moves), so it is reachable — but 'unusual but reachable', i.e. the brief's medium.
2. The 'seconds on a real desk' snapshot window (README:622) is stale prose: the measured full /node sweep is 0.28 s (HANDOVER.md:94, README:201). Scenario (b)'s window is ≤0.28 s on the session's first write, ≤1 s when the bucket is drained (e.g. by concurrent ramps), otherwise only the resolve/_leaf round-trip (ms). On the fake it was 0.08 s.
3. No documented guarantee is defeated. panic is specified purely as '24 mix/on 0, never blocked' (README:621-626, DESIGN.md:574, BRIEF:65) and 'Unmute afterwards with unmute' — it is momentary, not a latched state, and input channels/PRE taps/direct outs are documented as out of scope (fx_routing_scenes.md §11 option 1, HANDOVER:115). The 24 mutes are sent and hold (V1/V6: mix/on stayed 0). The level a surviving ramp reaches is exactly the ceiling-clamped destination the Tier-1/confirmed-Tier-2 call was already permitted to reach (bus/main 0 dB, ch +5 dB) — panic or not, the operator would face that level; what is lost is the operator's reasonable expectation that 'panic' froze the automation, so a surface un-mute a few seconds later opens a strip still gliding upward. That is a weakened guarantee / misleading post-emergency state, not a loud-or-unbounded change and not a failed mute → medium. (b) flips the outcome for one bus versus sequential ordering and the two 'ok' tool results mislead the model, but with no post-panic latch in the design an unmute landing 50 ms after panic is the same class as the model legitimately calling unmute 2 s after it.
4. 'Any number' of 60 s ramps is bounded in practice by the shared 50 writes/s bucket (≈10 writes/s per full-travel 60 s ramp after dedupe); beyond ~5 concurrent ramps _write raises RATE_LIMITED inside _run_ramp and that ramp dies mid-travel.
Net: real design gap (panic is not an epoch; ramps, parked writes — and, per the sibling B-findings, a CFS ring-out — all survive it), worth fixing, but medium under the brief's rubric.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-panic-2-src (branch wt/verify-panic-2-src, commit be32253), tests/integration/test_verify_panic2.py, 6/6 pass on HEAD code via FakeDesk; JSON in $SP/reports/verify-panic-2-src/. V1_bus_ramp_survives_panic: set_fader('bus.1',0,ramp_ms=3000) as a concurrent task, panic() at +0.5 s → panic ok 24/24 in 0.2 ms, ramps_alive ['/bus/01/mix/fader'], 129 fader writes after the panic over 2.57 s, bus 1 fader −34.3 → −0.01 dB while /bus/01/mix/on stayed 0 (matches claimant A1 byte-for-byte). V6_three_60s_ramps: three ramp_ms=60000 bus ramps all alive 1.5 s after panic and climbing (−59.5 → −58.1 dB) (matches A4). V3_inflight_unmute_after_panic: unmute('bus.1') as the session's first write, panic() 10 ms later → event order ['panic','snapshot','write /bus/01/mix/on'], unmute ok 'Bus 1 unmuted', /bus/01/mix/on=1 after the panic; the parked window on the fake was 0.082 s (real desk: 0.28 s dump, HANDOVER.md:94) (matches A3). V5_rate_limited_unmute_after_panic: bucket drained → unmute sleeps in acquire_write, panic fires, unmute lands after → bus 1 open. V2_client_cancel_stops_ramp (precondition check the claim omits): cancelling the pending set_fader coroutine (what mcp 2.2's dispatcher does on notifications/cancelled, jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:623-628 with default peer_cancel_mode='interrupt' at :272) → ramps_alive [], fader frozen at −34.34 dB 500 ms later — so an aborted/timed-out request does NOT leave an orphaned ramp; only a genuinely concurrent, un-cancelled request does. Code path refs: desk.py:222 (_ramps), 239-248 (close = only cancel site), 846-862 (ensure_pre_write_snapshot, own lock, panic does not take it), 871-884 (_write: no epoch re-check after its two awaits), 897-946 (_ramp/_run_ramp), 965 (ramp_ms ≤ 60 000), 1184-1220 (panic: 24 conn.set, no ramp cancel); server.py:312-327 (_tool), 429-461 (_bounded_level budget > ramp), 880-896/1081-1101 (set_fader/set_main_fader await the ramp inside the call), 1063-1075 (panic tool); policy.py:375-392; device.yaml:731-733 (ramp_step_ms 20, writes_per_second 50).
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's epoch design is sound; prioritise by value/risk: (1) In Desk.panic(), before the first send, synchronously cancel every task in self._ramps (no await) and call into CfsManager to abort any RINGOUT session to its start master — this alone closes the 60 s-ramp and ring-out cases with ~5 lines and no behavioural change elsewhere. (2) Add self._panic_epoch bumped in panic(); _write() captures it on entry and re-checks after ensure_pre_write_snapshot() and after acquire_write(), raising DeskError('PANICKED') only for writes that OPEN or RAISE (mix/on→1, a level above the current value) so CFS back-offs/notches and post-panic deliberate moves are unaffected; _ramp distinguishes panic-cancel (raise PANICKED, report the level it stopped at) from supersede. (3) Cap ramp_ms at ~10 s in _move_level/_int_arg (15 s was the longest real use, HANDOVER:99) to shrink every race. (4) Fix README:622 ('a few seconds') to the measured 0.28 s and document that panic is momentary and does not latch — or, if a latch is wanted, make un-muting any of the 24 panic addresses within N s of a panic a confirmed (Tier-2) action.

## D32. [medium] panic() is fire-once and unverified: while DEGRADED/unreachable it reports ok:true and is never re-asserted on reconnect; when connected, lost datagrams leave outputs live while the result says '24 muted (sent)'
*Angle:* panic · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/desk.py:1199-1209 (conn.set → on NotConnected fall back to send_raw once; no read-back, no retry, no pending state), desk.py:1218-1220 (raises NOT_CONNECTED only when nothing could be sent; no re-fire later), src/x32mcp/server.py:1069-1074 (_ok(...) even when delivered=='unconfirmed'), src/x32mcp/connection.py:879-889 (_recover re-sends only /xremote), connection.py:842-854 (socket lost → transport None → both set and send_raw raise), docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md:713 (Maillot's tools pace bursts; panic sends 24 datagrams in 0.2 ms), tests/integration/test_desk.py:373-386 (the existing 'degraded' test un-silences the fake BEFORE calling panic — it certifies the convenient case only)

**Mechanism.** Three reachable states: (1) desk/network actually down (BRIEF §9 PSU wobble, cable): state DEGRADED, conn.set refuses, send_raw fires 24 datagrams into the void, tool returns ok:true count 24 delivered 'unconfirmed'; when the reconnect loop recovers seconds later nothing remembers the panic, all outputs are live, and the model has already told the user it worked. (2) transport died (proactor error/abort → _handle_transport_lost): every send raises, panic returns NOT_CONNECTED in <1 ms, reconnect re-binds and recovers ~0.1-8 s later, panic not replayed. (3) CONNECTED but 1-2 of the 24 back-to-back UDP datagrams dropped (switch, Wi-Fi bridge to the tablet LAN, desk RX buffer): result says sent/24/failed [] while those outputs stay live — panic never reads mix/on back. Contrast cfs._restore_master (cfs.py:1324-1361) which retries every 0.5 s with read-back for 10 s: the emergency fader restore is more robust than the emergency mute.

**Exploit.** (1) pull the desk's network for 3 s during a howl → panic() → 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted … (unconfirmed)' ok:true → link returns → nothing muted, model believes it is. (3) any lossy hop: panic() → ok, 'sent', but e.g. Bus 7 (a wedge) still live.

**Evidence.** HEAD code: D1 (D1_panic_while_unreachable.json): fake silent → DEGRADED → panic ok:true delivered 'unconfirmed' count 24 → fake un-silenced → state connected → 24/24 outputs still mix/on=1. D2: _handle_transport_lost → panic → ok:false NOT_CONNECTED failed=24 in 0.0 s → auto-reconnected → 24/24 live. D4 (D4_lost_datagrams.json): dropped the /bus/07 and /mtx/03 datagrams in conn._send → panic ok:true count 24 failed [] delivered 'sent' → /bus/07/mix/on and /mtx/03/mix/on = 1. With the fix sketch: test_pending_panic_is_refired_when_the_desk_answers_again, test_panic_with_a_dead_socket_is_pending_and_refired (result carries pending:true; on connection.state→connected the burst is re-fired and verified, 0 live) and test_lost_panic_datagrams_are_caught_by_the_read_back (verified 24, live [], 0 un-muted) pass; panic still puts the first 24 datagrams on the wire in 0.2 ms and the tool returns in <1 s.

**Proposed fix.** Keep the instant burst, then: (a) re-send the 24 mutes at ~+30 ms and read all 24 mix/on back once concurrently (≤0.3 s budget), re-fire any that read 1, and return verified/live/unverified + delivered 'partial' instead of a bare 'sent'; (b) a background follow-up re-sends at +150/+500 ms (also covers a scene that was still loading when the panic landed — an ordering the FakeDesk cannot reproduce because it applies goscene synchronously); (c) Desk.panic_pending=True whenever delivery was unconfirmed/failed/any live, and a connection.state→connected subscriber re-fires + verifies and clears it; NOT_CONNECTED result says 'will be re-sent automatically' with pending:true; (d) server summary must not read as success when unverified. Implemented in worktree desk.py (_fire_mutes/_verify_mutes/_panic_follow_up/_on_state_event).

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — All three sub-claims reproduce exactly against HEAD a408a2a in my own worktree ($SP/worktrees/verify-panic-3-repro, commit 14cf52d, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic3.py, 4 passed), driven through the real server tool srv.panic() on a FakeDesk App.

Code path (desk.py:1184-1220): for each of 24 targets, conn.set(); on NotConnected fall back to conn.send_raw() once and flag unconfirmed; no read-back of mix/on, no retry, no pending flag, nothing subscribed to connection.state (grep: only cfs.py:522 subscribes; connection._recover at connection.py:879-889 re-sends only /xremote). server.py:1064-1074 wraps whatever comes back in _ok(...) => ok:true even when delivered=='unconfirmed'. DeskError NOT_CONNECTED is raised only when zero sends succeeded (desk.py:1218-1219).

(1) Desk unreachable (fake silent -> DEGRADED): tool returns ok:true, count 24, failed [], delivered 'unconfirmed', summary 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted in 0.2 ms (unconfirmed); the desk is degraded — verify on X32-Edit or the front panel'. The 25 datagrams reached the fake and were swallowed. After un-silencing, state -> connected; waited 1.5 s more: 24/24 outputs still mix/on=1. Nothing re-fires. A control test shows a second manual panic after reconnect does mute all 24 — the only missing piece is re-assertion/verification.
(2) Transport lost (_handle_transport_lost): panic returns in 0.2 ms with ok:false NOT_CONNECTED, failed = all 24; reconnect loop re-binds and recovers; 24/24 live; not replayed. NOTE: this case is honestly reported as a failure, so on its own it is low — the model can retry.
(3) CONNECTED, two datagrams (/bus/07, /mtx/03) silently dropped in conn._send: result ok:true, delivered 'sent', count 24, failed [] — and exactly those two outputs remain live (mix/on=1) with no caveat in the summary at all. panic never reads back. The project's own transport research says pace bulk writes 1–10 ms and warns of RX-side datagram loss on bursts (docs/research/transport.md:149, :649, :669); panic emits 24 datagrams in ~0.2 ms unpaced. Contrast cfs._restore_master (cfs.py:1324-1361) which retries every 0.5 s with read-back for 10 s — confirmed.

Nuances vs the claim: the D1 summary does carry '(unconfirmed) … verify on X32-Edit or the front panel', and README:621-626 documents the degraded/unconfirmed behaviour and calls panic a backstop, so 'the model has already told the user it worked' is somewhat overstated — but ok:true + 'muted': [24 keys] + '24 outputs muted' is what a client keys on. HANDOVER §4a's '24/24 verified muted' was a manual observation; the code never verifies.

Severity: high (not critical). It is not an LLM-driven exploit and needs a network fault, but it squarely answers REVIEW_BRIEF §3's question ('any state in which panic fails to silence the outputs?') with yes: (3) silently defeats panic per-output under packet loss with a clean 'sent' result, and (1) leaves the desk fully live after the link error clears while the emergency action was reported ok and is never re-asserted — 'leaves the desk in a dangerous state after an error' on the rubric. (2) alone would be low.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-panic-3-repro (SP=<review-scratch>), commit 14cf52d, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic3.py; run: $SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic3.py -> 4 passed in 9.3 s. Result JSONs under $SP/reports/verify-panic-3-repro/:
D1_panic_while_unreachable.json: tool_result {ok:true, summary:'PANIC: 24 outputs muted in 0.2 ms (unconfirmed); the desk is degraded — verify on X32-Edit or the front panel', count:24, failed:[], delivered:'unconfirmed'}, fake_rx_during_silence:25, state_after:'connected', n_live:24 (all of /main/st,/main/m,/bus/01-16,/mtx/01-06 mix/on == 1 after reconnect + 1.5 s).
D2_panic_dead_transport.json: tool_result {ok:false, error:{code:'NOT_CONNECTED', message:'panic could not reach the desk: not connected', muted:[], failed:[24 keys]}}, elapsed ~0.0002 s, state_after 'connected', n_live 24.
D3_lost_datagrams.json: tool_result {ok:true, summary:'PANIC: 24 outputs muted in 0.2 ms (sent)', count:24, failed:[], delivered:'sent'}, dropped:['/bus/07/mix/on','/mtx/03/mix/on'], live_after:['/bus/07/mix/on','/mtx/03/mix/on'].
D4 control: after the D1 sequence a second srv.panic() while connected -> delivered 'sent', 0 live.
Code refs: src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1220 (single set/send_raw, no read-back/retry/pending), src/x32mcp/server.py:1064-1074 (_ok regardless of delivered), src/x32mcp/connection.py:842-854 (_handle_transport_lost -> transport None), :879-889 (_recover re-sends only /xremote), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:522 (only connection.state subscriber), cfs.py:1324-1361 (_restore_master retry+read-back contrast), docs/research/transport.md:149,649,669 (UDP loss on bursts; pace 1–10 ms), docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md:713 (Maillot paces bursts), README.md:621-626 (documents unconfirmed-while-degraded, 'backstop').
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed direction; minimal version: keep the instant unpaced burst, then within the tool's 10 s budget (a) re-send the 24 mutes once after ~20-50 ms (cheap insurance against RX-buffer loss), (b) read all 24 mix/on back concurrently with a short timeout and re-fire any that read 1, returning verified/live/unverified lists and delivered in {'verified','partial','unconfirmed'}; (c) set desk.panic_pending when anything is unverified/failed and subscribe Desk to connection.state so the burst+verify is re-run on ->connected (clear on success or on an explicit unmute/set_main_mute so a later deliberate unmute is not fought); (d) server.panic must not emit ok:true/'N outputs muted' when nothing was verified — use ok:false with code PANIC_UNVERIFIED (or ok:true only for the verified subset) and say 'will be re-sent automatically when the desk answers'. Keep NOT_CONNECTED for the dead-socket case but also mark pending so the reconnect re-fires it.

> **Verifier 2: PARTIAL (medium)** — Mechanics hold end to end; framing/severity overstated.

Code path (HEAD a408a2a): server.py:1064-1074 panic → _desk() (server.py:225-235) lets DEGRADED through → Desk.panic (desk.py:1184-1220): per target conn.set (connection.py:520-530 → _require_writable refuses when DEGRADED, connection.py:739-745) → fallback conn.send_raw (connection.py:574-577 → _send, raises only if transport None/closing, connection.py:733-737) → unconfirmed=True. No GET of mix/on anywhere after the burst; result is 'sent' or 'unconfirmed' only. _ok() wraps it ok:true (server.py:283-288). On recovery _recover (connection.py:879-889) re-sends only /xremote; the only connection.state subscriber is cfs.py:522 (its own abort); nothing remembers or re-fires a panic (grep: desk.panic is published, never subscribed). _handle_transport_lost (connection.py:842-854) nulls the transport → both set and send_raw raise → all 24 in failed → DeskError NOT_CONNECTED (desk.py:1218-1219) → ok:false; _reconnect_loop (connection.py:937-954) re-binds and recovers; no replay. So: (1) DEGRADED+dead link → ok:true/unconfirmed/24, outputs live after auto-reconnect — TRUE. (2) transport lost → ok:false NOT_CONNECTED in ~0.2 ms, not replayed — TRUE but honest; a caller retry after reconnect mutes 24/24 (shown). (3) CONNECTED + datagram loss → 'sent'/24/failed [] with the dropped strips live — TRUE (no SET ack exists, transport.md:639; no read-back). Contrast with _restore_master (cfs.py:1324-1352: send_raw + get read-back every 0.5 s for 10 s) and recall_scene (polls /-show/prepos/current) is accurate: the emergency mute is the one unverified emergency write. test_desk.py:373-386 does un-silence the fake before calling panic — accurate.

Where the claim does not hold as stated: (a) 'the model has already told the user it worked' — in state (1) the summary is 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted in 0.2 ms (unconfirmed); the desk is degraded — verify on X32-Edit or the front panel', which is the documented contract (desk.py:31-34, README.md:621-625, GIG_CHECKLIST.md:187); ok:true + 'muted' wording is misleading but the caveat reaches the model. (b) State (2) is not a panic defeat: it fails loudly and fast, and nothing could be sent without a socket. (c) It defeats no documented guarantee — the documented promise is 'never blocked', not 'verified'; the docs explicitly call panic a fire-and-forget backstop with the physical mute/X32-Edit as the reflex (BRIEF.md:90, GIG_CHECKLIST.md:10-11,77). One doc inconsistency supports the claimant's intent: GIG_CHECKLIST.md:172 expects `delivered: "confirmed"`, a value the code can never produce.

Where the claim is understated: with the production watchdog (connection.py:248 watchdog_s=12; HANDOVER.md:118 'DEGRADED 14 s after the cable came out') the claim's own exploit ('pull the network for 3 s during a howl') never reaches DEGRADED — the state is still CONNECTED, conn.set succeeds, and panic returns ok:true delivered 'sent' with NO caveat while all 24 datagrams are lost (my V1b). Same root cause (no read-back), more misleading than state (1).

Real desk vs FakeDesk: (1)/(1b) identical on hardware when the break is at the desk/switch end or a PSU wobble (client sendto succeeds into a dead link; an X32 reboots to its stored state with outputs live; reconnect re-registers /xremote only). If the cable is pulled at the PC NIC the OS may raise OSError on sendto, which panic reports honestly as failed/NOT_CONNECTED. (3) The protocol gives no SET ack on a real desk either, so a lost datagram is equally undetectable; but actual loss was only injected synthetically (conn._send monkeypatch) — on the supported wired-LAN deployment the real desk took 24/24 at 0.4 ms (HANDOVER.md:114). Loss is plausible on the deferred Wi-Fi/Termux host (README.md:807-811 anticipates Wi-Fi hiccups) and transport.md:669 advises pacing bursts and treating silence as lost. FakeDesk applies everything synchronously, so scene-load ordering races are untestable here (claimant notes this correctly).

Severity re-assessed to MEDIUM: real, reproducible, cheap to fix, and squarely answers REVIEW_BRIEF §3's question ('any state in which panic fails to silence the outputs'), but it is an environmental failure (link loss at the instant of panic), not an LLM-reachable bypass; two of the three states are reported honestly (caveat or ok:false); no documented guarantee is defeated; and the silent-loss case is unobserved on the verified deployment. The CONNECTED-dead-link 'sent' misreport is the facet closest to high.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-panic-3-src (branch wt/verify-panic-3-src, commit bd7be40, no product edits), tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic3.py, 5 passed; JSON under $SP/reports/verify-panic-3-src/. V1_degraded.json: fake silent → DEGRADED → srv.panic() → {ok:true, delivered:'unconfirmed', count:24, failed:[], summary '...(unconfirmed); the desk is degraded — verify on X32-Edit or the front panel'} → un-silence → auto-reconnect to CONNECTED → 24/24 mix/on still 1. V1b_connected_cable_out.json: fake silent while state still CONNECTED (prod watchdog 12 s) → srv.panic() → {ok:true, delivered:'sent', failed:[], summary 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted in 0.2 ms (sent)'} (no caveat) → link back → 24/24 live. V2_transport_lost.json: conn._handle_transport_lost → srv.panic() → ok:false NOT_CONNECTED failed=24 in 0.16 ms → auto-reconnect → 24/24 live; a second srv.panic() after reconnect → 0 live (caller retry works). V3_lost_datagrams.json: dropped /bus/07/mix/on and /mtx/03/mix/on inside conn._send → {ok:true, delivered:'sent', count:24, failed:[]} → exactly those two still 1. V4_burst.json: 24 datagrams in 0.1 ms, fakedesk rx delta 26 (24 SETs + settle + heartbeat) — no read-backs issued. Note on the sandbox shim: tests/conftest.py delivers via loop.call_soon, so a test must yield (await asyncio.sleep) before un-silencing the fake or the 'lost' datagrams are processed after the link 'returns' — my first run showed 0 live for that reason; fixed by yielding 0.2 s while silent. Source refs: desk.py:1184-1220, server.py:225-235/283-288/1064-1074, connection.py:520-530/574-577/733-745/842-854/879-889/937-954, cfs.py:522 (only connection.state subscriber), cfs.py:1324-1352 (_restore_master read-back loop), HANDOVER.md:114,118, README.md:621-625,807-811, GIG_CHECKLIST.md:172 (expects delivered:'confirmed' — impossible), transport.md:639,669.
>
> *Better fix:* Keep the instant unpaced burst (real desk verified 24/24 at 0.4 ms), then in Desk.panic: (1) when a socket exists, re-send the 24 mutes once at ~+30 ms and read all 24 mix/on back concurrently with a ≤300 ms budget (request(..., retries=0)); re-fire any that read 1; return delivered 'verified' | 'partial' (live=[...]) | 'unverified' instead of 'sent', and make the server summary say 'sent, NOT verified — check X32-Edit/front panel' whenever not verified (never 'N outputs muted' on an unverified result; ok may stay true only for 'verified'/'partial' with live==[]). (2) Set Desk._panic_pending (timestamp) whenever the result is unverified/failed/any live; subscribe Desk to connection.state and on →connected within a bounded window (e.g. 60 s) re-fire+verify once and publish desk.panic{refired:true}; clear the pending flag on any explicit unmute/set_main_mute/restore so a stale panic cannot re-mute a show the operator already recovered by hand; NOT_CONNECTED result carries pending:true and says it will be re-sent on reconnect. (3) Fix GIG_CHECKLIST.md:172 to the values the code actually returns. This matches the existing precedents (_restore_master read-back loop, recall_scene prepos poll) and costs <0.4 s on the tool path.

## D33. [medium] No post-panic latch: 22 of the 24 outputs can be re-opened immediately by Tier-1 unmute() with no token and no show-mode block
*Angle:* panic · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:929-935 (unmute: Tier 1 for bus/mtx/ch/dca), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1015-1024 (set_mute → _write; only /main/*/mix/on is guarded via device.yaml tiers), src/x32mcp/policy.py:65-67 (show mode blocks scenes/ring-outs only), server.py:128-129 INSTRUCTIONS ('Unmute afterwards with unmute / set_main_mute')

**Mechanism.** panic() leaves no state behind. The threat model is a confused/runaway model; the natural next thing such a model does after 'sound stopped' is to 'restore sound' — unmute('bus.1'…'bus.16','mtx.1'…'mtx.6') are ordinary Tier-1 writes (50/s budget, no confirmation, allowed in show mode). Combined with the CFS finding (bus parked at -3 dB after the panic) or the ramp finding (levels climbed under the mute) this is the trigger that turns a silent-but-hot desk into a howl. Only Main LR/M/C need the set_main_mute token.

**Exploit.** panic() → unmute('bus.1') … unmute('mtx.6') (22 calls, ~0.1 s on the fake, <0.5 s at 50 writes/s on a desk) → every wedge/IEM/matrix feed live again; no user confirmation was ever requested.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_panic.py::test_E1_tier1_unmute_reopens_22_outputs_right_after_panic_without_confirmation PASSES on HEAD (E1_no_post_panic_latch.json): show_mode on, panic ok, then 22 unmute calls all ok in 0.08 s, 22 outputs mix/on=1; unmute('main.st') correctly refused GUARDED. Not addressed by the fix sketch (design decision).

**Proposed fix.** After a panic, treat un-muting any of the 24 panic targets as Tier 2 until the operator clears the latch: Desk keeps panicked_targets (set by panic, cleared per target by a confirmed unmute or by a new tool clear_panic(confirm_token)); Desk._write refuses mix/on→1 on those addresses without guarded=True (DeskError 'PANIC_LATCHED: use set_main_mute/unmute with confirmation'); server.unmute routes bus/mtx through _confirm() while latched. Cheap alternative: require force=True + a confirmation for unmute of bus/mtx within N minutes of a panic.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-panic-4-repro (branch wt/verify-panic-4-repro, commit a90c069) with tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic4.py against HEAD a408a2a code. With show_mode(True) on, panic() muted all 24 outputs (count 24, 0.2 ms, delivered=sent; fake desk confirms /bus/NN/mix/on, /mtx/NN/mix/on, /main/{st,m}/mix/on all 0). Then 22 plain unmute('bus.1'..'bus.16','mtx.1'..'mtx.6') calls: 22 ok, 0 requires_confirmation, 0 errors, 0.087 s wall (second run 0.113 s), and the fake desk shows all 22 mix/on back to 1. unmute('main.st') and unmute('main.m') are refused GUARDED (desk.py:_write tier check via device.yaml guarded '/main/st/mix/*','/main/m/mix/*'); set_main_mute('st', False) returns requires_confirmation=true and mains stay muted. Code path confirms there is no post-panic state at all: Desk.panic (desk.py:1184-1221) only publishes a 'desk.panic' event and bumps write_count; grep for panicked/latch finds nothing; server.unmute (server.py:927-935) → Desk.set_mute (desk.py:1015-1024) → _write (desk.py:871-884) whose only gates are tier_for(address) (bus/mtx mix/on are tier 1 in device.yaml:214/256), the pre-write snapshot and the 50/s token bucket (policy.py:375-396, full bucket so 22 writes never wait); show mode (policy.py:224-235, SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED at :75) only blocks scene_recall/scene_save/setup_ringout_eqs/ring_out/ring_out_system and is never consulted by mute/unmute. Minor nit in the claim: the SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED set is at policy.py:75-77, not 65-67 (66 is the __all__ entry). Severity: I keep medium. It is not a bug against a documented guarantee — README.md:621-626 and the panic docstring (server.py:1065-1067) explicitly say 'Unmute afterwards with unmute / set_main_mute', and bus/mtx mutes are Tier 1 by design (BRIEF §4). It does not by itself produce a loud or unbounded change (unmute restores the pre-panic level, which was itself clamped) and panic itself works, so not critical/high. But under the stated threat model (runaway/confused LLM) the emergency stop issued through the MCP can be fully released for 22 of 24 outputs (every wedge/IEM/matrix feed) by the same agent within ~0.1 s with no human in the loop and no show-mode protection, which weakens the e-stop under trivially reachable conditions; the danger becomes real when combined with state that changed under the mute (CFS bus parked, ramps that continued). Note the latch proposed in the fix only protects panics issued via MCP; a physical/X32-Edit mute by the operator is equally undoable by Tier-1 unmute, which is the same exposure and arguably the more important case.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-panic-4-repro (commit a90c069). Test file: tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic4.py — test_verify_no_post_panic_latch and test_rate_limit_is_the_only_brake, both PASS ('2 passed in 1.17s'). Result JSON written to $SP/reports/verify-panic-4-repro/E1_verify_no_post_panic_latch.json: {"panic":{"count":24,"elapsed_ms":0.2,"delivered":"sent"},"unmute":{"ok":22,"pending":[],"errors":[],"elapsed_s":0.087,"outputs_reopened_on_desk":22},"main_unmute":{"st":"GUARDED","m":"GUARDED"},"set_main_mute_first_call":{"requires_confirmation":true,"ok":false},"mains_still_muted":true,"dca_unmute_ok":true}; E1b_timing.json: 22 unmutes in 0.113 s. Code refs: server.py:927-935 (unmute, no _confirm, no show-mode check), server.py:1063-1075 (panic tool, docstring 'Unmute afterwards with unmute / set_main_mute'), desk.py:1015-1024 (set_mute → _write with guarded=False), desk.py:871-884 (_write: tier_for → snapshot → acquire_write → conn.set; no panic awareness), desk.py:1184-1221 (panic keeps no state, only events.publish('desk.panic')), policy.py:75-77 + 224-235 (show mode blocks only scenes/ring-outs), policy.py:375-396 (50/s bucket, panic bypass), device.yaml:214,256 (bus/mtx mix/on tier 1) vs :262 and guarded globs :708-710 (main mix/* tier 2), README.md:621-626 (documented design: unmute after panic with unmute/set_main_mute).
>
> *Better fix:* The proposed Desk-level latch is reasonable but should key on the address, not on who muted it: have Desk record panicked_at + the 24 panic addresses in panic(); in _write, if address is one of them, raw decodes to ON (unmute) and the latch is set, raise DeskError('PANIC_LATCHED') unless guarded=True; add server-side routing so unmute() on bus/mtx while latched goes through _confirm('unmute_after_panic', payload={target}) and calls set_mute(..., guarded=True); clear the latch per address on a confirmed unmute, wholesale via a confirmed clear_panic(), or on restore_snapshot. Keep mute (→0) always Tier 1 so the latch can never block silencing. Cheaper variant that also covers operator mutes made on the desk surface: make bus/mtx mix/on→1 (unmute of an output master) Tier 2 whenever show mode is on, since re-opening an output master mid-show is exactly the class of action show mode exists to gate; input-channel/DCA unmutes stay Tier 1.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain, end to end, has no gap:
1. server.unmute (src/x32mcp/server.py:929-937) -> desk.resolve -> Desk.set_mute(t, False) (desk.py:1015-1024) -> Desk._write(address, 1, ...) (desk.py:871-884). _write's only gates are (a) policy.tier_for(address) >= GUARDED and (b) policy.acquire_write() (token bucket). No show-mode check, no confirmation, no panic state.
2. Tier: device.yaml:214 declares `mix/on ... tier: 1` for every strip family; the guarded globs (device.yaml:708-721) cover only `/main/st/mix/*` and `/main/m/mix/*`. Descriptor.tier_for (descriptor.py:1041-1046) therefore returns 1 for /bus/NN/mix/on and /mtx/NN/mix/on and 2 only for the two mains. Measured: {/bus/01/mix/on:1, /mtx/06/mix/on:1, /main/st/mix/on:2, /main/m/mix/on:2}.
3. Show mode: Policy.check_show_mode_allows (policy.py:213-224) is only called from _confirm() and the scene/ring-out paths; SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED (policy.py:65-67) = scene_recall/scene_save/setup_ringout_eqs/ring_out/ring_out_system. unmute never consults it.
4. Rate limiter: 50 writes/s bucket (device.yaml:733), each unmute costs one slot plus a `_leaf` read round-trip that naturally paces the loop; 22 unmutes left 28 slots in the bucket; three back-to-back panic->22-unmute cycles (66 writes) never raised RATE_LIMITED.
5. panic() (desk.py:1184-1220) writes 24 `mix/on 0`, invalidates cache, publishes desk.panic, and returns. It records nothing on Desk or Policy; grep for panic across cfs.py/provision.py/policy.py/webui.py finds no subscriber that changes policy state. So the very next Tier-1 call can invert it.
Real desk vs FakeDesk: identical. `/bus/NN/mix/on ,i 1` = unmuted is VERIFIED on hardware (docs/HANDOVER.md §4a 'Mute semantics ... panel agrees'; panic 24/24 verified muted). conn.set is fire-and-forget UDP on both. Only real-desk differences are timing: each unmute first reads the leaf (panic invalidated the cache, so ~1 RTT each, a few ms), and if panic() was the session's first write the first unmute triggers the automatic pre-write snapshot (README: 'a few seconds on a real desk') — a delay, not a gate. 22 outputs re-open in well under 1 s after that.
Why not higher: this defeats no documented guarantee — it IS the documented design. BRIEF.md §4 lists mutes as Tier 1 and only 'main LR/mono fader+mute' as Tier 2; the panic tool docstring (server.py:1064-1067) and README.md:621-625 literally say 'Unmute afterwards with unmute / set_main_mute'. Panic itself is not defeated (it silences all 24; verified), the FOH PA (Main LR, M/C) stays behind a token, and unmute alone only restores the exact pre-panic state of that output (mix/on does not touch gain; bus faders remain clamped at the 0 dB ceiling by other Tier-1 paths). The 'howl' escalation in the claim depends on separate findings (CFS parking the bus master, ramps continuing under the mute); standalone, the worst case is re-creating whatever condition (e.g. wedge feedback) prompted the panic, at its previous level, with no human in the loop. Why not lower: under the brief's explicit threat model (confused/runaway LLM), panic is the one tool meant to stop that agent's damage, yet the same agent can undo 22/24 of it ~100 ms later with ordinary calls, in show mode, with no confirmation event ever emitted (0 policy.confirm_required events observed) — and those 22 outputs are the wedges/IEMs/matrix feeds, i.e. the hearing-relevant ones. Medium (design gap in defence-in-depth, reachable with ordinary calls) is the right grade; it is not 'high' (no documented guarantee broken) nor 'critical' (panic works, mains guarded, no unbounded level change from this path alone).
Minor inaccuracy in the claim: the 'Unmute afterwards with unmute / set_main_mute' text is in the panic tool docstring (server.py:1066) and README:624, not INSTRUCTIONS at server.py:128-129 (which only says panic is Tier 1, never blocked).
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-panic-4-src (branch wt/verify-panic-4-src, commit e4f79f2), test file tests/integration/test_verify_panic4.py, results in $SP/reports/verify-panic-4-src/.
V1 (test_V1_unmute_bus_mtx_after_panic_is_plain_tier1) PASSES on HEAD a408a2a: show_mode(True) -> panic() ok count=24, all 24 outputs mix/on=0 on the FakeDesk -> unmute('bus.1'..'bus.16','mtx.1'..'mtx.6'): 22/22 ok, 0 requires_confirmation, 0 policy.confirm_required events, elapsed 0.086 s, bucket_left 28.08 of 50, 22 outputs mix/on=1; unmute('main.st') and unmute('main.m') -> {code: GUARDED, 'Main LR mute is a guarded (Tier 2) parameter; use set_main_mute'} and both mains stay muted. policy.tier_for: /bus/01/mix/on=1, /mtx/06/mix/on=1, /main/st/mix/on=2, /main/m/mix/on=2 (V1_post_panic_unmute.json).
V2 (test_V2_repeat_panic_unmute_cycles_hit_no_gate) PASSES: 3 consecutive panic->22-unmute cycles, every cycle panic_ok=true, unmutes_ok=22, codes=['ok'], live=22 — the rate limiter never engages (V2_cycles.json).
Code refs: server.py:929-937 (unmute, no _confirm, no show-mode check); desk.py:1015-1024 (set_mute -> _write with guarded=False); desk.py:871-884 (_write gates = tier_for + acquire_write only); device.yaml:214 (mix/on tier 1), device.yaml:708-721 (guarded globs: only /main/st/mix/*, /main/m/mix/*); descriptor.py:1041-1046 (tier_for); policy.py:65-67 + 213-224 (show mode blocks scenes/ring-outs only); desk.py:1184-1220 (panic keeps no state); policy.py:375-397 (acquire_write, 50/s). Docs establishing this is intended: BRIEF.md:62-65 (§4 tiers: mutes Tier 1; Tier 2 = main LR/mono fader+mute), server.py:1064-1067 and README.md:621-625 ('Unmute afterwards with unmute / set_main_mute'), HANDOVER.md §4a (mute wire semantics and panic verified on the real X32).
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's latch is the right shape; make it concrete and minimal: (1) Desk.panic() sets self._panic_latch = set(done) (target keys) and publishes it in desk.panic; (2) Desk._write refuses raw==1 on `<prefix>/mix/on` for a latched target unless guarded=True -> DeskError('PANIC_LATCHED', '... was silenced by panic(); re-open it with unmute(target, confirm_token) or restore_snapshot'); (3) server.unmute: if desk.is_latched(t) route through _confirm('unmute_after_panic', summary naming the strip and its current fader level, payload={target}) and call desk.set_mute(..., guarded=True) on the confirmed second call, clearing that target from the latch; restore_snapshot and a confirmed set_main_mute also clear their targets; expose the latch in connection_status/dashboard so the operator sees 'PANIC LATCHED: 22 outputs'. (4) While at it, panic() should cancel every in-flight ramp (for task in self._ramps.values(): task.cancel(); self._ramps.clear()) and CfsManager should abort any armed/run state on the desk.panic event, so nothing keeps moving levels underneath the mute — that removes the escalation paths the claim leans on. Cheaper alternative if a token per wedge is too chatty at a gig: one confirmed clear_panic(confirm_token) that lifts the whole latch, after which unmute is Tier 1 again. Update the panic docstring/README ('Unmute afterwards...') to describe the confirmation.

## D34. [medium] connect()/disconnect() are Tier 0, unconfirmed and allowed in show mode: one confused call removes the panic path (and all control) mid-show
*Angle:* panic · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:643-665 (connect: 'Replaces any existing connection… Tier 0' → App.connect → conn.connect() closes the live socket first, connection.py:297-299), server.py:668-684 (disconnect: Tier 0), server.py:219-230 (_desk() raises NOT_CONNECTED for DISCONNECTED/CONNECTING, so panic() is refused), policy.py:65-67 (show mode does not cover them)

**Mechanism.** connect(host) tears down the working connection before probing the new host; a wrong/garbled IP leaves state DISCONNECTED after the /info timeout with no auto-reconnect (the reconnect loop only runs from DEGRADED). disconnect() does the same deliberately. From then on every tool including panic() answers NOT_CONNECTED until someone issues a correct connect(). Neither call asks for confirmation, neither is blocked in show mode, and both also abort a running CFS session (leaving its bus wherever the abort path puts it).

**Exploit.** show_mode(true) → connect('192.168.1.99') (hallucinated address, or the tablet's IP) → CONNECT_FAILED after ~1.5 s → panic() → {ok:false, NOT_CONNECTED 'call connect(host) first'}; or simply disconnect() → panic() → NOT_CONNECTED.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_panic.py::test_D3_unconfirmed_connect_to_wrong_host_removes_the_panic_path PASSES on HEAD (D3_connect_kills_panic.json): with show mode on, connect('127.0.0.1', 1) → CONNECT_FAILED, state 'disconnected', panic → NOT_CONNECTED; after reconnecting, disconnect() → ok, panic → NOT_CONNECTED; 24/24 outputs live throughout. Not addressed by the fix sketch.

**Proposed fix.** While a desk is CONNECTED (or DEGRADED), make connect(other host) and disconnect() go through the Tier-2 _confirm() dance and refuse them in show mode (SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS); additionally have App.connect probe the new host on a second socket and only swap connections once /info answered, so a failed connect never costs the existing link. Keep first-time connect (from DISCONNECTED) Tier 0.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-panic-5-repro (branch wt/verify-panic-5-repro, commit 6dd99b7), file tests/integration/test_verify_panic5.py, 5/5 pass on HEAD a408a2a code.

Mechanism verified in source: server.py:645-664 `connect` has parameters (host, port) only — no confirm_token, no check_show_mode_allows, docstring 'Replaces any existing connection… Tier 0'; it calls App.connect (server.py:192-200) which first `await self.cfs.abort('reconnect requested')` (abort only sets the wake flag for a ring-out, cfs.py:621-635, it does not wait for back-off/restore) and then conn.connect(); connection.py:297-298 `if self._transport is not None or self._tasks: await self.close()` tears down the live socket BEFORE `_resolve` (line 308) and before the /info probe, and on failure sets DISCONNECTED (lines 314, 322, 388-399). The reconnect loop only runs from DEGRADED (connection.py:911-939), so nothing ever brings the link back. server.py:668-684 `disconnect` likewise has no token and no show-mode check. server.py:222-231 `_desk()` raises NOT_CONNECTED for DISCONNECTED/CONNECTING, and panic() (server.py:1064-1067) goes through `_desk()`, so it is refused. SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED (policy.py:74-76) contains neither action.

What actually happens (all executed):
A) show_mode(true) → connect('127.0.0.1', 1) → CONNECT_FAILED ('no /info reply') after 0.5 s with the test timeouts (production defaults timeout_s=0.5 × 3 attempts = 1.5 s, connection.py:245-246) → conn.state 'disconnected' → panic() = {ok:false, NOT_CONNECTED 'not connected to a desk: call connect(host) first (last error: no /info reply from 127.0.0.1:1)'}; still 'disconnected' 1 s later (no auto-reconnect); connection_status/set_fader also NOT_CONNECTED; fake desk shows 24/24 main/bus/mtx outputs still ON. A correct connect() restores control, show_mode still True, panic then mutes 24.
B) show_mode(true) → disconnect() → ok (was connected) → panic() NOT_CONNECTED, 24/24 outputs live.
C) Even an unresolvable/garbled host ('x32..local') costs the link: CONNECT_FAILED 'cannot resolve', state disconnected, panic NOT_CONNECTED — because close() precedes _resolve().
D/E) The CFS side-claim also holds and is worse than 'wherever the abort path puts it': with a ring-out raising Bus 1 from -40 dB, connect('127.0.0.1',1) at master -34 dB → report final_stage ABORT, abort_reason 'reconnect requested', stages …RAISE,BACKOFF,ABORT but end_master_db == max_master_db == -34.0 (the 6 dB abort back-off write never reached the desk because the socket was already closed), no cfs.restore event (restored=null; _lose_connection only fires on DEGRADED, cfs.py:1296-1298), fake bus fader left at -34.03 dB = +6 dB above the operator's level, detector stopped, and panic() NOT_CONNECTED.

Severity: I keep it at medium. It does not itself move a level and panic is not 'defeated' while connected; BRIEF.md:90 explicitly says the emergency reflex is X32-Edit/physical, and recovery is one correct connect() away. But it is a reachable single confused Tier-0 call (hallucinated IP, tablet IP, typo) that silently removes the software panic path and all control mid-show with no confirmation and no show-mode protection, contradicting 'panic never blocked' (BRIEF.md:65) in spirit; scenario E (pre-show ring-out) additionally leaves a bus raised toward its feedback threshold, unmonitored and unrestorable via MCP, which borders on high.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-panic-5-repro (commit 6dd99b7), test file tests/integration/test_verify_panic5.py — `runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_verify_panic5.py` → 5 passed in 5.27 s. JSON captures under $SP/reports/verify-panic-5-repro/: A_connect_wrong_host.json {connect_params:[host,port], disconnect_params:[], bad_connect: CONNECT_FAILED 'no /info reply from 127.0.0.1:1', bad_connect_elapsed_s:0.5, state_after_bad_connect:'disconnected', panic_after_bad_connect: NOT_CONNECTED state 'disconnected', state_after_wait:'disconnected', live_before:[24,24], live_after:[24,24]}; B_disconnect.json {disconnect ok was 'connected' → 'disconnected', panic NOT_CONNECTED, live_after [24,24]}; C_garbled_host.json {connect('x32..local') CONNECT_FAILED cannot resolve, state 'disconnected', panic NOT_CONNECTED}; E_mid_ringout.json {master_at_connect:-34.0, report:{final_stage:ABORT, abort_reason:'reconnect requested', start_master_db:-40, max_master_db:-34, end_master_db:-34, connection_lost:false, restored:null}, stages_tail:[RAISE,RAISE,RAISE,RAISE,BACKOFF,ABORT], restores:[], fake_bus_fader_after:-34.03, bus_left_raised_db:5.97, panic NOT_CONNECTED}. Source refs: server.py:645-664 (connect, no token/show check), 192-200 (App.connect aborts CFS then conn.connect), 668-684 (disconnect), 222-231 (_desk NOT_CONNECTED), 1064-1067 (panic via _desk); connection.py:297-298 (close before resolve/probe), 314/322 (DISCONNECTED on failure), 911-939 (reconnect only from DEGRADED); policy.py:74-76 (SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED lacks connect/disconnect); cfs.py:621-635 (abort does not await back-off), 1296-1318 (restore only on DEGRADED).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix and add two points the reproduction exposed: (1) In X32Connection.connect (connection.py:288-323) do not close the existing transport up front — resolve and probe the new host on a fresh socket and swap only after /info answers; on any failure leave the old link and its heartbeat/watchdog tasks untouched (this alone makes a wrong-host connect harmless, including the unresolvable-name case C). (2) In App.connect/disconnect, when cfs.active and mode is RINGOUT, await cfs.stop() (which waits for the ABORT back-off and report) or run _restore_master to confirmation BEFORE touching the socket, so the bus is never left at its raised level (scenario E). (3) While state is CONNECTED/DEGRADED, route connect(other host) and disconnect() through _confirm() with the console name in the action_summary and add both to SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED; keep connect from DISCONNECTED and reconnect-to-the-same-host Tier 0. Optionally let panic() attempt a last-known-host reconnect+mute instead of returning NOT_CONNECTED when a console was connected earlier in the session.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap:
(1) server.py:643-665 connect() and 668-684 disconnect() carry no _confirm() dance and no policy.check_show_mode_allows(); SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED (policy.py:74-76) lists only scene_recall/scene_save/setup_ringout_eqs/ring_out/ring_out_system. (2) App.connect (server.py:192-201) -> X32Connection.connect (connection.py:288-299): `if self._transport is not None or self._tasks: await self.close()` runs BEFORE _resolve/_bind/_info, and self._host/_host_spelling are overwritten with the new host, so the previous desk's address is forgotten. (3) On /info silence connect() calls _teardown(), sets _error='no /info reply from <new>', state DISCONNECTED and raises RequestTimeout (connection.py:313-318); the heartbeat/watchdog tasks are only created after a successful /info (connection.py:340-344) and _reconnect_loop only runs while DEGRADED (connection.py:909-954), so nothing ever re-dials the old desk. Production timeouts are the X32Connection defaults timeout_s=0.5, retries=2 -> 1.5 s. (4) _desk() (server.py:225-235) raises NOT_CONNECTED for DISCONNECTED/CONNECTING, so panic() (server.py:1064-1073) is refused before Desk.panic runs; even if it weren't, Desk.panic (desk.py:1184-1222) has no transport to send from (_send, connection.py:733-737). (5) connection_status after the bad connect reports console=None and last_error naming the WRONG host; the good host survives nowhere in tool output (only X32_HOST env, if set). Recovery needs discover_consoles + connect(host) — Tier 0, ~2 s, but the model must think of it.
Real desk vs FakeDesk: the whole mechanism is client-side (socket teardown + state machine); a real X32 behaves identically — it keeps passing audio at its current levels, the /xremote subscription lapses after 10 s, nothing on the desk changes (writes_delta=0, 24/24 outputs live). A hallucinated LAN IP or the tablet's IP simply never answers /info -> same CONNECT_FAILED/DISCONNECTED (an ICMP unreachable would at most surface as a socket error -> still CONNECT_FAILED).
CFS side effect — the claim understates it: disconnect() calls cfs.stop() (cfs.py:587-613), which for a ring-out sets abort, wakes the loop and WAITS for ses.done, so BACKOFF (-6 dB) lands before conn.close(): verified master -30 -> raised -26 -> left -32. But App.connect calls cfs.abort() (cfs.py:621-636), which for RINGOUT only sets the flag and returns; conn.connect() then closes the socket immediately; the ring-out loop's _finalize_levels back-off write (cfs.py:1281-1290) hits X32ConnectionError, is logged and swallowed; _on_connection_event (cfs.py:1295-1303) only reacts to DEGRADED, not DISCONNECTED, so connection_lost stays False and _restore_master never runs. Verified: bus master left at the raised ring-out level (-26, up from -30; in a real run this is the edge-of-feedback level, up to the 0 dB ceiling), report says ABORT 'reconnect requested', restored=None, and the MCP is disconnected so panic() answers NOT_CONNECTED. That violates BRIEF §4 'abort: back off 6 dB' / 'halt on connection loss and restore starting fader level'. Reachability caveat: ring_out is refused in show mode and blocks its own tool call, so this needs a concurrent connect() tool call (FastMCP serves requests concurrently; a client issuing parallel calls or retrying after a client-side timeout) — unusual but reachable.
Severity: I keep MEDIUM. Mitigating: BRIEF.md:90 states emergency actions are never routed through the conversational path (panic() is secondary to X32-Edit/physical), the disconnected MCP can do no harm either, the desk is untouched, recovery is two Tier-0 calls, and tests/integration/test_server_tools.py:93-117 already asserts this behaviour (by design). On its own the 'panic unavailable' part borders on low. Aggravating: show mode is explicitly 'about accidents' (DESIGN §10) yet a one-argument unconfirmed Tier-0 call silently costs the live link mid-show with no fallback to the previous desk, and the connect()-during-ring-out path leaves a bus master raised with no back-off, no restore and no link — a 'dangerous state after an error' pattern under unusual-but-reachable conditions. Net: medium, not high (no level is raised by connect/disconnect themselves; the ring-out sub-case needs concurrency and is outside show mode), not low (documented abort/restore guarantees are defeated in that sub-case and the show-mode accident surface is real).
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-panic-5-src (branch wt/verify-panic-5-src, commit 0faad3c), tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic5.py, 3 passed in 6.3 s; JSON under $SP/reports/verify-panic-5-src/.
V1_claim_as_stated.json: show_mode(True) ok -> connect('127.0.0.1',1) -> {ok:false, CONNECT_FAILED 'no /info reply from 127.0.0.1:1'} in 0.5 s (test timeouts; 1.5 s with production defaults) -> conn.state 'disconnected', conn._tasks == [] (no reconnect loop), transport None, writes_delta 0, 24/24 outputs live; connection_status -> console None, last_error 'no /info reply from 127.0.0.1:1' (good host gone); panic() -> {ok:false, NOT_CONNECTED 'not connected to a desk: call connect(host) first (last error: no /info reply from 127.0.0.1:1)'}; still disconnected 1 s later. Reconnect -> disconnect() in show mode -> ok, state disconnected -> panic() NOT_CONNECTED.
V2_ringout_interrupted_by_connect_bad.json: confirmed ring_out(bus 1, -30 -> target -10, 1 dB/200 ms) running as a concurrent task; at master -26 call connect('127.0.0.1',1) -> CONNECT_FAILED; ring_out returns ok:true final_stage ABORT abort_reason 'reconnect requested', start -30, max -26, end_master_db -26, connection_lost false, restored null; FakeDesk /bus/01/mix/fader final -25.99 (LEFT RAISED, no 6 dB back-off, no restore); conn 'disconnected'; panic -> NOT_CONNECTED.
V3 control V2_ringout_interrupted_by_disconnect.json: same run interrupted by disconnect() -> 'stopped CFS² ringout session', end_master_db -32, desk fader -31.99 (backed off 6 dB below max), then disconnected.
Code refs: server.py:192-201 (App.connect: cfs.abort then conn.connect), server.py:643-684 (Tier 0 tools, no _confirm/show-mode check), server.py:225-235 (_desk NOT_CONNECTED gate), connection.py:288-318 (close-first, DISCONNECTED on /info timeout, tasks not started), connection.py:909-954 (reconnect only from DEGRADED), policy.py:74-76 (SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED), cfs.py:587-636 (stop waits, abort does not), cfs.py:1281-1303 (back-off failure swallowed; connection-loss restore keyed on DEGRADED only), desk.py:1184-1222 (panic needs a transport). Pre-existing suite already encodes the behaviour: tests/integration/test_server_tools.py:93-117.
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's fix is right in shape; make it concrete and cover the ring-out hole: (a) In server.connect/disconnect, when conn.state is CONNECTED or DEGRADED, require the Tier-2 _confirm() dance (payload bound to host:port / 'disconnect') and call policy.check_show_mode_allows('connect'/'disconnect') with both added to SHOW_MODE_BLOCKED; first-time connect from DISCONNECTED stays Tier 0. (b) In App.connect replace cfs.abort() with `await self.cfs.stop()` (the waiting path disconnect() already uses) so a running ring-out backs off/restores while the socket is still up; additionally have CfsManager treat a connection.state DISCONNECTED/CONNECTING event during a live session like DEGRADED (_lose_connection) as a belt-and-braces. (c) In X32Connection.connect, probe the new host on a second socket (/info round trip) and only close/swap the existing transport after it answers; on failure leave the old connection, its tasks and _host untouched and raise CONNECT_FAILED with 'still connected to <old>'.
