(d) Keep the last successfully connected host:port in ConnectionStatus (e.g. last_console) so NOT_CONNECTED errors can say 'reconnect with connect("<host>")' instead of naming the failed address.

## D35. [medium] 'Mutes all outputs' is over-claimed and the residual live paths are neither detectable nor documented to the operator (PRE/IN/EQ-tapped outs, DirectOut/P16/AES50/card, monitor/phones/solo, mute-group release)
*Angle:* panic · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* False · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md:405-407 (§4.7: taps without +M ignore the strip mute; only POST and +M follow it; factory pos default UNCONFIRMED §13 item 13), fx_routing_scenes.md:706-713 (§11 option 1 caveats: DirectOut src 26..73, AES50/CARD input blocks, AuxIN/TB, solo→AUX5-8, Monitor L/R are not silenced; options 3/4 listed), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1185-1187 (docstring honest: 'every POST-tapped output'), src/x32mcp/server.py:128 + 1065-1067 and README.md:204,621 + docs/GIG_CHECKLIST.md:10,79 + docs/BRIEF.md:65 ('mutes all outputs' / 'everything gone at once' — no caveat), device.yaml (no /outputs/* section at all → the server cannot read src/pos to warn, and snapshots do not capture output taps), fx_routing_scenes.md:481 (§5.1 hardmute semantics UNCONFIRMED: with Hard Mutes off, releasing a mute group may un-mute members that panic muted)

**Mechanism.** panic() implements §11 option 1 only. Physical outputs whose /outputs/{main,aux,p16,aes,rec}/NN/pos is IN/LC, <-EQ, EQ-> or PRE (no +M) keep passing the bus/main signal regardless of mix/on; P16/Ultranet personal-monitor feeds and recording/broadcast splits are very commonly DirectOut Ch taps (src 26..73) that bypass every bus master; AES50/card blocks routed from input banks likewise; the solo bus feeds phones/AUX5-8; talkback is untouched. A typical IEM rig on P16 direct outs therefore keeps a howling vocal mic in the singer's ears through a 'successful' panic. The operator-facing texts promise 'all outputs', and because /outputs is absent from device.yaml the server can neither check the tap positions at connect time nor include them in the pre-write snapshot/restore. Mute-group interaction after a panic is unknowable from code (UNCONFIRMED in research) and there is no writer for /config/mute in the repo, but the operator can release a group on the surface.

**Exploit.** Rig with P16 personal mixers fed from DirectOut Ch 1-16 (standard IEM setup) or OUT 1-8 taps set PRE for a broadcast split → feedback → panic() → ok 24 muted → IEMs / split still carry the ringing channel at full level.

**Evidence.** Not executable: FakeDesk and device.yaml do not model /outputs/*/src|pos or mute-group propagation, so this is established from the verified research text (§4.7 'Taps without +M are not affected by the strip's mute' VERIFIED; §11 caveat list VERIFIED) against the operator docs. reproduced=false for that reason.

**Proposed fix.** 1) Say it where the operator reads it: panic tool docstring/summary, INSTRUCTIONS, README and GIG_CHECKLIST: 'silences bus/matrix/main masters for POST or +M taps only; direct-out/P16/AES50/card/monitor/PRE-tapped feeds stay live — pull those on the surface'. 2) Add /outputs/{main,aux,p16,aes,rec}/NN {src,pos} to device.yaml; at connect (and in preflight) read them and warn once listing every output that panic cannot silence (pos not in {1,3,5,7,8} or src in 26..76). 3) Offer panic(inputs=True) = §11 option 3 (also /ch/01-32, /auxin/01-08, /fxrtn/01-08 mix/on 0 + /-action/clearsolo) for rigs with direct-out monitoring; 48 more datagrams is still <1 ms. 4) GIG_CHECKLIST pre-show item: set /-prefs/hardmute on, or verify mute-group release does not re-open panicked masters on this firmware.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Every software-checkable assertion in the claim reproduces against HEAD a408a2a in my own worktree (verify-panic-6-repro, commit 1752a1b, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic6.py, 7/7 pass):
(1) Desk.panic() (src/x32mcp/desk.py:1184-1221, _PANIC_FAMILIES=("main","bus","mtx") at :92) puts exactly 24 datagrams on the wire, in order /main/st/mix/on, /main/m/mix/on, /bus/01..16/mix/on, /mtx/01..06/mix/on, all ",i 0" — captured at the X32Connection._send chokepoint. Nothing is sent to /ch/*, /auxin/*, /fxrtn/*, /dca/*, /outputs/*, /-action/clearsolo, /config/mute, /config/solo, /config/talk; FakeDesk confirms ch.1/auxin.1/fxrtn.1/dca.1 stay ON. Desk.panic and the server tool take no arguments, so there is no way to widen the scope (§11 option 3/4 not implemented).
(2) device.yaml contains no "outputs" string at all; Descriptor.param_for_address returns None for /outputs/{main,aux,p16,aes,rec}/NN/{src,pos}, no node path starts with /outputs, tier_for('/outputs/main/01/src')==1 (unknown leaf). The auto pre-write snapshot (12 688 lines) has zero /outputs lines while it does carry /config/routing/* and /config/mute — so the server can neither read tap positions to warn at connect/preflight nor restore them. FakeDesk.get('/outputs/main/01/pos') is None: the emulator has no output-tap model, which is why the acoustic consequence cannot be executed in this sandbox.
(3) Operator/LLM-facing wording: server summary "PANIC: 24 outputs muted in …" with no caveat; tool docstring (server.py:1065-1067) and INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:128) both say to use it for "mute everything"; README.md:204 "It mutes all 24 outputs"; docs/GIG_CHECKLIST.md:11 "mutes all outputs in one go"; docs/BRIEF.md:65 "mutes all output buses + main". None of README/GIG_CHECKLIST/INSTRUCTIONS/tool doc mention POST/+M taps, direct outs, P16, AES50/card, monitor/solo. The only honest statement is the Desk.panic docstring (desk.py:1185-1187 "Silences every POST-tapped output (§11 option 1)") which the operator never sees.
(4) The physical residual paths rest on docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md §4.7 (line ~407, VERIFIED: "Taps without +M are not affected by the strip's mute; +M variants and POST are muted with the strip") and §11 option-1 caveat list (VERIFIED): PRE/IN/LC/<-EQ/EQ-> taps without +M, DirectOut src 26..73, AES50/CARD input blocks, AuxIN/TB, Monitor/solo. For DirectOut-fed outputs (the common P16 IEM rig) the conclusion does not even depend on tap semantics: panic writes no channel address, so a channel direct out cannot be affected by construction — and HANDOVER.md:114-115 / test_desk.py:368 record "input channels deliberately untouched" as intent.
Nuances/corrections to the claim: (a) "no writer for /config/mute in the repo" is slightly off — /config/mute is in the snapshot node list (device.yaml:678) so Desk.restore/restore_snapshot can rewrite mute-group masters via conn.slash; there is no tool that toggles them directly. The mute-group-release-reopens-panicked-masters interaction remains UNCONFIRMED research (§5.1) and is an operator surface action, i.e. speculative. (b) The exploit narrative "IEMs still carry the ringing channel at full level" overstates: if the PA/wedge outs are POST (believed factory default, UNCONFIRMED) panic breaks the acoustic loop and the ring dies; IEMs then carry the mic at normal level. The ring survives panic only when the loop itself runs through a non-POST/non-+M tap or a direct-out-fed speaker. A more concrete LLM-threat variant that does hold: a runaway client can stack +15 dB EQ boosts on a channel (eq_gain_abs_max_db: 15, Tier 1) and panic() does nothing for listeners on DirectOut/P16 feeds tapped EQ->/PRE/POST of that channel.
Severity: medium is fair. It is not a code defect and does not defeat panic's implemented contract (24/24 verified on real hardware, HANDOVER:114); the precise enumeration (Main LR/M-C, 16 buses, 6 matrices) appears in the tool doc, README table :496, GIG_CHECKLIST:79, and the checklist's golden rule (:9-11) already says panic is a backstop, not the plan. But two operator summaries and the runtime summary promise "all outputs"/"mute everything", the LLM will relay "everything is muted" by voice to a possibly non-expert user, and the descriptor gap means the server cannot detect or warn about rigs (PRE taps, direct-out monitoring — reachable, the latter common) where that statement is false. That is a documented guarantee weakened under reachable configuration, not an unbounded level change → medium, not high/critical.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-panic-6-repro (branch wt/verify-panic-6-repro, commit 1752a1b); test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic6.py (copy in $SP/reports/verify-panic-6-repro/). Run: runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic6.py -s → "7 passed in 0.92s".
- test_panic_writes_exactly_24_master_mutes_and_nothing_else: spy on conn._send + osc.decode; writes == ['/main/st/mix/on','/main/m/mix/on','/bus/01/mix/on'…'/bus/16/mix/on','/mtx/01/mix/on'…'/mtx/06/mix/on'], all args (0,); no address with prefix /ch/ /auxin/ /fxrtn/ /dca/ /outputs/ /-action/clearsolo /config/mute /config/solo /config/talk /headamp/; fakedesk /ch/01/mix/on==1, /auxin/01/mix/on==1, /fxrtn/01/mix/on==1, /dca/1/on==1; inspect.signature(Desk.panic).parameters==['self'], server panic tool takes no params.
- test_descriptor_knows_nothing_about_output_taps: descriptor.param_for_address(None) for /outputs/main/01/{pos,src}, /outputs/p16/01/src, /outputs/aux/01/pos, /outputs/aes/01/src, /outputs/rec/01/pos; no descriptor.nodes() path startswith /outputs; 'outputs' not in device.yaml text; descriptor.tier_for('/outputs/main/01/src')==1.
- test_fakedesk_does_not_model_output_taps: fakedesk.get('/outputs/main/01/pos') is None, fakedesk.get('/outputs/p16/01/src') is None (why the acoustic outcome is not executable; FakeDesk._meter_values also synthesises no /meters/4 output levels).
- test_snapshot_file_on_disk_has_no_outputs_lines: auto pre-write snapshot 20260921-120600-auto-pre-write.json, 12688 lines, 0 lines containing 'outputs'; '/config/mute' and 'routing/OUT' present.
- test_server_panic_summary_and_instructions_wording: srv.panic() → ok, count 24, summary startswith 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted in'; none of post/tap/direct/directout/p16/aes50/monitor/solo in summary; 'mute everything' in srv.panic.__doc__ and srv.INSTRUCTIONS; none of POST/'direct out'/DirectOut/P16/tap in either.
- test_operator_docs_wording: README.md contains 'It mutes all 24 outputs' (:204); GIG_CHECKLIST.md contains 'mutes all outputs in one go' (:11) and 'it is a backstop, not the plan'; BRIEF.md 'mutes all output buses + main' (:65); desk.py contains 'Silences every POST-tapped output' (:1187); regex POST-tapped|\+M|DirectOut|direct[- ]out absent from README and GIG_CHECKLIST.
Source refs: desk.py:92 _PANIC_FAMILIES=("main","bus","mtx"); desk.py:1184-1221 panic body (f"{t.osc_prefix}/mix/on" ← 0 only); server.py:108-135 INSTRUCTIONS, :1063-1075 panic tool; research fx_routing_scenes.md §4.7 pos enum + mute semantics (VERIFIED), §11 options 1-4 (~lines 706-713); §5.1 hardmute semantics UNCONFIRMED (~line 481); HANDOVER.md:114-115 'Input channels are deliberately untouched'; tests/integration/test_desk.py:368 asserts /ch/01/mix/on == 1 after panic (design intent). Not executed (cannot be, no hardware / no /outputs model): that a PRE/IN/EQ-tapped physical output keeps passing audio after mix/on 0, and mute-group release behaviour after panic.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix; ordering by value: (1) cheapest and most important — change the three operator strings the LLM relays: server.py panic summary → "PANIC: 24 mix masters muted (Main LR/M-C, bus 1-16, mtx 1-6). Direct-out/P16/AES50/card feeds, PRE-tapped outs, monitor/phones and talkback are NOT affected — pull those on the surface"; same sentence in the tool docstring, INSTRUCTIONS (server.py:128), README.md:204 and GIG_CHECKLIST.md:11. (2) Add a GIG_CHECKLIST pre-show item: read ROUTING→OUT 1-16 / AUX / P16 / AES tap = POST or +M for anything feeding a loudspeaker; note which feeds are direct outs; set Config→Mute System→Hard Mutes on. (3) Add /outputs/{main 01-16, aux 01-06, p16 01-16, aes 01-02, rec 01-02} {src,pos[,invert]} to device.yaml (research §4.7 has the verified types/enums, guarded glob "/outputs/*"), include them in the snapshot node list, and have connect()/connection_status emit a one-time warning listing outputs with pos∉{1,3,5,7,8} and src≥26 ("panic cannot silence: OUT 3 (PRE from MixBus 3), P16 1-16 (DirectOut Ch 1-16)…"). (4) Optional panic(inputs: bool=False) implementing §11 option 3 (/ch 01-32, /auxin 01-08, /fxrtn 01-08 mix/on 0 + /-action/clearsolo 1), still Tier 1/rate-limit-exempt; keep default False so the verified 24-message path and the 'inputs untouched' contract stay unchanged, and make unmute-after-panic guidance cover it.

> **Verifier 2: PARTIAL (medium)** — Core chain holds end to end; two sub-claims do not.

WHAT HOLDS (code + VERIFIED research, real desk behaves the same):
1. panic() is exactly fx_routing_scenes.md §11 option 1 and nothing more: desk.py:92 `_PANIC_FAMILIES = ("main","bus","mtx")`, desk.py:1184-1220 loops those 24 targets writing `{prefix}/mix/on 0` via conn.set (send_raw fallback). Executed in worktree verify-panic-6-src (tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic6.py, commit d7d2adc): the wire set is exactly 24 addresses matching ^/(main/(st|m)|bus/NN|mtx/NN)/mix/on$; no /ch, /auxin, /fxrtn, /outputs, /-action/clearsolo, /config/mute is touched; /ch/01, /auxin/01, /fxrtn/01 mix/on stay 1. This is deliberate (HANDOVER.md:114-115 'Input channels are deliberately untouched'; tests/integration/test_desk.py:368 asserts it).
2. On a real X32 a master's mix/on only affects physical outputs tapped POST or a +M variant (fx_routing_scenes.md:405-407 §4.7, VERIFIED from DOC p.40 + X32.c:385 + drewbrashler); §11 (lines 706-713, VERIFIED) explicitly lists what option 1 does NOT silence: /outputs/*/NN with pos IN/LC, <-EQ, EQ->, PRE; DirectOut taps src 26..73 (bypass every master); AES50/CARD blocks patched from input banks; AuxIN/TB; solo->phones/AUX5-8; Monitor L/R. FakeDesk has no audio model so this half cannot be executed, but it is hardware architecture, not emulator behaviour, so the real desk is the case where it matters and it would behave exactly as the research states.
3. Not detectable: device.yaml has no `outputs` family, no /outputs node paths and no /-prefs/hardmute (executed: `"outputs" not in descriptor.params`, no node path startswith /outputs, FakeDesk.get('/outputs/main/01/pos') is None). So connect/preflight cannot warn, and snapshots/restore_plan never capture or restore tap src/pos.
4. Not documented where the operator/LLM reads it: server.py:1065-1067 tool docstring, server.py:128 INSTRUCTIONS ('use it whenever the user says ... mute everything'), server.py:1072 result summary 'PANIC: 24 outputs muted', README.md:204 ('mutes all 24 outputs'), GIG_CHECKLIST.md:10 ('mutes all outputs in one go'), GIG_CHECKLIST.md:79 ('everything gone at once'), BRIEF.md:65 — none carries the POST/+M / direct-out caveat; only the internal desk.py:1187 docstring ('every POST-tapped output') and research §11 do. REVIEW_BRIEF.md:121 asks precisely 'is there any state in which it fails to silence the outputs?' — the honest answer is yes, and it is unstated to the operator. Mitigating: most of those texts do enumerate the 24 masters accurately, and GIG_CHECKLIST.md:8-10/README.md:625 already frame panic() as a backstop behind the physical mute/X32-Edit.

WHAT DOES NOT HOLD:
a) The headline exploit is overstated for the 'standard' rig it names. Feedback needs an acoustic loop through a loudspeaker; if the wedges/PA sit on POST-tapped bus/main outputs (factory default per general knowledge, §11 UNCONFIRMED item), panic breaks the loop and the howl decays within a few hundred ms — the P16 direct-out IEM then carries the ordinary (no longer ringing) mic signal, not 'the ringing channel at full level'. Panic genuinely fails to stop feedback only when the loop-sustaining speaker itself is on an untouched path: an OUT/AUX tap set IN/LC, <-EQ, EQ-> or PRE (no +M) feeding an amp, a powered wedge/hot-spot fed from a P16/aux DirectOut, or an AES50/card output block patched from a local-input bank into an amp. Those are reachable but unusual configurations. What does persist unconditionally through panic on direct-out/monitor/solo paths is any non-feedback sustained loud source (stuck note, DI hum blast, RF noise) into in-ears/phones — a real hearing concern for IEM wearers, but one panic was never scoped to address.
b) 'There is no writer for /config/mute in the repo' is wrong: device.yaml:678 puts /config/mute in family `config`, which nodes.py:111 `_RESTORE_EXCLUDED_FAMILIES` does not exclude, so restore_snapshot (Tier 2, confirmed) writes it via conn.slash when it differs (executed: test_restore_plan_can_write_config_mute passes). Not safety-relevant here (restore after a panic re-opens the masters anyway — it is the undo), but the statement is inaccurate.
c) The mute-group-release interaction is speculation stacked on an UNCONFIRMED research item (§5.1 line 482 hardmute semantics) plus an operator action on the surface; nothing in code can be shown either way. It is a reasonable GIG_CHECKLIST note, not a finding.

SEVERITY: stays medium, at its lower edge. It does not defeat panic (panic does what DESIGN §13/desk.py:574 specify and what HANDOVER verified 24/24 on hardware); it is a scope limitation inherent to §11 option 1 whose residual paths are reachable (PRE/IN taps, direct-out-fed speakers, P16 IEM rigs) and are neither detectable by the server nor stated in any operator- or LLM-facing text, while those texts say 'all outputs' / 'mute everything'. Not high: no documented code guarantee (cuts-only, floor, token, rate limit, show mode) is broken and the desk is not left in a worse state.
>
> *Evidence:* Executed (worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-panic-6-src, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_panic6.py, commit d7d2adc, 4 passed): (1) test_panic_wire_addresses_are_only_the_24_masters — wrapped conn.set/send_raw; panic() sent exactly 24 addresses, all ^/(main/(st|m)|bus/\d\d|mtx/\d\d)/mix/on$, none under /ch,/auxin,/fxrtn,/outputs,/-action,/config/mute; FakeDesk /ch/01,/auxin/01,/fxrtn/01 mix/on remain 1. (2) test_descriptor_has_no_outputs_taps — 'outputs' not in descriptor.params, no relpath contains 'outputs' or 'hardmute', no node path starts with /outputs. (3) test_fakedesk_has_no_outputs_state — /outputs/main/01/pos and /outputs/p16/01/src are None (unmodelled). (4) test_restore_plan_can_write_config_mute — /config/mute node family 'config' is not in nodes._RESTORE_EXCLUDED_FAMILIES (refutes the 'no writer' sub-claim). Source refs: src/x32mcp/desk.py:92, 1184-1220 (panic body; docstring 1185-1187 'Silences every POST-tapped output'); src/x32mcp/server.py:128 (INSTRUCTIONS 'mute everything'), 1065-1067 (tool docstring, no caveat), 1072 ('PANIC: N outputs muted'); README.md:204 ('mutes all 24 outputs'), 621-626; docs/GIG_CHECKLIST.md:10 ('mutes all outputs in one go'), 79; docs/BRIEF.md:65; docs/HANDOVER.md:114-115 (real desk: 24/24 muted, 'Input channels are deliberately untouched'); docs/REVIEW_BRIEF.md:121; docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md:405-407 (§4.7 pos enum, 'Taps without +M are not affected by the strip's mute' VERIFIED), 702-713 (§11 option 1 caveats VERIFIED; options 3/4; factory pos default UNCONFIRMED), 482 (§5.1 hardmute semantics UNCONFIRMED); device.yaml:662-687 (config node families: routing/userrout/links/mute/solo/talk — no /outputs), 678 (/config/mute captured), 707-720 (guarded globs); src/x32mcp/nodes.py:111,1201-1227 (restore_plan excludes only show/stat/prefs/action). Audio-path consequence not executable (FakeDesk has no signal model); it rests on the VERIFIED research and applies to the real desk unchanged.
>
> *Better fix:* Keep the claimant's fixes 1-3, reprioritised: (1) cheapest and most valuable — add one sentence to the panic tool docstring, the result summary, INSTRUCTIONS, README:204 and GIG_CHECKLIST:10/79: 'mutes the 24 mix masters; outputs tapped POST/+M go silent; direct-out (P16/personal-mixer, record split), PRE/IN-tapped outs, phones/solo and input-patched AES50/card feeds stay live — pull those on the surface', so the LLM never tells the user 'everything is muted'. (2) Add an optional `panic(inputs: bool = False)` (§11 option 3: /ch/01-32, /auxin/01-08, /fxrtn/01-08 mix/on 0 + /-action/clearsolo 1; ~49 more datagrams, still sub-ms) for direct-out/IEM rigs; keep default False so the current 'unmute the bus restores the mix' recovery story is unchanged, and have restore_snapshot remain the undo. (3) Add read-only /outputs/{main,aux,p16,aes,rec}/NN {src,pos} to device.yaml (Tier 0 params + snapshot node family) and emit a one-time warning at connect/first snapshot listing outputs panic cannot silence (pos not in {1,3,5,7,8}, or src >= 26); this also makes snapshots capture tap changes. (4) GIG_CHECKLIST pre-show item: confirm OUT/P16 tap positions and whether monitoring is on direct outs; the hardmute/mute-group note can be added as advice but should be labelled unverified.

## D36. [medium] No RTA frame-liveness interlock: when meter frames stop the ring-out keeps raising the master blind to the 0 dB ceiling and reports DONE
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1147-1197 (_run_ringout raises on a timer; never checks ses.last_ts / ses.frames), cfs.py:1126-1133 (_dwell = wait_for(wake, dwell) only), cfs.py:901-929 (_arm starts the source but does not wait for a first frame), cfs.py:1221-1252 (_verify_decay treats 'no frames' as 'not decayed' but RAISE has no equivalent)

**Mechanism.** Detections are the only thing that stops the raise loop, and detections need frames. If /meters/15 frames stop (lease/renew lost, desk UI busy, LiveMeters subscription never matched — the exact class of bug HANDOVER found on the real desk — or an injected source dies) the consumer simply blocks on the queue, ses.pending stays empty and the loop executes RAISE every dwell until `master_db >= target` (default target = 0 dB bus ceiling), then BACKOFF 3 dB and DONE with 'no feedback detected'. A deaf detector is indistinguishable from a quiet room.

**Exploit.** ring_out(bus, target_gain_db=None) confirmed; any time after ARM the meter stream stalls (or never starts). The bus master climbs from e.g. -20 dB to 0 dB in 1 dB/1.5 s steps with open mics and nobody listening for feedback; report says DONE, gain_before_feedback 'no feedback detected'. Same blindness applies to feedback_watch (operator believes the guard is armed).

**Evidence.** test_x3e_frames_stop_mid_run_ringout_raises_blind_to_ceiling — injected SyntheticRta stopped (rta.stop()) at master ≥ -17 dB and a 30 dB/s ring injected at the same moment; observed: report final_stage DONE, max_master_db 0.0, notches [], frames after the stop ≤ 2, ≥ 15 further RAISE stages above the stop level, fake fader parked at -3.0. PASSED.

**Proposed fix.** Gate every RAISE on fresh frames: require ses.last_ts newer than ~0.3 s (≥ persistence_frames+1 frames received since the previous raise) before writing; if no frame for > 1 s → abort_reason 'RTA frames stopped' → BACKOFF/ABORT. In _arm, wait (≤ 2 s) for the first frame and refuse to arm (CfsError NO_FRAMES) otherwise; in watch mode publish cfs.abort and stop when frames stall so the operator knows the guard is off.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-1-repro (branch wt/verify-cfs-1-repro, commit 795a90f), test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs1.py, 5/5 PASSED against unmodified src.

Code facts (all confirmed by reading): cfs.py:1147-1198 `_run_ringout` — the only exits from the raise loop are a pending Detection, `master_db >= target` (1168), budget spent, a write refusal, or abort_reason; it never reads `ses.last_ts`/`ses.frames`/`ses.frame_event`. cfs.py:1126-1133 `_dwell` is `wait_for(ses.wake, dwell)` only (wake is set solely by detections/abort). cfs.py:901-929 `_arm` calls `self._frames.start()` (923) and returns; no wait for a first frame. cfs.py:1221-1252 `_verify_decay` is the only place that waits on `frame_event` (and correctly fails closed), RAISE has no counterpart. cfs.py:1296 `_on_connection_event` only reacts to connection.state=degraded, i.e. the control-link watchdog — a stalled /meters stream with a healthy control link never reaches it. meters.py:367-468 LiveMeters has `last_rx_age_s` but nothing in cfs consults it; `running` is just 'renew task exists', which is also what get_cfs_status reports.

What actually happens (executed):
A) SyntheticRta injected, rta.stop() + inject 30 dB/s ring at master -17 dB: 0 frames after the stop, 17 further RAISE stages -16…0 dB, report final_stage DONE, max_master_db 0.0, notches [], detections 0, summary 'no feedback detected; DONE', fake fader parked at -3.0. Exactly the claimant's numbers.
A′) control, same ring with the source alive: detected at -13 dB, notched -3/-6/-9, ABORT 'not tamed', max_master -13, end -19 — so the only difference between a safe run and a blind climb is frame delivery.
B) Frame source that starts OK but never emits (unmatched subscription class of bug): `_arm` accepts it (frames=0 for the whole session), 20 RAISEs -20→0 dB, DONE, end -3.0, 'no feedback detected'.
C) Production wiring — default LiveMeters(conn,15) against the FakeDesk; at -17 dB the fake's meter subs are cancelled and renewals ignored while every GET/heartbeat is still answered: connection.state never leaves 'connected' (no 'degraded' event, connection_lost False), 0 frames after the stall, 17 blind raises to 0.0, DONE/no notches — while the fake's closed-loop ring at 2.4 kHz grew to its -3 dBFS cap (fake_ring_level_end -3.0). I.e. the room howls for the rest of the run and the report says no feedback.
D) feedback_watch on the dead source: state.mode stays 'watch', no cfs.abort, no warning after 1.5 s; stop() report frames 0, final_stage DONE — operator believes the guard is armed.

Nuance (not a refutation): with the shipped dwell 1500 ms a *transient* stall that the desk heals on LiveMeters' next 5 s renew only blinds ~3 raises (~3 dB); the full blind climb needs frames to stay away ≥ ~30 s (subscription never matched / request form rejected — the exact real-desk bug class in HANDOVER —, RTA meter datagrams dropped on Wi-Fi, desk not honouring renewals, LiveMeters on the wrong type, or an injected/fixture source ending) or to never start at all (B), which `_arm` does not check. All of these are reachable in the field.

Severity: high (agree with claimant). The fader move itself is the Tier-2-confirmed, ceiling-bounded raise, so not 'critical' by the scale's letter; but it defeats the documented ring-out guarantee (DESIGN §15 'dwell while detector runs; on detection → HOLD, no further raises', BRIEF §5 detect→cut <100 ms) silently, produces sustained uncut feedback in the room during the climb, parks the bus at ceiling−3 dB = ~10 dB above its real gain-before-feedback (control run: GBF -13 dB) and files a DONE/'no feedback detected' report that ring_out_system and later preflights will trust. Same deafness makes feedback_watch a placebo.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-1-repro (commit 795a90f), tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs1.py — run: `$SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs1.py -s` → 5 passed in 8.39 s. JSON evidence in $SP/reports/verify-cfs-1-repro/{A_frames_stop_mid_run,A_control,B_dead_source,C_livemeters_stall,D_watch_dead_source}.json.
A_frames_stop_mid_run: stopped_at {master_db -17.0, frames 3}; frames_total 3 (0 after stop); raises_after_stop 17; fader_writes -19,-18,…,-1,0.0 then -3.0; final_stage DONE; abort_reason null; max_master_db 0.0; target_db 0.0; notches []; detections 0; gain_before_feedback null; fake fader -2.98; summary 'ring-out Bus 1: master -20.0 -> -3.0 dB (highest 0.0), 0 notch(es), no feedback detected; DONE'.
A_control (source alive): detections 4, notch band 22 to -9.0 dB, gain_before_feedback -13.0, max_master_db -13.0, end -19.0, ABORT 'ring at 2333 Hz not tamed'.
B_dead_source: source_started 1, frames 0, raises 20, max 0.0, end -3.0, DONE, 'no feedback detected'.
C_livemeters_stall (default LiveMeters): stalled_at {-17.0, frames 3}; frames_after_stall 0; raises_after_stall 17; connection_states_seen [] ; conn_state_end 'connected'; connection_lost false; max 0.0; end -3.0; notches []; detections 0; fake_ring_level_end -3.0 (ring at cap, uncut); DONE.
D_watch_dead_source: state after 1.5 s mode 'watch', candidate null, aborts [], stop() report frames 0 / DONE.
Code refs: cfs.py:1147-1198 (_run_ringout, no frame check; exit at 1168 only on master>=target), cfs.py:1126-1133 (_dwell), cfs.py:901-929 (_arm; frames.start() at 923, no first-frame wait), cfs.py:1016-1047 (_consume just blocks on queue.get()), cfs.py:1221-1252 (_verify_decay is the only frame_event consumer), cfs.py:1296 (_on_connection_event: degraded only), meters.py:418-420 (LiveMeters.last_rx_age_s exists, unused by cfs), server.py:1703-1716 (get_cfs_status reports frames.running = renew task alive, not frames arriving).
>
> *Better fix:* Claimant's fix is right; concretely: (1) in `_run_ringout` before each `_write_master` require `ses.last_ts` within max(0.3 s, 3×frame period) AND `ses.frames` advanced by ≥ persistence_frames+1 since the previous RAISE (else skip the raise and re-dwell); if no frame for >1 s set abort_reason='RTA frames stopped' → BACKOFF(abort_backoff_db)/ABORT — fail closed exactly like `_verify_decay` already does. (2) `_arm`: after `frames.start()` wait ≤2 s on `ses.frame_event`; on timeout disarm and raise CfsError('NO_FRAMES') so neither ring_out nor feedback_watch can arm deaf (also lets `_calibrate_floor` run on real frames). (3) Watch mode: a watchdog task in `_consume`'s owner that publishes cfs.abort {reason:'RTA frames stopped'} and closes the session (or at minimum flips state/get_cfs_status to 'deaf') when last_ts ages >1 s. (4) Expose LiveMeters.last_rx_age_s/frames_received in get_cfs_status and the dashboard instead of `running`. (5) Optionally have LiveMeters re-send the /meters request immediately (not on the 5 s cadence) when last_rx_age_s exceeds ~0.5 s, to heal transient lease loss fast — but the cfs-side interlock is the safety fix; faster renewal alone is not.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — MECHANISM — holds end to end, no gap. src/x32mcp/cfs.py `_run_ringout` (1147-1197): each iteration is pop pending → if none: `master_db >= target`? → `nc.spent`? → `_write_master(nxt)` → stage RAISE → `_dwell` (1126-1133 = wait_for(ses.wake, dwell) only). Nothing on this path reads `ses.frames`, `ses.last_ts`, `ses.frame_event`, `self._frames.running`/`LiveMeters.last_rx_age_s` (grep: `ses.frames` is only incremented in `_consume` 1030 and copied into the report 1391; `last_rx_age_s` is never read anywhere). Detections are produced only inside `_consume` (1019-1047), which blocks on `q.get()`; with no frames `ses.pending` stays empty and `ses.wake` is only set by detections/abort/connection loss. Target defaults to `min(policy.level_ceiling_db, ringout.master_ceiling_db)` = 0 dB (device.yaml policy.bus_fader_max_db 0 / main_fader_max_db 0, ringout.master_ceiling_db 0); step 1 dB, dwell 1500 ms; the write budget (50/s) and relative clamp (6, show-mode 3) never bite a 1 dB/1.5 s climb. So a session with a dead sensor executes exactly ceil((0-start)/step) RAISE stages, then `_finalize_levels` BACKOFF 3 → DONE, `first_feedback_master_db` None → report 'no feedback detected'. `_arm` (901-929) starts the source and returns without waiting for a first frame, and `set_rta_source`'s read-back mismatch is 'logged, never raised' (meters.py:25, 935-940) — the ring-out never once proves its sensor is alive before or while it raises gain. The only asymmetry the code has is in `_verify_decay` (1221-1252), where no-frames ⇒ not-decayed ⇒ deepen ⇒ eventually ABORT; RAISE has no counterpart. feedback_watch shares `_consume`, so a stalled stream there is a silently disarmed guard (no cfs.abort, cfs.state still 'watch').

REPRODUCED (own worktree, 4/4 pass, incl. the production LiveMeters path, not only an injected source): A) LiveMeters over FakeDesk, desk-side /meters/15 stream cancelled + lease=0 at master −17 dB while OSC control stays alive, 30 dB/s ring injected into the fake analyser at that moment → 17 further RAISE stages with 0 frames, DONE, max 0.0, parked −3.0, notches [], gain_before_feedback None, `conn.connected` still True. B) NullSource (never a frame) → 20 raises −20→0, DONE, frames 0. C) frames keep flowing but timestamps step back 30 s after 20 frames (what a backwards wall-clock step does: LiveMeters stamps with time.time, meters.py:390, and `_consume` 1028 discards `frame.ts <= ses.last_ts`) → 82/102 frames silently discarded, a 30 dB/s 2.4 kHz ring injected at −15 dB produced 0 detections, DONE at 0 dB. D) control, same ring without the step → HOLD at −18, notch, ABORT, backed off to −24. The loop logic is host-side, so a real X32 behaves identically: the bus/Main fader physically walks to 0 dB with open mics and any acoustic ring is never cut; in ring_out_system the false DONE then lets the next bus start raising too.

WHERE THE CLAIM OVERSTATES (triggers, not mechanism): (1) 'LiveMeters subscription never matched — the exact class of bug HANDOVER found' is wrong: HANDOVER §4a records /meters/15 and /meters/1 streaming at 20 fps on X32RACK FW 4.13; the M5/M7 defects were read-after-write races and detector maths, not a dead meter stream. (2) 'lease/renew lost' self-heals: LiveMeters re-sends every 5 s against a 10 s lease (meters.py:437-451), so one lost datagram costs nothing and two consecutive losses cost ≤5 s ≈ 3 blind raises (+3 dB), not a climb to the ceiling; a full blind climb needs sustained meters-only loss. (3) 'injected source dies' is test/demo-only (server.py wires LiveMeters unless App(frames=) is passed). (4) A TOTAL outage is not this bug: every raise first does a live `_leaf` GET of the fader (desk.py set_level 990 → _move_level; cache invalidated by the previous write), which times out → DeskError TIMEOUT → `_lose_connection` (cfs.py 1183-1185), and the connection watchdog degrades → restore path. Real-desk conditions that DO reach the blind climb: a backwards host clock step (W32Time/NTP step, resume-from-sleep correction, manual change on the Windows FOH PC) deafens for the step size via cfs.py:1028; a desk/firmware whose RTA meter does not stream (fw < 2.0 has no /meters/15, meters.md §13) or throttles meters under load ('may be variable according to console's ability to fulfill requests', meters.md:73, magnitude UNCONFIRMED) rings out blind from ARM because `_arm` never waits for a frame; sustained selective loss on a poor WiFi link gives repeated multi-second blind windows.

SEVERITY re-assessed to MEDIUM: the level change is bounded by the operator-confirmed Tier-2 ceiling (the same −start→0→−3 trajectory a quiet-room ring-out legitimately takes), so not 'critical'; no written guarantee promises a liveness check, but DESIGN §15's premise ('dwell while detector runs; on detection → HOLD, no further raises', BRIEF §5 <100 ms detect→cut) is void whenever the single sensor is silent, the system cannot tell a deaf detector from a quiet room, gives the operator/LLM no signal, and files a false 'DONE / no feedback' report — a closed-loop gain controller with no sensor watchdog. Reachable on a real desk but under unusual conditions ⇒ 'guarantee weakened under unusual but reachable conditions'. (Arguable as high if one counts 'leaves the desk hot with a false all-clear after a sensor error'.)
>
> *Evidence:* Code: cfs.py:1147-1197 `_run_ringout` (no frame/last_ts check between `_write_master` and `_dwell`); cfs.py:1126-1133 `_dwell` = wait_for(ses.wake) only; cfs.py:1019-1047 `_consume` (sole producer of detections; 1028 drops ts<=last_ts; 1030 `ses.frames += 1` is the only liveness counter and is only reported at 1391); cfs.py:901-929 `_arm` (frames.start() then publish, no first-frame wait); cfs.py:507 default `LiveMeters(self._conn, 15)` with clock=time.time (meters.py:390); meters.py:418-420 `last_rx_age_s` defined, never read anywhere in src/; meters.py:437-451 renew every 5 s, send failure only logged; meters.py:25,935-940 RTA source mismatch logged not raised; device.yaml:723-740 (bus/main ceiling 0, writes_per_second 50, relative 6/3), 825-830 (step 1, dwell 1500, master_ceiling_db 0); desk.py:948-980 `_move_level` returns the requested value as after_db (no read-back) but set_level:990 does a live `_leaf` GET first → total outage is caught, meters-only outage is not. Docs: HANDOVER §4a lines 96-98 (/meters/15 streams at 20 fps on the real desk — contradicts the claim's 'never matched' trigger); meters.md:73 (frame rate 'may be variable according to console's ability'); meters.md §1.3:104-105 (10 s lease, resend every 5 s tolerates one lost datagram); DESIGN.md §15:625-630.
Tests (worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-1-src, commit a54d222, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs1.py, `runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs1.py -s` → 4 passed in 7.25 s): A (production LiveMeters, fake desk's /meters/15 stream killed at −17 dB, control alive): final_stage DONE, aborted False, start −20.0, max_master_db 0.0, end_master_db −3.0, target 0.0, gain_before_feedback None, notches 0, raises_after_kill 17, frames_after_kill 0, conn.connected True, fake fader −3.0. B (NullSource): frames 0, 20 RAISE stages, DONE, max 0.0, end −3.0. C (clock step −30 s after 20 frames): emitted 102, consumed 20, discarded 82, 2.4 kHz 30 dB/s ring injected at −15.0 dB → detections 0, notches [], DONE, max 0.0. D (control, no step): ABORT 'ring at 2333 Hz not tamed', max_master_db −18.0, end −24.0, notches 1. Result note: $SP/reports/verify-cfs-1-src/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Keep the claimant's fix (gate RAISE on fresh frames; abort on stall; first-frame wait in _arm; watch-mode stall → cfs.abort) but make it robust to the clock path found here: (1) stamp frames with a monotonic clock (LiveMeters clock=time.monotonic, or have `_consume` track liveness by arrival count/loop.time() rather than frame.ts) so a wall-clock step cannot silently discard the stream — today cfs.py:1028 turns a clock step into total deafness with frames still 'arriving'; (2) in `_run_ringout`, before each `_write_master`, require `ses.frames - frames_at_last_raise >= persistence_frames+1` AND loop.time()-last_arrival < ~0.3 s, else skip the raise and, after >1 s of silence, set abort_reason 'RTA frames stalled' → normal BACKOFF/ABORT (abort_backoff_db), never DONE; (3) `_arm`: await the first valid 100-band frame (≤2 s) and refuse with CfsError('NO_FRAMES') otherwise, and treat `rta.verified is False` as a blocker (or at least require an explicit override) rather than a log line; (4) expose `frames`/`last_frame_age_s` in CfsState/get_cfs_state and the dashboard so a silent guard in feedback_watch is visible, and publish cfs.abort + stop the watch when frames stall; (5) count discarded (non-monotonic/wrong-length) frames and surface them in the report so a deaf run can never read as 'no feedback detected'.

## D37. [medium] show_mode(true) during a run does not stop it: the ring-out keeps raising the bus for the rest of the run
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/policy.py:206-217 (check_show_mode_allows is an entry check), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:652 and server.py:1746/1792 (checked once, before preflight), cfs.py:1147-1197 (no re-check per raise), policy.py:196-204 (setter only publishes policy.show_mode; cfs does not subscribe)

**Mechanism.** Show mode is documented as 'ring_out and ring_out_system are refused'. The refusal is evaluated only when the tool is called. A run already in RAISE continues: each raise is an absolute set_level (no relative guard at all, see finding 1, so even the ±3 dB show-mode limit is irrelevant) until target/ceiling. For ring_out_system the NEXT stages are refused (each stage re-enters ring_out) but the current bus keeps climbing.

**Exploit.** ring_out(1) confirmed at sound-check; band walks on, operator/LLM calls show_mode(true) expecting the desk to be left alone; Bus 1 master keeps stepping up to 0 dB during the first song.

**Evidence.** test_x3c_show_mode_switched_on_mid_run_does_not_stop_ringout: policy.show_mode=True set at master ≥ -17 dB; observed report DONE, max_master_db -3.0 (target), and policy.check_show_mode_allows('ring_out') raises afterwards (a new run would be refused). Also test_x6b via the srv.show_mode tool. PASSED.

**Proposed fix.** CfsManager: subscribe to 'policy.show_mode' and on on=True call abort('show mode') (ring-out backs off / system run stops); and re-check self._policy.check_show_mode_allows('ring_out') at the top of each _run_ringout iteration before writing the master (PolicyError there already maps to the normal ABORT+BACKOFF path at cfs.py:1177-1179).

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-3-repro (branch wt/verify-cfs-3-repro, commits 3c8288a = repro tests, 6689c65 = prototype fix), test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs3.py, against the real FakeDesk/Desk/Policy/CfsManager and the real server tools.

Code path (verified by reading): Policy.show_mode setter (src/x32mcp/policy.py:213-222) only flips the flag and publishes 'policy.show_mode'; check_show_mode_allows (policy.py:224-235) is a pure entry check. It is called once per ring-out at cfs.py:652 (ring_out) / cfs.py:727 (ring_out_system) and server.py:1746 / 1792, all BEFORE preflight. CfsManager subscribes only to 'connection.state' (cfs.py:522); nothing in src/ subscribes to 'policy.show_mode' (grep: only the publisher). The RAISE loop _run_ringout (cfs.py:1147-1198) never consults show mode; each raise is Desk.set_level (absolute, no relative check by design, desk.py:20-23) via _write_master (cfs.py:1115-1124). So a run already past ARM is unaffected by show_mode(true) and walks to target/ceiling. The codebase itself acknowledges show mode 'can land mid ring-out' (tests/integration/test_cfs.py:533-552, README.md:724) but only handled the back-off side, not stopping the raise.

Observed (unpatched HEAD a408a2a), all 4 tests PASS:
- test_v1 (CfsManager direct): Bus 1 master -20 dB, ring_out(1) default target (0 dB ceiling), 1 dB steps; policy.show_mode=True set at the RAISE that reached -17 dB. Report: final_stage DONE, aborted False, max_master_db 0.0, end_master_db -3.0. Fader writes with show mode ON: -16,-15,...,-1,0 (17 raises) then the -3 back-off; fake desk fader ends at -3.0 dB (+17 dB above where the operator left it). Afterwards policy.check_show_mode_allows('ring_out') raises SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS and a new cfs.ring_out is refused - proving it is an entry-only check.
- test_v2 (MCP tools): token dance srv.ring_out(1) confirmed, then srv.show_mode(True) called as a concurrent tool call at -17 dB. Tool returned ok:true, summary 'Show mode ON ... refused: ring_out, ring_out_system, ...' while the confirmed run kept writing -16..0 dB and finished DONE (max 0.0, end -3.0); connection_status shows show_mode True; only a NEW srv.ring_out is refused (SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS).
- test_v3 (ring_out_system, plan Bus1 then Bus2, target -6): show_mode(true) during stage 1 -> stage 1 kept raising Bus 1 to -6.0 (DONE, end -9.0, +11 dB under show mode), stage 2 skipped with error SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS, consolidated report aborted:false. Matches the claim's 'next stages refused, current bus keeps climbing'.
- test_v1b (control): cfs.stop() (== feedback_watch_stop tool) at the same point DOES abort immediately (max -17, ABORT, back-off to -23), so the abort machinery exists and is simply not wired to show mode.

Severity: I keep 'high'. Show mode is documented as the mid-set/'on at doors' state in which ring-outs are refused (README.md:206-207, 633-636, 742; server instructions server.py:129), and the show_mode tool affirmatively tells the LLM ring_out is refused while the server autonomously continues raising a bus master up to +20 dB (defaults: 1 dB every 1.5 s, ~30 s) with an audience presumed present - the guarantee is defeated in substance. Mitigations that keep it below critical: the raise is bounded by the target/0 dB ceiling the human confirmed moments earlier, steps are 1 dB with dwell, the detector keeps notching, the run ends with the normal -3 dB back-off, and feedback_watch_stop/panic still work. Reachability caveat: the ring_out tool call blocks for the whole run, so from one stdio MCP session this needs a client that issues concurrent tool calls (MCP allows it) or a client-side timeout that returns control without cancelling; ring_out_system 'can run for many minutes', which makes a mid-run show_mode call realistic. Claim's line numbers for policy.py are off by ~10 lines (setter 213-222, check 224-235); everything else matches.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-3-repro; tests: tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs3.py (4 passed in 4.3 s on unpatched code, commit 3c8288a). Captured JSON under $SP/reports/verify-cfs-3-repro/: v1_report.json -> switched_at master -17.0; report {final_stage: DONE, aborted: false, start -20.0, max_master_db 0.0, end -3.0, target 0.0}; fader_writes [(-19,F),(-18,F),(-17,F),(-16,T),(-15,T),...,(-1,T),(0.0,T),(-3.0,T)] (T = show_mode on at write time). v2_report.json -> show_mode tool summary 'Show mode ON: relative moves limited to ±3 dB; refused: ring_out, ring_out_system, scene_recall, scene_save, setup_ringout_eqs'; ring_out result ok, DONE, max 0.0, end -3.0; identical write trace; subsequent srv.ring_out -> SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS. v3_report.json -> stages [{bus 1, DONE, max -6.0, end -9.0}, {bus 2, skipped, error SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS}], aborted false, summary '1 bus(es) done, 1 skipped'. v1b control: cfs.stop() at -17 -> ABORT 'stopped by operator', max -17.0, end -23.0.
Prototype fix (commit 6689c65, src/x32mcp/cfs.py +12 lines): CfsManager subscribes to 'policy.show_mode' and on on=True with an active session schedules self.abort('show mode switched on') (also flags a system run via _stop_system); plus self._policy.check_show_mode_allows('ring_out') inside the try at the top of each raise (PolicyError -> existing ABORT+BACKOFF branch cfs.py:1177-1179). With it: v1/v2 -> ABORT 'show mode switched on' at max -17.0, single back-off write to -23.0, no raise under show mode; v3 -> stage 1 ABORT at -17, Bus 2 never raised, system report aborted:true 'show mode switched on'; the three exploit tests now FAIL as intended and the full existing suite still passes (912 passed; the only other failure, tests/test_targets_events_config.py::test_settings_defaults_and_env, asserts the cwd basename == 'x32-mcp' and fails in any worktree, unrelated).
>
> *Better fix:* The claim's fix is right and I validated it (commit 6689c65): (1) CfsManager.__init__: events.subscribe(self._on_show_mode_event, types={'policy.show_mode'}); handler: if ev.data['on'] and self.active -> create_task(self.abort('show mode switched on')) tracked in self._bg (same pattern as _on_connection_event); unsubscribe in close(). abort() wakes the dwell immediately, backs the ring-out off (forced write, so the ±3 dB clamp cannot refuse it), stops a watch, and flags a system run so no further bus is raised. (2) Defence in depth: self._policy.check_show_mode_allows('ring_out') inside the existing try: before _write_master in _run_ringout so a raise can never be written under show mode even if the event is missed (PolicyError already routes to ABORT+BACKOFF). Additionally worth doing at the tool layer: in server.show_mode(on=True), if a.cfs.active, await a.cfs.abort('show mode') (or cfs.stop()) and say so in the summary ('aborted running ring-out on Bus N, backed off to X dB') instead of the currently misleading 'refused: ring_out' while a run is live; and have cfs_status/connection_status surface the contradiction if it ever recurs.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap: (1) server.show_mode (server.py:1272-1282) only assigns Policy.show_mode; the setter (policy.py:215-222) flips _show_mode and publishes 'policy.show_mode' — nothing subscribes to that event (CfsManager subscribes only to 'connection.state', cfs.py:522; grep confirms no other subscriber). (2) check_show_mode_allows (policy.py:224-235) is called exactly once per run, before preflight: server.py:1746 (tool) and cfs.py:652 (CfsManager.ring_out); ring_out_system at server.py:1792/cfs.py:727. (3) _run_ringout (cfs.py:1146-1197) loops RAISE→dwell with no policy consultation other than what Desk.set_level does; _write_master (cfs.py:1115-1124) → Desk.set_level → _move_level (desk.py:949-980), which by explicit design applies only the family ceiling clamp and NO check_relative (comment desk.py:956-961; module doc desk.py:20-23). policy.relative_limit_db (3 dB in show mode) is therefore never evaluated during the run — step_db was validated against it once at entry (cfs.py:661) with show mode off (≤6). (4) So after show_mode(true) lands mid-run the loop keeps writing /bus/NN/mix/fader upward every dwell until target/ceiling/budget, then backs off 3 dB (DONE) — the author even anticipated show mode landing mid-run and only guaranteed the back-off (README:723-724, cfs.py:37-40, and the repo's own tests/integration/test_cfs.py:533-551 'show_mode(True) is Tier 0 and can land mid ring-out'), i.e. this is a conscious entry-gate design, not an oversight in an abort path. (5) ring_out_system: each later stage re-enters self.ring_out → check_show_mode_allows raises PolicyError → caught at cfs.py:748-753 and the stage is 'skipped' (loop continues, raising nothing); the current stage's bus keeps climbing exactly as in (4). Real desk: identical — the flag, the loop and the writes are all server-side; the FakeDesk only matters for whether the run ends DONE (closed-loop taming) or ABORT, not for whether it keeps raising. Reachability: stdio transport, one client (server.py:1949); show_mode(true) must arrive as a concurrent MCP request while that client's ring_out call is still outstanding (the Python MCP SDK serves requests concurrently; feedback_watch_stop 'abort a running ring-out' (server.py:1674) presupposes exactly this), so it is reachable wherever the stop tool is. Why MEDIUM not HIGH: the literally documented show-mode guarantees still hold — a NEW ring_out is refused with SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS (verified), and the ±3 dB limit is documented to apply to adjust_* only (README:576, desk.py:20). What continues is the Tier-2 action the user already confirmed (summary names start→target), bounded by min(target, master_ceiling_db=0 dB, bus_fader_max_db=0), in ≤6 dB steps with dwell, time-bounded by the loop and _ring_out_timeout (server.py:1719-1724), ending in a forced back-off; the operator's documented stops (feedback_watch_stop → ABORT+6 dB back-off, verified; client cancel → CancelledError → _unwind, cfs.py:704-708; panic mutes) still work. It is a real gap against show mode's stated purpose ('no ring-outs mid-set', README:206,742) under an unusual-but-reachable condition, not an unbounded/unconfirmed level change. Note the claim's line ref policy.py:206-217 is off (setter 215-222, check 224-235); otherwise its file refs are accurate. Default target is the 0 dB ceiling (cfs.py:678, server.py:1758), so the 'steps up to 0 dB' narrative is the default case, given the user confirmed that summary.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-3-src (commit a56bbf8), tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs3.py, run with runtests.sh: 3 passed. (a) test_show_mode_mid_run_run_continues_to_target: bus 1 from -20, target -3, step 1, dwell 60 ms; policy.show_mode=True set on the RAISE at -17.0; observed 14 further RAISE stages -16.0…-3.0, fader writes [-19…-3, -6], report final_stage DONE, max_master_db -3.0, end_master_db -6.0, fake desk fader -6.0, policy.show_mode still True at the end. (b) test_show_mode_mid_run_with_6db_steps_ignores_3db_limit: start -30, step_db=6 (accepted: limit 6 with show mode off), show_mode=True on first RAISE (-24.0); subsequent raises -18.0, -12.0, -6.0 at 6 dB per absolute set_level write despite relative_limit_db now 3 → DONE, end -9.0. (c) control test_new_run_refused_and_stop_tool_path_works: with show mode on, cfs.ring_out raises PolicyError SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS before any write (documented guarantee intact); cfs.stop() scheduled at RAISE -17 → final_stage ABORT 'stopped by operator', max -17.0, end -23.0 (6 dB abort back-off) — the intended kill path works. Claimant's x3c/x6b results are consistent with mine. Code refs: policy.py:215-222 (setter publishes only), policy.py:224-235 (entry check), cfs.py:522 (only 'connection.state' subscribed), cfs.py:652/727 + server.py:1746/1792 (single pre-run check), cfs.py:1146-1197 (loop, no re-check), cfs.py:1115-1124 → desk.py:949-980 (absolute move, ceiling clamp only, no check_relative), cfs.py:748-753 (system: later stages skipped on PolicyError), tests/integration/test_cfs.py:533-551 (author knowingly lets a run continue in show mode, guarantees only the back-off), README.md:206,633-636,723-724,742.
>
> *Better fix:* Two-part, cheap: (1) in CfsManager.__init__ subscribe to types={'policy.show_mode'} next to the 'connection.state' subscription (cfs.py:522) and on on=True schedule self.abort('show mode switched on') as a bg task (same pattern as _on_connection_event, cfs.py:1296-1305): abort() sets ses.abort_reason and ses.wake, so a RAISE/dwell exits immediately via the normal ABORT path (forced abort_backoff_db lowering, report) and _stop_system() halts a system run before the next stage; a WATCH session is disarmed. Unsubscribe in close(). (2) Defence in depth inside _run_ringout: at the top of each iteration, `if self._policy.show_mode: ses.abort_reason = ses.abort_reason or 'show mode switched on'; break` — do NOT raise PolicyError there as the claimant suggests: the except at cfs.py:1177-1179 only wraps _write_master, so a raise at loop top would escape to ring_out's BaseException handler (cfs.py:709-713), unwind and re-raise, turning the tool result into an error instead of a report. Optionally have server.show_mode(True) await a.cfs.abort(...) directly and mention the aborted session in its summary so the operator sees it. Add a test mirroring test_ring_out_backs_off_even_in_show_mode asserting final_stage ABORT with max_master_db == the level at which show mode was switched on.

## D38. [medium] Plan-before-write: a failed notch write leaves NotchController committed; next detection writes −9 dB (or −6) as the desk's first cut, phantom notches consume budget and are reported
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/detector.py:567-584 (_apply mutates _gains/_touched/_notches before any write), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1070-1086 (_notch: write failure logged, no rollback), cfs.py:413-415 (_DeskGeqWriter validates against its own last-written belief, so the jump passes validate_notch), src/x32mcp/policy.py:372-395 (shared token bucket, max_wait 1 s → RATE_LIMITED is a realistic failure under an LLM write loop), cfs.py:1364-1389 (report lists controller notches, not desk state)

**Mechanism.** plan() commits depth d to the controller, then set_band_gain → Desk._write → acquire_write raises RATE_LIMITED (or NOT_A_GEQ/NOT_CONNECTED). _notch returns None; controller says band at −3 (then −6) while the desk is at 0. The writer's validate_notch(current=self.gains.get(band,0)=0, new=−9) is a legal 'cut', so the next successful write puts −9 dB on the desk in one step (guarantee: −3 dB steps). After three failures the band is 'at −9' in belief and can never be cut again; in ring_out a single refused write aborts the run immediately as 'not tamed' and the report/cfs_status/validate matched_session logic list a notch that does not exist. Budget is charged for cuts that never happened.

**Exploit.** feedback_watch(1) armed; a runaway client loop (set_fader/set_send ×100 in a second) empties the 50/s bucket; feedback starts at 2.5 kHz → detections at t, t+1 s fail RATE_LIMITED; loop stops; detection at t+2 s writes /fx/5/par/22 = −9 dB in one datagram. Variant: keep the bucket empty for 3 s → that band is never cut for the rest of the session while it howls.

**Evidence.** test_x1_failed_notch_write_then_9db_single_step: bucket drained (policy._bucket_tokens=-1e4), ring injected; observed cfs.state notches band 22 depth −6.0 with zero desk.write events and fake par/22 still 0.5 raw (0 dB), budget_left 5; bucket refilled → first set_geq_band write value gain_db == −9.0, fake par/22 = −9 dB. test_x1b: in ring_out one RATE_LIMITED notch write → ABORT 'not tamed', report notches[0].depth_db −3.0 with no GEQ write on the desk. test_x4d: DES2 loaded mid-session → write blocked NOT_A_GEQ but belief −3. All PASSED.

**Proposed fix.** Split NotchController.plan() into a pure propose(det) → (band, current, new) and commit(band, new, ...) called by CfsManager._notch only after writer.set_band_gain succeeded (charge _touched/budget in commit); on exception nothing changes, so the next detection re-proposes −3. In ring_out treat a PolicyError RATE_LIMITED on a notch as retry-after-wait (≤1 s) rather than 'untamable'. Give CFS notch writes their own limiter lane (they are the safety writes) capped by 3×budget per session.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (high)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-4-repro (branch wt/verify-cfs-4-repro, commit 7320096), file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs4.py — 7 passed / 1 skipped against unmodified HEAD a408a2a code, FakeDesk closed loop.

Mechanism is exactly as claimed: detector.py:567-572 NotchController._apply() sets _gains[band]=new and _touched.add(band) inside plan(), before cfs.py:1070-1086 _notch() attempts ses.writer.set_band_gain(); any DeskError/PolicyError/X32ConnectionError/CfsError there is logged (cfs.py:1083) and returns None with no rollback; only NOT_CONNECTED is escalated (cfs.py:1084). _DeskGeqWriter validates against its own last-WRITTEN gains (cfs.py:413, still 0.0), so the next successful write validate_notch(0, -6/-9) is a legal cut and lands on the desk as one datagram. Budget is charged via _touched for a cut that never happened; state/report/cfs_status list ses.nc.notches (cfs.py:559, 1364), i.e. belief not desk.

All sub-claims held: (a) watch: two refused writes -> belief -6, desk 0 dB, budget 6->5, zero GEQ datagrams; bucket refilled -> first /fx/5/par/22 write is gain_db -9.0 (0 -> -9 dB in one step; guarantee is -3 dB steps). (b) three refused writes -> band pinned at -9 in belief; with a full bucket two further detections produce no write at all, desk flat, ring sits at its cap for the rest of the session; stop() report says notches=[band 22, -9.0 dB, detections 3], budget_left 5, while validate_ringout_eqs reads the desk as flat (matched_session None). (c) ring_out: one refused notch write -> stages HOLD,BACKOFF,ABORT (no NOTCH), abort_reason 'ring at 2333 Hz not tamed: GEQ band cannot cut deeper than -9 dB or the notch budget is spent' (misleading), report notches[0].depth_db -3.0 phantom, budget 5, no GEQ write. (d) NOT_A_GEQ mid-session -> belief -3/no write; GEQ restored -> first write -6.

Nuance on the claimed exploit path (does not weaken the finding): RATE_LIMITED needs the shared bucket ~50 reservations deep, i.e. >=~100 write coroutines in flight at once. I measured 130 concurrent Desk.set_mute -> 30 RATE_LIMITED in 1.08 s (reachable for a script/runaway client issuing parallel calls), but 3 parallel srv.set_fader(ramp_ms=6000) tool calls do NOT drain it (ramps are back-pressured to <=k outstanding reservations; bucket sat at ~-3, notch written normally at -3). However I found a far more mundane trigger the claim did not list: set_geq_band first READS /fx/5 (type check, desk.py:1417, read cache TTL 2 s); a single lost /node reply (+its retry) -> DeskError TIMEOUT (desk.py:360), which _notch does not treat as connection loss (unlike the raise path, cfs.py:1181) -> belief -3, desk 0, connection never degraded, watch continues, next detection writes -6 dB in one step. Reproduced 3/3 with fakedesk.drop_next(2), zero client load; same drop in ring_out -> ABORT 'not tamed' with a phantom -3 notch and connection_lost False. On a real UDP/Wi-Fi FOH link this is the realistic path.

Severity stays HIGH per the scale: defeats documented guarantees (-3 dB steps; budget accounting; report/status truthfulness) and leaves the desk in a dangerous state after an error (a howling band CFS believes is fully notched and will never touch again this session, while the operator is told it was cut -9 dB). Not critical: the erroneous write is a cut (max -9 dB on one GEQ band), never a boost or level raise, and ring_out fails toward back-off/abort. Side observation from a discarded run: when RATE_LIMITED hits during RAISE, the forced back-off write is also refused (cfs.py:1287 log 'back-off write failed ... RATE_LIMITED'), leaving the master at the raised level — separate issue, not scored here.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-4-repro, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs4.py, run via runtests.sh: '7 passed, 1 skipped in 33.63s'. Captured output:
- test_x1_watch_failed_writes_then_single_9db_step: AFTER REFUSED WRITES: belief_depth_db -6.0, budget_left 5, geq_writes [], desk_par22_raw 0.5 (0 dB), rate_limited_events 2, cfs_notch_events 0, writer_gains[22]=0.0, nc_gains[22]=-6.0. After refill: FIRST GEQ WRITE {'address': '/fx/5/par/22', 'value': {'gain_db': -9.0, 'band': 22, 'side': 'A'}}, desk raw 0.2 (= -9 dB); cfs.notch event depth -9.0 detections 3.
- test_x1v_three_failures_band_dead_for_session: detections 5, geq writes [], desk par22 0.5, ring level -3.0 (at cap); report notches [{'band': 22, 'depth_db': -9.0, 'detections': 3, ...}], budget_left 5, summary 'watch on Bus 1: 1 notch(es) from 5 detection(s)'; validate_ringout_eqs: flat True, notches [], matched_session None.
- test_x1b_ringout_one_refused_write_aborts_with_phantom_notch: stages [...,'RAISE','HOLD','BACKOFF','ABORT'] (no NOTCH); report ABORT 'ring at 2333 Hz not tamed: GEQ band cannot cut deeper than -9 dB or the notch budget is spent', notches [{'band':22,'depth_db':-3.0,'detections':1}], budget_left 5, geq writes [], desk par22 0.5, master -20 -> -19 (highest -13).
- test_x4d_not_a_geq_mid_session_leaves_belief_committed: belief -3.0, geq writes [], budget_left 5; after slot restored first write gain_db -6.0.
- test_x5_single_dropped_node_reply_diverges_then_jumps (3/3 runs): armed=True dropped=2 belief=-3.0 writes=[] desk_raw=0.5 states=[] (never degraded) mode=WATCH; first datagram that reached the GEQ: {'gain_db': -6.0}, desk raw 0.3.
- test_x5b_ringout_single_dropped_reply_aborts_with_phantom_notch: stages HOLD,BACKOFF,ABORT; 'not tamed'; notches [{'depth_db': -3.0}]; geq writes []; connection_lost False.
- test_reach_rate_limited_via_real_desk_writes: '130 concurrent set_mute: 30 RATE_LIMITED, 0 other errors, 1.08 s' (follow-up assertion skipped: bucket had recovered to 0.1 once the gather returned).
- test_reach_via_tool_surface_parallel_ramps: 'bucket after 1.2 s of 3 ramps: 8.0, rate_limited events 0'; first detection belief -3.0, desk -3.0, write [-3.0]; DIVERGED False — parallel ramps alone do not trigger it. Probe tests/integration/test_probe_ramp_rate.py: k=2/3/4 concurrent 3-s ramps hold the bucket at about -k (-1.9/-2.9/-3.9), never near the -49 needed for RATE_LIMITED.
Code refs: detector.py:567-572 (_apply commits), cfs.py:1070-1086 (_notch no rollback; :1084 only NOT_CONNECTED escalates), cfs.py:413 (writer validates vs own belief), desk.py:1417 (/fx/N type read before write) + desk.py:360 (TIMEOUT), cfs.py:1161 (misleading abort text), cfs.py:559/1364 (state/report from nc.notches), policy.py:389-390 (RATE_LIMITED). Result note: $SP/reports/verify-cfs-4-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed split: make NotchController.plan() pure (propose -> band/current/new) and add commit() that CfsManager._notch calls only after writer.set_band_gain returned; charge _touched/budget and bump detections in commit. Additionally: (1) in _notch treat DeskError TIMEOUT like the raise path does (cfs.py:1181) — at minimum retry the write once, since the failing step is the pre-write /fx/N type READ, not the write; better, cache the slot type for the session (it was validated at arm) so a notch is a single fire-and-forget SET with no read in front of it, and re-verify type only on /fx/N/type push events. (2) Make _DeskGeqWriter the single source of truth for band gains (controller reads writer.gains) so belief cannot fork. (3) In ring_out, distinguish 'write refused/transient' from 'cannot cut deeper/budget spent' in abort_reason and retry a transient failure within the VERIFY window instead of aborting. (4) Give CFS notch writes and the ring-out back-off write a reserved limiter lane (or panic-style bypass bounded by 3 x budget writes per session) so client load can never starve the safety writes.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain read end to end and it holds with no gap; only the claimed trigger narrative needs narrowing.

1. Commit-before-write is real: detector.py:551-584 NotchController.plan() -> _apply() writes self._gains[band]=new, self._touched.add(band) (budget) and the Notch depth/detections BEFORE anything is sent. cfs.py:1070-1086 _notch() then calls ses.writer.set_band_gain(); on (DeskError, PolicyError, X32ConnectionError, CfsError) it logs 'notch write failed' and returns None — no rollback of nc._gains/_touched/_notches. _DeskGeqWriter (cfs.py:410-416) only updates its own self.gains after a successful Desk.set_geq_band, so its validate_notch(current=self.gains.get(band,0)=0, new) sees 0->-6 / 0->-9 as a legal cut (policy.py:405-422 checks only new<=0, new<=cur, new>=-9). Nothing else re-quantises the step: Desk.set_geq_band (desk.py:1403-1431) is a raw write with no relative/step check. So the next successful write sends the controller's accumulated depth in one datagram; after three failures _can_deepen(band) is False forever and plan() returns None for that band (adjacent bands still open new notches). Report/status (cfs.py:557-559, 1364-1386) list ses.nc.notches (belief), while notch_log only has real writes, so the report is self-inconsistent (notches=[-9, detections 3, confidence None], notch_log=[]). Budget is charged (touched) for the phantom band. Divergence does not cross sessions: a new session's `existing` comes from the desk read (cfs.py:886-889), and provision._matched_session simply returns None for a phantom notch.

2. Which failures can actually reach that except-clause on a REAL desk (conn.set is fire-and-forget, so the send itself almost never raises):
 a) PolicyError RATE_LIMITED (policy.py:375-395): shared bucket, capacity=rate=50, reserve-and-sleep, refuse only when the queued wait exceeds max_wait_s=1 s, i.e. when >=~50 reservations are already queued. Every Desk write path (ramps, restore, tools) acquires sequentially inside its own task, so this needs >=~50 write calls IN FLIGHT CONCURRENTLY. The MCP SDK dispatcher start_soon()s every tools/call (mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:684) and server.py/_tool/Desk have no serialising lock, so a client that pipelines/parallelises ~100 Tier-1 calls reaches it; a runaway loop that awaits each call does NOT (it is merely throttled to 50/s, bucket never below ~-1). The claim's 'set_fader/set_send x100 in a second' is therefore true only for a concurrent burst, false for a sequential one (measured: n=1 and n=40 concurrent loops -> no RATE_LIMITED, notch -3 written; n=120 -> notch refused). CFS notch writes have no priority lane, so under such a burst the safety write is the one that loses.
 b) DeskError TIMEOUT from set_geq_band's `await self._section(f'/fx/{n}')` (desk.py:1417 -> _require -> _unreachable) whenever the 2 s node cache is stale and all /node retries (3 x 0.5 s on the product defaults) go unanswered — plain UDP reply loss / busy desk. This is independent of the FakeDesk and of the rate limiter and is the most realistic real-hardware path; one lost reply-set gives a -6 dB single step, two give -9.
 c) DeskError NOT_A_GEQ if the slot's type changes mid-session (Tier-2 set_fx_type or the desk surface); write correctly blocked but belief advances; if the GEQ comes back the next write is the accumulated depth.
 d) NOT_CONNECTED -> _lose_connection stops a WATCH / restores a RINGOUT, so that path ends the session and the divergence is moot.
 In ring_out any single refused notch makes _notch_and_verify return False -> abort 'not tamed' -> BACKOFF -6 dB (force=True) -> ABORT report with the phantom notch; the abort itself is the safe direction (and if the bucket is still saturated the back-off write can also be refused, leaving the master at its peak — the claimant's separate x3d).

3. FakeDesk vs real desk: the defect is entirely host-side Python state handling (detector.py/cfs.py); the desk only determines which exception fires. A real X32 behaves identically for (a) and (c); (b) is MORE likely on real hardware than on the in-memory FakeDesk (HANDOVER §4a documents reads timing out ~500 ms per attempt and packet-drop-sensitive tests). The SyntheticRta only affects whether detections recur (they do on a real howl too: cooldown_s=1 s, prominence override keeps emitting).

4. Severity re-assessment -> MEDIUM, not high. What is defeated: the documented '-3 dB steps' shaping (a 6/9 dB cut lands in one step) and the truthfulness of cfs_status/report/budget; and, worst case, CFS silently stops protecting exactly the band that is howling while telling the operator/LLM a -9 dB notch is in. What is NOT defeated: cuts-only, the -9 dB floor, strip/slot targeting, show mode, tokens; no boost or loud event can result — every erroneous write is a deeper cut (safe direction) and the un-cuttable-band case fails to 'do nothing' (the operator still has panic/faders). The triggers are unusual-but-reachable (>=50 concurrent in-flight writes, or a fully lost /node reply set, or a mid-session FX-type change), which matches the brief's 'guarantee weakened under unusual but reachable conditions'. The misleading report during a live ring is the part that matters most operationally.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-4-src (branch wt/verify-cfs-4-src, commit 93ac393), tests/integration/test_verify_cfs4.py, 5 passed in 28 s via $SP/runtests.sh; no policy internals poked (unlike the claimant's _bucket_tokens=-1e4):
- test_a_sequential_runaway_loop_never_rate_limits_the_notch: 1 loop hammering desk.set_mute while a 2.4 kHz ring is injected -> policy.rate_limited events 0, flood.refused 0, bucket >= -2, first set_geq_band write gain_db -3.0. => sequential 'x100/s' narrative REFUTED.
- test_b[n=40]: 40 concurrent set_mute loops -> bucket -41.0, 0 RATE_LIMITED, notch -3.0 written at t+0.23 s, desk par/22 -3 dB (guarantee holds, just delayed).
- test_b[n=120]: bucket pinned -49.6, 42411 policy.rate_limited events; belief timeline band 22: -3.0@0.22 s, -6.0@1.25 s, -9.0@2.25 s with ZERO set_geq_band writes, fakedesk /fx/5/par/22 = 0.0 dB, budget_left 6->5. Flood stopped, ring silenced 1.6 s (bucket full), ring re-injected: NO GEQ write in 6 s (plan() returns None), desk still 0 dB, belief -9. Saved report: notches=[{band 22, depth_db -9.0, detections 3, confidence None}], notch_log=[], budget_left 5.
- test_c[lost=1]: conn.node_many patched to return None for 'fx/5' once (= all retries of one /node reply lost) -> DeskError TIMEOUT inside Desk.set_geq_band -> belief -3.0, 0 writes, desk 0 dB, budget 5; next detection: first desk write gain_db -6.0, par/22 -6 dB (6 dB single step). test_c[lost=2]: first desk write -9.0 (9 dB single step).
Code refs: detector.py:567-572 (_apply mutates before write), cfs.py:1080-1086 (except -> return None, no rollback), cfs.py:413-415 (writer validates against its own last-written gains), desk.py:1417/362-366 (NOT_A_GEQ/TIMEOUT sources inside set_geq_band before acquire_write), policy.py:384-393 (RATE_LIMITED only when queued wait >1 s => >=50 queued reservations), mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:684 (requests dispatched concurrently), cfs.py:559/1364 (status/report from nc.notches), cfs.py:1157-1163 (ring_out: one None -> 'not tamed' abort).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed split, with specifics: (1) make NotchController.plan() pure — return a proposal (band, current_db, new_db, is_new_band) without touching _gains/_touched/_notches; add commit(proposal, bus, session_id, ts) that does the mutation; CfsManager._notch calls commit only after writer.set_band_gain returned (cfs.py:1081), so any exception leaves belief == desk and the next detection re-proposes the same -3 dB step; budget is charged in commit. (2) Have _DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain additionally assert new >= current - |notch_step_db| - eps (step-size invariant at the last line of defence), so even a future logic slip cannot send a >3 dB jump. (3) Do the /fx/N type lookup for the session once at arm time (it is already known from preflight: ses.fx_slot/side and the par layout) and pass the resolved par address to the writer, removing the per-notch /node read that can TIMEOUT; keep NOT_A_GEQ detection via the xremote push/periodic revalidation instead. (4) Rate limiter: give CFS notch writes a reserved lane (e.g. acquire_write(priority=True) drawing from a small dedicated bucket of ~5/s, itself capped by 3 x budget per session) so a client burst can delay but never refuse the safety write; in ring_out treat RATE_LIMITED/TIMEOUT on a notch as 'retry once after wait_s (<=1 s)' before declaring 'not tamed'. (5) Build report/status 'notches' from writer.writes/notch_log (desk-confirmed) and flag any belief/desk mismatch explicitly rather than presenting belief as fact.

## D39. [medium] RTA source is neither required to verify at arm nor monitored during the session: Bus N's GEQ is notched (to −9 dB) on audio from another strip
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* high · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/cfs.py:910-917 (_arm stores set_rta_source result; `verified=False` does not block), src/x32mcp/meters.py:926-940 (mismatch only logged), cfs.py:1016-1044/_consume (no check of /-stat/rtasource or /-prefs/rta/* pushes for the life of the session); server.py:1500-1504 only stops the SERVER's own get_rta from moving it

**Mechanism.** The detector's only input is /meters/15, which follows the console-global RTA source pref. Anyone at the desk (RTA page, Solo with solo-priority re-enabled, X32-Edit) can re-point it; the write may also simply not take (unverified). CFS² keeps attributing every frame to the session bus and cuts that bus's GEQ — up to budget×3 writes, each band to −9 dB — for feedback (or a synth note) that lives on a different strip, while the real bus is unprotected. In ring_out the VERIFY step then fails (cut does nothing) → deepen to −9 → ABORT, i.e. 3 pointless cuts per ring plus an aborted run.

**Exploit.** feedback_watch(1) armed (Tier 1, no confirmation). Engineer solos the keyboard channel with RTA solo-priority back on, or opens Meters→RTA and picks Ch 1. Keyboard sustains a note / a wedge on another bus rings at 2.5 kHz → server writes /fx/5/par/22 −3, −6, −9 on Bus 1's GEQ, 1 s apart; cfs_status still says rta_source bus.1.

**Evidence.** test_x4_rta_source_moved_mid_watch_cuts_bus1_geq_for_foreign_audio: after arming (rta verified True) the fake's /-prefs/rta/source is set to CH01 (/-stat/rtasource no longer equals stat_expected); ring injected → observed three set_geq_band writes [−3.0, −6.0, −9.0] all to /fx/5/par/22, cfs.state.mode WATCH and rta_source 'bus.1' unchanged. test_x4b_arming_proceeds_when_rta_source_is_not_verified: fake ignores the /-prefs/rta/source write → res['rta']['verified'] False yet mode WATCH and the first cut lands on /fx/5/par/22. Both PASSED.

**Proposed fix.** _arm: if not ses.rta.verified → disarm and raise CfsError('RTA_UNVERIFIED') (ring_out: before any raise). During the session poll /-stat/rtasource (1 leaf GET per second, or react to /xremote pushes of /-prefs/rta/source|pos|options) and when it differs from ses.rta.stat_expected: watch → stop with abort_reason 'RTA source changed'; ring-out → abort+backoff. Never plan a notch from frames received after a mismatch.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-cfs-6-repro (commit 8251bb4, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs6.py, 3 passed against the real cfs.py/meters.py + FakeDesk over the in-memory UDP shim). Every factual element of the claim holds:

1. No verification gate at arm: cfs.py:909-916 `_arm` stores `ses.rta = await set_rta_source(...)` and proceeds regardless of `ses.rta.verified`; meters.py:926-940 only logs a /-stat/rtasource mismatch (documented as deliberate at meters.py:24-25 "a mismatch is logged, never raised"). Test B: with the fake refusing the /-prefs/rta/source write, feedback_watch(1) returned rta={verified: False, stat_actual: 168, stat_expected: 146}, published cfs.state{mode:'watch', rta_verified: False}, mode WATCH, rta_source 'bus.1', and a ring on MAIN produced /fx/5/par/22 writes [-3,-6,-9] on Bus 1's GEQ.

2. No monitoring during the session: cfs.py:1016-1044 `_consume` and `_notch` (1067-1092) never consult /-stat/rtasource or /-prefs/rta/*; grep shows the only RTA-source references in cfs.py are the arm call, the report dict and the cfs.state event. The connection DOES receive the console's /xremote push (connection.py:831-834 publishes an "update" event) — test A captured [{'/-prefs/rta/source': [2]}, {'/-stat/rtasource': [98]}] — and CFS ignores it. Test A: after a verified arm (stat 146), a front-panel move to CH01, then a 2.4 kHz ring → three set_geq_band writes [-3.0,-6.0,-9.0] all to /fx/5/par/22 (Bus 1 GEQ, FX5 side A), desk par = 0.2 (-9 dB), cfs.state still WATCH / rta_source 'bus.1' / budget 6→5, no cfs.abort, stop() report final_stage DONE, aborted False. The foreign ring is untouched (FakeDesk closed loop follows the RTA-source strip's insert, fakedesk.py:1103-1131), exactly the real-desk physics.

3. ring_out path: Test C: source moved to CH01 at the first RAISE; the bus master was raised -19→-14 dB with /-stat/rtasource = 98 (detector blind to Bus 1) — no check anywhere in the RAISE loop; on the foreign ring: HOLD, NOTCH/VERIFY ×3 all ok=False, writes [-3,-6,-9] to /fx/5/par/22, BACKOFF, ABORT 'not tamed', and the saved report still says rta {target:'bus.1', verified: True}.

4. server.py:1500-1504 only refuses the server's own get_rta(target=other) while CFS is active; I additionally checked the LLM cannot re-point the RTA mid-session by other tools (no generic set tool; restore_snapshot never restores -prefs, nodes.py:1204; scenes do not carry prefs), so the trigger is external: an engineer on Meters→RTA / X32-Edit / another OSC client, Solo with Solo-Priority re-enabled, or a desk that does not apply the pref.

Severity re-assessment → medium, not high. All enumerated write guarantees still hold in every run: cuts only, -3 dB steps, -9 dB floor (no 4th write), within budget (≤ budget bands × 3 writes), rate-limited, and on the session's own bus GEQ — the damage is bounded tonal damage to Bus N plus a false 'watching bus.1 (verified)' assurance while the real bus is unprotected.
