The mid-session trigger requires a human/other client to change a console-global pref (unusual but reachable in a live venue); the verified=False-at-arm trigger depends on desk behaviour that is UNCONFIRMED (HANDOVER §4a shows the write took on the real X32; a slow (>0.2 s) pref apply would also yield verified=False with the source actually correct, so a naive hard refusal would create false refusals). That matches the rubric's 'guarantee weakened under unusual but reachable conditions'. The strongest argument for high is the ring_out case: a confirmed Tier-2 procedure keeps raising a live wedge master toward the 0 dB ceiling (device.yaml ringout.master_ceiling_db) while the server has been explicitly told (pushed /-prefs/rta/source, /-stat/rtasource) that its detector is no longer on that bus — the hold-on-detection supervision that justifies ring_out (BRIEF §5, DESIGN §14) is void for the rest of the run. Master stays ceiling/target-bounded and the back-off still happens, so I keep it at medium (top of band).
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-6-repro, commit 8251bb4, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs6.py — `runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs6.py -s` → 3 passed in 7.8 s. Captured output:
A (watch, source moved mid-session): GEQ writes [('/fx/5/par/22', -3.0), ('/fx/5/par/22', -6.0), ('/fx/5/par/22', -9.0)]; fakedesk /-prefs/rta/source=2, /-stat/rtasource=98 (expected 146); pushes seen by X32Connection: [{'address':'/-prefs/rta/source','args':[2]},{'address':'/-stat/rtasource','args':[98]}]; cfs.state: WATCH, rta_source 'bus.1', budget_left 5, notch band 22 depth -9.0 detections 3 conf 1.0; fakedesk /fx/5/par/22 = 0.2; stop() report final DONE, abort_reason None.
B (desk ignores source write): arm rta={'target':'bus.1','source_index':50,'stat_expected':146,'stat_actual':168,'verified':False}; cfs.state event rta_verified False, mode 'watch'; fakedesk source still 72 (MAIN); ring → writes [-3.0,-6.0,-9.0] to /fx/5/par/22.
C (ring_out, moved at first RAISE): stages PREFLIGHT,SNAPSHOT,ARM,RAISE×6,HOLD,NOTCH,VERIFY,NOTCH,VERIFY,NOTCH,VERIFY,BACKOFF,ABORT; fader writes [-19,-18,-17,-16,-15,-14,-20] with /-stat/rtasource=98 throughout; GEQ writes [-3,-6,-9] to /fx/5/par/22; all VERIFY ok=False; abort_reason 'ring at 2333 Hz not tamed: GEQ band cannot cut deeper than -9 dB or the notch budget is spent'; report rta {'stat_actual':146,'verified':True,'target':'bus.1'}.
Code refs: cfs.py:909-916 (_arm, no gate on ses.rta.verified), cfs.py:928-929 (only place verified is surfaced), cfs.py:1016-1044 (_consume) and 1067-1092 (_notch) — no RTA-source check; meters.py:926-940 (mismatch logged, 2 attempts 0.1 s apart), meters.py:24-25 (documented 'never raised'); connection.py:831-834 (push → cache + 'update' event, unused by CFS); server.py:1500-1504 (BUSY guard covers only get_rta); nodes.py:1204 (-prefs never restored, so no LLM path to re-point mid-session). Notes: $SP/reports/verify-cfs-6-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* (1) Arm: keep set_rta_source's writes but verify for longer before deciding (poll /-stat/rtasource up to ~1 s, not 2×0.1 s — the desk applies prefs asynchronously, so a hard refusal on the current 0.2 s window would produce false RTA_UNVERIFIED refusals on real hardware); if still != stat_expected → _disarm, clear _ses, raise CfsError('RTA_UNVERIFIED', stat_actual/expected in the payload); in ring_out this must happen before the first RAISE. (2) Session: CfsManager already shares the EventBus with the connection — subscribe to 'update' events for rta.source_param/pos_param/options_param/stat_param (the console pushes them over /xremote, proven in test A) and, as a belt-and-braces against a lapsed /xremote lease, GET stat_param once per dwell/verify cycle (ring_out) or every ~1 s (watch). On mismatch: set a ses.rta_lost flag checked at the top of _notch (never plan/commit a notch from frames after the mismatch — also avoids the plan-before-write divergence), watch → _finish with abort_reason 'RTA source changed to <X>', ring_out → abort + abort_backoff_db. Optionally one automatic re-point attempt (re-run set_rta_source, reset the detector's trackers/cooldowns so stale growth history from the foreign strip is discarded) before aborting in watch mode. (3) Surface it: cfs_status/state should carry rta_verified and the live stat value, and the report's rta block should record the last observed stat, not just the arm-time one (test C's report claims verified:true for a run that was blind after ARM).

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap:
(1) Arm does not gate on verification. cfs.py:903-929 `_arm`: `ses.rta = await set_rta_source(self._conn, self._d, ses.target)`; only RtaSourceError→BAD_ARGUMENT and NotConnected→NOT_CONNECTED abort; `ses.rta.verified` is merely echoed in the cfs.state event (cfs.py:928-929), the feedback_watch result (cfs.py:582 `_rta_dict`) and the report (cfs.py:1388). Both feedback_watch (cfs.py:571-579) and ring_out (cfs.py:~690 `await self._arm(ses)`) go through it. meters.py:926-940: a /-stat/rtasource mismatch after 2 reads is `log.warning` only. This is deliberate design, not an oversight (docs/GIG_CHECKLIST.md:228 'rta.verified: true; if not, record stat_actual'; meters.md §5.2/pitfall 15: /-stat/rtasource may be stale after a console reset), but the effect is as claimed: an unverified analyser still drives autonomous GEQ writes.
(2) No monitoring for the life of the session. `_consume` (cfs.py:1024-1052) feeds every /meters/15 frame to the detector; grep of src/ shows no read of /-stat/rtasource or /-prefs/rta/* anywhere after arm (only cfs.py:916 invalidates the cache). CfsState.rta_source is the static `ses.target.key` (cfs.py:561), so cfs_status and the report keep asserting bus.N and the arm-time verified/stat_actual. server.py:1500-1504 only refuses the server's OWN get_rta(target=other) while active; its docstring (server.py:1493-1494, shown to the LLM) even promises 'while a CFS2 session runs the RTA stays on its bus', which nothing enforces against the console. No other MCP tool writes /-prefs/rta (no generic set_param), so the LLM alone cannot trigger this; the trigger is external: METERS→RTA encoder 6, X32-Edit/any OSC client, Solo Priority re-ticked + a solo (set_rta_source clears bit 5 once at arm, meters.py:893-896, never re-asserts it), or the arm write not taking / stale stat.
(3) Consequence path. Detections from foreign audio go to `_notch` (cfs.py:1071-1097) → NotchController.plan → _DeskGeqWriter on the SESSION bus's fx_slot/side fixed at `_open_session` (cfs.py:887-889). WATCH: each foreign ring/held note (prominence override ≥25 dB/6 frames, detector.py:350-367, no frequency window in device.yaml) opens or deepens a band, −3 per cooldown_s=1 s to −9, up to notch_budget_default=6 bands. RINGOUT: `_notch_and_verify` (cfs.py:1207-1225) cannot see a drop → deepen ×3 → 'not tamed' → ABORT + abort_backoff_db 6 (safe direction) — identical to the existing tests/integration/test_cfs.py::test_ring_out_untamable_ring_backs_off open-loop case. Meanwhile Bus N itself is unmonitored: in WATCH the human raising the wedge has no net; in RINGOUT, if the foreign source is quiet, `_run_ringout` keeps raising 1 dB/1.5 s to min(bus_fader_max_db 0, master_ceiling_db 0) deaf to Bus N ringing (reasoning only — the FakeDesk has no acoustic loop model tying master level to ring onset).
Real desk vs FakeDesk: the fake's behaviour here mirrors VERIFIED ground truth — meters.md §5: '/meters/15 takes no source argument … the stream is simply the console's RTA, whatever it is currently analysing'; /-prefs/rta/source is one console-global pref (X32 manual §7.3 encoder 6); Solo Priority 'replaces the RTA source with the monitor solo bus whenever a channel solo is active … affects both the GEQ RTA view and the dedicated RTA tab'. A GEQ cut on Bus N physically cannot attenuate audio on an unrelated bus/channel, exactly what fakedesk._refresh_cuts models (cuts taken from the GEQ inserted on the RTA-source strip). Caveats: (a) if the RTA is re-pointed to a channel that feeds Bus N and the loop runs through Bus N's wedge, the cut is still effective, so not every re-point is harmful; (b) UNCONFIRMED extra real-desk trigger the fake does not model: opening another FX slot's GEQ page may switch what /meters/15 carries (meters.md §5.3 'Which of the three analyses /meters/15 carries when a PEQ or GEQ page is displayed … UNCONFIRMED'); (c) whether the real desk pushes /-prefs/rta changes over /xremote is not established (transport.md §4: 'some changes or user actions will not be reported'), so a fix must poll /-stat/rtasource rather than rely on pushes; (d) M7 (HANDOVER §4b) shows set_rta_source does take on the real desk (feedback caught on Main LR), so the 'write does not take' branch is the less likely trigger; the mid-session human re-point is the realistic one (Tier-1 watch can stay armed for a whole show on a console the engineer is also using).
Severity re-assessment: MEDIUM (upper end), not high. Every documented notch guarantee still holds and I checked them in the repro: cuts only, −3 dB steps, −9 dB floor, ≤ budget bands, written to the session bus's own GEQ side (the documented target) — so 'wrong strip' in the rubric's sense (write lands on a strip other than the session's) is not what happens; what fails is evidence attribution and the implicit 'the detector is listening to Bus N' precondition, under an external, unusual-but-reachable condition (someone moves the console's single RTA mid-session) — the rubric's definition of medium. Harm is bounded and non-loud: ≤6 pointless 1/3-octave cuts to −9 dB on a live mix, an aborted ring-out (fails safe with back-off), silent loss of feedback protection with status/report still claiming rta verified on bus.N, and — by reasoning only — a deaf ring-out raise bounded by the 0 dB ceiling at 1 dB/1.5 s with the operator attending a confirmed Tier-2 run. Not critical (no unbounded/loud change, panic unaffected), and no Tier-2 action becomes unconfirmed.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-6-src (branch wt/verify-cfs-6-src, commit 79f9730), tests/integration/test_verify_cfs6_src.py, run with $SP/runtests.sh → 3 passed in 7.4 s:
• test_v1_watch_rta_moved_mid_session_bus1_geq_cut_for_foreign_audio: standard Bus 1 provisioning, feedback_watch(1) → rta.verified True, stat_expected 146. Front-panel move fakedesk.set_value('/-prefs/rta/source','CH01') → /-stat/rtasource 98 and `await conn.get('/-stat/rtasource') == 98` (one leaf GET would have caught it). inject_ring(2400 Hz, 20 dB/s) on the now-foreign analyser → desk.write set_geq_band [-3.0, -6.0, -9.0] all to /fx/5/par/22 (Bus 1 = FX5L side A), no 4th write after 1.5 s, fake par22 raw 0.2 (−9 dB). Then inject_note(1000 Hz, −15 dB) (held synth note) → prominence override → /fx/5/par/18 −3.0. cfs.state.mode WATCH, rta_source 'bus.1', budget_left 4; all writes ∈ {−3,−6,−9} on /fx/5/par/1..31; stop() report still says rta.verified True, stat_actual 146.
• test_v2_unverified_rta_source_does_not_block_arming: fake ignores the /-prefs/rta/source store (source left at CH03) → res.rta.verified False, stat_actual 100, yet mode WATCH; injected ring → first write /fx/5/par/22 −3.0.
• test_v3_ringout_rta_moved_after_arm_aborts_with_three_pointless_cuts: ring_out(1, target 0 dB, dwell 400 ms, decay_verify_s 0.3); at the first RAISE the source is re-pointed to CH01 and a 15 dB/s ring injected → GEQ writes [−3,−6,−9] on /fx/5/par/22, final_stage ABORT 'not tamed', end_master_db == max_master_db − 6.0, report rta.verified True, desk /-stat/rtasource 98, par22 at −9 dB.
Source anchors: cfs.py:903-929 (_arm, no branch on verified), cfs.py:561/582/1388 (static rta_source / arm-time rta dict in state, result, report), cfs.py:1024-1052 (_consume, no source check), meters.py:893-896 (solo priority cleared once), meters.py:926-940 (mismatch → warning), server.py:1489-1506 (get_rta guard + docstring promise), device.yaml detector notch_budget_default 6 / override_prominence_db 25 / no frequency window, ringout master_ceiling_db 0, policy bus_fader_max_db 0; docs/research/meters.md §5 (VERIFIED: /meters/15 has no source arg; Solo Priority semantics; UNCONFIRMED GEQ-page analysis; pitfall 15 stale /-stat/rtasource), docs/research/transport.md §4 (pushes not guaranteed for all params), docs/GIG_CHECKLIST.md:228 (unverified treated as informational), docs/HANDOVER.md §4b (set_rta_source works on the real desk).
>
> *Better fix:* Do not make `verified=False` alone fatal for everything: /-stat/rtasource is documented as possibly stale after a console reset (meters.md pitfall 15) and the gig checklist already expects to see it, so a hard refusal would block a working desk. Instead: (1) at arm, on mismatch retry once via `/-action/setrtasrc ,i (stat_expected mod 98)` + re-read; if still mismatched → ring_out/ring_out_system (autonomous raises) raise CfsError('RTA_UNVERIFIED') before the SNAPSHOT/ARM stages; feedback_watch arms only with an explicit `accept_unverified_rta=True`, otherwise refuses with stat_actual in the error. (2) During the session run a cheap side task (or piggyback on `_consume` every ~20 frames) that GETs /-stat/rtasource once per second — poll, do not rely on /xremote pushes (not guaranteed for -prefs on the real desk) — and compares against the value READ at arm (not the computed expectation, so a stale-but-stable stat does not false-abort) and, if pushed, the cached /-prefs/rta/source and options bit 5. On change: clear ses.pending, reset the detector's candidates, then WATCH → stop with abort_reason 'RTA source changed to <target_for_rta_source(...)>' (optionally one re-assert attempt with a cfs.warning event first), RINGOUT → abort + backoff; never call `_notch` for a detection whose frames postdate the last good poll (carry a `rta_ok_ts` and drop detections with det.ts > it once a mismatch is seen). (3) Report truthfully: CfsState/cfs_status should expose the live stat value and a `rta_ok` flag rather than the static ses.target.key, and the report should record any source change. (4) Fix the get_rta docstring ('the RTA stays on its bus') so the LLM is not told a guarantee that does not exist.

## D40. [medium] GEQ slot/insert never re-validated mid-session: a stereo type load makes side-B writes hit the shared pars (other bus cut too); a re-patched insert sends the cuts to another strip's EQ
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/desk.py:1417-1424 (set_geq_band: side 'B' on a stereo GEQ/TEQ silently maps to par 1-31), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:884-889 (session freezes fx_slot/side from preflight), cfs.py:1070-1086 (_notch never re-checks insert sel/on or fx type), provision.py:400-412 (the shared-stereo / same-insert blockers exist only at preflight)

**Mechanism.** Preflight refuses a stereo GEQ shared by two strips and an insert point used twice, but the session then writes (slot, side) blindly for minutes/hours. set_geq_band re-reads /fx/N type per write and, for a stereo type, uses par 1-31 whatever the side — so a Bus-2/FX5R session whose slot was reloaded as 'GEQ' now cuts the L/R-linked bands that Bus 1 (FX5L) runs through. If the operator moves FX5L to another strip (or turns the bus insert OFF), the cuts land on that strip's insert EQ (or on nothing) while the session bus rings on un-notched; ring_out then burns 3 cuts and aborts.

**Exploit.** setup_ringout_eqs([1,2]) (FX5L→Bus1, FX5R→Bus2); feedback_watch(2); someone loads 'GEQ' (stereo) into FX5 from the surface, or re-inserts FX5L on Ch 5; next ring on the RTA → /fx/5/par/22 written −3…−9: Bus 1's (or Ch 5's) EQ is cut by a session that validated only Bus 2.

**Evidence.** test_x4c_fx_type_changed_to_stereo_mid_session_cut_lands_on_shared_pars: session side 'B' slot 5; /fx/5/type set to GEQ mid-watch; validate_ringout_eqs([2]) NOW reports 'shared with Bus 1'; ring → first write address /fx/5/par/22 (not par/54), fake par/22 = −3 dB. test_x4e_insert_repatched_mid_watch_cuts_land_on_another_strips_geq: bus.1 insert OFF + ch.5 insert FX5L mid-watch → writes [−3,−6,−9] to /fx/5/par/22 (Ch 5's insert GEQ at −9 dB). Both PASSED. (Non-GEQ type → blocked by NOT_A_GEQ, test_x4d.)

**Proposed fix.** set_geq_band: raise DeskError('GEQ_SIDE') when side=='B' and the type is stereo (callers must ask for 'A' explicitly), and accept an `expect_type` argument that must match. In _notch, before writing, re-read the two cached sections `{bus}/insert` and `/fx/{slot}` and require sel==ses.sel, on==True, type==preflight type and no other strip on the same sel (get_inserts is cached 2 s); mismatch → abort 'GEQ insert changed' without writing.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-7-repro (commit e96e6de, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs7.py, 8 passed in 19 s against the unmodified a408a2a code + FakeDesk).

Code facts verified: (1) desk.py:1403-1431 set_geq_band re-reads /fx/N type on every write and, for a type in geq.fx_types_stereo (GEQ/TEQ), uses par_a_first/par_a_master regardless of side (desk.py:1419-1420) — side 'B' silently becomes pars 1-31/32, which per docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md §2 (line 222-223) are the L/R-linked bands both inserted strips run through. (2) cfs.py:880-897 _open_session freezes fx_slot/side/sel from the preflight InsertInfo into _Session and _DeskGeqWriter; (3) cfs.py:1070-1093 _notch -> _DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain (cfs.py:410-416) only runs policy.validate_notch (cuts-only/step/floor) and Desk.set_geq_band(slot, side, ...) — no re-read of {bus}/insert sel/on, no fx-type comparison, no other-strip-on-same-sel check. The only callers of preflight/validate_ringout_eqs in cfs.py are at session start (cfs.py:574 feedback_watch, :669 ring_out) and the system-plan builder (:1412). (4) The 'stereo … shared with' and 'also inserted on' blockers live only in provision.validate_ringout_eqs (provision.py:400-411), consumed by preflight.

Behaviour observed: baseline Bus-2/FX5R session on GEQ2 writes /fx/5/par/54 (correct). After /fx/5/type -> 'GEQ' mid-watch (via fset+invalidate, and separately via only the fake's /xremote push + 2 s TTL), validate_ringout_eqs([2]) and preflight(2) both report 'stereo GEQ in FX slot 5 is shared with Bus 1: a cut would hit both', yet the armed session stays in WATCH and its next ring writes /fx/5/par/22 = -3 dB with value.side=='B' — the band Bus 1 (FX5L) passes through. Insert re-patch: bus.1 insert OFF/sel OFF + ch.5 insert FX5L ON mid-watch -> get_inserts shows ch.5 on slot 5 side A, validate([1]) not ok, but the session (now open-loop, the bus has no GEQ in path) deepens and writes [-3,-6,-9] to /fx/5/par/22, leaving Ch 5's insert GEQ 2.5 kHz band at -9 dB (raw 0.2). Insert merely bypassed -> same three writes to a GEQ no longer in the path (budget burns, bus rings un-notched). ring_out variant: insert moved to Ch 5 at RAISE -> exactly 3 writes to /fx/5/par/22 (-3/-6/-9), report aborted 'not tamed', Ch 5's GEQ left at -9 dB after the back-off. Non-GEQ type (DES2) mid-watch is blocked by NOT_A_GEQ with zero writes (session silently continues, not aborted).

Severity: agree with medium. It defeats the 'right strip only / no shared stereo GEQ' guarantee that preflight documents, but only when someone changes the FX type or re-patches the insert on the surface (or via another client) while a session is armed — unusual but entirely reachable during a minutes-long watch. Damage is bounded: cuts only, -3 dB steps, -9 dB floor and budget all still hold (asserted), so no loud/unbounded change; the harm is up to -9 dB notches on another strip's EQ (or on both buses of a now-stereo GEQ) plus an un-notched ringing bus and a wasted budget/aborted ring_out. Note the FakeDesk does not reset pars on a type load whereas the real X32 re-initialises FX params, which only widens the controller/desk divergence in case (a).
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-7-repro, commit e96e6de, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs7.py; run: runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs7.py -v -> 8 passed in 19.21s.
- test_a0_baseline_side_b_dual_geq_writes_par_54: provision([1,2]), feedback_watch(2) -> geq {fx_slot 5, side 'B', sel FX5R}; ring 2400 Hz -> first desk.write address '/fx/5/par/54', fake par/54=0.4 (-3 dB), par/22 untouched (0.5). PASS.
- test_a1_stereo_type_loaded_mid_watch_side_b_session_cuts_shared_pars: same session; fset /fx/5/type='GEQ'; validate_ringout_eqs([2]).ok False with reason containing 'shared with Bus 1'; preflight(2).ok False same blocker; cfs.state.mode still WATCH; ring -> first write address '/fx/5/par/22', value.side=='B', gain_db -3.0; fake /fx/5/par/22 == 0.4; state.notches[0] side 'B' slot 5. PASS.

Code refs: desk.py:1416-1422 (type branch: stereo -> par_a_first/par_a_master for any side), cfs.py:884-895 (session freezes ins.fx_slot/ins.side/ins.sel), cfs.py:410-416 (_DeskGeqWriter: validate_notch + set_geq_band only), cfs.py:1070-1086 (_notch: plan -> write, no insert/type re-check), provision.py:400-411 (bypassed/shared-stereo/same-sel blockers only in validate_ringout_eqs), cfs.py:574/669 (preflight called once at arm), docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md:222-223 (GEQ/TEQ stereo: bands 1..31 L/R linked, 33-64 unused).
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's fix is sound; concretely: (1) Desk.set_geq_band: add expect_type: str | None and refuse (DeskError('GEQ_CHANGED')) when the live /fx/N type differs; raise DeskError('GEQ_SIDE') for side 'B' on a stereo type instead of folding it onto pars 1-31 (provision never allocates 'B' on a stereo graphic, so no legitimate caller needs that mapping). (2) In cfs._notch before nc.plan (so the controller does not commit a depth it will not write — also addresses the already-known plan-before-write divergence), re-read the two cached sections f'{target prefix}/insert' and f'/fx/{ses.fx_slot}' (both ride the 2 s node cache / xremote invalidation, so cost is ~0 on the hot path) and require insert.sel == ses.sel, insert.on, fx type == ses.preflight.geq.insert.fx_type; cheaply also scan desk.get_inserts() (cached) for another strip on the same fx_slot (stereo) or same side (dual). On mismatch: publish cfs.abort 'GEQ insert changed: …', write nothing, and end the session (watch -> DONE/ABORT report; ring_out -> back-off path). (3) Optionally have Desk._on_push watch '/fx/{slot}/type' and '*/insert/*' while a session is armed and proactively abort, so the operator learns immediately rather than at the next ring.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain read end to end and executed; no gap found.

(1) Preflight-only validation: provision.validate_ringout_eqs (src/x32mcp/provision.py:391-412) is the only place that refuses "stereo GEQ shared with <other strip>" and "<sel> also inserted on <other strip>" / "insert bypassed". cfs.feedback_watch (cfs.py:565-579) and cfs.ring_out (cfs.py:660-668) call preflight once under the lock; _open_session (cfs.py:884-897) copies ins.fx_slot/ins.side/ins.sel into _DeskGeqWriter and _Session. Nothing afterwards re-reads them: CfsManager subscribes only to "connection.state" (cfs.py:522); _notch (cfs.py:1070-1093) -> _DeskGeqWriter.set_band_gain (cfs.py:410-416) checks only bus==self.bus and policy.validate_notch, then Desk.set_geq_band(self.fx_slot, self.side, ...).

(2) set_geq_band (desk.py:1403-1431) re-reads the /fx/N section on every write through the 2 s read cache (device.yaml policy.read_cache_ttl_s: 2.0, desk.py:212/313), and that cache entry is also dropped by a pushed /xremote update (connection keeps /xremote alive, connection.py:892; Desk._on_push desk.py:282-284). For a type in geq.fx_types_stereo it uses par_a_first/par_a_master regardless of side (desk.py:1421-1422); only a non-GEQ type raises NOT_A_GEQ. So a session frozen at (slot 5, side 'B') writes /fx/5/par/NN (1..31) once the slot holds GEQ/TEQ. docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md §2.2 (VERIFIED, DOC p.104) confirms on the real desk GEQ/TEQ par 1..31 are the L/R-linked bands and 33..63 are unused, and §3.4 confirms FXnL/FXnR of one slot can sit on two different strips; so on a real X32 the cut lands on the linked curve that Bus 1 (FX5L) also runs through — exactly the condition preflight refuses. The FakeDesk models the same mapping (fakedesk.py:1122-1124), so fake and real agree.

(3) Re-patched insert: the writer addresses (slot, side), never the strip, so if FX5L is freed from the session bus and inserted on another strip (or the bus insert is bypassed), the writes go to whatever now owns FX5 side A. On a real desk the surface refuses selecting an in-use insert point, so the operator must first set the bus's sel OFF (the sequence tested); insert/sel and insert/on changes are ordinary pushed parameters, so nothing differs from the fake. In WATCH mode the session keeps cutting to -9 at each ringing band (bounded by budget) on the foreign strip while its own bus rings un-notched and it never notices; in RINGOUT mode the state machine fails safe but late: HOLD (no further raise, cfs.py:1155-1166), three NOTCH/VERIFY rounds of decay_verify_s=1.5 s each on the wrong EQ, then ABORT "not tamed" and a forced abort_backoff_db=6 dB back-off (cfs.py:1240-1262).

(4) Reachability: primary trigger is an external actor (surface, X32-Edit, another OSC client) changing the FX rack type or insert routing during a session that runs for minutes (watch) — unusual but realistic at a sound check. It is also LLM-reachable, but only through confirmed Tier-2 tools: server.recall_scene (server.py:1124-1147), restore_snapshot and apply_patch_plan have no cfs.active interlock (only connect/disconnect/get_rta check it, server.py:196/675/1500), and Desk.recall_scene ran unrefused during an armed watch in my test. setup_ringout_eqs cannot produce the stereo case (it only loads GEQ2).

(5) Real-desk-only aggravation not visible on the fake: loading a new effect resets its pars to defaults (X32CopyFX FXdef rows for GEQ/GEQ2 are all 0.0), so after a type load the controller's gains/existing belief is stale: bands it believes at -9 are never re-cut (ring persists → ring-out aborts), bands believed at -3 get -6 as the desk's first step. Still cuts-only and ≥ -9 dB.

Severity re-assessment: medium is right. No level increase is possible (validate_notch keeps cuts-only, -3 steps, -9 floor; budget intact); panic/show-mode/tokens unaffected; ring-out holds and backs off. What is defeated is the wrong-strip/shared-GEQ guarantee that preflight documents by refusing it — but only under an out-of-band routing change after arming (or a user-confirmed scene recall), i.e. "guarantee weakened under unusual but reachable conditions". Worst audible outcome: up to notch_budget (6) bands of another mix's/channel's insert GEQ cut by up to 9 dB, plus ~4.5 s of un-notched ringing before the ring-out back-off; in watch mode the human is driving gain and gets no warning that the notches are going nowhere useful.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-7-src (branch wt/verify-cfs-7-src, commit f33faba), tests/integration/test_verify_cfs7.py — 6 passed (runtests.sh, 16 s). Unlike the claimant's tests, front-panel changes were made with fakedesk.set_value only (pushed over /xremote, NO desk.invalidate()), so the Desk cache had to notice on its own:
- v0 (control): with FX5=GEQ (stereo) or Ch5 also on FX5R BEFORE arming, feedback_watch(2) raises PREFLIGHT_FAILED ("shared with Bus 1" / "also inserted on Ch 5") — the blockers exist only there.
- v1: provision [1,2] (FX5=GEQ2, Bus1=FX5L, Bus2=FX5R); feedback_watch(2) → geq {fx_slot 5, side 'B', sel FX5R}; cache primed with type GEQ2; panel load /fx/5/type=GEQ → push evicted '/fx/5' from desk._cache within 2 s; validate_ringout_eqs([2]) now not ok ("stereo GEQ in FX slot 5 is shared with Bus 1"); ring at 2.4 kHz → first set_geq_band write address /fx/5/par/22, value {side:'B', gain_db:-3.0}; fake par/22=-3.0 dB, par/54=0.0; ring decays >3 dB in 0.4 s (R path uses the linked bands) while /bus/01/insert/sel is still FX5L,on → Bus 1 shares the cut; cfs.state.mode still WATCH.
- v2: watch on Bus 1; panel: bus.01 insert/sel OFF, ch.05 insert/sel FX5L + on; validate now reports "no FX insert on Bus 1"; ring → writes [-3,-6,-9] all to /fx/5/par/22; fake par/22 = -9.0 dB (Ch 5's insert EQ); Bus-1 ring never attenuated; exactly 3 writes (capped at -9), session still WATCH.
- v3 (ring_out 1, target 0, step 1, dwell 100): re-patch + ring injected at RAISE ≥ -17 → stages HOLD, NOTCH×3, VERIFY×3 (all ok=False), no RAISE after HOLD, ABORT "ring at 2400 Hz not tamed", printed: max_master=-14.0 end=-20.0, HOLD→BACKOFF 4.50 s, writes [('/fx/5/par/22',-3.0),(-6.0),(-9.0)]; fader read-back -20.0; Ch5 par/22=-9 dB.
- v4: bus.01 insert/on OFF mid-watch → [-3,-6,-9] still written to /fx/5/par/22 (own bypassed GEQ), unnoticed.
- v5: scene 10 holds {FX5=GEQ2, Ch5 insert FX5L, Bus1 insert OFF}; live: Bus 1 provisioned on FX5L, watch armed; Desk.recall_scene(10) (what server.recall_scene calls after the token; no cfs.active check at server.py:1124-1147) was NOT refused → ch.05 sel=FX5L, bus.01 sel=OFF → ring → 3 writes to /fx/5/par/22, par/22=-9 dB, session still WATCH.
Code refs: desk.py:1417-1424 (stereo → par_a regardless of side), cfs.py:410-416 (writer: no slot/insert/type re-check), cfs.py:884-897 (freeze from preflight), cfs.py:522 (only connection.state subscribed), cfs.py:1070-1093 (_notch), provision.py:391-412 (blockers only in validate_ringout_eqs), device.yaml:739 read_cache_ttl_s 2.0, :817-822 notch_max_db -9 / decay_verify_s 1.5, fx_routing_scenes.md §2.2 table (GEQ/TEQ par 1..31 L/R linked, VERIFIED) and §3.4 (one insert point per strip; dual sides on two strips).
>
> *Better fix:* The proposed `GEQ_SIDE` raise (side 'B' on a stereo type) would break a case preflight currently ACCEPTS — a bus alone on FXnR of an unshared stereo GEQ/TEQ (validate_ringout_eqs only refuses stereo when another strip shares the slot) — so either preflight must also refuse/normalise that case or drop this part. Prefer: (1) give _DeskGeqWriter the preflight fx_type and have Desk.set_geq_band take `expect_type`; raise DeskError('GEQ_CHANGED') when the per-write re-read type differs (free: the /fx/N section is already read per write) and make _notch treat GEQ_CHANGED/NOT_A_GEQ as a session abort ('GEQ in FX slot N changed at the desk'), not a logged skip. (2) Push-driven guard instead of per-notch polling: while a session is armed, CfsManager subscribes to pushed updates (conn.on_update or a Desk invalidation hook) for /fx/{slot}/type, {bus}/insert/{sel,on} and any */insert/sel whose new value equals ses.sel, and aborts/stops the session on any hit — zero added detect→cut latency (BRIEF §5 <100 ms), and get_inserts (≈80 sections) never runs in the hot path. (3) Belt and braces in _notch: read the cached, push-invalidated `{bus}/insert` section before writing and require sel==ses.sel and on==True. (4) server.py: refuse recall_scene / restore_snapshot / setup_ringout_eqs / apply_patch_plan(include_source) / set_channel_config while a.cfs.active (or stop the watch first and say so), mirroring the existing get_rta/connect interlocks. (5) With the lead finding: roll NotchController back when the write raises, and after a GEQ_CHANGED abort re-read the slot's bands so `existing` matches a type load that reset the pars.

## D41. [medium] BACKOFF / abort lowering write goes through the rate limiter and is not retried: a client burst at that instant leaves the bus master parked at the highest level reached
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/cfs.py:1280-1292 (_finalize_levels: one attempt, any DeskError/PolicyError → 'back-off failed', end_master = max), cfs.py:1115-1124 (_write_master → Desk.set_level → desk.py:871-877 acquire_write), src/x32mcp/policy.py:372-395 (RATE_LIMITED when the queued wait > 1 s); contrast panic (desk.py:1190, bypass) and _restore_master (cfs.py:1324-1359, raw retried writes)

**Mechanism.** The one write whose job is to make the bus safe again (DONE −3 dB margin, ABORT −6 dB, operator stop, cancellation unwind) is an ordinary Tier-1 write: it competes for the shared 50/s bucket with whatever the LLM is doing and gets exactly one attempt. Under the stated threat model (runaway loop hammering write tools) RATE_LIMITED is likely precisely when the operator hits feedback_watch_stop. Result: ABORT reported, master left at max_master_db (at/near the feedback level), no margin.

**Exploit.** ring_out running; LLM loop issues ~100 set_send calls in a second; operator says stop → feedback_watch_stop → abort → BACKOFF write raises RATE_LIMITED → report 'ABORT — back-off failed', Bus master stays at the highest level the run reached.

**Evidence.** test_x3d_backoff_write_rate_limited_leaves_master_at_max: bucket drained in the BACKOFF stage callback; observed final_stage ABORT, abort_reason contains 'back-off failed', end_master_db == max_master_db == −6.0 (no −3 dB margin), fake fader −6.0. PASSED.

**Proposed fix.** Lowering CFS master writes should use the emergency lane: Desk.set_level(..., priority=True) → acquire_write(panic=True) (a lowering write can never be dangerous), retried 3× and, if the Desk path still fails, fall back to the _restore_master raw-write loop targeting min(start_master_db, master−back). Report restored/attempts like the connection-loss path.

> **Verifier 1: PARTIAL (medium)** — MECHANISM CONFIRMED exactly as claimed; TRIGGER NARROWER than the claim's exploit text.

Code path (worktree wt/verify-cfs-8-repro @3a97070, HEAD a408a2a): cfs.py:1261-1292 `_finalize_levels` computes final=master-back (DONE 3 dB / ABORT 6 dB), publishes BACKOFF, then makes exactly ONE attempt `await self._write_master(ses, final, force=True)`; `except (DeskError, PolicyError, X32ConnectionError)` -> log.warning, `ses.abort_reason = ses.abort_reason or "back-off failed: ..."`, final_stage ABORT, `end_master_db = ses.master_db` (the max). `_write_master` (cfs.py:1115-1124) -> Desk.set_level -> _move_level -> _ramp -> `_write` -> `policy.acquire_write()` (desk.py:877, no panic=True) -> policy.py:375-395 raises RATE_LIMITED when the queued wait exceeds max_wait_s=1.0 s. `force=True` only defeats the relative clamp/show mode, not the limiter. The same single-attempt path serves operator stop (cfs.stop -> abort_reason -> loop break -> _finalize_levels), cfs.abort, and the cancellation/_unwind path (cfs.py:951-967 shields the same _finalize_levels). Nothing retries; the session closes (mode idle). Contrast: Desk.panic uses acquire_write(panic=True) (desk.py:1190) and _restore_master (cfs.py:1324-1359) uses raw retried writes — neither is used for the back-off.

What actually happens (all executed against FakeDesk):
A (claimant's method: bucket drained in the BACKOFF stage callback via 100 concurrent acquire_write reservations): final_stage ABORT, abort_reason 'back-off failed: RATE_LIMITED: ... next slot is 1.02 s away', end_master_db == max_master_db == -6.0 (no 3 dB margin), fake /bus/01/mix/fader -5.99, last fader write was the RAISE to -6.0, session idle. Identical to the claim's evidence.
C (how deep the burst must be): with a full bucket, 99 concurrent in-flight writes -> the back-off queues 1.00 s and LANDS; 100 -> RATE_LIMITED; 150 -> still RATE_LIMITED but the bucket floor is ~-50 (refused calls reserve nothing), so it recovers one slot per 20 ms. I.e. the back-off is refused only while >=~100 writes are in flight (>=~50 if the bucket was already empty), and that window closes ~20 ms after arrivals stop unless the flood is sustained.
B1 (the claim's literal exploit: a runaway LLM LOOP awaiting each set_send, 192 calls in 3 s, then operator stop): back-off LANDED (max -8 -> end -14, fader -14.03). A sequential loop never holds more than one reservation, so it cannot trigger this. A2: 120 real concurrent set_send created at the BACKOFF instant also lose the race (each needs a read round trip first; back-off lands at -9.0).
B2/E (client PIPELINING tool calls, i.e. concurrent set_send not awaited, sustained across the stop): at one call/4 ms (250/s offered) the abort back-off failed in 2 of 3 CFS-level runs (fader parked at max -11.0, abort_reason stays 'stopped by operator' — the back-off failure is not even named in the report, only visible as end==max) and landed after a ~1.2 s queue in the others; at one call/8 ms it always landed; at one call/2 ms and /1 ms (>=500/s) it is deterministic and worse than claimed: the ring-out's own RAISE write is refused first ('raise refused by policy: RATE_LIMITED'), the run aborts by itself, the 6 dB abort back-off is ALSO refused, and the tool result reads 'master -20.0 -> -13.0 dB (highest -13.0) ... ABORT' with Bus 1 left 6-7 dB above where the operator had it (fake fader -13.01 / -14.03); feedback_watch_stop then reports 'Nothing to stop: CFS2 is idle'. Test E drives this through the real tool functions (srv.ring_out token dance + pipelined srv.set_send + srv.feedback_watch_stop); the MCP SDK dispatches requests concurrently (mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py _spawn -> tg.start_soon) and server.py has no tool-level lock, so a client that pipelines requests reaches this state.
D: with the bucket pinned at -49.97, Desk.panic() still mutes all 24 outputs in 0.2 ms — the operator's real emergency lane is intact.

Verdict PARTIAL: the defect (safety-restoring write shares the LLM's bucket, single attempt, no priority lane, no raw fallback, failure under-reported) is real and reproduced; but it needs >=~100 Tier-1 writes concurrently in flight at the back-off's acquire (a pipelining client at >=~250 calls/s sustained, deterministic at >=500/s), not 'an LLM loop issuing ~100 set_send calls in a second' — an awaited loop is paced by the limiter at 50/s and never blocks the back-off (B1), and a burst that starts at the back-off instant loses the race (A2). Severity stays medium: a documented guarantee (DONE -3 dB margin / ABORT -6 dB back-off, 'nothing may leave the master raised') is defeated and the bus master is left above the operator's setting with the session closed and nothing retrying, but only under an unusual (though reachable via the concurrent MCP dispatcher) load shape; no new louder level is produced (the fader stays where the confirmed ring-out already had it) and panic is unaffected.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-8-repro (branch wt/verify-cfs-8-repro, commit 3a97070), test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs8.py — 22 passed in 76.9 s; JSON results in $SP/reports/verify-cfs-8-repro/.
A_bucket_drained_at_backoff.json: final_stage ABORT, abort_reason 'back-off failed: RATE_LIMITED: too many writes: the next slot is 1.02 s away (limit 50/s)', start -20.0, max -6.0, end -6.0, fake_fader -5.99, slots at BACKOFF event 50.0, fader_writes end at -6.0 (no lowering write), mode_after idle.
C_depth_n{49,60,98,99,100,150}.json: back-off refused = None,None,None,None(waited 1.002 s),RATE_LIMITED,RATE_LIMITED; slots before back-off 1.0,-10,-47.99,-48.99,-49.99,-49.97; n=150 -> 50 of the hogs themselves refused (floor ~-50).
B1_sequential_loop_stop.json: 192 awaited set_send in 3 s (all ok, slots 0.01 at stop) -> ABORT 'stopped by operator', max -8.0, end -14.0, fake_fader -14.03 (back-off landed).
A2_burst_created_at_backoff.json: 120 concurrent set_send created at BACKOFF -> DONE, end -9.0, fader -9.0 (all 120 ok).
B2_pipelined_flood_stop_p4_r{0,1,2}.json: backoff_landed False/True/False; failures: max -11.0 == end -11.0, fake_fader -10.98, abort_reason 'stopped by operator' (back-off failure unnamed), flood ok 245 / RATE_LIMITED ~378 of ~622. p8: landed both runs (slots only -0.9). p2 and p1 (2 runs each): landed False, abort_reason 'raise refused by policy: RATE_LIMITED ... 1.01 s', max==end -13.0/-14.0, fake_fader -13.01/-14.03, stop_took 0.0 s (run had already aborted itself).
E_tools_flood_stop_p{4,2,1}.json (srv.ring_out with token + pipelined srv.set_send + srv.feedback_watch_stop): p4 landed (end -17 = max -11 - 6); p2/p1 landed False: ring_out tool summary 'ring-out Bus 1: master -20.0 -> -13.0 dB (highest -13.0), 0 notch(es) ... ABORT: raise refused by policy: RATE_LIMITED', fake_fader -13.01 (-14.03 at p1), feedback_watch_stop -> 'Nothing to stop: CFS2 is idle', flood 247 ok / 984 RATE_LIMITED of 1231.
D_panic_with_bucket_pinned.json: slots -49.97, panic muted 24, failed [], elapsed 0.0 s, /main/st/mix/on False.
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed direction; concretely: (1) give CFS's lowering master writes an emergency lane — a Desk method (or set_level(..., priority=True)) that, for a strictly-lowering bus/main fader write, calls acquire_write(panic=True), skips the ramp, and retries 3x; (2) if that still fails (or raises anything), fall back to the existing _restore_master raw fire-and-forget + read-back loop targeting max(FADER_FLOOR, min(start_master_db, master_db - back)) and report restored/attempts as the connection-loss path does; (3) record the back-off outcome explicitly (e.g. report fields backoff_ok / backoff_error and append '; back-off FAILED, master left at X dB — lower Bus N now' to the summary) instead of `abort_reason or ...`, which today hides a failed back-off behind 'stopped by operator' / 'raise refused by policy'; (4) separately, stop an LLM flood from aborting a confirmed ring-out mid-raise at all: give CFS its own small limiter (or reserve a few slots/s of the shared bucket for CFS/priority writes) so RAISE/NOTCH/BACKOFF writes never compete with Tier-1 tool traffic, and consider capping concurrent in-flight Tier-1 tool writes in server.py (a semaphore) since the MCP dispatcher runs requests concurrently.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Code path (end to end): _finalize_levels (cfs.py:1280-1292) issues exactly one _write_master(final, force=True); _write_master (cfs.py:1115-1124) -> Desk.set_level -> _move_level -> _ramp/_run_ramp -> Desk._write (desk.py:871-877) -> policy.acquire_write() with panic=False; acquire_write (policy.py:375-395) raises RATE_LIMITED when (1 - bucket_tokens)/50 > max_wait_s=1.0, i.e. when >=50 reservations are already queued. The except (DeskError, PolicyError, X32ConnectionError) at cfs.py:1287-1291 logs, sets end_master_db = ses.master_db (the raised level), no retry, no fallback to the _restore_master raw loop (cfs.py:1324-1359, used only for connection loss), and _finish disarms. panic() (desk.py:1190) is the only write lane exempt. force=True does nothing for the limiter (_move_level ignores force entirely). The limiter is purely local (Policy), so a real X32 behaves identically to the FakeDesk here; nothing in docs/research or HANDOVER changes this chain. DESIGN §15 promises 'ABORT with backoff abort_backoff_db' and cfs.py:710 says 'nothing may leave the session armed and the master raised' — that guarantee is defeated when the condition holds.

Where the claim is overstated: the trigger is NOT 'an LLM loop issuing ~100 set_send calls in a second'. A sequential runaway loop is throttled by the limiter's reserve-and-sleep and never sees RATE_LIMITED (my test: 150 back-to-back set_fader writes during the run, 0 RATE_LIMITED, back-off landed at max-6). RATE_LIMITED needs >=50 acquirers simultaneously sleeping in the bucket at the instant the back-off acquires, which through the tool surface means ~100 write tool calls in flight within the ~1 s before BACKOFF (measured: 60 simultaneous distinct-address set_send calls -> bucket -10.9, back-off fine; 100 -> bucket -49.8, back-off refused) or >=~50 sustained parallel looping writers. Server is stdio-only (server.py:1949) = one client; the MCP SDK dispatcher spawns a task per request (mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py _spawn) so a client that pipelines tools/call can do it, but Claude Code's 10-way tool parallelism or any serial client cannot. The claimant's own evidence set policy._bucket_tokens = -10000 in the BACKOFF callback (redteam-cfs test_x3d), i.e. simulated the state rather than generating load. Hence: mechanism and outcome confirmed, reachable only from a massively-parallel client -> 'unusual but reachable' = medium stands (not high).

Two aggravating details the claim missed: (a) on the operator-stop and raise-refused paths abort_reason is already set, so `ses.abort_reason or f"back-off failed"` swallows the failure — the report says only 'stopped by operator' / 'raise refused by policy' and the sole trace is end_master_db == max_master_db (the claimant saw 'back-off failed' only because their run failed on the DONE path); (b) under sustained saturation no operator action is needed: the RAISE write is refused (cfs.py:1177-1179 -> abort) and the back-off ~immediately after hits the same saturated bucket, parking the master at the last raised level (measured: 60 parallel writers -> ABORT 'raise refused by policy: RATE_LIMITED', end = max = -14 dB, fader -14). Level is bounded by the 0 dB bus ceiling and panic still works, so not critical.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-cfs-8-src (commit 1038be5), tests/integration/test_verify_cfs8.py, test_verify_cfs8_timing.py, test_verify_cfs8_burst2.py — all drive srv.* tool functions (make_app on FakeDesk + SyntheticRta, setup_ringout_eqs, ring_out bus 1 from -20 toward -6 dB, dwell 200 ms; burst + srv.feedback_watch_stop fired from the RAISE>=-12 stage event). Results: distinct-address simultaneous set_send burst N=60 ramp0: write_slots at BACKOFF -10.9, end -18.0 (max-6, OK); N=100 ramp0: slots -49.8, policy.rate_limited fired for the back-off, final ABORT reason 'stopped by operator', end_master_db=-12.0==max_master_db, fake /bus/01/mix/fader -12.0; N=120, N=200 ramp0 and N=120 ramp300: identical parking at -12; N=100/200 spread over 1 s: OK (-18). Same-32-address burst N=120: only 64 writes (ramps on one address supersede), slots 41, OK. Sequential hammer 150 writes: rl_events=0, end -18 OK. Sustained parallel writers started at RAISE>=-14 with no stop: N=10 -> DONE end -9 normal; N=60 -> client 1359 ok / 2585 RATE_LIMITED, ring-out ABORT 'raise refused by policy: RATE_LIMITED: ... next slot is 1.02 s away', end=max=-14.0, fader -14.0, stages ...RAISE,BACKOFF,ABORT. 18 test runs passed. Source refs: cfs.py:1280-1292, 1115-1124, 1177-1179, 706-715, 951-967; desk.py:871-884, 944-975, 1190; policy.py:205-208, 375-395; device.yaml policy.writes_per_second 50; server.py:1949 (stdio only); .venv mcp/shared/jsonrpc_dispatcher.py:606,671-686 (per-request spawn).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the direction of the proposed fix, with specifics: (1) route every LOWERING CFS master write (BACKOFF, abort, unwind) through the emergency lane — Desk.set_level(..., priority=True) -> _write(..., panic=True) -> acquire_write(panic=True); a downward single-step fader write cannot be dangerous and must not compete with client traffic. (2) Retry it (e.g. 3x) and verify with a read-back (today after_db is the requested value, not a desk read — desk.py:967-971 — so on a real desk a dropped UDP packet is also undetected); if still not confirmed, fall back to the existing _restore_master raw send_raw+get loop targeting min(start_master_db, master_db - back) and report restored/attempts. (3) Record the back-off failure independently of abort_reason (e.g. backoff_ok=false / append to abort_reason) so the stop and raise-refused paths do not hide it. (4) Optionally give CFS its own limiter partition or serialize MCP write tools server-side (a semaphore around Tier-1 write tools) so one pipelining client cannot hold 50 reservations at once.

## D42. [medium] dwell_ms=0 (and step_db up to 6) accepted: ring_out becomes a +20 dB jump to the ceiling in <0.5 s with the detector unable to see a single persistence window
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1751-1752 (_int_arg(dwell_ms, 0, 60000); step only > 0), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:655-665 (step ≥ 0.1 and ≤ policy.relative_limit_db = 6 dB; dwell ≥ 0), cfs.py:1126-1133 (_dwell(0) returns immediately), device.yaml:729 relative_max_db 6, detector persistence_frames 3 × 50 ms

**Mechanism.** The safety story of ring_out is 'raise slowly, listen between steps'. Nothing enforces a dwell long enough for the detector to produce a detection (≥3 frames + write latency ≈ 200 ms) nor a step small enough for feedback onset to be caught before the next step. With dwell 0 the loop is RAISE→RAISE→… at limiter speed; with step 6 it is 4 writes for 20 dB. The Tier-2 summary does print 'in 6 dB steps (dwell 0 ms)', but a confused/jailbroken client supplies exactly these numbers and a user confirming by voice at FOH will not parse them.

**Exploit.** ring_out(bus=1, target_gain_db=0, step_db=6, dwell_ms=0, confirm_token=…) → /bus/01/mix/fader −14, −8, −2, 0 dB written within a few ms of each other (un-ramped), then −3; detector saw ≤ 12 frames for the whole run.

**Evidence.** test_x3f_dwell_zero_step_six_slams_20db_in_a_blink: observed fader writes [−14.0, −8.0, −2.0, 0.0] then −3.0, first→last raise < 0.5 s, report frames ≤ 12, final DONE at max 0.0. PASSED.

**Proposed fix.** Ring-out-specific bounds in both server.ring_out/ring_out_system stage validation and CfsManager.ring_out: dwell_ms ≥ max(500, (persistence_frames+2)×frame_period_ms + 100); step_db ≤ 2.0 (a `ringout.max_step_db` in device.yaml, independent of relative_max_db); reject otherwise (BAD_ARGUMENT).

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-cfs-9-repro (commit 96d0106, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs9.py, 5 tests, all pass) through the real MCP tool layer with the Tier-2 token dance on a FakeDesk (bus 1 GEQ provisioned, Ch1/Ch2 routed at -20 dB, master -20 dB, SyntheticRta 50 ms frames).

Validators: server.ring_out (src/x32mcp/server.py:1748-1751) only requires step_db > 0 and dwell_ms in 0..60000; CfsManager.ring_out (src/x32mcp/cfs.py:655-665) requires 0.1 <= step_db <= policy.relative_limit_db (=6, device.yaml:729) and dwell_ms >= 0. _dwell() (cfs.py:1126-1132) with 0 s returns after one loop tick; _write_master uses ramp_ms=0 single writes (cfs.py:1115-1124) and Desk.set_level applies no relative guard on absolute moves (desk.py:959-965), so the only pacing is the token bucket (50/s, bucket full).

Observed for ring_out(1, target_gain_db=0, step_db=6, dwell_ms=0, confirm_token=tok): /bus/01/mix/fader written -14.0, -8.0, -2.0, 0.0 at t=0.0/0.4/0.8/1.1 ms, then back-off -3.0 at 1.4 ms; whole confirmed call 0.32 s; report frames=1, detections=0, notches=[], final_stage DONE, end_master -3 dB (bus left +17 dB above where the operator had it). With a 2.4 kHz ring (20 dB/s) injected at the first RAISE (feedback onset), the run still finishes DONE at 0 dB with the ring never seen (1 frame < persistence_frames 3) and never notched — the ring is left growing on a bus 17 dB hotter. Control with step 1 dB / dwell 200 ms on the same ring: detected after 2 raises at -18 dB, HOLD/NOTCH, master never above -18, backed off to -24. So the claim's mechanism and numbers hold, and in fact it is worse than stated: not '<0.5 s' but ~1 ms for the full +20 dB, i.e. functionally one un-ramped +20 dB step, and the detector sees essentially nothing (1 frame, vs the claimed <=12). ring_out_system stage validation also accepts {step_db: 6, dwell_ms: 0} (server.py:1815-1820). Side observation: the server stage mints a Tier-2 token for step_db 6.01 or 20 (no upper bound at server.py:1749-1750); the confirmed call is then refused by CfsManager with BAD_ARGUMENT and nothing is written (verified: fader stayed -20) — wasted round trip, not a safety hole.

Severity: I keep MEDIUM, not higher. (a) The action is confirmed Tier-2 and the summary the user sees literally says 'in 6 dB steps (dwell 0 ms)'; (b) the level is bounded by the 0 dB bus/main ceiling — not unbounded; (c) crucially, the identical +20 dB un-ramped jump on the same bus with the same open mics is already available to the client UNCONFIRMED via Tier-1 set_fader('bus.1', 0, ramp_ms=0) (verified: single write 0.0, ok, no token), so ring_out(dwell 0, step 6) adds no loudness capability an attacker lacks. What it does defeat is ring_out's own documented procedure guarantee (DESIGN/cfs module doc and tool docstring: raise in steps, dwell, detector notches rings as they appear) in exactly the scenario engineered to provoke feedback (mics deliberately open and routed, operator told an automated guarded procedure will run) — a guarantee weakened under unusual but trivially reachable arguments = medium on the given scale. Not 'high' because none of the enumerated hard guarantees (cuts-only, -9 floor, -3 steps, strip, budget, show mode, token, rate limit) is broken and the desk is not left in an error state; not 'critical' because it is confirmed and ceiling-bounded.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-9-repro (branch wt/verify-cfs-9-repro, commit 96d0106), tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs9.py — 5 passed. JSON results in $SP/reports/verify-cfs-9-repro/.
exploit_step6_dwell0_ring.json: action_summary "Ring out Bus 1 'Bus01': raise its master from −20.0 dB to 0.0 dB in 6 dB steps (dwell 0 ms), cutting up to 6 notch(es) on GEQ FX5L; open mics: Ch 1 'Ch01', Ch 2 'Ch02'. Nobody may play or sing during the run."; fader_writes [-14.0,-8.0,-2.0,0.0,-3.0] at [0.0,0.4,0.8,1.1,1.4] ms; run_wall_s 0.32; frames 1; detections 0; notches []; final_stage DONE; max_master 0.0; end_master -3.0; ring injected 0.1 ms after first write, still present un-notched after run (rings_after band 69 2400 Hz).
exploit_step6_dwell0_quiet.json: identical write sequence/timing, DONE at 0.0, desk fader -2.98 after.
control_step1_dwell200_ring.json: same ring, step 1/dwell 200: fader_writes [-19.0,-18.0,-24.0], frames 98, detections 5, HOLD→NOTCH at -18 dB, notch band 22, master never above -18.
bounds.json: {step 6, dwell 0} → token minted; {step 6.01} and {step 20} → token minted at server stage, confirmed step-20 call → BAD_ARGUMENT 'step_db 20 exceeds the 6 dB single-call relative move limit', fader unchanged (-19.97); dwell -1 → BAD_ARGUMENT; system stage {step 6, dwell 0} → token minted; system {step 6.5} → BAD_ARGUMENT.
baseline_set_fader.json: srv.set_fader('bus.1', 0.0, ramp_ms=0) → ok True, requires_confirmation None, single write [0.0], desk fader -0.01 — same jump, Tier 1, no token.
Code refs: server.py:1748-1751 (step>0 only; dwell 0..60000), server.py:1815-1820 (system stage: dwell>=0, step<=relative_limit), cfs.py:655-665 (0.1<=step<=relative_limit_db=6, dwell>=0), cfs.py:1115-1124 (_write_master ramp_ms=0), cfs.py:1126-1132 (_dwell(0) immediate), cfs.py:1174-1197 (RAISE loop), desk.py:959-965 (no relative guard on absolute set_level), device.yaml:729 relative_max_db 6, device.yaml:801 persistence_frames 3, policy writes_per_second 50 (device.yaml:733).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed direction; make it concrete and enforce it in ONE place that every entry point passes through (CfsManager.ring_out, cfs.py:655-665), with the server stages (server.py:1748-1751 and 1815-1820) mirroring it only so the Tier-2 summary is never minted for arguments the manager will refuse: (1) add ringout.min_dwell_ms and ringout.max_step_db to device.yaml (e.g. 500 ms and 2 dB) and refuse BAD_ARGUMENT outside [min_dwell_ms, 60000] / [0.1, max_step_db]; derive a hard floor min_dwell_ms >= (detector.persistence_frames + 2) * frame_period_ms + 100 so a config edit cannot reopen the hole; decouple max_step_db from policy.relative_limit_db (that limit no longer applies to set_level at all, so tying step to it is meaningless). (2) Independently of arguments, ramp each raise (ramp_ms = policy.ramp_default_ms instead of 0 in _write_master for raising writes) so even a max step is spread over 300 ms of frames. (3) Optionally require at least persistence_frames new RTA frames (ses.frames delta) between consecutive RAISEs rather than wall-clock dwell alone, which also covers a stalled meter stream. Also add the missing upper bound on step_db in server.ring_out so 6.01/20 do not mint a token.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap:
1. Argument acceptance. server.ring_out (src/x32mcp/server.py:1749-1752) checks only `step > 0` and `_int_arg(dwell_ms, "dwell_ms", 0, 60000)` (server.py:395-401, lo=0). ring_out_system's per-stage validation (server.py:1806-1813) is `0 < step <= policy.relative_limit_db` and dwell 0..60000. CfsManager.ring_out (cfs.py:652-665) enforces 0.1 <= step <= relative_limit_db (device.yaml:729 relative_max_db 6; the show-mode 3 dB value never applies because ring_out is refused in show mode) and dwell >= 0. Nothing else bounds either value: Desk.set_level/_move_level no longer applies the relative clamp to absolute moves (desk.py:959-964), so the cfs comment 'else the first raise is refused by the relative clamp' is stale and the 6 dB cap in cfs is the only step bound.
2. Loop timing. _run_ringout (cfs.py:1147-1197) does pop_pending -> target check -> _write_master(nxt) -> RAISE -> _dwell(dwell_ms/1000). _write_master (cfs.py:1115-1124) calls set_level/set_main_level with ramp_ms=0 -> policy.ramp_steps(...,0) returns [to_db] (policy.py:439-440) -> one un-ramped write per step. _dwell(0) (cfs.py:1126-1133) is asyncio.wait_for(wake.wait(), timeout=0): one loop yield then TimeoutError, i.e. no listening window. Each raise costs one rate-limiter token; the bucket holds writes_per_second=50 (device.yaml:733, policy.py:375-396), so 4-15 raises never wait. The detector cannot emit before persistence_frames=3 (device.yaml:801) x 50 ms frames (>=150 ms; the prominence override needs 6 frames = 300 ms), and confidence needs a growth estimate over frames. When master >= target the loop breaks 'target reached' -> _finalize_levels BACKOFF -3 (cfs.py:1274-1291) -> _finish -> _disarm (cfs.py:969-979): the detector is torn down. There is no post-target listen. So any feedback provoked by the slam is never observed by CFS2 and the session ends DONE with the master left at ceiling-3 dB.
3. Confirmation. ring_out's Tier-2 summary does print 'in 6 dB steps (dwell 0 ms)' (server.py:1763-1770) and the token is bound to that payload, so single-bus reachability requires the human to approve those numbers. But ring_out_system's action_summary (server.py:1826-1832) is a fixed sentence ('each bus master is raised to its target (default 0 dB) with automatic GEQ notches, then backed off 3 dB') that never mentions per-stage step_db/dwell_ms/target/open mics; they only travel in the structured `stages` extra. A plan {"stages":[{"bus":"main","step_db":6,"dwell_ms":0}]} therefore yields a confirmation text indistinguishable from a default run, and it targets Main LR. This is an aggravation the claimant understated.
4. Limits on the claim: a start at -oo is a preflight blocker (provision.py:649), so the worst case is a finite low start (e.g. -80 dB -> 0 dB = 14 slammed steps), not -90. The move is ceiling-bounded (bus_fader_max_db/main_fader_max_db/master_ceiling_db = 0 dB, device.yaml:725-726,830), so it is not unbounded and it is a confirmed Tier-2 action; hence not critical. It does hollow out the documented state machine ('raise by step_db, dwell dwell_ms while detector runs', DESIGN.md:624-625; README:715 documents 0.1..6 dB steps but no dwell floor), under non-default but trivially reachable arguments -> medium is the right grade (arguably high for the ring_out_system/Main LR path where the human cannot see the parameters at all).
5. Real desk vs FakeDesk: identical logic; the only difference is per-raise latency. Each raise is one GET round trip (the previous write invalidated the cached node, desk.py:871-884 + _leaf desk.py:371-377) plus one fire-and-forget SET. HANDOVER §4a measured ~3 ms per awaited line on the wired studio LAN (restore: 3 sections in 9 ms), so 4 raises ~12-20 ms and 14 raises ~40-70 ms on hardware, still far inside one detector persistence window; the X32 applies fader SETs to DSP immediately (ramps were needed precisely because un-ramped writes jump, HANDOVER §4a). The FakeDesk deliberately does not couple bus level to ring growth (fakedesk.py:43-44), so it cannot show the slam causing feedback; on a real stage, jumping an un-rung wedge or Main LR with open SM58s from -20 (or -80) to unity in tens of ms is exactly the condition ring-out exists to approach slowly, and any resulting howl is left running at -3 dB with the detector already disarmed. The automatic ring_out has not yet been run on the real desk (M7 was feedback_watch), so nothing in HANDOVER contradicts this.
>
> *Evidence:* Own worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-9-src, commit 6141444, tests/integration/test_verify_cfs9.py (5 passed), driven through the MCP tools with the real token dance (make_app + srv.ring_out / srv.ring_out_system on the FakeDesk with LiveMeters):
- test_server_accepts_dwell0_step6_and_runs_blind: action_summary = "Ring out Bus 1 'Bus01': raise its master from -20.0 dB to 0.0 dB in 6 dB steps (dwell 0 ms), cutting up to 6 notch(es) on GEQ FX5L; open mics: Ch 1 'Ch01', Ch 2 'Ch02'. Nobody may play or sing during the run." Confirmed run: fader writes [-14.0, -8.0, -2.0, 0.0, -3.0], first->last raise span 0.0011 s, whole tool call 0.322 s, report frames=1, detections=0, notches=[], stages PREFLIGHT,SNAPSHOT,ARM,RAISE x4,BACKOFF,DONE; desk fader ends -2.98 dB; a 40 dB/s ring injected at the first RAISE event was never detected and CFS2 was idle (disarmed) afterwards with the ring still growing.
- test_server_dwell0_step6_from_minus_inf (start -80 dB; -oo itself is refused by preflight 'Bus 1 master is -oo - set a starting level first'): writes -74.1, -68.1, ... -2.1, 0.0 then -3.0 = +80 dB in 14 un-ramped writes spanning 0.004 s, frames=1, DONE at max 0.0.
- test_control_default_step_catches_the_same_ring (step 1 dB, dwell 300 ms, same ring injected at first RAISE): detected and notched (band 22, 2.5 kHz, -3 dB) with gain_before_feedback_db = -18.0, i.e. the dwell is what makes the detector useful.
- test_ring_out_system_main_hides_step_and_dwell_from_the_summary: plan {"stages":[{"bus":"main","step_db":6.0,"dwell_ms":0}]} -> token minted; action_summary = "System ring-out of 1 stage(s) in order: Main LR - each bus master is raised to its target (default 0 dB) with automatic GEQ notches, then backed off 3 dB; one confirmation covers the whole run. Nobody may play or sing during the run." (no 'dwell', no '6 dB'; parameters only in the `stages` extra). Confirmed run: /main/st/mix/fader writes [-14.0, -8.0, -2.0, 0.0, -3.0], raise span 0.00099 s, stage report DONE max 0.0 end -3.0 frames 1; Main LR left at -2.98 dB.
- test_server_step_above_6_and_negative_dwell_rejected: dwell_ms=-1 -> BAD_ARGUMENT at the server; step_db=6.5 single-bus mints a token (server only checks >0) and is refused BAD_ARGUMENT by CfsManager only after confirmation ('step_db 6.5 exceeds the 6 dB single-call relative move limit'); ring_out_system stage step 6.5 -> BAD_ARGUMENT, step 6.0/dwell 0 -> token minted.
Code refs: server.py:1749-1752, 1806-1813, 1826-1833, 395-401; cfs.py:652-665, 1115-1133, 1147-1198, 1254-1291, 969-979; policy.py:375-396, 430-452; desk.py:959-965; device.yaml:725-733, 801, 812-813, 825-830; provision.py:649; fakedesk.py:43-44; DESIGN.md:624-625; README.md:715-716; HANDOVER.md §4a (3 sections restored in 9 ms; ramps needed because un-ramped writes jump), §4b (only feedback_watch was run live).
>
> *Better fix:* Enforce ring-out-specific bounds at the choke point CfsManager.ring_out (cfs.py:652-665), which both tools and ring_out_system stages pass through, and mirror them in server.ring_out / ring_out_system stage validation so a bad value is refused BEFORE a token is minted (today step_db 6.5 mints a token and only fails after the human confirmed): add device.yaml ringout.max_step_db (e.g. 2.0, independent of policy.relative_max_db) and ringout.min_dwell_ms = max(500, (detector.override_persistence_frames + 2) * frame_period_ms + decay margin), reject outside [min_dwell_ms, 60000] / [0.1, max_step_db] with BAD_ARGUMENT. Additionally: (a) make ring_out_system's action_summary enumerate each stage's bus, target, step_db, dwell_ms, notch_budget and open mics exactly as ring_out's does, since that text is the only thing the human's consent is bound to; (b) add one final dwell/listen at the target before BACKOFF->DONE so the last raise is always observed by the detector before it is disarmed; (c) update the stale comment at cfs.py:660 (Desk.set_level no longer applies the relative clamp, so this check is the only step bound).

## D43. [medium] ring_out_system stage arguments reach CfsManager unvalidated: target_gain_db='nan' raises after _arm() outside its try/except and leaves a zombie armed session (BUSY forever, stop() hangs 30 s)
*Angle:* cfs · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1808-1822 (stage notch_budget/dwell/step validated, target_gain_db not; plan is dict[str, Any]), src/x32mcp/cfs.py:675 (`min(float(target_gain_db), ceiling)` → nan; also defeats the 'target ≤ master' refusal at 676 and 'target reached' at 1168 — only the 0 dB policy ceiling stops the raise), cfs.py:694-703 (try/except covers _snapshot_stage/_arm only; the log.info at 702-703 calls format_db(nan) → ScaleError AFTER self._ses was set at 917 and frames/consumer started), cfs.py:587-613 (stop(): non-watch path only sets wake and waits _STOP_WAIT_S=30 for a loop that is not running; never clears _ses), cfs.py:749 (ring_out_system catches CfsError/DeskError/PolicyError only)

**Mechanism.** float('nan') from the JSON string 'nan' propagates; ScaleError escapes ring_out between arming and the guarded _run_ringout block, so neither _unwind nor _finish runs: the RTA stays pointed at the bus, LiveMeters keeps renewing, the detector task keeps consuming, _require_idle raises BUSY for every later feedback_watch/ring_out, and each feedback_watch_stop blocks 30 s and returns without clearing. The safety feature is dead until the server restarts. (The single-bus ring_out tool happens to reject NaN earlier only because its summary string calls format_db.)

**Exploit.** ring_out_system(plan={'stages':[{'bus':1,'target_gain_db':'nan'}]}) → token → confirm → tool error 'NaN is not a valid dB value'; cfs_status → mode ringout; feedback_watch(1) → BUSY; feedback_watch_stop → 30 s hang, still BUSY.

**Evidence.** test_x6_ring_out_system_stage_target_nan_leaves_a_zombie_armed_session (through srv.* tools after make_app): observed confirm call ok False, srv.cfs_status mode 'ringout', srv.feedback_watch(1) error code BUSY, asyncio.wait_for(a.cfs.stop(), 2.0) times out, a.cfs.active still True (teardown needed manual _ses surgery; the earlier direct-cfs variant hung the full 30 s in close()). PASSED.

**Proposed fix.** Validate every stage field in server.ring_out_system exactly like the single-bus tool (target_gain_db via policy._num → finite float or None) and again in CfsManager.ring_out (_num on target/step/dwell). Widen the arming try/except in ring_out to cover everything from _open_session to the start of _run_ringout (on any BaseException: _disarm, self._ses=None, ses.done.set()), move the log line inside it, and make stop() able to finalize a RINGOUT session whose runner is gone (track the runner task; if None/done → _unwind).

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-cfs-10-repro (commit aa24021, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs10.py, 3 tests, all PASS against unmodified src). Code path verified by reading: server.py:1808-1822 validates stage notch_budget/dwell_ms/step_db but passes target_gain_db through untouched; cfs.py _system_stages (1399+) copies it verbatim; cfs.py:675 `min(float('nan'), ceiling)` returns nan (ceiling<nan is False), cfs.py:676 `nan <= master` is False so the 'already at/above target' refusal is skipped; _open_session + _snapshot_stage + _stage('ARM', target_db=_db1(nan)=nan) + _arm all succeed (_db1 uses round(), events.publish does not serialise), so _arm sets self._ses=ses (cfs.py:917), subscribes frames, starts the consumer task and frames. The arming try/except (694-700) ends there; the very next statement, log.info at 702-703, calls scales.format_db(nan) which raises ScaleError('NaN is not a valid dB value') (scales.py:270-279). ScaleError(ValueError) is not in ring_out_system's `except (CfsError, DeskError, PolicyError)` (cfs.py:749) so it escapes through `finally: self._system=None`; neither _run_ringout's BaseException handler nor _unwind/_finish ever runs. Result: _ses stays a RINGOUT session in stage ARM with done unset; _require_idle raises BUSY for feedback_watch/ring_out/ring_out_system; get_rta(other bus) BUSY; stop() (cfs.py:587-613) takes the non-watch branch: sets abort_reason+wake, waits _STOP_WAIT_S=30 s on ses.done (nobody will set it), logs a warning and returns {stopped: True} WITHOUT clearing _ses; abort() likewise only sets wake; _lose_connection for RINGOUT only spawns a restore task, so even a disconnect does not clear it. close() also sits 30 s. Only a process restart recovers. The single-bus ring_out tool is immune only by accident (server.py summary uses _db_from_float(target) → format_db raises before a token is minted) — confirmed by the control test. Severity: I agree with medium. No level change results (the state machine never ran: bus master verified unchanged on the FakeDesk, RINGOUT-mode consumer only queues detections in ses.pending and writes no GEQ cuts), panic is unaffected, and reaching it needs show mode off plus a human-confirmed Tier-2 token for a plan whose stages payload visibly says target_gain_db:'nan' (the action_summary text itself says 'raised to its target (default 0 dB)' and does not surface the NaN). But once triggered it silently kills the documented live-show safety feature (feedback_watch) and all ring-out tooling for the rest of the process, feedback_watch_stop lies ('stopped: True' after a 30 s hang while still armed), the RTA source is left pointed at the bus, LiveMeters keeps renewing, and shutdown hangs 30 s — a documented guarantee (stop/abort always disarms; 'nothing may leave the session armed') defeated under an unusual-but-reachable input. Not high: the desk itself is not left in a dangerous audio state.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-10-repro, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs10.py (FakeDesk + make_app + SyntheticRta, setup_ringout_eqs([1,2]) confirmed, ch1/ch2 → bus1 at -20, bus1 master -20). Observed output (-s): control `srv.ring_out(1, target_gain_db='nan')` → {'ok': False, 'error': {'code':'BAD_ARGUMENT','message':'NaN is not a valid dB value'}} with no token and cfs idle. Exploit: `srv.ring_out_system({'stages':[{'bus':1,'target_gain_db':'nan','dwell_ms':10}]})` → requires_confirmation with stages [{'bus':1,'target_gain_db':'nan','dwell_ms':10}] (token minted); confirm call → ok False, BAD_ARGUMENT 'NaN is not a valid dB value'. Then: leaked session 2026…-ringout-bus01 mode RINGOUT stage ARM target_db nan done False; a.cfs._system None; srv.cfs_status → mode 'ringout', stage ARM, rta_source 'bus.1', frames running True; consumer task alive and ses.frames still increasing; fake /-prefs/rta/source == BUS01; /bus/01/mix/fader unchanged (-20.0). srv.feedback_watch(1) and (2) → BUSY; srv.ring_out(1,-19) mints a token then confirm → BUSY 'CFS² is already ringout on Bus 1 (…)'; srv.get_rta('bus.2') → BUSY. Injected 2.4 kHz ring → 1 detection queued in ses.pending, 0 notches written (no runner). asyncio.wait_for(a.cfs.stop(), 3.0) → TimeoutError, _ses still the same session, cfs.active True. With _STOP_WAIT_S monkeypatched to 1.0: srv.feedback_watch_stop → ok True stopped True mode ringout after 1.0 s, session still armed, feedback_watch(1) still BUSY; a.cfs.abort('operator abort') → still armed/BUSY, cfs_status still 'ringout'. Un-patched run (test_real_stop_wait_is_30s): 'un-patched feedback_watch_stop returned after 30.0s: stopped=True still armed=True', feedback_watch(1) BUSY afterwards; PASSED in 31.3 s. Teardown required manual surgery (ses.done.set(); _ses=None) or a patched _STOP_WAIT_S, else close() blocks 30 s. All cited line numbers in the claim (server.py:1808-1822, cfs.py:675/676/694-703/917/587-613/749/1168) match HEAD a408a2a.
>
> *Better fix:* The claimant's fix is right; concretely: (1) server.ring_out_system stage loop: `if item.get('target_gain_db') is not None: item['target_gain_db'] = policy._num(...)` (finite float) mirroring step/dwell/budget, and include the per-stage target in action_summary so the human sees it. (2) CfsManager.ring_out: reject non-finite target/step/dwell up front (`math.isfinite`) — defence in depth for direct callers and the no-plan path. (3) Structural: extend the arming try/except to cover everything after _open_session up to entering the `_run_ringout` try (move the log.info inside the guarded region, and on failure call `await self._disarm()` as well as `_ses=None; done.set()` — today's except at 697-700 would also leak frames/consumer if _arm's own post-`_ses` steps raised). (4) Make stop()/abort() able to finalize a RINGOUT session with no live runner: keep a handle to the runner (or a `ses.running` flag set on entering _run_ringout); if absent/done, run `_unwind(ses)` directly instead of waiting 30 s, and never return stopped:True while `self._ses is ses`. (5) ring_out_system's per-stage except should treat any unexpected Exception as 'stage failed → ensure disarmed → stop the run' rather than letting it escape with state half-torn.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain read end to end, no gap found:
1. server.py:1802-1825 (ring_out_system, plan branch): a stage dict is copied and only notch_budget/dwell_ms/step_db/patch_file are validated; target_gain_db is passed through untouched (plan is dict[str, Any], so the MCP layer accepts the JSON string "nan"). _confirm mints a token for the NaN plan (payload is JSON-safe: it is a string) and the second call reaches a.cfs.ring_out_system(cfs_plan).
2. cfs.py:737-749: ring_out_system calls self.ring_out(bus, target_gain_db=st.get("target_gain_db") ...) inside `except (CfsError, DeskError, PolicyError)` only.
3. cfs.py:675: `target = min(float("nan"), ceiling)` -> nan (builtin min keeps the first arg because `ceiling < nan` is False). cfs.py:676 `nan <= master+1e-9` is False, so the 'already at/above target' refusal is skipped. _open_session does not look at target; _stage("ARM", target_db=_db1(nan)) -> _db1 returns round(nan,1)=nan without raising (cfs.py:167-179) and EventBus.publish swallows subscriber errors (events.py:47-52); _arm (cfs.py:901-929) sets self._ses = ses at 917, subscribes frames, creates the _consume task and starts the frame source. All of that is inside the try/except BaseException at 694-700 and succeeds.
4. cfs.py:702-703: the log.info AFTER the `async with self._lock` block and BEFORE the `try: await self._run_ringout(ses)` block eagerly evaluates format_db(ses.target_db); scales.py:270-279 format_db raises ScaleError("NaN is not a valid dB value") for NaN. Neither cleanup path (694-700 arming except; 706-716 _unwind) covers this line, so self._ses stays set, frames keep running, the consumer task lives, ses.done is never set.
5. ScaleError(ValueError) is not in ring_out_system's except tuple (749) -> propagates through `finally: self._system = None` (766) -> server _tool wrapper -> _error_dict maps ValueError to BAD_ARGUMENT (server.py:289). Result: mode property (cfs.py:539-544) reports RINGOUT; _require_idle (804-811) raises BUSY for every feedback_watch/ring_out/ring_out_system; stop() (587-613) takes the non-WATCH branch: sets abort_reason, wake.set(), waits _STOP_WAIT_S=30 s on ses.done that nobody will set, logs a warning and returns {"stopped": True} while _ses is still the zombie. abort() likewise only sets wake. close() waits 30 s then _disarm()s (shutdown only). Only a process restart recovers CFS².
FakeDesk vs real desk: the fault is entirely host-side Python (argument evaluation of a log call); the desk only has to let preflight and set_rta_source succeed, which is the normal working path on real hardware (HANDOVER §4a ring-outs run). A real X32 behaves identically.
Immediate audio impact is nil: the master is never raised (raise happens in _run_ringout, never entered), and in RINGOUT mode _consume (1016-1046) only appends detections to ses.pending (unbounded growth) and never writes GEQ. What is lost: the feedback suppressor is unavailable (BUSY) for the rest of the process lifetime, feedback_watch_stop lies (stopped:true) after a 30 s hang per call, RTA source stays hijacked, LiveMeters keeps renewing. Aggravation I found and executed: the zombie is a RINGOUT session, so the first connection.state=degraded event at any later time runs _on_connection_event -> _lose_connection -> _restore_master (1296-1350), which send_raw()s the STALE start_master_db (no policy, no rate limit, no confirmation) every 0.5 s for up to 10 s. If the operator has since pulled that bus down, a network blip slams it back up to the arming level (repro: operator -60 dB -> after simulated blip -19.97 dB, restored=True). That is a real-desk-identical latent unconfirmed level raise, but it needs the chain NaN plan + human Tier-2 confirmation of it + operator lowering the bus + a DEGRADED blip that recovers within 10 s.
Severity: keep MEDIUM. Entry condition (an LLM emitting the string 'nan' for a stage target AND a human confirming the plan) is unusual but reachable through the MCP schema; consequence is loss of the safety feature until restart plus a misleading stop() and a latent raw master restore, not an immediate loud change. Not high because no documented cut/step/floor/budget/token/rate guarantee is defeated on the normal path and the desk audio is untouched at the moment of failure; not low because recovery requires a restart and the latent restore write bypasses policy.
Claim details checked: line refs 1808-1822, 675/676, 694-703, 917, 587-613, 749, 1168 all match HEAD a408a2a; single-bus ring_out indeed rejects NaN only incidentally via _db_from_float(target) in its summary (server.py ring_out, before _confirm), confirmed in the repro (BAD_ARGUMENT, mode stays idle). Non-NaN garbage strings (e.g. 'loud') raise ValueError at 675 before _open_session, so no zombie; 'inf' collapses to the ceiling; '-inf' is refused at 676 — NaN is the only zombie-producing value. Proposed fix is right; minimally: validate target_gain_db to a finite float in server.ring_out_system and cfs.ring_out (math.isfinite after float()), move the log line inside the arming try (or drop format_db from it), and let stop() finalize a RINGOUT session with no live runner (track the runner task; if absent -> _unwind).
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-cfs-10-src (branch wt/verify-cfs-10-src, commit 55543fd), test tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_cfs10.py::test_stage_target_nan_leaves_zombie_session — PASSED in 4.3 s through srv.* tools after make_app with SyntheticRta on the FakeDesk (bus 1 set up via setup_ringout_eqs, mics routed, master -20 dB).
