Attempted refutations that failed: (a) 'plan=None semantically means all validating buses, so the user consented to that' — the action_summary explicitly enumerates 'N stage(s) in order: Bus 1 …', and the module contract says the token executes only what the summary described; (b) 'the extra bus would be skipped' — no, preflight passes under normal wedge conditions and the report shows it was rung out, not skipped; (c) 'needs a second confirmed MCP action' — no, variant B shows a single front-panel INSERT un-bypass (insert/on 0→1), or equally X32-Edit inserting a GEQ / a scene change at the desk, suffices with zero further MCP confirmations; (d) read cache (2 s TTL) cannot mask the change because the confirmation round trip is far longer.
Conditions: plan=None form only; within confirm_token_ttl_s=300 s (device.yaml:738) some bus's (or Main LR's) GEQ status flips to valid (GEQ/TEQ loaded+inserted+insert on+not shared) AND that bus passes preflight (mics routed, unmuted, finite master below 0 dB). Real desk: identical — the flaw is pure server-side token/plan logic; the reads involved (/bus/NN/insert on|sel, /fx/N/type) are VERIFIED addresses in docs/research/fx_routing_scenes.md §2/§3.4, and the 300 s TTL exists precisely because the operator keeps working the desk/X32-Edit during the conversational confirmation (device.yaml:734-737), which is exactly when inserts get toggled. Consequence is bounded (0 dB ceiling, 1 dB/1.5 s steps, feedback-detected, −3 dB park) but is a large net level rise on an output nobody confirmed (−20→0→−3 dB = +17 dB in the repro; Main LR to 0 dB if its GEQ is the one that becomes valid). Severity stays medium: documented token-binding/wrong-strip guarantee defeated, but only under a coincidental desk-state change inside the token window.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-8-src, commit 49981de, tests/integration/test_verify_f8.py — 3 passed in 3.9 s (FakeDesk + App + SyntheticRta, descriptor.ringout.dwell_ms=10):
- test_f8_variant_a_second_confirmed_setup_adds_a_bus: setup_ringout_eqs([1]) confirmed; bus1 master −2 dB, bus2 master −20 dB, ch1/2 send to both; ring_out_system() → pending, stages==[1], action_summary contains '1 stage(s) in order: Bus 1' and not 'Bus 2'; setup_ringout_eqs([2]) confirmed; ring_out_system(confirm_token=token) → ok, report stages printed: [(1, None, -2.0, 0.0, -3.0), (2, None, -20.0, 0.0, -3.0)] — bus 2 NOT skipped, start −20.0, max_master_db 0.0, end −3.0; fakedesk /bus/02/mix/fader == −3.0 (+17 dB net on an unlisted bus).
- test_f8_variant_b_front_panel_insert_unbypass_adds_a_bus: GEQs on [1,2], /bus/02/insert/on=0 at summary time → pending stages==[1]; fset /bus/02/insert/on=1 (one desk button, no MCP call); confirmed call → stages [(1,…,0.0,−3.0),(2, None, −20.0, 0.0, −3.0)], /bus/02/mix/fader == −3.0.
- test_f8_contrast_setup_ringout_eqs_pins_its_concrete_plan: token for setup_ringout_eqs([1,2]); fset /bus/03/insert/sel=FX5L; confirming call → ok:false BAD_TOKEN (the sibling tool fails closed, ring_out_system does not).
Code refs: server.py:1794-1801 (stages derived, not bound), :1827 payload={'plan': None}, :1833 stages only in envelope extra, :1836 cfs.ring_out_system(None); cfs.py:1399-1413 _system_stages re-derives with target_gain_db=None; cfs.py:668-669 target=ceiling 0 dB; policy.py:353-360 guard compares payload only; device.yaml:725,738,830.
>
> *Better fix:* Treat plan=None as sugar resolved in the FIRST call: build the explicit stage list [{bus: label, target_gain_db: None, step_db, dwell_ms, notch_budget}] from validate_ringout_eqs, mint the token over payload={'stages': that list} (same shape the explicit-plan form should also bind, minus 'patch' objects → bind patch_file string), and on the confirming call re-derive, compare, and raise BAD_TOKEN/STALE_PLAN if the validating set differs (fail closed, like setup_ringout_eqs), then execute cfs.ring_out_system({'stages': stored}) — never pass None to cfs from the server. Optionally drop the plan=None re-derivation inside CfsManager._system_stages for server callers (keep it only for direct/test use) so there is a single source of truth for what runs.

## D49. [medium] F6: tool path arguments escape X32MCP_HOME — get_ringout_report('../x' or '/abs/path') reads any *.json on the machine into the model context; export_patch_plan writes .yaml/.csv (mkdir -p) anywhere; patch_file args read any .yaml/.csv
*Angle:* llm-surface · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/cfs.py:302-315 (ReportStore.load: path = self.dir / f'{sid}.json' with no containment; absolute sid replaces dir), src/x32mcp/server.py:1858-1865 (get_ringout_report returns **rep — the whole foreign JSON — plus markdown), server.py:535-550 (_patch_path accepts absolute and '../' relative paths for read and write), src/x32mcp/patches.py:520-532 (write_patch_plan: parent.mkdir(parents=True)), server.py:1913-1923 (the x32://patches resource IS confined with Path(name).name — the tools are not), src/x32mcp/config.py:38-39 (home from env only; fine)

**Mechanism.** pathlib joins let '..' segments and absolute strings override report_dir/patch_dir. get_ringout_report merges the loaded dict into the tool envelope, so e.g. ~/.config/gcloud/application_default_credentials(.json), ~/.claude.json or any token cache ending in .json is returned verbatim to the model (and onward to transcripts/logs). export_patch_plan creates files/directories at arbitrary locations (content is desk channel data; overwriting an existing non-patch YAML is prevented because the merge-load fails first — verified). Not a hearing risk; it is credential exposure and filesystem reach from a tool surface that is supposed to be about a mixer.

**Exploit.** get_ringout_report(id='../../.config/gcloud/application_default_credentials') or get_ringout_report(id='/Users/jim/.claude') → ok:true with the file's keys (refresh_token, oauth tokens…) in the result; export_patch_plan('/Users/jim/Library/LaunchAgents/x.yaml') → file created; discover_mics(1, patch_file='/anywhere/thing.yaml') → parsed/echoed in errors.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_llm_surface.py::test_F6_report_id_and_patch_file_escape_the_home_dir PASSES: a credentials.json placed outside report_dir is returned by both the '../elsewhere/credentials' and the absolute-path form (rep['refresh_token']=='s3cr3t'); export_patch_plan wrote tmp/not-home/deep/x.csv and ../../escaped.yaml outside home; overwriting an existing compose.yml was refused BAD_PATCH (guard holds).

**Proposed fix.** Sanitise ids like ReportStore.save does (alnum/-_. only) and resolve()+is_relative_to(self.dir) before reading; in _patch_path reject absolute paths and any resolved path not under patch_dir/home (same rule as the resource); never splat a loaded report into the envelope without checking it has the report schema.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-llm-surface-9-repro (commit aea4fd9), test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_f6.py, 12/12 pass against unmodified src (HEAD a408a2a). Everything sandboxed in pytest tmp_path (home=tmp/home, foreign files in tmp/elsewhere*); no real credential file touched.

(1) get_ringout_report read-anywhere: src/x32mcp/cfs.py:302-305 ReportStore.load does `path = self.dir / f"{sid}.json"` with no sanitising/containment. Both `../../elsewhere/credentials` (relative from report_dir) and an absolute `/…/elsewhere/credentials` return ok:true and, because server.py:1865 does `_ok(..., **rep)`, the foreign dict's keys (type, client_id, refresh_token='s3cr3t') appear verbatim at top level of the tool envelope (tests f6a, f6b). Boundaries I measured: '.json' is always appended so only *.json files are reachable (f6d: secret.txt -> NOT_FOUND), and _read rejects non-dict JSON (f6c: a JSON list -> NOT_FOUND 'unreadable'). Most token caches (gcloud ADC, ~/.claude.json, etc.) are dict-shaped *.json, so the stated exploit is realistic. Contrast: ReportStore.save (cfs.py:253) DOES sanitise ids to alnum/-_., so the asymmetry is clearly unintended.

(2) export_patch_plan write-anywhere: server.py:535-550 _patch_path takes absolute paths as-is (`bases=[raw]`), joins '../' relative paths under patch_dir/home without resolve/containment, and calls expanduser() so '~/x.yaml' also works; patches.py:521-532 write_patch_plan does parent.mkdir(parents=True). Verified: absolute tmp/not-home/deep/nested/x.csv created with its missing parents (f6e); '../../escaped.yaml' landed at tmp_path/escaped.yaml, outside home (f6f); '~/tilde.yaml' written under $HOME (f6l, HOME redirected to tmp). Content is desk channel data (attacker controls channel names ≤12 chars only), suffix restricted to .yaml/.yml/.csv. Overwrite guard holds as the claimant said: an existing foreign compose.yml (f6g) and an empty settings.yaml (f6h) are refused BAD_PATCH before any write, contents intact — because export merges via load_patch_plan first and that raises.

(3) patch_file read of foreign .yaml/.csv: discover_mics(1, patch_file=abs) opens and parses the foreign file (f6i). Nuance vs. the claim's 'parsed/echoed in errors': content leakage is small — a mapping-shaped YAML yields only "missing 'channels' list" + the path (no keys, no values echoed); a bare-list YAML echoes unknown KEY NAMES and echoes VALUES only for known column names (channel/color/source/mic/monitor_bus), e.g. "channel must be a number 1..32, not 'tok_ABC123'" (f6k). So this leg is filesystem reach + existence/shape oracle, not a general content exfil.

(4) Control confirmed: the x32://patches/{name} resource (server.py:1917 Path(name).name) is confined — absolute name -> ResourceNotFoundError in patch_dir (f6j). The tools lack the same rule.

Severity: agree with medium. Not a hearing/show risk and no Tier-2/panic bypass; it is credential exposure into model context/transcripts plus arbitrary-location file/dir creation from a Tier-0 (no confirmation, no rate-limit relevance) tool surface reachable by a confused or injected LLM client. Does not fit 'high' on the given scale (no documented desk guarantee defeated), clearly above 'low' hygiene because a single unconfirmed Tier-0 call returns secrets.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-9-repro, commit aea4fd9, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_f6.py — `runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_f6.py -s` → 12 passed in 2.41s. Key output: RELATIVE: {"ok": true, "summary": "None on bus None: None", "type": "authorized_user", "client_id": "cid", "refresh_token": "s3cr3t", "session_id": "credentials", "path": ".../home/ringout_reports/../../elsewhere/credentials.json"}; ABSOLUTE: same with path .../elsewhere/credentials.json; LIST (json array) → NOT_FOUND 'unreadable'; secret.txt → NOT_FOUND; EXPORT ABS: "Exported 32 channel(s) to .../not-home/deep/nested/x.csv" (parents created); EXPORT REL: file .../home/patches/../../escaped.yaml resolves to tmp_path/escaped.yaml, not under home; TILDE: "Exported 32 channel(s) to .../fakehome/tilde.yaml"; OVERWRITE compose.yml → ok:false BAD_PATCH "missing 'channels' list", content unchanged; EMPTY settings.yaml → BAD_PATCH "top level must be a mapping…", unchanged; PATCH_FILE thing.yaml (api_key/password mapping) → BAD_PATCH, echoes api_key value: False, echoes key names: False; BARE-LIST secrets.yaml → "row 1: unknown field 'api_key' …; channel must be a number 1..32, not 'tok_ABC123'" (key name + known-column value echoed, 'hunter2' not echoed); RESOURCE: ResourceNotFoundError "no patch plan '/…/elsewhere5/x.yaml' in …/home/patches" (confined). Code refs: src/x32mcp/cfs.py:302-315 (load, no containment) vs cfs.py:252-253 (save sanitises); src/x32mcp/server.py:535-550 (_patch_path: absolute accepted, expanduser, no resolve/is_relative_to), server.py:1347-1349 (export path → write), server.py:1860-1865 (**rep splat), server.py:1913-1923 (resource confined via Path(name).name); src/x32mcp/patches.py:521-532 (mkdir parents=True). Result note: $SP/reports/verify-llm-surface-9-repro/result.json.
>
> *Better fix:* Claimant's fix is right; concretely: (a) ReportStore.load: apply the same alnum/-_. sanitiser save() uses, then `p=(self.dir/f"{sid}.json").resolve(); if not p.is_relative_to(self.dir.resolve()): raise CfsError("NOT_FOUND")`; also have get_ringout_report return the report under a `report=` key (or whitelist keys: session_id, mode, bus, notches, stages, detections, levels…) instead of `**rep`, so a stray dict can never surface arbitrary keys. (b) _patch_path: drop expanduser(), reject `raw.is_absolute()` and any candidate whose `.resolve()` is not relative_to patch_dir.resolve() (or home.resolve()) for both must_exist branches — mirror the resource's rule; keep the existing suffix whitelist. (c) write_patch_plan: only mkdir when the parent is under patch_dir (or drop parents=True). Add regression tests for '../', absolute, and '~' forms on get_ringout_report, export_patch_plan, apply_patch_plan and the patch_file= args of discover_mics/feedback_watch/ring_out/ring_out_system.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Causal chain read end to end and executed; it has no gap for the core sub-claim (get_ringout_report), holds with caveats for the other two.

1) get_ringout_report (the material part). server.py:1860-1865: `rep = a.reports.load(str(id)); return _ok(_report_summary(rep), markdown=ReportStore.markdown(rep), **rep)`. cfs.py:302-315 ReportStore.load: `sid = str(session_id).strip(); path = self.dir / f"{sid}.json"; if path.exists(): rep = self._read(path)` — no sanitising, no resolve()/is_relative_to. pathlib semantics: '../..' segments climb out of report_dir; an absolute right operand (`/Users/x/...` on macOS, `C:/Users/jim/...` on the author's Windows box) REPLACES self.dir. _read (cfs.py:269-278) returns any JSON object (dict) verbatim (+session_id/path). _ok has a positional-only `summary` (server.py:276-281) and pops a foreign 'ok'/'summary', so key collisions do not raise. markdown() (cfs.py:321-397) uses .get() with defaults throughout, so a foreign dict with no report keys renders without error; the only way it fails is if the foreign file happens to have a key named notches/stages/existing_cuts whose items are not dicts (then AttributeError -> INTERNAL envelope, nothing echoed — verified). The tool is Tier 0, takes no token, is not rate-limited, and does not even need a desk connection (a = _app() only; verified with connect=False), so FakeDesk-vs-real-desk is irrelevant: identical on real hardware. Constraint: only files whose name ends in '.json' and whose top level is an object are reachable ('.json' is always appended; a list/str top level -> NOT_FOUND). Realistic fixed-path targets that satisfy that: ~/.config/gcloud/application_default_credentials.json (refresh_token+client_secret), %APPDATA%/gcloud/... on Windows, ~/.claude/.credentials.json (Claude Code OAuth access/refresh tokens, plaintext on Windows/Linux — reachable as id='C:/Users/jim/.claude/.credentials'), ~/.docker/config.json auths, ~/.azure/*.json. Contrast proving this is an oversight, not design: the sibling SnapshotStore.load (nodes.py:865-884) resolves ids only against files enumerated in its own dir, ReportStore.save (cfs.py:253-254) sanitises ids to alnum/-_., and the x32://patches/{name} resource (server.py:1916, comment 'never leave patch_dir') strips to Path(name).name. DESIGN.md principle 8 ('Files live under X32MCP_HOME') is the stated intent the tool violates.

2) export_patch_plan write reach. server.py:535-550 _patch_path: `raw = Path(file).expanduser(); bases=[raw] if absolute else [patch_dir/raw, home/raw, raw]`; with must_exist=False returns bases[0] (+'.yaml' if no suffix) — absolute and '../' paths pass straight through; patches.write_patch_plan (patches.py:521-532) does `p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` then atomic write. Tier 0, no token. Verified: creates nested directories and a .yaml/.csv anywhere the process can write, including via '../../../x.csv' relative to patch_dir. Limits (all verified): suffix must be .yaml/.yml/.csv (a .plist/.json target -> BAD_PATCH, so the claimant's LaunchAgents example creates an inert .yaml that launchd never loads); an existing file is only overwritten if it already parses as a valid patch plan (merge-load at server.py:1348 raises PatchError first for compose.yml-style content, empty files, bare lists); written content is structured `channels: [{channel,name(<=12 chars, desk/LLM-controlled),color,source}]` — low integrity value. This part alone is low/hygiene.

3) patch_file read reach (discover_mics/feedback_watch/ring_out/apply_patch_plan). _patch_path accepts any absolute/traversing .yaml/.yml/.csv for reading. But the 'parsed/echoed in errors' disclosure is overstated: _rows_from_yaml (patches.py:420-436) on a secrets-style mapping raises 'missing channels list' with no content; only unknown field NAMES and specific invalid field values (color/source/mic/channel/monitor_bus) of list-of-mapping files are echoed in PatchError.problems; a top-level unknown key only yields a warning naming the key. Verified: api_key value and row 'owner' secret did not appear in the discover_mics envelope. Negligible.

Severity: not a hearing/show risk (no desk write is involved in the read path; the write path writes files, not OSC). Under the brief's rubric it does not map to critical/high. It is a real confidentiality boundary defeat on a Tier-0, unconfirmed, connection-independent tool that a jailbroken or prompt-injected client (injection vectors exist in this server's own outputs: scribble-strip/scene names read from the desk, patch-plan notes, other reports) can drive with one call to pull machine credentials into the model context/transcripts; realistic high-value .json targets exist at well-known paths on the author's documented Windows setup. Medium is the right overall grade, carried entirely by sub-claim 1; sub-claims 2 and 3 are low.
>
> *Evidence:* Executed in worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-llm-surface-9-src (branch wt/verify-llm-surface-9-src, commit 8f66744), file tests/integration/test_verify_f6.py — 3 passed:
- test_reportstore_load_escapes: ReportStore(home/ringout_reports).load('../../elsewhere/adc') and load('<abs>/elsewhere/adc') both return {'refresh_token':'s3cr3t',...}; load of a non-.json file (id_rsa) and of a top-level-list .json raise CfsError NOT_FOUND; ReportStore.markdown(foreign dict) renders without error.
- test_tool_get_ringout_report_returns_foreign_json (make_app connect=False — no desk at all): srv.get_ringout_report('../../outside-home/creds') -> {'ok': True, 'summary': 'innocent', 'markdown': '# CFS² ring-out — Bus None ...', 'refresh_token': 's3cr3t', 'client_id': 'abc.apps.googleusercontent.com', 'type': 'authorized_user', 'session_id': 'creds', 'path': '<tmp>/ringout_reports/../../outside-home/creds.json'}; absolute-path form identical; a foreign file containing 'notches': {'a':1} -> ok:false INTERNAL "AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'" with the token NOT echoed (boundary of the leak); srv.patch_resource('../../outside-home/p.yaml') raises ResourceNotFoundError (the resource IS confined).
- test_tool_patch_paths_escape: srv.export_patch_plan('<tmp>/not-home/deep/nested/x.yaml') -> ok, file and 3 new directories created outside home; export_patch_plan('../../../climbed.csv') -> ok, resolved path not under home; export to 'evil.plist' -> BAD_PATCH, nothing created; export onto existing 'services: {web: ...}' cfg.yaml -> BAD_PATCH, file untouched; discover_mics(1, patch_file='<abs>/secrets.yaml' with api_key + owner secrets) -> ok:true, 'api_key leaked: False row value leaked: False'.
Code refs: src/x32mcp/cfs.py:302-315 (load, unsanitised join), cfs.py:253-254 (save sanitises — asymmetry), cfs.py:269-278 (_read returns any dict), src/x32mcp/server.py:1860-1865 (**rep splat, Tier 0, no _desk()), server.py:276-281 (_ok positional-only summary, pops ok/summary), server.py:535-550 (_patch_path absolute/'..' passthrough, must_exist=False -> bases[0]), server.py:1347-1349 (merge-load guard), src/x32mcp/patches.py:527 (mkdir parents=True), patches.py:473-474/525-526 (suffix whitelist), patches.py:420-436 (yaml errors do not echo values), server.py:1913-1923 (resource confined via Path(name).name), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:865-884 (SnapshotStore.load confined by enumeration — the correct sibling pattern), docs/DESIGN.md:28 principle 8.
>
> *Better fix:* ReportStore.load: mirror SnapshotStore.load — sanitise the id with the same alnum/-_. filter save() uses (or reject any id containing '/', '\\', ':' or '..'), then resolve strictly inside self.dir: `path = (self.dir / f'{sid}.json').resolve(); if not path.is_relative_to(self.dir.resolve()): raise CfsError('NOT_FOUND')`, and fall back to prefix matching over self.list() only. In get_ringout_report, do not splat: return a whitelisted projection (session_id, mode, bus, bus_name, started, ended, notches, stages, detections, levels, final_stage, aborted...) or at least require rep.get('mode') in {'ringout','watch','system'} and isinstance(rep.get('notches'), list) before returning anything. _patch_path: for writes (must_exist=False) refuse absolute paths and require the resolved target to be under patch_dir (or home); for reads either apply the same rule or, if reading a user's own file elsewhere is a wanted feature, keep it read-only but still reject '..' segments and never mkdir outside home (move the parents=True mkdir behind an is_relative_to(home) check in write_patch_plan's caller). Add regression tests: traversal and absolute ids -> NOT_FOUND; export outside home -> BAD_ARGUMENT; resource and tool share one resolver.

## D50. [low] Node-text injection via names: restore lines render str fields unsanitised; Desk.label lets control characters/newlines through and a quote in a snapshot name re-frames the rest of the /ch/NN/config line (icon/colour/SOURCE)
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* medium · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/nodes.py:361-368 (format_token 'str': f'"{value}"' — no quote/control-char handling), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1154-1170 (label: only '"' replaced, '\n' etc. pass, 12-char cut), src/x32mcp/nodes.py:266-292 (tokenizer: quoted group runs to the next '"', across newlines), docs/research/transport.md §6.6/§9.2 (XslashSetString reads to the closing quote else to space/newline; embedded quotes UNCONFIRMED on a real desk)

**Mechanism.** restore_plan renders the snapshot's config/name straight into the '/' line. A name containing '"' — reachable from a hand-edited/crafted snapshot file, or a name typed on the console/X32-Edit and captured by snapshot_desk — closes the string early on the desk side, and the remainder of the NAME becomes the icon, colour and config/source tokens (source is Tier 2; index 49-64 = BUS01-16 would loop a bus back into a channel that feeds it). Through label_channel the quote is downgraded, but a newline is accepted and written to the desk and survives into snapshot files and restore lines ('\n/dca/1 ON 9' fits the 12-char limit); whether the real desk's '/' parser treats the embedded newline as a line break is UNCONFIRMED (the emulator keeps it inside the quotes).

**Exploit.** label_channel(1, name='\n/dca/1 ON 9') → ok; snapshot_desk → any later restore of ch.1 config sends '/ch/01/config "\n/dca/1 ON 9" 1 RD 1'. Crafted snapshot name 'Vox" 7 YE 50' → restore_snapshot(…, scope='ch.1') (+token) sends '/ch/01/config "Vox" 7 YE 50" 1 RD 1'.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_bypass.py::test_name_injection_into_restore_lines — INJECT-a shows the restore line with the raw newline; INJECT-b the crafted line; INJECT-c: after the confirmed restore the FakeDesk has name='Vox' icon=7 color='YE' (taken from inside the 'name'), source unchanged only because the fake cannot parse '50"' (a C atoi-style parser would read 50 = BUS02). Real-desk behaviour not testable here → partial.

**Proposed fix.** format_token('str'): replace '"' with "'" and strip \r\n\t/control chars before quoting; Desk.label: reject/strip control characters; restore_plan: refuse (list in failed) any section whose str field contains a quote or newline rather than sending it; parse side: log a warning when a /node name contains them.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (medium)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-8-repro (branch wt/verify-bypass-8-repro, commit b1ead1f), test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify8.py (8 cases, all pass against the unmodified product code, FakeDesk + App + server tools).

Every fact in the claim holds:
(1) nodes.py:361-368 format_token for node_fmt 'str' is f'"{value}"' with no handling of an embedded '"' or control characters; nodes.py:1201-1229 restore_plan renders snapshot values straight through it; desk.py:1312-1341 Desk.restore sends each line via conn.slash (connection.py:542-554 only .strip()s the ends, an embedded '\n' survives).
(2) desk.py:1154-1170 Desk.label replaces only '"' (→ apostrophe) and truncates to 12; '\n', '\r', '\t', DEL pass and are written to the desk (only '\x00' is rejected, and that by the OSC encoder as INTERNAL, not by validation). label_channel(1, name='\n/dca/1 ON 9') → ok; the fake stores the raw newline; snapshot_desk records it; a later scoped restore sends the single OSC string '/ch/01/config "\n/dca/1 ON 9" 1 RD 1'. On the FakeDesk (same tokenizer, quoted group spans the newline) it is inert: name restored, /dca/1 untouched. Whether real X32 firmware splits a '/' ,s payload on '\n' is UNCONFIRMED in docs/research/transport.md (§6.6/§9.2) and the emulator source is not available here, so the newline half is a hygiene finding with unknown real-desk effect — exactly as the claimant caveated.
(3) Crafted snapshot name 'Vox" 7 YE 50' → confirmed restore (scope ch.1) sends '/ch/01/config "Vox" 7 YE 50" 1 RD 1'; FakeDesk ends with name='Vox', icon=7, color=YE(3), source unchanged (token '50"' unparsable) — identical to the claim's INJECT-c. I went further: with a trailing space in the crafted name ('Vox" 7 YE 50 ') the line becomes '/ch/01/config "Vox" 7 YE 50 " 1 RD 1', the product/fake tokenizer yields [..., '50', '" 1 RD 1'] and the FakeDesk's /ch/01/config/source becomes 50 = BUS02 (config/source is node:int, Tier 2, guarded '/*/config/source'). The confirmation preview for that restore listed ONLY "Ch 1 'Ch01' name 'Vox\" 7 YE 50 ' → 'Ch01'" — a Tier-2 re-source of a channel onto a mix bus (digital feedback-loop material) happened under a token whose action_summary described a rename. '" 1 RD 49 ' (10 chars, fits the 12-char name limit) does the same → BUS01.
(4) No file access needed: a name containing '"' set on the desk itself (console keyboard / X32-Edit / any OSC app — outside policy.py's reach) breaks the READ side too. With ch.1 named '" 1 RD 49 ' the desk's /node reply is '/ch/01/config "" 1 RD 49 " 1 RD 1'; get_channel(1) reports name='' source='BUS01' (desk is really IN01), snapshot_desk stores source BUS01, and after the engineer renames the channel a confirmed restore_snapshot(scope='ch.1') writes '/ch/01/config "" 1 RD 49' → FakeDesk source=49 (BUS01), a source the desk never had. Here the preview does list "source BUS01 → IN01" (before=snapshot, after=live), so a human could catch it, but the snapshot — the product's undo — is silently corrupted on a Tier-2 field. 'A"B C' → get_channel source=None/icon=None/colour='1', restore line '/ch/01/config "A"B"'. Benign 'Tom 12"' round-trips on the fake only because both sides share the glued-quote heuristic; the real desk's XslashSetString 'reads to the closing quote' so '"Tom 12"" 1 RD 1' is malformed there (outcome UNCONFIRMED).

Severity: medium (agree). It weakens documented guarantees — restore writes exactly the snapshot's values; the Tier-2 preview describes what will be written; guarded config/source changes only when shown — under unusual but reachable conditions (a '"' followed by space-separated tokens inside a strip name, via a crafted snapshot file or a name typed outside MCP). Not high/critical: the LLM-only path (label_channel) cannot inject a quote (downgraded) and the newline it can inject is inert on the emulator model and unverified on hardware; the quote paths need either filesystem write access (with which one could put "config/source": "BUS02" in the file directly, though then the preview would show it) or a non-MCP actor; and every restore write still sits behind the Tier-2 token. The concrete demonstrated harm is preview/undo integrity for a Tier-2 field, plus mis-reads in get_channel/snapshot.
>
> *Evidence:* Test: <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-8-repro/tests/integration/test_redteam_verify8.py (8 passed); captured output in $SP/reports/verify-bypass-8-repro/output.txt. Key lines:
INJECT-a: label_channel(1,'\n/dca/1 ON 9') → {"ok":true,"applied":{"name":"\n/dca/1 ON 9"}}; fake stored '\n/dca/1 ON 9'; snapshot section {'config/name': '\n/dca/1 ON 9', ... 'config/source': 'IN01'}; restore slash line sent '/ch/01/config "\n/dca/1 ON 9" 1 RD 1' (written 1/1); after: name='\n/dca/1 ON 9', dca1 (on,fader) unchanged (1, 0.7498).
INJECT-b 'Vox" 7 YE 50': preview "...1 setting(s)...: - Ch 1 'Ch01' name 'Vox\" 7 YE 50' → 'Ch01'"; line '/ch/01/config "Vox" 7 YE 50" 1 RD 1'; fake after {'name':'Vox','icon':7,'color':3,'source':1}; tokens [...,'"Vox"','7','YE','50"','1','RD','1'].
INJECT-b 'Vox" 7 YE 50 ' (trailing space): preview names only the rename; line '/ch/01/config "Vox" 7 YE 50 " 1 RD 1'; fake after {'name':'Vox','icon':7,'color':3,'source':50} (BUS02); tokens [...,'50','" 1 RD 1'].
INJECT-b '" 1 RD 49 ': line '/ch/01/config "" 1 RD 49 " 1 RD 1'; fake after source 49 (BUS01).
INJECT-c: label_channel(2,'Vox" 7 YE 50') → applied 'Vox' 7 YE 50', quote_replaced true; '\r\nX', '\tTab', '\x7fdel' accepted and stored verbatim; '\x00nul' → ok:false.
INJECT-d (name typed on desk, no file access) '" 1 RD 49 ': desk node line '/ch/01/config "" 1 RD 49 " 1 RD 1'; get_channel → {'name':'','source':'BUS01'} (desk really IN01); snapshot section source 'BUS01'; after rename to 'Kick', restore preview "- name '' → 'Kick' / - source BUS01 → IN01", line '/ch/01/config "" 1 RD 49', fake after source 49. 'A"B C': get_channel source None, colour '1', icon None; restore line '/ch/01/config "A"B"'. 'Tom 12"': round-trips on the fake, line '/ch/01/config "Tom 12"" 1 RD 1'.
Code refs: src/x32mcp/nodes.py:361-368 (format_token str), :266-292 (tokenize: quote group to next '"', across '\n', glued tail), :1201-1229 (restore_plan), src/x32mcp/desk.py:1154-1170 (label: only '"' handled), :1312-1341 (restore → conn.slash), src/x32mcp/connection.py:542-554 (slash strips ends only), device.yaml:231 (config/source node:int tier 2), docs/research/transport.md:538 and :634 (XslashSetString reads to closing quote; embedded quotes UNCONFIRMED).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix, with two additions. (1) Write side: format_token('str') must never emit a token the desk cannot frame — map '"'→"'" and strip \r\n\t and other C0/DEL before quoting; Desk.label (and save_scene name/notes, any other str write) should reject control characters with BAD_ARGUMENT rather than pass them. (2) restore_plan/Desk.restore: refuse to render a section whose str field contains '"' or a control char (list it in failed with a reason) instead of sending a re-framed line — for /ch|auxin/NN/config specifically, consider always writing config/source as its own leaf (conn.set with the enum index after the tier check) so a name can never spill into the Tier-2 source column. (3) Read side (new): parse_node_line should flag a config line whose token count exceeds the node's field count or whose name token is followed by an unterminated quote group, mark the section as unparsable (→ DeskState.missing) rather than shifting columns, so get_channel/snapshot never report a fabricated source; log a WARNING naming the strip. (4) Bind the confirm token to a hash of the rendered restore lines (or of the snapshot file content), not just {id, scope}, so a file edited between preview and confirm, or a preview that under-describes the lines, cannot be confirmed.

> **Verifier 2: PARTIAL (low)** — Code facts all check out: nodes.py:361-369 format_token('str') emits f'"{value}"' with no quote/control handling; nodes.py:1201-1230 restore_plan renders snapshot values raw and desk.py:1312-1326 sends them via conn.slash (the only slash caller); desk.py:1154-1170 Desk.label only downgrades '"' and cuts to 12 chars (LF/CR/TAB pass; NUL is rejected by the OSC encoder); nodes.py:266-292 tokenizer's quoted group spans an embedded LF; nodes.py:864-890/596-611 SnapshotStore.load/DeskState.from_dict apply zero validation to file contents. But the two exploit chains diverge sharply in strength:

(1) Newline via label_channel (the only LLM-reachable path through MCP tools): reproduced end to end (label -> desk -> snapshot_desk -> restore line '/ch/01/config "\n/dca/1 ON 9" 1 RD 1' sent with a valid token). On the FakeDesk it is inert (DCA 1 untouched) — and the fake is not independent evidence either way because fakedesk.py:722-728 parses '/' text with the client's own parse_node_line. For a real desk the chain needs the '/' handler to split its single ,s argument on LF into two commands; the only parser model available (X32.c XslashSetString, transport.md §6.6) reads to the closing quote, DOC describes '/' as one node line per message, and nothing in docs/research or HANDOVER supports LF splitting. Causal gap -> this half is REFUTED as an exploit (unverified speculation; if it were true it would be critical, since '\n/dca/1 1 10' also fits 12 chars).

(2) Quote re-framing: on the desk side this chain is solid for a real X32, not just the fake — README/DOC real-desk captures show '/ ,s ch/01/config "" 1 YE 1' is parsed as quoted-name then positional icon/colour/source, so '/ch/01/config "Vox" 7 YE 50 " 1 RD 1' (trailing space in the crafted name) sets source=50=BUS02; I reproduced exactly that on the fake after a token-confirmed restore whose preview said only "name 'Vox\" 7 YE 50 ' → 'Ch01'" (the claimant's no-trailing-space variant leaves '50"', which the fake rejects and a C atoi would likely accept — moot given the trailing-space variant). HOWEVER the entry point is not reachable through the MCP tool surface: label_channel and apply_patch_sheet both go through Desk.label which replaces the quote (verified: 'Vox" 7 YE 50' -> "Vox' 7 YE 50", quote_replaced=true); save_scene names live under /-show which restore never renders; no tool writes snapshot files. It needs (a) write access to snapshots/*.json (outside the stated threat model; in the documented Claude Code deployment the LLM does have file tools, but then it can equally bypass every guardrail, and a fully jailbroken LLM can self-confirm set_channel_config(source='BUS02') anyway — the injection's only marginal value is deceiving a human who reads the restore preview), or (b) a human/other OSC client naming a strip with a quote (realistic: 'Kick 22"'), where the payload is not attacker-chosen and both how the real desk prints an embedded quote in /node output and how it re-parses it are UNCONFIRMED (transport.md §6.3/§9.2). My console-typed simulations show the client side is self-consistent for 'Tom 12"' and '12"Tom' (quote survives into the file and the restore line '/ch/05/config "Tom 12"" 1 MG 5'), while '12" Tom' is mis-captured (name='12', icon=None, color='1', source=None) so restore emits nothing for it — garbling, not a targeted source change.

Net: a genuine defense-in-depth hole (restore trusts str/token fields from disk and from the desk, and the Tier-2 preview under-describes a re-framed line), worth the cheap fix, but no demonstrated path by which the threat-model adversary (LLM confined to MCP tools) defeats a documented guarantee on a real desk. Downgraded from medium to low.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-8-src, commit 64cf1d2, tests/integration/test_verify_bypass8.py (7 passed): test_v8_newline_path — label_channel(1,'\n/dca/1 ON 9') ok, fakedesk name == payload, snapshot stores it, confirmed restore_snapshot(scope ch.1) sends the line, DCA1 (on,fader) before==after=(True,-0.01) i.e. inert on the fake. test_v8_crafted_snapshot_quote['Vox" 7 YE 50 '] — restore line '/ch/01/config "Vox" 7 YE 50 " 1 RD 1', preview text mentions only the name, fake after confirm: name='Vox' icon=7 color='YE' source='BUS02' (was IN01); variant without trailing space: source unchanged (fake cannot parse '50"'). test_v8_console_typed_quote — 'Tom 12"' and '12"Tom' round-trip with the quote into snapshot and restore line; '12" Tom' captured as {name:'12', icon:None, color:'1', source:None}, restore_plan -> [] . test_v8_label_strips_quote — quote replaced by apostrophe (quote_replaced=True); NUL rejected ('string contains NUL'). Source refs: nodes.py:369 (f'"{value}"'), nodes.py:279-284 (find closing quote across LF), nodes.py:1201-1230 restore_plan, nodes.py:596-611 from_dict no validation, desk.py:95-96/_NAME_BAD, desk.py:1154-1170 label, desk.py:1319-1326 restore->conn.slash, fakedesk.py:722-728 (_apply_node_text uses client parse_node_line — fake is not independent), server.py:1240-1262 restore_snapshot preview built from _diff field values, patches.py:585 (patch sheet -> desk.label), device.yaml:114 ch_source[50]=BUS02, device.yaml:291 fx par node: token (second unsanitised str-ish format), transport.md §6.6 (real-desk '/ ,s ch/01/config "" 1 YE 1' positional after closing quote; XslashSetString reads to closing quote), §6.3/§9.2 (embedded quotes UNCONFIRMED on a real desk).
>
> *Better fix:* Keep the claimant's fix but make restore the enforcement point since files and desk-typed names bypass Desk.label: (1) nodes.format_token 'str' and 'token': raise NodeParseError (not silently rewrite) on '"', '\r', '\n', '\t' or any C0 control char, and on str longer than the spec's max (12 for config/name); (2) restore_plan: catch that per section, skip the section and return it in a 'refused' list that Desk.restore/restore_snapshot surface in both the preview and the result, so the human sees 'ch.1 config NOT restorable: name contains a quote' instead of a rename; (3) Desk.label: strip/refuse control characters (name.isprintable()) in addition to the quote downgrade; (4) SnapshotStore._load_path: re-validate every section value through its ParamSpec (enum membership, numeric range, str charset/length) and reject the file on failure — snapshot JSON is runtime-loaded untrusted input in the Claude Code deployment; (5) parse_node_line: WARNING (not DEBUG) when a config line has extra/unreadable tokens after a quoted name, so a console-typed 'Kick 22"' is visible at capture time.

## D51. [low] CFS² arming writes /-prefs/rta/* through the raw connection (no rate-limiter slot, no tier check, no desk.write event) while get_rta uses the policy shim
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* low · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/cfs.py:911 (set_rta_source(self._conn, …)), src/x32mcp/meters.py:888-923 (conn.set source/pos/options/autogain/det), src/x32mcp/server.py:1460-1484 (_RtaPrefsWriter exists but is only used by get_rta)

**Mechanism.** The lead reviewer's observation, measured: feedback_watch/ring_out arm emits 4-5 desk-state writes that bypass Policy.acquire_write and the desk.write audit event; the addresses are tier 1 so no tier violation today, but a future guarded pref (or a descriptor typo in rta.*_param) would be written unchecked, and the dashboard/event log never shows them.

**Exploit.** feedback_watch(1) → events: write ×4 on /-prefs/rta/{source,pos,autogain,det}, desk.write ×0.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_bypass.py::test_cfs_arm_rta_prefs_bypass_rate_limiter — 'ARM: raw /-prefs/rta writes [/-prefs/rta/source, /-prefs/rta/pos, /-prefs/rta/autogain, /-prefs/rta/det] desk.write events []'.

**Proposed fix.** Move _RtaPrefsWriter into desk.py (Desk.rta_writer(target)) and have CfsManager._arm pass it to set_rta_source instead of self._conn.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (low)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-bypass-9-repro (commit b27e26e), test file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify9.py, 4/4 pass against the unmodified product code (FakeDesk + App + SyntheticRta, tools called via srv.*).

Code path is exactly as claimed: CfsManager.__init__ stores the raw X32Connection (cfs.py:504 `self._conn = getattr(desk,'conn',None) or desk._conn`); `_arm` calls `set_rta_source(self._conn, ...)` (cfs.py:911); meters.set_rta_source then does `await conn.set(...)` for source (meters.py:888), pos (:889), options if Solo-Priority bit set (:903), autogain (:915), det (:923). X32Connection.set (connection.py:520-530) has no policy hook — it just sends and publishes the raw 'write' event. The policy-respecting shim `_RtaPrefsWriter` (server.py:1460-1484: tier check → GUARDED, ensure_pre_write_snapshot, policy.acquire_write, desk.write event) is instantiated only at server.py:1510 inside get_rta.

Measured (test_A): feedback_watch(1) → raw 'write' events on /-prefs/rta/{source,pos,autogain,det} (4), desk.write events [] (0 total for the whole arm), Policy.acquire_write calls 0; fake desk ends with source=BUS01, autogain=False, det=PEAK, /-stat/rtasource=146. Contrast get_rta('bus.2') on the same app: same 4 raw writes, 4 desk.write events (tier 1, tool=get_rta), 4 acquire_write calls. test_B: the confirmed ring_out(1) run behaves identically (4 raw rta-pref writes, desk.write only for the two /bus/01/mix/fader moves, acquire_write=2). test_C (the 'future guarded pref' half): adding a guarded glob for /-prefs/rta/det to the app's descriptor makes policy.tier_for return 2; get_rta('bus.2') then returns GUARDED and det stays RMS, but feedback_watch(1) still writes det=PEAK raw — no tier check on the CFS path. test_D: with the token bucket drained to −500 (set_fader and get_rta both refused RATE_LIMITED, wait 10 s), feedback_watch still arms and emits all 4 pref writes with 0 acquire_write calls — the rate limiter is not consulted at all.

Severity stays LOW: the addresses are Tier-1 analyser prefs (device.yaml:558-567), the writes are a fixed 4–5 per arm (once per session / per ring_out_system stage), values are constants chosen for safety (autogain off, PEAK, solo-priority cleared), nothing audible changes, and the pre-write snapshot IS still taken in _arm (cfs.py:906) so undo is preserved. What is actually lost today is (a) the desk.write audit trail/dashboard visibility for these desk-state changes and (b) rate-limiter accounting for ≤5 writes; the tier bypass is only hypothetical (no rta pref is guarded). Incidental observation, not part of the claim: _RtaPrefsWriter itself is non-atomic — in test_C get_rta wrote source/pos/autogain (moving the RTA to bus.2) before refusing det with GUARDED, and set_rta_source has no rollback.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree: <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-9-repro (branch wt/verify-bypass-9-repro, commit b27e26e). Run: `$SP/runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify9.py -s` → 4 passed in 3.74 s. Output:
test_A: 'ARM (feedback_watch): raw /-prefs/rta writes [/-prefs/rta/source, /-prefs/rta/pos, /-prefs/rta/autogain, /-prefs/rta/det]' / 'desk.write events on /-prefs/rta []' / 'ALL desk.write events []' / 'Policy.acquire_write calls 0' / 'fake desk now: autogain=False det=PEAK source=BUS01 stat=146'; then 'get_rta(bus.2): raw /-prefs/rta writes [source,pos,autogain,det]' / 'desk.write events on /-prefs/rta [source,pos,autogain,det]' / 'Policy.acquire_write calls 4'.
test_B: 'ring_out: raw /-prefs/rta writes [source,pos,autogain,det]' / 'desk.write on /-prefs/rta []' / 'ALL desk.write [/bus/01/mix/fader, /bus/01/mix/fader]' / 'acquire_write calls 2, total raw writes 6'.
test_C: 'get_rta with guarded det -> {code: GUARDED, message: /-prefs/rta/det is a guarded (Tier 2) parameter} raw rta writes so far [source,pos,autogain]' then 'feedback_watch with guarded det -> ok=True; raw rta writes [source,pos,autogain,det]; desk det now PEAK'.
test_D: 'policy write with drained bucket -> RATE_LIMITED (next slot 10.02 s)', 'get_rta(bus.2) with drained bucket -> RATE_LIMITED', 'feedback_watch with drained bucket -> ok=True raw rta writes=[source,pos,autogain,det] acquires=0'.
Source refs verified: cfs.py:504 (raw conn captured), cfs.py:906 (snapshot still taken), cfs.py:911 (set_rta_source(self._conn,…)), meters.py:888/889/903/915/923 (conn.set), connection.py:520-530 (set publishes only 'write'), server.py:1460-1484 (_RtaPrefsWriter), server.py:1510 (only user of the shim).
>
> *Better fix:* The proposed fix is right in direction: move the shim out of server.py into desk.py as `Desk.rta_writer(target, tool)` (get → conn.get; set → tier_for check, ensure_pre_write_snapshot, policy.acquire_write, conn.set, publish desk.write with tool='feedback_watch'/'ring_out'/'get_rta'), have CfsManager._arm call `set_rta_source(self._desk.rta_writer(ses.target, tool=mode), …)` and get_rta use the same object, and drop CfsManager's direct `self._conn` use for prefs (keep it only for LiveMeters subscriptions and the deliberate _restore_master send_raw). Two refinements: (1) map PolicyError RATE_LIMITED during arm to a CfsError so the tool envelope stays clean, or acquire the 4–5 slots up front before the first pref write so arming is all-or-nothing; (2) make set_rta_source check tiers for every address it intends to write BEFORE the first conn.set (or restore source/pos on a later failure) — today _RtaPrefsWriter can refuse det/options after already moving the RTA source, leaving a half-applied pref set.

> **Verifier 2: PARTIAL (low)** — Mechanism holds, one stated consequence is false, and there is no safety/hearing consequence.

CONFIRMED part (causal chain end to end): server.feedback_watch (server.py:1659-1669) -> CfsManager.feedback_watch (cfs.py:566-578: lock, _require_idle, preflight, _open_session) -> _arm (cfs.py:900-916) -> set_rta_source(self._conn, ...) where self._conn is the raw X32Connection taken from desk._conn (cfs.py:504). set_rta_source (meters.py:860-941) calls conn.set() 2-5 times (source, pos always; options if bit5 set; autogain if !=0; det if !=1). X32Connection.set (connection.py:520-530) only checks writability, sends the UDP packet, invalidates its cache and publishes a `write` event: no Policy.tier_for, no Policy.acquire_write, no Desk.write_count, no `desk.write` event. get_rta(target) by contrast wraps the same recipe in _RtaPrefsWriter (server.py:1460-1484: tier check, ensure_pre_write_snapshot, acquire_write, desk.write). ring_out and ring_out_system arm through the same _arm, so they share the path. A real desk behaves identically: the sets are fire-and-forget UDP either way, the console does not echo our own /-prefs writes (desk.py:233-235 relies on the `write` event precisely for that), and per HANDOVER §4b the real X32 had auto-gain ON and detector RMS, so the first arm writes the same 4 prefs as the FakeDesk (fakedesk.py:159-160 defaults autogain True, det RMS); re-arms write 2.

REFUTED part: "the dashboard/event log never shows them" is wrong. connection.set publishes `write {address,args}` (connection.py:530); EventBus retains it (non-transient, events.py); DashboardServer mirrors every non-frame event to the browser (webui.py:449-452) and the page even styles `write` rows and prints the address (webui/index.html:321-323 `type === 'write' ? ' write'`). In addition _arm publishes cfs.state{rta_source, rta_verified} (cfs.py:926-927) and the tool result carries the rta dict (cfs.py:203-207, 580-584). What is missing is only the richer `desk.write` flavour (tier/tool/target) and the write_count increment. The claimant's own evidence line ("events: write x4") shows this.

No-consequence parts: (a) "no tier check" - all five addresses are Tier 1 today (device.yaml:558-566 rta/* tier:1; no guarded glob under /-prefs, device.yaml:708-721; Policy.tier_for returns 1 for each, verified), and the LLM client cannot influence which addresses are written (only a bus 1..16/'main' -> an int 50..65/72 into /-prefs/rta/source via rta_source_index). The "future guarded pref / descriptor typo" scenario is a trusted-config hypothetical (device.yaml also defines the tiers themselves), outside the confused/jailbroken-LLM threat model. (b) "no rate-limiter slot" - true but immaterial: /-prefs/rta/* are analyser/display prefs (meters.md §5.3 manual quotes: RTA source, pre/post tap, auto-gain, detector), not in the audio path, so nothing audible; the writes are 2-5 per arm, serialized behind CfsManager._lock + _require_idle + a multi-read preflight; a runaway feedback_watch/feedback_watch_stop loop on the in-memory FakeDesk reached 7 cycles in 2.2 s = 7.2 unmetered pref writes/s against a 50/s bucket (a real desk over UDP is slower still). (c) Snapshot-before-write is preserved: _arm calls desk.ensure_pre_write_snapshot() before set_rta_source (cfs.py:903-908) and /-prefs/rta is in the node sweep (device.yaml:704) so the snapshot covers the prefs about to change; the only divergence from Desk._write is that a dump failure is logged and arming proceeds, while the first GEQ notch still goes through Desk._write -> ensure_pre_write_snapshot and raises. (d) Show mode is not involved: feedback_watch is intentionally allowed in show mode, ring_out/ring_out_system check it before arming (cfs.py:652,727; server.py:1746,1792), and Desk._write itself never consults show mode. (e) DESIGN §12 specifies `set_rta_source(conn, d, target)` taking the connection (DESIGN.md:484) and desk.py:233-234 names meters.set_rta_source as an expected non-Desk writer, so this is the designed path, in mild tension with principle 7 (DESIGN.md:26); the later _RtaPrefsWriter for get_rta created the inconsistency.

Severity re-assessment: low (hygiene/consistency). No loud or unbounded level change, panic unaffected, no Tier-2 write, none of the documented guarantees (cuts-only, -9 dB, -3 dB steps, strip, budget, show mode, token, rate limit in any meaningful sense) is defeated, and the desk is not left in a dangerous state (prefs persist after stop - source BUS01, autogain off, det PEAK - which is deliberate per HANDOVER §4b and cosmetic for the operator's RTA screen).
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-9-src, commit c62f3a7, tests/integration/test_verify_bypass9.py (2 passed). test_verify_arm_rta_prefs_path output: TIERS all 1 for /-prefs/rta/{source,pos,options,autogain,det}; after feedback_watch(1): EVENT ORDER ['update','update','update','write','write','write','write','cfs.state']; RAW write events [('/-prefs/rta/source',[50]),('/-prefs/rta/pos',[1]),('/-prefs/rta/autogain',[0]),('/-prefs/rta/det',[1])]; desk.write events on /-prefs/rta: []; bucket 43.094 -> 50.0 (no slot taken, refilled during preflight); desk.write_count 7 -> 7; cfs.state event {'mode':'watch',...,'rta_source':'bus.1','rta_verified':True}; tool result rta {'source_index':50,'verified':True,...}; fake desk after arm source BUS01 pos POST autogain False det PEAK, unchanged after stop. Contrast get_rta('bus.2'): desk.write ['/-prefs/rta/source','/-prefs/rta/pos','/-prefs/rta/autogain'] AND raw write events for the same, bucket 50.0 -> 47.088. test_verify_runaway_arm_loop_write_volume: 7 arm/stop cycles in 2.22 s -> 16 raw /-prefs/rta writes (7.2/s) vs limiter 50/s, no other raw writes. Code refs: cfs.py:504 (self._conn = desk._conn), cfs.py:903-916 (_arm: ensure_pre_write_snapshot then set_rta_source(self._conn,...)), meters.py:883-923 (conn.set x2-5), connection.py:520-530 (set: _require_writable, send, invalidate, publish 'write'), server.py:1460-1484 (_RtaPrefsWriter used only at server.py:1510), device.yaml:558-566 (rta/* tier 1), 704 (/-prefs/rta in sweep), 708-721 (guarded globs, none under /-prefs), webui.py:449-452 + webui/index.html:321-323 (dashboard mirrors and styles 'write' events), desk.py:233-235 (Desk subscribes to 'write' to invalidate cache; comment names meters.set_rta_source), DESIGN.md:26 vs DESIGN.md:484.
>
> *Better fix:* Claimant's fix is fine as hygiene: lift _RtaPrefsWriter into desk.py (e.g. Desk.prefs_writer(tool, target) doing tier_for -> ensure_pre_write_snapshot -> policy.acquire_write -> conn.set -> write_count/desk.write) and pass it from CfsManager._arm (tool='feedback_watch'/'ring_out') as well as get_rta, so both paths are identical and DESIGN principle 7 holds literally. Two cautions: keep the hot notch path's latency budget untouched (arm is not latency-critical, so taking 2-5 limiter slots there is harmless, but arming can now raise RATE_LIMITED right after a burst such as restore_snapshot - map it to a CfsError and retry rather than leaving a half-armed session), and change DESIGN §12's `set_rta_source(conn, ...)` signature to document the writer protocol. Optional cosmetic follow-up: record the pre-arm source/autogain/det in RtaSourceResult and restore them in _finish so the operator's RTA screen is left as found.

## D52. [low] export_patch_plan ('Tier 0') writes to any absolute .yaml/.yml/.csv path and creates directories; the bare name 'device.yaml' resolves to <home>/device.yaml and is only saved by the merge step failing to parse it
*Angle:* bypass · *attacker severity:* low · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:535-550 (_patch_path: absolute paths taken as-is; relative names searched in patch_dir, then home), src/x32mcp/server.py:1339-1354 (export: merge = load_patch_plan(path) if it exists), src/x32mcp/patches.py:521-532 (write_patch_plan: mkdir(parents=True), tmp+replace)

**Mechanism.** A read-classified tool performs arbitrary file writes wherever the client points it (suffix-limited). export_patch_plan('device.yaml') targets the descriptor that defines the policy/tiers because _patch_path falls back to home; today the pre-write merge parse raises BAD_PATCH on it (accidental protection) — any existing file that happens to parse as a patch plan, or any new path, is overwritten/created (e.g. clobbering a hand-maintained patches file's non-merged fields, or dropping files outside the data dirs).

**Exploit.** export_patch_plan('/any/where/deep/x.csv') → created; export_patch_plan('device.yaml') → BAD_PATCH naming <home>/device.yaml.

**Evidence.** tests/integration/test_redteam_bypass.py::test_export_patch_plan_overwrites_the_descriptor — 'PATH-6: export_patch_plan('device.yaml') -> BAD_PATCH ; absolute path outside the data dirs written: …/not-a-data-dir/deep/x.csv' (descriptor still loads afterwards).

**Proposed fix.** Confine export (and _patch_path for writes) to settings.patch_dir: accept a basename only, reject absolute paths/'..', never fall back to home for must_exist=False, and refuse to overwrite a file that is not already a valid patch plan explicitly rather than by accident.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (low)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree $SP/worktrees/verify-bypass-10-repro (branch wt/verify-bypass-10-repro, commit ecde4ca), test tests/integration/test_verify_bypass10.py::test_bypass10_export_patch_plan_paths — 1 passed. All filesystem effects were confined to tmp_path (settings.home = tmp copy containing a copy of device.yaml; $HOME monkeypatched so '~' expands into tmp).

What the code does (src/x32mcp/server.py:535-550 _patch_path): Path(file).expanduser(); absolute -> taken as-is; relative -> tried under patch_dir, then settings.home, then cwd; must_exist=False falls through to bases[0] (+'.yaml' if no suffix). export_patch_plan (server.py:1339-1354) then does merge=load_patch_plan(path) if path.is_file(), and patches.write_patch_plan (patches.py:521-532) mkdir(parents=True) + tmp write + replace. No confinement to patch_dir, unlike the patch resource at server.py:1917 (Path(name).name  # never leave patch_dir) and unlike the README:523 contract ('T0 ... write to patches/<file>.yaml|.csv').

Observed:
(1) export_patch_plan('<tmp>/not-a-data-dir/deep/deeper/x.csv') -> ok, file created with three new parent dirs, outside patch_dir (32 rows).
(2) export_patch_plan('device.yaml') and export_patch_plan('device') -> both resolve to <home>/device.yaml (patch_dir has none, home does) and return BAD_PATCH "<home>/device.yaml: missing 'channels' list"; descriptor sha256 unchanged, Descriptor.load still works. So the descriptor is targeted and protected only by the merge-parse failing — exactly as claimed.
(3) '../escaped.yaml' -> written to <home>/escaped.yaml (patch_dir/.. escape).
(4) '~/sub/tilde.yml' -> written to $HOME/sub/tilde.yml (expanduser), dir created.
(5) Suffix limit holds: '/…/evil.txt' -> BAD_PATCH, nothing created (not even the parent dir); existing suffix-less file and existing non-plan mapping YAML ('foo: 1') and empty YAML -> BAD_PATCH, contents intact. But an existing YAML that happens to be a bare list of row-like mappings ('- {channel: 1, name: keep-me}') parses as a plan and IS overwritten.
(6) Existing valid plan by absolute path outside patch_dir (copy of patches/example_band.yaml) -> overwritten/expanded from 16 to 32 rows, all 16 hand-authored names replaced by desk state; owner/band kept (documented merge behaviour).

Severity stays LOW (hygiene): no audio path, no policy weakening at runtime (Descriptor is loaded once at App init, server.py:152; a clobbered device.yaml would only fail-closed at next start, and the clobber does not actually happen), written content is desk-derived YAML/CSV (12-char names, colour/source enums) not attacker-arbitrary bytes, and suffix is restricted to .yaml/.yml/.csv. It is a Tier-0-labelled tool doing unconfined file creation/overwrite (absolute, '..', '~'), and the descriptor's safety is accidental rather than designed.
>
> *Evidence:* Test: <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-10-repro/tests/integration/test_verify_bypass10.py (1 passed in 1.03s via $SP/runtests.sh). Results JSON: $SP/reports/verify-bypass-10-repro/results.json. Key output: abs_deep_csv {ok:true, exists:true, under_patch_dir:false}; device_yaml {ok:false, code:BAD_PATCH, path:<home>/device.yaml, summary:"export_patch_plan failed: <home>/device.yaml: missing 'channels' list", descriptor_unchanged:true}; device_bare {code:BAD_PATCH, path:<home>/device.yaml}; dotdot {ok:true, resolved:<home>/escaped.yaml}; tilde {ok:true, file:$HOME/sub/tilde.yml}; bad_suffix {code:BAD_PATCH, file_exists:false, parent_created:false}; existing_nosuffix/existing_other_yaml/existing_empty_yaml {BAD_PATCH, intact}; existing_list_yaml {ok:true, overwritten:true}; overwrite_valid_plan {ok:true, merged:true, rows 16->32, names_replaced:16, owner_kept:true, band_kept:true}. Code refs: src/x32mcp/server.py:535-550 (_patch_path: expanduser, absolute as-is, patch_dir->home->cwd fallback, must_exist=False -> bases[0]), server.py:1347-1349 (merge parse then export/write), src/x32mcp/patches.py:521-532 (write_patch_plan mkdir(parents=True)+tmp.replace), patches.py:418-433 (_rows_from_yaml: bare list accepted; mapping needs 'channels' -> why device.yaml is refused), server.py:1917 (patch_resource confines with Path(name).name — the export path does not), server.py:152 (Descriptor loaded once), README.md:523 (documented target patches/<file>).
>
> *Better fix:* Agree with the proposed fix; concretely: in export_patch_plan resolve writes with a dedicated helper that takes Path(file).name only (reject absolute, '~', any '/' or '..'), roots it at settings.patch_dir, forces suffix in {.yaml,.yml,.csv}, and checks resolved_path.is_relative_to(patch_dir.resolve()); keep _patch_path's home/cwd fallback for reads only (must_exist=True). Before overwriting an existing file, require that it already parses as a PatchPlan AND lives in patch_dir, and say so explicitly in the error rather than relying on the merge parse to fail. Optionally drop mkdir(parents=True) in write_patch_plan for the export path (patch_dir is already created at App start, server.py:176).

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (low)** — Causal chain read end to end and executed; every link holds.

1) Path resolution (src/x32mcp/server.py:535-550 `_patch_path`): `raw = Path(file).expanduser()`; absolute -> bases=[raw] taken as-is (also `~` expands); relative -> bases=[patch_dir/raw, home/raw, raw(CWD)], and `..` is not stripped. With must_exist=False the FIRST EXISTING candidate is still returned (loop at 541-545 runs before the must_exist branch), otherwise bases[0] (+'.yaml' if no suffix). So bare 'device.yaml': patches/device.yaml absent (default) -> home/device.yaml exists (config.py:40 device_yaml defaults to home/'device.yaml'; home = X32MCP_HOME or repo root) -> the live descriptor path is returned. Verified: resolves to <home>/device.yaml; if patches/device.yaml exists it wins instead.

2) Export (server.py:1341-1354): no tier/policy/show-mode/rate-limit check at all (pure Tier 0); `merge = load_patch_plan(path) if path.is_file()`; then patches.export_patch_plan (patches.py:633-680) reads /ch/NN/config/{name,color,source} from the desk and calls write_patch_plan (patches.py:521-532): suffix must be .yaml/.yml/.csv, then `p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`, tmp write, `tmp.replace(p)`. So any absolute or `..`-relative .yaml/.yml/.csv (or suffix-less -> .yaml) path the server process can write is created, with intermediate directories. Non-patch suffix (.json etc.) is refused before mkdir (patches.py:647-648) -> suffix limit holds.

3) 'Accidental protection' of the descriptor: device.yaml's top level is a mapping with keys meta/scales/enums/strips/params/nodes/guarded/policy/rta/geq/detector/ringout/mics and no `channels` -> _rows_from_yaml raises PatchError('missing channels list') (patches.py:430-431) -> _error_dict maps to BAD_PATCH (server.py:287-288) -> no write, no .tmp left, descriptor still loads. Same for the absolute path to device.yaml. The protection is genuinely incidental: nothing in export checks the target against settings.device_yaml or confines to patch_dir, while the sibling resource x32://patches/{name} (server.py:1917 `Path(name).name  # never leave patch_dir`) and README.md:523 ('Write ... to patches/<file>.yaml|.csv') show confinement was the documented intent.

4) Overwrite of existing files: rows are parsed strictly (unknown row field -> PatchError, patches.py:331-341), so a foreign YAML/CSV is overwritten only if it is already a valid patch plan; in that case unknown TOP-LEVEL keys (only warned, patches.py:434-436) and comments are silently dropped (tool response warnings=[] because the exported plan's warnings are fresh). mic/owner/monitor_bus/notes/band/venue are merged as documented.

Real desk vs FakeDesk: irrelevant here — the only desk interaction is GETs of /ch/NN/config/*, which a real X32 answers identically; the finding is host-filesystem behaviour of the server process. In-session audio safety is unaffected: the Descriptor/Policy are loaded once at App.__init__ (server.py:152), so even a hypothetical descriptor clobber would only break the NEXT start (Descriptor.load would raise; server would not come up) rather than loosen live policy. Content of dropped files is fixed-schema YAML/CSV (band/venue/channels rows; names <=12 chars from the desk, owner/notes only from a merged existing plan), so it is a location primitive, not a content-injection primitive. Severity stays LOW (hygiene): no level change, no guarantee in the safety tier list is defeated; it is a read-classified tool with an unconfined write side effect reachable by a confused/jailbroken client.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-bypass-10-src (commit f7fe9b5), test tests/integration/test_verify_bypass10.py::test_export_patch_plan_paths (isolated home = tmp copy of device.yaml; FakeDesk via srv.connect) — 1 passed. RESULTS (saved to $SP/reports/verify-bypass-10-src/results.txt): resolve_device_yaml -> <home>/device.yaml; export_patch_plan('device.yaml') -> ok=False BAD_PATCH, descriptor bytes unchanged, no device.yaml.tmp, Descriptor.load still OK; export_patch_plan(<abs home>/device.yaml) -> BAD_PATCH; export_patch_plan('<tmp>/not-a-data-dir/deep/deeper/x.csv') -> ok=True, file+dirs created; export_patch_plan('../../escaped/y.yaml') -> ok=True, written at <tmp>/escaped/y.yaml (outside home; reported file path keeps the unresolved '..'); export_patch_plan('<tmp>/elsewhere/noext') -> ok=True writes noext.yaml; '<tmp>/evil/x.json' -> BAD_PATCH, nothing written; existing foreign 'setting: 1' YAML -> BAD_PATCH, untouched; existing bare-list row with unknown field -> BAD_PATCH, untouched; existing valid plan with extra top-level key 'stagebox' + comment -> ok=True, overwritten, 'stagebox' and comment gone, owner merged, warnings=[]; with patches/device.yaml present the bare name resolves to patches/device.yaml instead. Code refs: server.py:535-550, 1341-1354, 287-288, 1917; patches.py:430-436, 468-483, 521-532, 633-680; config.py:39-40,63; README.md:523.
>
> *Better fix:* In export_patch_plan resolve writes with a dedicated helper: `name = Path(file).name`; reject if `name != file` stripped of an optional 'patches/' prefix, if absolute, or if any part is '..'; force suffix in {.yaml,.yml,.csv} (default .yaml); target = settings.patch_dir / name; additionally `if target.resolve() == settings.device_yaml.resolve(): raise BAD_ARGUMENT`. Keep write_patch_plan's mkdir only for patch_dir itself (ensure_dirs already does it) — drop `parents=True`. For the overwrite case, refuse (BAD_PATCH with an explicit 'not a patch plan, will not overwrite' message) rather than relying on the merge parse, and carry the merged plan's 'ignoring unknown top-level key' warnings into the tool response so silent field loss is visible. Optionally apply the same basename-only rule to _patch_path(must_exist=True) (apply_patch_plan / ringout patch_file) and drop the home and CWD fallbacks, so the read side cannot be pointed at arbitrary files either (its strict-parser error messages echo offending values).

## D53. [low] recall_scene / save_scene tokens bind the slot index only: the scene named in the summary can be replaced (or an 'empty' slot filled) before the redeem and the token acts on the new content
*Angle:* tokens · *attacker severity:* low · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/server.py:1135-1140 (recall payload = {'index'}; name only in summary), server.py:1163-1168 (save payload = {'index','name','notes'}; 'slot is empty'/'OVERWRITES' only in summary)

**Mechanism.** The summary is computed from the scene list at call 1 ('Recall scene 9 'Soundcheck''; 'Save … (slot is empty)'). Within the 300 s TTL the slot can change — a separately confirmed save_scene into the same slot, or the operator saving from the front panel. The redeem recalls whatever is in the slot now (a full-desk state replacement the user did not see named) or silently overwrites a scene the summary promised was empty. Requires a slot change in the window, hence low; a wrong whole-desk recall mid-show is nonetheless loud.

**Exploit.** recall: save_scene(9,'Soundcheck') [confirmed]; recall_scene(9) → T ('Recall scene 9 'Soundcheck''); save_scene(9,'FULL BLAST walk-in') [confirmed]; recall_scene(9, confirm_token=T) → ok, name 'FULL BLAST walk-in'. save: save_scene(11,'mine') → T ('slot is empty'); slot 11 filled at the desk; save_scene(11,'mine',confirm_token=T) → ok, operator's scene overwritten.

**Evidence.** test_recall_scene_token_binds_the_slot_index_not_the_scene (line 214) and test_save_scene_token_does_not_bind_slot_occupancy (line 359) PASS (res['name']=='FULL BLAST walk-in'; fakedesk.scenes[11]['name']=='mine').

**Proposed fix.** Include the slot's current name and has_data (recall: also the currently loaded scene index) in the payload so a changed slot gives BAD_TOKEN and a re-summarise.

> **Verifier 1: CONFIRMED (low)** — Reproduced from scratch in worktree verify-tokens-6-repro (commit 89f3e34, tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens_6.py, 4 passed). server.py:1135 builds recall payload = {'index': int(slot['index'])}; the slot name / has_data and the currently-loaded scene go only into the summary string and the `scene=`/`current=` extras (server.py:1136-1140), which _confirm (server.py:331-344) does not pass to policy.guard. save_scene payload = {'index','name','notes'} (server.py:1163); the slot's occupancy/existing name is summary-only (server.py:1164-1168). policy.guard (policy.py:348-359) compares action + payload only, so any change to the slot's CONTENT inside the 300 s TTL (device.yaml:738) is invisible to the token check. Executed results: (1) save 9 'Soundcheck' [confirmed]; recall_scene(9) -> token T, summary "Recall scene 9 'Soundcheck'", stored payload {'index': 9}; save 9 'FULL BLAST walk-in' [confirmed]; recall_scene(9, T) -> ok, "Recalled scene 9 'FULL BLAST walk-in' (verified; previous 0)". (2) Same with the slot changed purely at the fake desk's front panel (slot 1 renamed/re-stored to 'Walk-in LOUD'): redeem ok, recalls 'Walk-in LOUD'. (3) save_scene(11,'mine') -> T with "(slot is empty)", payload {'index':11,'name':'mine','notes':''}; operator fills slot 11 at the desk ('Operator monitor mix', visible via list_scenes has_data=True); save_scene(11,'mine',T) -> ok, fakedesk.scenes[11]['name']=='mine' (operator scene silently overwritten, no OVERWRITES warning ever shown). Control test confirms the binding that does exist works (different index or different save name -> BAD_TOKEN). Severity kept at low: the documented guarantee (token bound to action+payload, single-use, TTL) is intact — the payload honestly is the request's arguments; what is stale is state-dependent summary text, a TOCTOU shared by every Tier-2 tool (e.g. set_main_fader's 'from X' is also extra-only). Exploiting it needs the slot to change within the 300 s window either via a second, separately user-confirmed save_scene (whose own summary disclosed the overwrite) or an out-of-band front-panel action; the LLM alone cannot trigger it without an extra confirmation. Impact if it does line up is a whole-desk recall of content the user did not see named (potentially loud) or loss of an operator's scene, so it is worth the cheap fix but not a guarantee defeat.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-6-repro, commit 89f3e34, file tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens_6.py — run: runtests.sh $WT tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens_6.py -s -> "4 passed in 2.40s". Printed: recall token payload: {'index': 9}; redeem result: ok=True summary="Recalled scene 9 'FULL BLAST walk-in' (verified; previous 0)" name='FULL BLAST walk-in'. Front-panel variant: ok=True "Recalled scene 1 'Walk-in LOUD'". save token payload: {'index': 11, 'name': 'mine', 'notes': ''}; after operator filled slot 11, redeem ok=True "Saved scene 11 'mine'", fakedesk.scenes[11]['name']=='mine'. Control: recall_scene(0, token-for-1) -> BAD_TOKEN; save_scene(12,'b', token-for-'a') -> BAD_TOKEN. Code refs: src/x32mcp/server.py:1135 (payload={'index'}), :1136-1140 (name/current only in summary+extras), :1163-1168 (save payload lacks has_data/existing name), :331-344 (_confirm passes only payload to guard), src/x32mcp/policy.py:348-359 (guard compares action+payload), device.yaml:738 (confirm_token_ttl_s: 300).
>
> *Better fix:* Proposed fix is right and cheap: recall_scene payload should include the slot's current name + has_data and the currently loaded index (e.g. {'index', 'name', 'loaded': cur['index']}); save_scene payload should include the slot's existing has_data and existing name ({'index','name','notes','slot_has_data','slot_name'}). Both are re-read on call 2 already (_resolve_scene / list_scenes run before _confirm), so a changed slot naturally yields BAD_TOKEN and a fresh summary with no extra desk traffic. More generally, consider a convention that any state quoted in a Tier-2 action_summary (e.g. set_main_fader's 'from' level, set_main_mute's 'currently') is folded into the bound payload rather than passed as **extra, so the summary the user approved is always the state acted on.

> **Verifier 2: CONFIRMED (low)** — Causal chain, read end to end, has no gap:
1. server.py:1131-1140 recall_scene: slot = _resolve_scene(desk, scene); payload = {"index": int(slot["index"])}; the slot name and the currently-loaded scene appear ONLY in `summary`. server.py:1162-1168 save_scene: payload = {"index","name","notes"}; slot occupancy ('slot is empty' / 'OVERWRITES <name>') appears ONLY in `summary`.
2. _confirm (server.py:331-345) -> policy.guard (policy.py:347-360): with a token it calls consume_token (checks existence, TTL, action) and then only `stored != dict(payload)`. The summary recomputed at call 2 is never compared with the stored summary, so anything that lives only in the summary is unbound. TTL is 300 s (device.yaml:738).
3. At redeem, recall_scene re-reads the slot (list_scenes, 2 s node cache) but only uses it to reject an EMPTY slot (server.py:1132-1133) and to rebuild a summary that is discarded; desk.recall_scene(idx) (desk.py:1251-1288) fires `/-action/goscene ,i idx` guarded only by show-mode + tier/rate limiter. save_scene at redeem likewise ignores the re-read has_data and sends `/save ,siss scene idx name notes` (desk.py:1290-1311), which overwrites.
4. Real desk vs FakeDesk: identical. fx_routing_scenes.md §6.4: `/-action/goscene ,i N` 'is equivalent to highlighting + GO on the desk' and loads whatever slot N holds; `/save scene` overwrites the slot. Nothing desk-side ties the recall to a name. So on a real X32 a slot replaced within the window (front-panel Store, X32-Edit, another OSC client, or a second confirmed MCP save) is recalled/overwritten on redeem exactly as on the fake.
Nuances that bound the severity (why it stays LOW, arguably hygiene against the LLM threat model):
- A confused/jailbroken LLM cannot manufacture dangerous 'new content' by itself: the only MCP path that changes a slot is save_scene, which needs its own user-confirmed token (and its summary says OVERWRITES 'Soundcheck', so the user is told) and stores the desk's CURRENT state — recalling it afterwards is acoustically a near no-op (demonstrated: main fader unchanged). The loud variant needs a human at the desk/X32-Edit to store a hot scene into exactly that slot inside the 300 s window, then the user to approve the stale token — a TOCTOU with a cooperating human, not an LLM exploit.
- 'Mid-show' is mitigated: show mode refuses scene_recall/scene_save at redeem before the guard (server.py:1130/1157, _confirm:335, desk.py:1254/1292) — verified the stale token gets SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS.
- The by-name recall path is incidentally protected: call 2 re-resolves the name, so a replaced/renamed slot yields UNKNOWN_SCENE or a different index -> BAD_TOKEN; only the by-index form is exposed (but the same token minted by name still redeems if call 2 passes the index).
- An emptied slot is caught before the guard (BAD_ARGUMENT), so recall can never target an empty slot via a stale token.
- The server's own contract (server.py:31-34: 'the token payload is the normalised request (e.g. the resolved scene index) … so a token can only execute what its action_summary described') is what is technically violated for the name/occupancy parts of the summary; DESIGN.md §10 never specified content binding, and the X32 exposes no scene content hash/mtime, so even the proposed fix binds only the label + hasdata (an operator re-storing different content under the same name in the window remains undetectable).
Claimant's evidence caveat: their save test only mutated fakedesk.scenes[11] without setting the /-show/showfile/scene/011/hasdata leaf, so at call 2 the Desk still read the slot as empty; my repro sets name+hasdata like a real Store does (list_scenes shows 'Operator show', a fresh call 1 says OVERWRITES) and the stale token still overwrites — the conclusion stands.
>
> *Evidence:* Worktree <review-worktrees>/verify-tokens-6-src (branch wt/verify-tokens-6-src, commit c4459a0), tests/integration/test_redteam_verify_tokens6.py — 6 passed in 3.7 s:
(1) test_recall_token_redeems_after_operator_replaced_the_slot_and_it_is_loud: save_scene(9,'Soundcheck') confirmed with main −20/ch1 −30; recall_scene(9) -> token T (summary "Recall scene 9 'Soundcheck'"); operator stores a +10/+10 state into slot 9 as 'FULL BLAST walk-in' (fakedesk.scenes[9] + /-show/showfile/scene/009/name|hasdata set), pulls desk back to −20/−30; desk.invalidate(); list_scenes shows slot 9 = 'FULL BLAST walk-in'; recall_scene(9, confirm_token=T) -> ok, res.name=='FULL BLAST walk-in', fakedesk main/st fader == +10.0 and ch01 == +10.0 (whole-desk replacement the user never saw named; +30 dB on main).
(2) test_recall_token_without_cache_invalidation_also_redeems: same without invalidate -> ok, main jumps −40 -> +10.
(3) test_recall_by_name_is_incidentally_protected: token minted via recall_scene('Soundcheck'); after replacement recall_scene('Soundcheck', T) -> UNKNOWN_SCENE (token untouched), but recall_scene(9, T) -> ok 'FULL BLAST walk-in'.
(4) test_mcp_only_path_content_is_just_current_desk_state: claimant's MCP-only sequence — second save needs its own confirmed token (summary OVERWRITES 'Soundcheck'), stale recall token redeems (name 'FULL BLAST walk-in') but main fader stays −20.0: not loud by itself.
(5) test_save_token_redeems_over_a_slot_filled_in_the_window: save_scene(11,'mine') -> T ('slot is empty'); operator Store into 11 ('Operator show', hasdata=1); a fresh call 1 now says "OVERWRITES the existing scene 'Operator show'"; save_scene(11,'mine',confirm_token=T) -> ok, fakedesk.scenes[11].name=='mine' (operator scene silently overwritten).
(6) test_show_mode_still_refuses_the_stale_token: show_mode(True) then recall_scene(9, T) -> SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS.
Code refs: server.py:1135 payload={'index'}; server.py:1164 payload={'index','name','notes'}; policy.py:353-360 guard compares payload only; policy.py:329-345 consume_token checks TTL/action only; device.yaml:738 confirm_token_ttl_s: 300; desk.py:1262 /-action/goscene write; desk.py:1298 /save request; fx_routing_scenes.md:553 goscene semantics (real desk loads whatever the slot holds, honours safes).
>
> *Better fix:* Bind what the summary promised: recall payload {"index", "name": slot["name"], "has_data": True} (optionally "current": cur["index"] if you want a changed loaded-scene to force a re-summarise — stricter, may annoy); save payload {"index","name","notes","slot_name": slot["name"], "slot_has_data": slot["has_data"]}. Before recomputing at redeem, drop the cached /-show/showfile/scene/NNN section (desk._drop) so a front-panel Store older than the call but younger than the 2 s cache is seen. Accept the residual: the X32 exposes no scene content hash/mtime, so a re-store under the same name inside the TTL is undetectable; a shorter TTL for scene_recall/scene_save (e.g. 60 s) narrows that window. A generic alternative is to have guard() also compare the stored summary string with the recomputed one, which closes this whole class (anything shown but not in the payload) for every Tier-2 tool at once.

## D54. [low] Latent: require_confirmation(action=None) mints a wildcard token and Policy.guard payload equality is Python == (True == 1 == 1.0); show-mode refusal leaves tokens redeemable after show_mode(false)
*Angle:* tokens · *attacker severity:* low · *reproduced by attacker:* True · *status:* confirmed

**Where:** src/x32mcp/policy.py:277-279 (action optional), policy.py:306 (action check skipped when tok.action is None), policy.py:325 (stored != dict(payload)), src/x32mcp/server.py:331-336 (show-mode check precedes guard, token not consumed on SHOW_MODE_BLOCKS)
